# What Other Tools Do That Max Does Not, Does Less Well, or Does Differently

Generated by `build_max_gaps.py` from `max_gaps.json`. Edit the JSON, not this file.

Each row began as a candidate from a documentation scan of MadMapper,
TouchDesigner, Isadora, cables.gl or plugdata. **Unreviewed** rows have had one quick
check against Max's object list, refpages and the installed packages, by an
agent that does not know Max well. Treat them as leads, not findings.

## GPU compute/shaders

### Audio spectrum and waveform delivered to shaders as textures (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `audioFFT`, `audio`, `SIZE`, `ATTACK`, `DECAY`, `RELEASE`, `Audio Analysis Channels`
- **What it does:** A shader input declared as spectrum or waveform receives a one-pixel-high texture of up to 512 values, with attack, decay and release smoothing applied to each band before the shader sees it.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.catch~`, `jit.gl.texture`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). jit.catch~'s refpage: turns MSP signals into a stream of matrices, which can be sent to a shader as a texture. The FFT and the per-band smoothing are patched separately.
- **Source:** <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MaterialsDoc.md> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/writing-custom-glsl-materials>
- id: `madmapper--audio-spectrum-and-waveform-delivered-to-shaders-as-textures`

### Shader libraries for noise and 2D shapes, included by name (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `MadNoise.glsl`, `MadSDF.glsl`, `MadCommon.glsl`, `#include`, `NOISE_TEXTURE_BASED`
- **What it does:** Three libraries ship with the program and are pulled into any shader with an include line: many kinds of noise in 2D and 3D with derivatives, signed-distance-field shapes with fill, stroke, glow and repetition, and colour helpers.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.bfg`, `jit.bfg`, `JitLygia (installed package, not read)`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). jit.gl.bfg's refpage: generates textures from a library of procedural basis functions (noise, fractal, distorted) on the GPU. That gives noise as a texture source, not as functions to call inside your own shader. A package named JitLygia is in this machine's Packages folder but is not in the repo's package library and was not read.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/14.-technical-notes/glsl-shader-functions-reference> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MaterialsDoc.md>
- id: `madmapper--shader-libraries-for-noise-and-2d-shapes-included-by-name`

### Shader whose controls are built from its own header (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `Materials`, `materialColorForPixel`, `INPUTS`, `FLAGS`, `floatRange`, `generate_as_define`, `ISFs`, `AI / Code Editor`
- **What it does:** A shader file starts with a JSON block that lists its parameters. MadMapper reads it and makes the sliders, checkboxes, colour pickers and menus, grouped by label, and every one is at once available to controllers, cues and timelines. The editor recompiles as you type.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gl.pix.codebox`, `jit.gl.isf (ISF)`, `jit.gl.bfg`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'isf': package object jit.gl.isf (package entry: renders ISF files to GL textures) is in the repo's library, but no ISF package folder was found in this machine's Packages. jit.gl.pix's refpage says it can export 'isf'. jit.gl.slab and jit.gl.shader load JXS shader files with params. jit.gl.bfg's refpage lists getparamlist, getparamtype, getparamdefault, which shows shader parameters can be enumerated; a control still has to be made for each.
- **Notes:** The gap is the automatic interface and its automatic exposure to cues and controllers, not shader loading.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/writing-custom-glsl-materials> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MaterialsDoc.md> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/toolbar-and-workspace>
- id: `madmapper--shader-whose-controls-are-built-from-its-own-header`

### Shaders that output coloured paths instead of pixels (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Laser output
- **There:** `Laser Materials`, `laserMaterialFunc`, `POINT_COUNT`, `RENDER_SETTINGS`, `mm_LastFrameData`, `MadLaserMaterialShapeLibrary.glsl`, `Laser Generators`, `Laser Line FX`
- **What it does:** A laser material is a GLSL function called once per sample point that returns a 2D position, a colour and a shape number; a change of shape number starts a new path. The previous frame's points can be read back for motion that depends on the past. A small library adds move-to, line-to and curve-to calls.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gen`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). No laser-specific object. The mechanism itself (a shader writing positions and colours into a small texture, one row each) could be done with jit.gl.slab (refpage: generate and process images with fragment shaders) or jit.gl.pix, then read back; nothing interprets such a texture as paths.
- **Notes:** Status is 'partial' only because the texture trick is available in Max; the path interpretation and laser output are absent.
- **Source:** <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/LaserMaterialsDoc.md> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/Libraries/MadLaserMaterialShapeLibrary.md> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin>
- id: `madmapper--shaders-that-output-coloured-paths-instead-of-pixels`

### Editable GPU particle system (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Particles
- **There:** `Particle POP`, `Feedback POP`, `Force Radial POP`, `Palette:particlesGpu`
- **What it does:** Particles are points with attributes on the GPU. The simulation is a feedback loop of ordinary operators the user can open and change, with birth from attributes, forces, pre-roll, and millions of points.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.p.shiva`, `jit.p.vishnu`, `jit.p.bounds`, `jit.gl.tf`, `jit.mo (bundled package)`
- **Checked:** Searched Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, plus the mira package registry: 1,326 names with digests) and refpage digests and descriptions (1,389 pages: max-ref, msp-ref, jit-ref, m4l-ref and the bundled packages' docs) for particle: jit.p.shiva, jit.p.vishnu and jit.p.bounds (matrix-based, CPU) and jit.gl.tf for GPU feedback with a hand-written shader. No ready GPU particle object was found.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Particle_POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Feedback_POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:particlesGpu>
- id: `touchdesigner--editable-gpu-particle-system`

### GPU geometry operators with attributes (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Geometry, meshes and instancing
- **There:** `POP`, `Math Mix POP`, `Neighbor POP`, `Trace POP`, `Ray POP`, `Projection POP`, `GLSL POP`
- **What it does:** A family of about a hundred operators that create and change points, lines and polygons on the GPU. Every point can carry any named attributes, and operators do maths on them without code. Results render directly or drive lights and lasers.
- **Closest in Max:** `Jitter Geometry (bundled package, jit.geom.* objects)`, `jit.gl.tf`, `jit.gl.buffer`, `jit.mo (bundled package)`, `jit.gl.mesh`
- **Checked:** Found the bundled Jitter Geometry package (27 refpages, jit.geom.* plus jit.geometry: decimate, subdivide, remesh, distribute, topoints and others) and jit.gl.tf with jit.gl.buffer (transform feedback on mesh data, shader required). The jit.geom refpages were not read for whether they run on the GPU or carry custom attributes.
- **Notes:** The closest Max idea is a float jit.matrix as an attribute table, processed by jit.gen or jit.gl.pix.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Learning_About_POPs> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Neighbor_POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Ray_POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Trace_POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Projection_POP>
- id: `touchdesigner--gpu-geometry-operators-with-attributes`

### Ray casting against geometry as data (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Geometry, meshes and instancing
- **There:** `Ray POP`, `Hardware Ray Tracing`
- **What it does:** Casts a ray from every point and reports what it hits (distance, primitive, normal, attributes, bounces), optionally using the GPU's ray tracing hardware.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.phys.world`
- **Checked:** Searched Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, plus the mira package registry: 1,326 names with digests) and refpage digests and descriptions (1,389 pages: max-ref, msp-ref, jit-ref, m4l-ref and the bundled packages' docs) for ray trace, raycast: no match by name. jit.phys.world's refpage lists raytest and screenraytest methods against physics bodies. A restir.jxp pass file ships for jit.gl.pass, which is a rendering effect, not ray results as data.
- **Notes:** Hardware ray tracing is not supported on macOS, per the docs.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Ray_POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Hardware_Ray_Tracing>
- id: `touchdesigner--ray-casting-against-geometry-as-data`

### Show the final compiled shader of whatever is active (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderInfo`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderInfoUniforms_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.GetShader`
- **What it does:** Placed anywhere in the render tree, ShaderInfo shows the full vertex and fragment source of the shader in use at that point, including code added by effect ops, and its uniforms with their current values.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.material`, `jit.gl.pbr`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): jit.gl.shader has dump, getparamlist and getparamval messages for a shader you loaded yourself. jit.gl.material and jit.gl.pbr have getparamlist and getparamval. Nothing was found that prints the generated source of jit.gl.material or jit.gl.pbr.
- **Notes:** Small, but it is what makes generated materials debuggable.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/04_debugging_shaders/debugging_shaders> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/shader/shader> · <https://cables.gl/ops/Ops.Gl.Shader>
- id: `cables-gl--show-the-final-compiled-shader-of-whatever-is-active`

### Paste-in ShaderToy shaders with automatic inputs (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `GLSL Shader`
- **What it does:** A fragment shader is typed or pasted into the actor's editor and compiled on the spot. The compiler recognises ShaderToy code, so most shaders from that site run unchanged, and time and mouse inputs appear on the actor when the shader uses them.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gl.pass`
- **Checked:** Read refpages: jit.gl.slab processes textures with fragment shaders and exposes shader params (getparamlist, param); jit.gl.pass runs post-processing passes from a JXP file. Max shaders live in a .jxs XML wrapper that declares params and binds; grep of the Max install docs for 'shadertoy' found nothing. So the capability is there and deeper; the paste-and-run path is not.
- **Notes:** Only the first sixth of the KB tutorial was read. JitLygia is installed and may ease shader work; not checked.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=513> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000025645-glsl-shader-actor-tutorial>
- id: `isadora--paste-in-shadertoy-shaders-with-automatic-inputs`

### Smooth, tempo-aware animation values computed outside the shader (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `GENERATORS`, `time_base`, `animator`, `damper`, `adsr`, `linear_filter`, `ease_filter`, `pass_thru`, `bpm_sync`, `link_speed_to_global_bpm`, `CLOCK_TIME`
- **What it does:** The shader's header can ask the host for helper values: a time that advances at a controllable speed without jumping when the speed changes, optionally locked to the beat; shaped cycles; and smoothed or eased versions of any input. They arrive as uniforms.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `line`, `slide`, `transport`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). Not searched as a feature: in Max these values are made in the patch and sent to the shader as params, which is more general. MadMapper is better only in that the shader file carries the request itself, so a shared shader animates correctly with no patch around it.
- **Notes:** Refpages: line generates ramps from one value to another in a set time; slide smooths a stream of values; transport runs a tempo-based clock. These are the patch-side equivalents.
- **Source:** <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MaterialsDoc.md> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MadMapperSurfaceFxDoc.md> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/writing-custom-glsl-materials>
- id: `madmapper--smooth-tempo-aware-animation-values-computed-outside-the-shader`

### Texture feedback as one explicit operator (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Feedback and trails
- **There:** `Feedback TOP`, `Palette:feedback`, `Feedback CHOP`
- **What it does:** A feedback operator names the downstream node whose last frame it should output, so a loop is drawn with ordinary wires and no cycle error. It has a reset that passes its input through, and a pulse to clear. The control-data version does the same for channels and can add the time since the last frame.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fx.wake`, `jit.fx.tp.delay`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gl.texture`
- **Checked:** jit.fx.wake refpage: video feedback with a convolution stage; attributes fb, ff, gain, bleed. jit.fx.tp.delay refpage. No object with 'feedback' in its name is in obj-qlookup.json or the Jitter Tools list. scans/c74-forum/forum_insights.md describes a capture → matrix → gen route for trails. patching/MAX_PATCHING.md: a message feedback loop overflows the stack unless broken with delay, pipe or deferlow.
- **Advice for Max:** Use jit.fx.wake for trails. For a custom loop, feed a jit.gl.pix its own previous output through a named jit.gl.texture, and always give the loop a reset. A small abstraction with reset and bypass, like the TouchDesigner operator, is worth building.
- **Notes:** The custom-loop wiring is not tested in Max.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Feedback_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:feedback> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Feedback_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--texture-feedback-as-one-explicit-operator`

### Compute shaders in the graph (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Compute shaders
- **There:** `Ops.Extension.WebGpu.CompCompute`, `Ops.Extension.WebGpu.ComputeStorageInput`, `Ops.Extension.WebGpu.ComputeStorageOutput`, `Ops.Extension.WebGpu.ComputeUniform`, `Ops.Extension.WebGpu.ArrayToGpuBuffer`, `Ops.Extension.WebGpu.GpuBufferToArray`
- **What it does:** The WebGPU extension runs compute shaders from source typed into an op, with ops that bind storage buffers and uniforms and move arrays to and from the GPU.
- **Closest in Max:** `Compute / jit.gpu.compute`, `Compute / jit.gpu.buffer`, `Compute / jit.gpu.image`, `Compute / jit.gp.world`, `jit.gl.tf`
- **Checked:** Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, 1,323 names) and refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): 'compute' matches no core object. packages/query_packages.py search 'compute shader' and 'jit.gpu': the installed Compute package has jit.gpu.compute (runs a GLSL compute shader through Vulkan), jit.gpu.buffer, jit.gpu.image, jit.gpu.tomatrix, jit.gpu.totexture and a GPU particle system built on them. jit.gl.tf (core) does transform feedback.
- **Notes:** Not a gap on this machine: Max has it through a package, with help files and a particle system on top. The cables op pages have no written documentation. Listed so a later pass does not rediscover it.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Extension.WebGpu.CompCompute> · <https://cables.gl/ops/Ops.Extension.WebGpu>
- id: `cables-gl--compute-shaders-in-the-graph`

### Every image effect has its own blend mode, amount and mask (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ImageCompose_v4`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.DrawImage_v3`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Blur`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ColorMap_v2`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Kaleidoscope_v2`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Noise.PerlinNoise_v2`
- **What it does:** Effects in an ImageCompose chain all work on one texture, in order. Most of them carry the same three extras: a blend mode, an amount, and a mask texture that limits where the effect lands. Dry/wet and masking need no extra ops.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fx.* (83 objects)`, `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.fx.tr.xfade`, `jit.fx.co.alphablend`, `jit.gl.pix`
- **Checked:** jit.fx.blur refpage (Jitter Tools package): attributes are adapt, amt, bypass, dim, drawto, enable; no blend, mix or mask. jit.gl.slab refpage: 18 attributes, none for mixing with the input. Mixing is done by separate objects: jit.fx.tr.xfade ('a traditional cross fade'), the jit.fx.co.* compositing family, jit.xfade for matrices. Only jit.fx.blur's attribute list was read in full; the other 82 jit.fx refpages were checked by name.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, wrap an effect and a crossfade into one abstraction with an amount inlet and a mask inlet, and reuse it for every effect. Each Max effect outputs a new texture, so keep the dry cord and blend it back yourself.
- **Notes:** Max's side has one real plus: every stage's output is a cord that can be tapped or sent elsewhere. In cables that needs ImageComposeSnapshot.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ImageCompose_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.DrawImage_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Blur> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.FastBlur_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ColorMap_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Kaleidoscope_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Color_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Noise.PerlinNoise_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/2_intermediate/image_composition/image_composition>
- id: `cables-gl--every-image-effect-has-its-own-blend-mode-amount-and-mask`

### Shader uniforms become ports automatically (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.Shader.CustomShader_v2`, `UNI`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.SetUniformFloat_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderDefine`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderInfoUniforms_v2`
- **What it does:** Write 'UNI float name;' in a custom shader and the op grows a number port called name. A sampler uniform becomes a texture port. The op also has a Has Errors output. Other ops set a uniform or a #define on whichever shader is active, and read all uniform values back.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.pix`, `JXS <param> and <bind> tags`
- **Checked:** Userguide jitter/jxs_file_format.json: a uniform must also be declared in the JXS file with a <param name type default> tag and tied to a program with a <bind> tag. jit.gl.slab and jit.gl.shader refpages: values are set with a 'param <name> <values>' message; getparamlist, getparamtype, getparamdefault and getparamdescription report what the file declares. Neither object gains inlets or attributes for its params. jit.gl.pix refpage: params come from the gen patcher.
- **Advice for Max:** After loading a JXS, send getparamlist and build the controls from the answer instead of typing param names from memory. A small v8 helper that asks a slab for its param list, types and defaults and makes one labelled control per param would give Max the cables behaviour, and is worth building once.
- **Notes:** For shaders written as a gen patcher, jit.gl.pix already keeps the parameter and the code in one place.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.CustomShader_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.SetUniformFloat_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.SetUniformTexture_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderDefine> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderInfoUniforms_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/shader/shader>
- id: `cables-gl--shader-uniforms-become-ports-automatically`

### Plain GLSL files, uniforms found automatically and set by their own names (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `glsl_vertex`, `glsl_fragment`, `glsl_program`, `glsl`
- **What it does:** Each shader stage is loaded from an ordinary .vert or .frag file and compiled by its own object; glsl_program links them and becomes a state object in the chain. It discovers the shader's uniform variables itself, and a uniform called bla is set by sending the message 'bla 0.5'. Uniform values are kept across a re-link. Upstream adds one object, glsl, that takes a base name and finds the files.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.pix`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.shader (file is 'the shader description file (JXS)'; param 'sets the given shader parameter ... as defined in a JXS file'; getparamlist) and jit.gl.slab, and the userguide topic 'The JXS File Format': a JXS file is XML that must declare each parameter with a param tag and bind it to a program with a bind tag, with the GLSL inside program tags. The description says programs can also be 'submitted individually' but no message for that is listed.
- **Advice for Max:** A shader found online will not load in Jitter as it is. Keep a minimal JXS template: one param tag and one bind tag per uniform, the GLSL pasted into the program tags, then send 'param <name> <value>'. Use getparamlist to confirm Jitter sees the uniforms; an undeclared uniform is silently never set.
- **Notes:** glsl_program can also fill a uniform array from a Pd table by name; whether a JXS param can be an array was not checked.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/glsl_program-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/glsl_fragment-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/glsl_vertex-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/18.gl_shading_language.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/01.simple_texture.pd> · <https://github.com/umlaeute/Gem/blob/HEAD/help/glsl-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--plain-glsl-files-uniforms-found-automatically-and-set-by-their-own-names`

### Shader drawn once at a set size, or drawn per surface at output resolution: the user chooses (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `Generators`, `Materials`, `Render To Texture`, `Resolution`
- **What it does:** The same shader can run two ways. As a Generator it is rendered once into a texture of a size you type in, and every surface using it shares that picture. As a Material it is rendered again on each surface, at the projector's resolution, which is sharper and costs more. Dragging a Material into the Generators list switches it.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gl.shader`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.slab's refpage: it renders a shader into a texture; 'adapt' (default 1) takes the size from the input, and 'dim' (default 256 256) is used when adapt is off; 'dimscale' scales the output. Read jit.gl.shader's refpage: it compiles a shader and binds it to an object, so it is drawn with that object's geometry. jit.gl.pix's refpage calls it essentially a jit.gl.slab whose shader comes from a Gen patcher.
- **Advice for Max:** Make the choice on purpose. Give a shader that has no input an explicit size, `@adapt 0 @dim <w> <h>`: the refpage only defines the output size for that case, and its default is 256 x 256. Use the slab (one render, shared by every plane) for heavy shaders shown in several places, and jit.gl.shader on the plane itself when one surface must stay sharp at any size.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MaterialsDoc.md> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--shader-drawn-once-at-a-set-size-or-drawn-per-surface-at-output-resolution-the-user-chooses`

### Noise textures made on the GPU (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Random and probability
- **There:** `Noise TOP`, `Palette:noise`
- **What it does:** A generator makes Perlin, simplex, sparse, cell and random noise images, several of them computed on the GPU, with seed, period, harmonics and a transform of the noise space. A second input can supply the noise coordinate for each pixel.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.bfg`, `jit.bfg`, `jit.noise`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.bfg refpage: procedural basis functions (noise, fractal, distorted) run as GLSL shaders, with attributes basis, scale, offset, zoom, time and others. jit.bfg refpage: the same family on the CPU. jit.noise refpage: white noise matrices with a seed.
- **Advice for Max:** Use jit.gl.bfg for noise textures and animate its own attributes, instead of jit.noise followed by blurs and scaling.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Noise_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:noise>
- id: `touchdesigner--noise-textures-made-on-the-gpu`

### Shader uniforms as parameter pages (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `GLSL TOP`, `GLSL Multi TOP`
- **What it does:** A shader operator lists its uniforms on pages by kind: colours, vectors, arrays filled from a channel operator, matrices and compile-time constants. A button loads the uniform names from the code. Inputs arrive as ready-declared sampler arrays, and compile errors appear in an attached info table.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.shader`, `JXS <param>`, `param_connect`
- **Checked:** Userguide 'The JXS File Format': a <param> tag declares each uniform with type, default and description. jit.gl.slab refpage: param message, getparamlist, getparamdefault, file, embed, autowatch. Userguide 'Connecting Parameters': jit.gl.slab shader parameters are suppliers for param_connect, a two-way link to a UI object. No array-from-list uniform is described.
- **Advice for Max:** Declare every uniform as a <param> with a default and a description, and control it from a UI object through param_connect instead of `param name $1` message boxes. Pass a list of values to a shader as a one-row float texture.
- **Notes:** Session 1 has 'Compute shaders as an operator'; this entry is about how uniforms are exposed.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/GLSL_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/GLSL_Multi_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Write_a_GLSL_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Specialization_Constants> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Write_a_GLSL_MAT>
- id: `touchdesigner--shader-uniforms-as-parameter-pages`

### Size, pixel format and edge behaviour on every image operator (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `Resolution TOP`, `Texture Sampling Parameters`, `Texture Extend Modes`, `Texture Filtering`, `Remap TOP`
- **What it does:** Every image operator carries the same settings for output resolution, pixel format, input filtering and what happens past the edge (hold, zero, repeat, mirror). The warp operator's page warns that an 8-bit map gives jagged results and asks for 16-bit or float.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gl.texture`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.slab and jit.gl.pix refpages: attributes adapt, dim, dimscale, type, rectangle, filter. jit.gl.texture refpage: adapt, dim, type, wrap, filter, mipmap, anisotropy. Userguide 'Textures': adapt copies the size and type of the input unless dim is set.
- **Advice for Max:** Fix @dim (with @adapt 0) at the head of a chain so the working size is a decision. Give any texture that holds coordinates or data a float16 or float32 @type, for the same reason TouchDesigner's warp page gives. Set @wrap on the jit.gl.texture when a shader reads past the edge.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Resolution_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Texture_Sampling_Parameters> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Texture_Extend_Modes> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Texture_Filtering> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Remap_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Displace_TOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--size-pixel-format-and-edge-behaviour-on-every-image-operator`

### Effect chain resolution set once at the head (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ImageCompose_v4`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ImageComposeAspectRatio`, `Ops.Gl.Textures.CopyTexture_v3`
- **What it does:** The ImageCompose op at the top of a chain sets the size, pixel format, filter and wrap of the texture, and every effect under it works at that size. The performance guide's fix for a slow blur is to make that one ImageCompose smaller.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab @adapt / @dim / @dimscale / @type`, `jit.gl.texture`, `jit.gl.pix`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.slab refpage: adapt (default 1, output size follows the input), dim, dimscale ('Useful for upsampling or downsampling texture output'), type (default auto, taken from the input). jit.gl.texture refpage: adapt, dim, type. Each object in a chain carries its own size settings.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the size follows the first texture in the chain, so set it there: one jit.gl.texture or slab with @adapt 0 @dim, and leave the rest on @adapt 1. For a heavy blur put @dimscale 0.5 0.5 on the slab before it, which is the same trick the cables guide gives.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ImageCompose_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.ImageComposeAspectRatio> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Textures.CopyTexture_v3> · <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/02_usual_suspects/usual_suspects>
- id: `cables-gl--effect-chain-resolution-set-once-at-the-head`

### Geometry shaders as a separate stage object (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `glsl_geometry`, `glsl_program`
- **What it does:** glsl_geometry loads a geometry shader file; glsl_program is then told the input primitive type, the output type and the number of vertices produced. The examples draw forty circles from one primitive and turn each triangle into a fan.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.tf`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topic 'The JXS File Format' ('there can be an optional geometry shader') and jit.gl.shader (program_param: 'a geometry shader program parameter'). jit.gl.tf (Jitter Tools package) runs transform feedback on a jit.gl.mesh with a jit.gl.shader.
- **Advice for Max:** Jitter supports the stage inside the JXS file, as a third program tag. Set its input and output types with program_param before expecting it to draw.
- **Notes:** Upstream's glsl help lists compute and tessellation file endings in brackets; whether JXS accepts those stages was not checked.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/glsl_geometry-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/glsl_program-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/11.geometry.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/12.tri2fan.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--geometry-shaders-as-a-separate-stage-object`

### Grab the window into a texture for feedback (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Feedback and trails
- **There:** `pix_snap2tex`, `pix_snap`
- **What it does:** pix_snap2tex copies the current window contents straight into a texture and applies it to the shape below, so last frame's picture can be drawn into this frame. The examples use it for video feedback, full-screen motion blur and a shader game of life. pix_snap does the same but returns a CPU image.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `jit.gl.node`, `jit.gl.texture`, `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.fx.conway`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world (output_texture: 'all rendering is captured to texture and the texture name is output'), jit.gl.node (capture), jit.gl.texture and jit.gl.slab. A captured texture can be wired back to an object in the same context. jit.fx.conway (Jitter Tools package) 'performs Conway's Game of Life ... on incoming textures'.
- **Advice for Max:** In Jitter, take the frame from jit.world @output_texture 1 (or a jit.gl.node capture) and wire it back through a jit.gl.slab or jit.gl.pix that mixes it with new content. Give the loop its own jit.gl.texture so the frame being read is not the one being written.
- **Notes:** That a separate texture is needed to break the loop comes from this repo's forum notes, not from a refpage.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_snap2tex-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_snap-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/07.feedback.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/08.MotionBlur.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/04.game_of_life.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--grab-the-window-into-a-texture-for-feedback`

### Multi-pass shader pipelines built from framebuffers (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `gemframebuffer`, `glsl_program`, `mesh_square`, `single_blur`
- **What it does:** A pass is a framebuffer with a shader drawing a full-size quad; the next pass uses its texture. The examples build a two-input distortion-and-mix pipeline and a separable blur made of fourteen small passes, each pass an abstraction.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gl.pass`, `jit.fx.cf.gaussian`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.slab ('process texture data ... using fragment shaders'; inputs, outputs, dim, type), jit.gl.pix, jit.gl.pass (sub-passes defined in a JXP file; 'chaining multiple jit.gl.pass objects') and the userguide topic 'Render Passes'. A slab is the framebuffer, the quad and the shader in one box, and passes connect with a texture cord.
- **Advice for Max:** Jitter is ahead here: one jit.gl.slab per pass, wired in order. What Gem's version teaches is what a slab hides, which helps when a slab chain misbehaves: each pass has its own size, pixel type and filter, so set @dim, @type and @adapt on each slab that must not inherit them.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/08.multi_pass_rendering.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/14.blur.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/single_blur.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/07.framebuffer_and_shader.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--multi-pass-shader-pipelines-built-from-framebuffers`

### Physics computed in shaders with float framebuffers (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `gemframebuffer`, `glsl_program`, `mesh_square`
- **What it does:** One example runs a cloth-like mass and spring model entirely on the graphics card: positions live in float textures, a shader computes forces and new positions, and a vertex shader reads the texture to move a mesh. Its comment gives 64 x 92 masses and twelve links per mass.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.tf`, `jit.gl.buffer`, `jit.gp.world`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.pix and jit.gl.slab (type attribute for float textures), jit.gl.tf and jit.gl.buffer (transform feedback on a mesh, Jitter Tools package), and the Compute package's jit.gp.world. The repo's forum notes describe the same texture-feedback pattern.
- **Advice for Max:** The same design works in Jitter with float32 textures: one jit.gl.pix for the state update in a feedback loop, and a jit.gl.mesh whose shader reads that texture. Treat the Gem example as a worked plan for it.
- **Notes:** The shader sources (mass.frag, link.frag and others) were not read, only the patch.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/10.GPGPU_Physical_model.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/09.vertex_texture_fetching.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--physics-computed-in-shaders-with-float-framebuffers`

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Compute shaders
- **There:** `GLSL TOP`, `GLSL POP`, `GLSL Advanced POP`, `Compute Shader`
- **What it does:** A shader mode that is not tied to drawing: threads read and write textures or geometry buffers at any location, in several passes.

A grep of the gl3 package folder for 'compute' found nothing but its package-info file.

### Shader effects that add to any material (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects`, `FresnelGlow`, `VertexDisplacementMap_v5`, `Twist_v3`, `Bend_v2`, `SplineDeform_v2`, `ColorArea_v5`, `AreaDiscardPixel_v2`, `PerlinAreaDeform_v4`, `{{MODULE_VERTEX_POSITION}}`, `{{MODULE_COLOR}}`
- **What it does:** 35 ops that sit after a material and insert their own vertex or fragment code into whatever shader is active: displacement, twist, bend, glow, colouring or discarding inside a 3D region. Several stack on one material, and they work on built-in and hand-written shaders alike, because every shader has marked insertion points.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.material`, `jit.gl.pbr`, `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.pass`, `jit.geom.twist`, `jit.geom.displace`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): jit.gl.material and jit.gl.pbr generate shaders that adapt to textures and lights; their attribute lists show no way to add vertex or fragment code. jit.gl.shader manages one complete shader. jit.gl.pass processes rendered passes, after the geometry stage. jit.geom.twist, jit.geom.displace, jit.geom.waves deform geometry in the Jitter Geometry package, on the geometry rather than in the material's shader.
- **Notes:** The gap is composability: in Max, adding a vertex deformation to a lit material appears to mean writing a whole shader. Whether jit.gl.material's generated shader can be exported and edited was not checked.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/shader/shader> · <https://cables.gl/ops/Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.FresnelGlow> · <https://cables.gl/ops>
- id: `cables-gl--shader-effects-that-add-to-any-material`

## audio

### Analog circuit simulation from a netlist typed in the box (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `circuit~`
- **What it does:** The box text is a list of components (resistor, capacitor, diode, transistor, op-amp, tube, potentiometer, voltage source, probe), each with numbered nodes. Signal inlets can drive any value, each probe becomes an outlet, and named part models (for instance 12AX7 or 2N3904) can be chosen.
- **Closest in Max:** `gen~`, `abl.device.autofilter~`
- **Checked:** No circuit simulator in obj-qlookup.json. query_packages.py 'circuit' returned only objects whose descriptions mention circuits (for instance Abl.AutoFilter~), none that takes a netlist.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/circuit~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--analog-circuit-simulation-from-a-netlist-typed-in-the-box`

### Discrete-summation (DSF) band-limited oscillator (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `blip~`
- **What it does:** Generates a sum of cosine partials in closed form: fundamental, number of partials, lowest harmonic and a multiplier that scales each next partial. Cost does not grow with the partial count. Based on Csound's gbuzz.
- **Closest in Max:** `oscbank~`, `ioscbank~`, `gen~`
- **Checked:** Registry and package search. No object named for buzz or DSF in obj-qlookup.json. query_packages.py 'band-limited' returned CNMAT sinusoids~ and unrelated objects. oscbank~ and ioscbank~ are additive banks whose cost grows with the number of oscillators.
- **Notes:** Could be written in gen~ from the formula. The tutorial's comments call the object gbuzz~ while the box is blip~.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/blip~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.03-Sound.Generators/12-Oscillators/3.Band.Limited(Anti-Aliased)/2.[blip~].DSF.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--discrete-summation-dsf-band-limited-oscillator`

### Port of the Mutable Instruments Plaits macro-oscillator (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `plaits~`, `plaits.m~`
- **What it does:** Twenty-four synthesis engines behind one object, with timbre, harmonics and morph inputs, a trigger and a level input, and pitch in Hz, MIDI or a -1 to 1 control voltage.
- **Closest in Max:** `abl.dsp.meldosc~`, `vst~`
- **Checked:** No plaits object in the registry; query_packages.py 'plaits' returned nothing. The bundled abl.dsp.meldosc~ ('Meta-oscillator') is a different multi-engine oscillator.

### A full noise palette with seeds (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Random and probability
- **There:** `white~`, `pink~`, `brown~`, `gray~`, `velvet~`, `dust~`, `dust2~`, `perlin~`, `lfnoise~`, `stepnoise~`, `rampnoise~`
- **What it does:** Beyond white and pink: brown noise as a bounded random walk with a settable step, gray noise, velvet noise, sparse random impulses with a density (dust~), 1-D Perlin noise, and held or ramped low-rate noise. Every one takes a -seed flag and a seed message, and picks a fresh seed on each load otherwise. pink~ takes the number of octaves.
- **Closest in Max:** `noise~`, `pink~`, `rand~`, `sah~`, `abl.dsp.velvetnoise~`, `abl.dsp.pulsate~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. noise~: white, with a 'classic' attribute. pink~: no arguments or messages. rand~: random values at a rate, linearly interpolated. Bundled Ableton DSP: abl.dsp.velvetnoise~ and abl.dsp.pulsate~ ('Random pulse emitter'). No brown~, dust~ or perlin object in the registry. No seed message is listed for noise~, pink~ or rand~.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: Held low-rate noise in Max is noise~ into sah~ clocked by a phasor~; ramped is rand~. Brown noise is noise~ scaled and summed in a leaky accumulator. What Max lacks most here is a seed on its signal noise sources, so a repeatable random patch needs the randomness made at control rate (random and drunk have a seed attribute) or in gen~.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/white~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pink~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/brown~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/velvet~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/dust~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/perlin~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/lfnoise~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/stepnoise~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rampnoise~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.03-Sound.Generators/13-Noise-Chaos/2.[white~].pd>

### Event detectors that answer with impulses, not bangs (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `status~`, `changed~`, `changed2~`, `zerocross~`, `above~`, `match~`, `schmitt~`, `detect~`, `trig2bang~`
- **What it does:** Detectors for change, direction change, zero crossing (three outlets: up, down, either), threshold crossing in each direction, equality with listed values and time between triggers. Each outputs a one-sample impulse on a separate outlet per event kind, so the result can drive other signal-rate objects directly.
- **Closest in Max:** `edge~`, `change~`, `zerox~`, `thresh~`, `delta~`, `spike~`, `what~`, `sah~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. edge~: bangs on zero/non-zero transitions. change~: outputs 1, -1 or 0 for direction. zerox~: count of crossings per vector and a click outlet. thresh~: Schmitt trigger with low and high levels (signal out). spike~: reports the interval between transitions as a float. what~: impulse when the input crosses listed values.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: Most detectors exist in Max but several answer at control rate (edge~, spike~). To stay sample-accurate, build the impulse from signal objects: delta~ then a comparison gives an impulse on change; thresh~ then delta~ gives threshold crossings. The tutorial measured the cost of leaving the signal domain: a bang lands on a block edge, so a 500 ms pulse no longer measures as 500 ms.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/status~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/changed~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/changed2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/zerocross~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/above~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/match~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/schmitt~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/detect~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.01-The.Basics/01-Pd.Quickstart/6.Audio.DSP.Pd/4.AudioXControl-Rate/7.Control.Rate2.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--event-detectors-that-answer-with-impulses-not-bangs`

### Fade curves by name, and a gate-driven auto fade (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `fader~`, `autofade~`, `autofade2~`, `autofade.mc~`, `xfade~`
- **What it does:** fader~ turns a 0-1 value into a gain through one of seven lookup curves (quartic, sin, sqrt, hann, lin, hannsin, linsin). autofade~ fades its inputs in when a gate goes above zero and out when it returns, over a time in ms, with the gate's value as the top gain. The tutorial maps the curves to pan laws: linear and hann give 6 dB, sine and square-root 3 dB, the two mixed ones 4.5 dB.
- **Closest in Max:** `line~`, `selector~`, `gate~`, `matrix~`, `live.gain~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. selector~ and gate~ have a ramptime attribute that fades when the open input or outlet changes. matrix~ has a ramp attribute. No object in the registry maps a 0-1 value through a named fade curve.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: For switching, use selector~ @ramptime or gate~ @ramptime instead of hand-built fades. For a mute with a fade, line~ into *~ is linear; for an equal-power or quartic law, shape the line with a lookup (cycle~ used as a quarter-sine, or pow~). A tiny abstraction that takes a curve name would be worth having. The shape of the selector~ and gate~ fade (linear or equal power) is not stated in the refpage passage that was read.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/fader~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/autofade~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/autofade2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.02-Basic-Processing/10-Signal-Routing/2.Pan/2.Pan.Law.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--fade-curves-by-name-and-a-gate-driven-auto-fade`

### Feedback and cross-modulated sine oscillators (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Feedback and trails
- **There:** `fbsine~`, `xmod~`, `xmod2~`
- **What it does:** fbsine~ feeds a sine's output back into its own phase, with an averaging filter in the loop that can be switched off. xmod~ is two sines that modulate each other, by frequency or by phase, with both outputs available.
- **Closest in Max:** `gen~`, `cycle~`, `abl.dsp.foldfm~`
- **Checked:** No such object in obj-qlookup.json. The technique needs feedback of one sample. In a Max patcher a signal feedback loop needs a delay of at least one signal vector (tapin~/tapout~), so this has to be done in gen~ with history.
- **Notes:** The tutorial's point for anyone building it: the result depends on the sample rate, because one sample is a different time at each rate.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/fbsine~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/xmod~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/xmod2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.05-Synthesis(Basic)/24-Modulation.Synthesis/2.FM_PM/7.Feedback.Cross.Modulation/1.Feedback.pd>

### Gate and trigger housekeeping at signal rate (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `timed.gate~`, `gatehold~`, `gaterelease~`, `gatedelay~`, `trig.delay~`, `trig.delay2~`, `toggleff~`, `gate2imp~`, `trighold~`, `float2imp~`
- **What it does:** Small single-purpose objects that work on gate and impulse signals with sample accuracy: turn an impulse into a gate of fixed length, hold a gate open for a while after it closes, close it a set time after it opened, delay only the gate-on edge, delay an impulse with or without restart, flip between 0 and 1 on each trigger. Most also exist as control-rate twins.
- **Closest in Max:** `ramp~`, `sah~`, `delay~`, `edge~`, `train~`, `dk.triggerdelay~ (Data Knot)`, `dk.triggerlength~ (Data Knot)`
- **Checked:** Refpages. ramp~: one ramp started by a signal change, with duration, retrigger and reset attributes and a bang when done. sah~ has a 'duration' attribute (output duration in samples). edge~ reports transitions as bangs, so it leaves the signal domain. Package search: Data Knot (installed) has dk.triggerdelay~, dk.triggerlength~, dk.triggershape~ and others.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: A fixed-length gate from a trigger in Max is ramp~ with a duration, compared against 1 (the ramp is below 1 while it runs). A trigger delay is delay~ on the impulse. Keep these in the signal domain; going through edge~ and delay loses sample accuracy.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/timed.gate~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/gatehold~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/gaterelease~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/gatedelay~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/trig.delay~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/trig.delay2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/toggleff~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/gate2imp~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/14-Trigger-LFO-LFNoise/2.trig.gate.delay.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--gate-and-trigger-housekeeping-at-signal-rate`

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `grain.sampler~`, `grain.live~`, `grain.synth~`

Grains can be reversed by probability, started together or scattered, shaped by a chosen window, and snapped to a scale in cents.

### Matrix phase-modulation synthesizer with 2, 4 or 6 operators (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `pm2~`, `pm4~`, `pm6~`, `pm~`, `fm~`, `pm6.m~`
- **What it does:** Sine operators with a ratio, detune, level and pan each, and a full modulation matrix: every operator can modulate every other and itself. The tutorial says one pm6~ can reproduce any of the DX7's 32 routings. pm~ and fm~ are the two-oscillator versions.
- **Closest in Max:** `cycle~`, `abl.dsp.simplefm~`, `abl.dsp.fmbass~`, `abl.dsp.harmonicfm~`, `gen~`
- **Checked:** Registry and bundled packages. The Ableton DSP package that ships with Max 9.1.5 has abl.dsp.simplefm~ ('Two-operator FM oscillator'), abl.dsp.fmbass~ ('Three operator FM oscillator') and abl.dsp.harmonicfm~. No object with a free N-by-N modulation matrix was found. cycle~'s right inlet is a phase input, so operator chains can be patched; self-modulation needs a one-sample loop, which a Max patcher cannot make outside gen~.
- **Notes:** A matrix PM voice would be a good gen~ abstraction: N phase accumulators, an N-by-N index matrix with the diagonal as feedback, and the two-sample average the tutorial describes to tame feedback noise.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/pm6~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pm4~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pm2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pm~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/fm~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.05-Synthesis(Basic)/24-Modulation.Synthesis/2.FM_PM/8.DX7.pd>

### Panning and spreading across any number of channels with a spread control (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `pan~`, `pan.mc~`, `spread~`, `spread.mc~`, `rotate~`, `rotate.mc~`, `xgate2~`, `xselect2~`
- **What it does:** pan~ places one input on a ring of N outputs by an azimuth from 0 to 1 that wraps, with a spread value that widens or narrows how many neighbours receive it. spread~ distributes M inputs evenly over N outputs, rotate~ turns a whole N-channel field, and xgate2~ / xselect2~ take a fractional index so a signal can sweep across channels.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.mixdown~`, `mc.stereo~`, `matrix~`, `abc.vbap~ (abclib)`, `Panning Tools package`
- **Checked:** Refpage: mc.mixdown~ 'performs N-way panning' of each input channel to an output count, with pancontrolmode, linearpanmode and autogain attributes; in mode 0 the pan value is a circular map. Registry: no pan~ or rotate object. Packages: Panning Tools and abclib (abc.vbap~) are installed.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: For N-channel panning in Max use mc.mixdown~: its pan input is the azimuth and its pan modes cover circular and linear layouts. It has no spread control and nothing rotates a whole field, so those two still need a package or a matrix~ driven by computed gains.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/pan~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/spread~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rotate~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/xgate2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/xselect2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.02-Basic-Processing/10-Signal-Routing/2.Pan/4.[pan~].[pan.mc~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--panning-and-spreading-across-any-number-of-channels-with-a-spread-control`

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `conv~`
- **What it does:** Convolves the input with an impulse response held in an array, cut into partitions of a chosen size so the latency is one partition. The cyclone help file calls it 'much better and more powerful' than buffir~.

- **Notes:** Core Max has no long-response convolver; HISSTools is the standard answer.

### Randomness triggered by impulses, with weights (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Random and probability
- **There:** `chance~`, `rand.i~`, `rand.f~`, `drunkard~`, `randpulse~`, `randpulse2~`
- **What it does:** chance~ sends each incoming impulse to one of several outlets chosen by weights given as arguments (the weights are counts, not percentages). rand.i~ and rand.f~ output a new random value in a range on each trigger, drunkard~ takes a bounded random step, and all accept a seed and multichannel triggers.
- **Closest in Max:** `sah~`, `noise~`, `subdiv~`, `gate~`, `stash~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. sah~ on noise~ gives a new random value per trigger. subdiv~ has prob and lockprob attributes: a probability per subdivision of a phasor. No object routes a signal trigger to weighted outlets.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: A random value per trigger is noise~ into sah~. For probabilistic rhythm, subdiv~ @prob does at the clock what chance~ does after it. Weighted routing of one trigger to several destinations has to be patched (a held random value compared against thresholds).
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/chance~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rand.i~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rand.f~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/drunkard~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/randpulse~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/20-Stochastic/3.[chance~].pd>

### Ready-made delay effects: reverse, filtered-and-limited, ping-pong (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `revdelay~`, `filterdelay~`, `ping.pong~`, `delay.m~`
- **What it does:** revdelay~ plays its delay line backwards in segments. filterdelay~ puts a resonant lowpass, a DC blocker and a soft clipper inside the feedback loop, so feedback above 1 is safe. ping.pong~ is a stereo bouncing delay.
- **Closest in Max:** `tapin~`, `tapout~`, `abl.device.delay~`, `abl.device.echo~`, `upshot_reversedelay (Upshot)`
- **Checked:** Bundled Ableton DSP: abl.device.delay~ ('Stereo delay') and abl.device.echo~ ('Modulation delay'). No reverse-delay object in the registry; query_packages.py 'reverse delay' found upshot_reversedelay in an installed package.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: The filtered-delay recipe is the part to copy into any Max feedback delay: inside the loop put a lowpass, a DC blocker and a soft clipper (tanh~ or overdrive~). Then no feedback setting can blow it up. The tutorial's reverse-delay recipe: sweep the tap from 0 to the line length at twice the rate of 1 / line length.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/revdelay~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/filterdelay~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/ping.pong~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.07-Sampling.Delay.Granulation/30-Delay(Ring-Buffer)/6.Filtered.delay.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.07-Sampling.Delay.Granulation/30-Delay(Ring-Buffer)/5.Reverse.delay.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--ready-made-delay-effects-reverse-filtered-and-limited-ping-pong`

### Small equal-power pan, balance and crossfade objects (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `pan2~`, `pan4~`, `balance~`, `xfade~`, `xfade.mc~`
- **What it does:** pan2~ and balance~ take a position from -1 to 1 so that an audio signal can drive them; pan4~ takes x and y; xfade~ crossfades two sources of N channels each, equal power by default and linear with a flag.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.mixdown~`, `mc.stereo~`, `matrix~`, `*~`
- **Checked:** Registry: there is no pan~, pan2 or xfade~ object in obj-qlookup.json (the only names containing 'pan' or 'fade' are panel, jit.xfade and unrelated ones). mc.mixdown~ 2 pans to stereo.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: Max has no plain stereo panner or crossfader object, which surprises people. Use mc.mixdown~ 2 for a pan, or the tutorial's recipe for either job: position 0-1, divided by 4, into cos~ and a sine lookup, giving two gains whose squares add to 1.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/pan2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pan4~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/balance~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/xfade~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.02-Basic-Processing/10-Signal-Routing/1.Crossfade/2.SinCos-EqualPower-Crossfade.pd>

### Spectral freeze as an object (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `pvoc.freeze~`, `freeze~`
- **What it does:** A gate freezes the input's spectrum and holds it as a steady sound until released. One version is a phase vocoder at speed zero with a crossfade in and out; the other holds the last frame of a tracked-partial analysis in an oscillator bank.
- **Closest in Max:** `pfft~`, `abl.device.spectraltime~`, `fftz.residency~ (FFTease)`, `dk.onsetfreeze~ (Data Knot)`
- **Checked:** No freeze object in core MSP; it is a pfft~ patch. Bundled abl.device.spectraltime~ ('Spectral delay') and several ABL reverbs were returned by a package search for 'freeze'. FFTease and Data Knot (installed) have dedicated objects.

### Spectral morph between two sounds (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `morph~`
- **What it does:** Crossfades the magnitudes and, separately, the phases of two inputs in the frequency domain, so it can cross-synthesize either way or morph fully.
- **Closest in Max:** `pfft~`, `fftz.morphine~ (FFTease)`, `fluid.audiotransport~ (FluCoMa)`
- **Checked:** No such object in core MSP; it is a small pfft~ patch. Package search 'spectral morph' found FFTease's morphine~ and FluCoMa's audio transport, both installed.
- **Notes:** The tutorial's cheaper cross-synthesis: divide A's real and imaginary parts by A's magnitude, multiply both by B's magnitude; no polar conversion.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/morph~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.09-Spectral.Processing/35-Advanced/1.Cross.Synthesis/3.[morph~].pd>

### Sustain pedal with a sostenuto-like mode (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `suspedal`
- **What it does:** Holds note-offs while the pedal is down. A 'tonal' mode sustains only the notes already held when the pedal went down, so new notes play normally. Four policies for repeated notes are offered.
- **Closest in Max:** `sustain`
- **Checked:** sustain refpage: holds note-offs until released; repeatmode 0 (historical), 1 (re-trigger), 2 (stop last). No mode that sustains only the notes held at pedal-down.
- **Notes:** A sostenuto needs a little logic around sustain: at pedal-down, capture the set of held notes; let later note-offs pass unless the note is in that set.

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `shaper~`, `power~`, `drive~`, `fold~`, `wrap2~`

### A bang for every DSP block ([bang~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `bang~`
- **What it does:** [bang~] outputs a bang after each block of audio is computed, at the same logical time. Used to poll analysis objects in step with the audio.
- **Closest in Max:** `snapshot~`, `metro`, `dspstate~`
- **Checked:** No bang~ in the registry. snapshot~ refpage: has its own interval attribute and reports periodically without an external clock.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the polling clock is built into the readers: give snapshot~ (and the meters) an interval instead of banging them.

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `slop~`

### Per-subpatch block size, overlap and sample rate ([block~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `block~`, `switch~`, `inlet~`, `outlet~`, `hold`, `pad`, `lin`
- **What it does:** [block~ size overlap resample] sets the block size, an overlap factor and an up- or down-sampling factor for one window and its children. [inlet~] and [outlet~] convert at the boundary, with a choice of hold, zero-pad or linear resampling, and overlap-add on the way out.
- **Closest in Max:** `poly~`, `pfft~`, `gen~`
- **Checked:** poly~ refpage: vs attribute (signal vector size for the loaded patch), up and down arguments/messages, resampling attribute (high-quality filters, on by default). pfft~ handles FFT size and overlap. No per-subpatcher block control outside poly~/pfft~ found in the registry (no 'block' names).
- **Advice for Max:** To run part of a Max patch at another vector size or oversampled, load it in poly~ with vs / up. Max filters the resampling for you; Pd leaves the anti-alias filter to the patcher.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/block~-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/inlet-outlet-help.pd> · <Documentation/3.audio.examples/J07.oversampling.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>

### Signal threshold detector that outputs bangs, with debounce (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `threshold~`
- **What it does:** [threshold~ high high-ms low low-ms] is a Schmitt trigger: a bang out the left when the signal reaches the high level, a bang out the right when it falls below the low one, each with its own debounce time.
- **Closest in Max:** `thresh~`, `edge~`
- **Checked:** thresh~ refpage: outputs a 0/1 signal with low and high thresholds. edge~ refpage: bangs on zero/non-zero transitions. No debounce time on either.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max chain thresh~ into edge~ for the two bangs, and add debounce with onebang or a delay.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/threshold~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--signal-threshold-detector-that-outputs-bangs-with-debounce`

### Sub-sample-accurate ramps scheduled from one message ([vline~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `vline~`, `line~`
- **What it does:** [vline~] takes target, ramp time and start delay, places breakpoints between samples, and queues several segments from one message box. [line~] rounds to block edges.
- **Closest in Max:** `line~`, `curve~`, `zigzag~`
- **Checked:** line~ refpage: 'sample-accurate when Scheduler in Audio Interrupt is enabled'; takes lists of value/time pairs. zigzag~ and curve~ refpages read. None takes a per-segment start delay in the form vline~ does.
- **Advice for Max:** For tight envelopes in Max, turn on Scheduler in Audio Interrupt, or generate the envelope at signal rate (gen~, function into line~). A Pd message like '1 10, 0 300 150' becomes a single line~ list with the hold as a segment.
