### Audio analysis op made for visuals (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Ops.WebAudio.AudioAnalyzer_v2`, `Ops.WebAudio.FFTAreaAverage_v3`, `Ops.WebAudio.AnalyzerTexture_v2`
- **What it does:** One op takes audio and gives an FFT array, a waveform array, the frequency of each bin and RMS level, with ports for FFT size, smoothing and dB range. FFTAreaAverage averages a chosen rectangle of the spectrum into one number, for 'how much bass'. AnalyzerTexture writes a spectrogram texture.
- **Closest in Max:** `pfft~`, `fffb~`, `peakamp~`, `snapshot~`, `jit.catch~`, `spectroscope~`
- **Checked:** spectroscope~ refpage: a display; its one outlet is listed as undefined, so it gives no data. pfft~ refpage: spectral processing inside a subpatch with fftin~ and fftout~. fffb~ refpage: a bank of bandpass filters with one signal outlet each. peakamp~ and snapshot~ refpages: signal level to a number at an interval. jit.catch~ refpage: signals into matrices, four modes. Getting 'a list of band levels' takes several of these wired together.
- **Advice for Max:** For audio-reactive visuals in Max, a filter bank is the short path: fffb~ into one peakamp~ per band, joined into a list and smoothed with slide. Package that as one abstraction with band count and smoothing as settings; it is the most reused piece in this kind of patch and Max does not ship it.
- **Notes:** Installed third-party packages (FluCoMa, zsa and others in packages/package_objects.json) have analysis objects; they were not compared here.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.WebAudio.AudioAnalyzer_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.WebAudio.FFTAreaAverage_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.WebAudio.AnalyzerTexture_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/8_audio/2_realtime_visualization/realtime_visualization>
- id: `cables-gl--audio-analysis-op-made-for-visuals`

### Feedback set as a decay time on delays, combs and allpasses (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Feedback and trails
- **There:** `fbdelay~`, `allpass.rev~`, `comb.filt~`, `comb.rev~`, `ffdelay~`, `del~`
- **What it does:** The feedback amount on these objects is, by default, a time: how many ms the repeats take to fall 60 dB. A -gain flag switches to a plain coefficient. Delay time is in ms with -samps for samples, all have 'clear', and the plain delays have 'freeze'.
- **Closest in Max:** `comb~`, `allpass~`, `teeth~`, `tapin~`, `tapout~`, `delay~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. comb~: arguments max delay, delay, gain, feedforward and feedback coefficients. allpass~: max delay, delay, gain. teeth~: separate feedforward and feedback delays with gains. tapin~ has a freeze message. delay~ counts in samples or tempo-relative values. None takes a decay time.
- **Advice for Max:** Thinking in decay time is the better interface, because a fixed coefficient rings for a different time at every delay length. In Max compute the coefficient first: the tutorial gives gain = exp(ln(0.001) * delay / t60), with both times in the same unit. Send that to comb~ or allpass~ whenever the delay time changes.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/fbdelay~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/allpass.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/comb.filt~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/comb.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/ffdelay~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/del~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.10-Filters&Reverb/36-Filters(Advanced)/5.Resonators/4.[comb.filt~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--feedback-set-as-a-decay-time-on-delays-combs-and-allpasses`

### Smoothers that take milliseconds, with separate up and down times (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `glide~`, `glide2~`, `lag~`, `lag2~`, `smooth~`, `smooth2~`, `slew~`, `slew2~`, `glide`, `lag`, `smooth`, `slew`
- **What it does:** Four kinds of signal smoother with one naming scheme: glide~ (fixed time, linear or a power curve), lag~ (one-pole filter, time to settle), smooth~ (fixed time with a curve factor shared with the envelope objects), slew~ (fixed speed in units per second). A 2 suffix gives separate rising and falling values, and each has a control-rate twin without the tilde.
- **Closest in Max:** `rampsmooth~`, `slide~`, `deltaclip~`, `line~`, `onepole~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. rampsmooth~: arguments are ramp-up and ramp-down in samples. slide~: 'Filter a signal logarithmically' with slide-up and slide-down values. deltaclip~: limits the change per sample between a minimum and maximum slope. line~ takes times in ms but only follows messages, not a signal.
- **Advice for Max:** The Max objects do the same four jobs but count in samples: rampsmooth~ is glide2~, slide~ is close to lag2~, deltaclip~ is slew2~. Convert with mstosamps~, and for a slew limit in units per second divide by the sample rate, or the patch changes behaviour when the sample rate does. A small set of abstractions that take ms would remove a common source of error. The tutorial's lag formula: coefficient = exp(ln(0.001) / (ms * sample rate in kHz)).
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/glide~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/glide2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/lag~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/lag2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/smooth~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/slew~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/slew2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/16-Lines/2.[glide~].[lag~].[slew~].[slop~].pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.10-Filters&Reverb/36-Filters(Advanced)/8.Lag.slew/1.Lag.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--smoothers-that-take-milliseconds-with-separate-up-and-down-times`

### A stated rule for the delay and ordering of wireless signal connections (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `send~`, `receive~`, `delwrite~`, `delread~`, `throw~`, `catch~`
- **What it does:** If the reader is sorted before the writer the signal is exactly one block late, otherwise not at all. DSP order follows cords between subpatches, so putting writer and reader in two subpatches joined by a dummy cord fixes the order.
- **Closest in Max:** `send~`, `receive~`, `tapin~`, `tapout~`
- **Checked:** send~ and receive~ refpages: 'can introduce a delay of varying lengths', depending on the number of objects, feedback loops and multiprocessing between top-level patchers. No rule for forcing an order is given there.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, do not carry a timing-critical or feedback signal over send~/receive~. Use a cord, tapin~/tapout~ for delay loops, or gen~. Keep send~/receive~ for mixes and meters where a vector of delay does not matter.
- **Source:** <Documentation/3.audio.examples/G05.execution.order.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/send-receive-tilde-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/delay-tilde-objects-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--a-stated-rule-for-the-delay-and-ordering-of-wireless-signal-connections`

### Audio level watcher with a noise floor that rescales, and a trigger level (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Random and probability
- **There:** `Sound Level Watcher`, `Sound Level Watcher++`, `Sound Frequency Watcher`, `Sound Frequency Bands`
- **What it does:** Reports the level of a live input as 0 to 100. A 'min level' input sets a floor: below it nothing is sent, and above it the output is rescaled so the floor reads 0. A 'trig level' input fires a trigger each time the level rises past it. The frequency versions do the same for one band or for a run of FFT bands.
- **Closest in Max:** `peakamp~`, `zmap`, `past`, `thresh~`, `dot.schmitt`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `peakamp~` reports the peak amplitude since its last report, at an interval in ms. `zmap` maps and clips a range. `past` bangs when a number rises above a threshold. `thresh~` detects a signal above a set level (digest only).
- **Advice for Max:** The Max chain is `peakamp~ 50` to `zmap <floor> 1. 0. 1.` to a threshold with a dead band. Copy the floor-and-rescale step: without it a quiet room never reads zero and every mapping after it is offset.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=739 (Actors Reference: Sound Level Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=741 (Actors Reference: Sound Level Watcher++)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=737 (Actors Reference: Sound Frequency Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=736 (Actors Reference: Sound Frequency Bands)>
- id: `isadora--audio-level-watcher-with-a-noise-floor-that-rescales-and-a-trigger-level`

### Sample player with numbered voices and routing written as text (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Polyphony and voices
- **There:** `Sound Player`, `Tone Generator`, `Sound Preload`
- **What it does:** Sound Player loads a file into memory and plays it on one of 16 'sampler channels'. A second sound on the same channel cuts the first off; different channels overlap; channel 0 picks a free one. Routing from file channels to device outputs is one text input such as 's1:d3, s2:d4@-3dB', so another actor can compute it. Loops have a crossfade length in samples.
- **Closest in Max:** `playlist~`, `groove~`, `buffer~`, `matrix~`, `poly~`, `polybuffer~`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `playlist~`: clips with a `selection`, `loop`, `speed`, `channelcount` and `loopreport`. `groove~`: loop points, and `loopinterp` for interpolation around them. `matrix~`: any inlet to any outlet with a gain, set by `connect` or list messages, a `ramp` time to avoid clicks, and a dictionary form of its whole state. `poly~` has voice allocation and a `steal` attribute.
- **Advice for Max:** Max splits this into parts, and each part is stronger: `poly~` for voices and stealing, `matrix~` for routing. Keep all routing in one `matrix~` with `@ramp`, driven by messages, so a routing can be stored and recalled as data, which is what Isadora's routing string is for.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=742 (Actors Reference: Sound Player)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=790 (Actors Reference: Tone Generator)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=745 (Actors Reference: Sound Preload)>
- id: `isadora--sample-player-with-numbered-voices-and-routing-written-as-text`

### Audio analysis arrives as a few named channels: amplitude, bass, medium, treble, beat counts (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tempo and clocks
- **There:** `Audio Analysis Channels`, `amplitude`, `bass`, `medium`, `treble`, `bpm`, `beatCount`, `4_beats`
- **What it does:** The audio input is analysed once, on channels chosen in the preferences, and offered everywhere as named values. Any slider can be tied to one from its right-click menu.
- **Closest in Max:** `peakamp~`, `fffb~`, `snapshot~`, `slide`
- **Checked:** Read the refpages: peakamp~ reports a signal's peak amplitude; fffb~ is a fixed bank of bandpass filters with one outlet per band; snapshot~ turns a signal into numbers at an interval; slide smooths a stream of values. Max has far more analysis than MadMapper, but nothing arrives pre-named.
- **Advice for Max:** Build the analysis once, in one place: input, a three-band split, an envelope per band, then `s AMP`, `s BASS`, `s MID`, `s TREBLE`. Every visual reads those names. Analysing the same input separately beside each effect is the usual Max mistake this avoids.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/9.-master-settings/audio-input-and-beat-detection> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences>
- id: `madmapper--audio-analysis-arrives-as-a-few-named-channels-amplitude-bass-medium-treble-beat-counts`

### Audio analysis delivered as ready control channels (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Palette:audioAnalysis`, `Audio Spectrum CHOP`, `Analyze CHOP`, `Envelope CHOP`, `CHOP to TOP`
- **What it does:** A ready component outputs low, mid and high levels, kick and snare detection, rhythm and spectral centroid as channels, each with an on/off switch to save time. The spectrum operator can output one sample per hertz, and any channel set can be written into a float texture.
- **Closest in Max:** `peakamp~`, `average~`, `snapshot~`, `pfft~`, `jit.catch~`, `zsa.centroid~ (package)`
- **Checked:** peakamp~, average~ (bipolar, absolute or rms) and snapshot~ refpages. fft~ and pfft~ refpages. spectroscope~ refpage: a display, with no data outlet listed. jit.catch~ refpage: signal into matrices in four modes. Package search: zsa.centroid~ and pnp.centroid~ for spectral centroid, CNMAT 2threshattack~ and Data Knot dk.cvonset~ for onsets.
- **Advice for Max:** For band levels use filters or pfft~ into average~ rms and snapshot~. For a spectrum as a texture use jit.catch~ into jit.gl.texture. An abstraction that outputs low, mid, high and onset as plain numbers would give students what the TouchDesigner component gives.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:audioAnalysis> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Audio_Spectrum_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Analyze_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Envelope_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/CHOP_to_TOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--audio-analysis-delivered-as-ready-control-channels`

### A reverb toolkit from building blocks to finished units (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `echo.rev~`, `fdn.rev~`, `mono.rev~`, `stereo.rev~`, `free.rev~`, `giga.rev~`, `plate.rev~`, `allpass.rev~`, `comb.rev~`
- **What it does:** Separate objects for each classic design: a feedforward early-reflection stage with a settable number of stages, a feedback delay network whose line count and lengths can be set, freeverb, gigaverb, a Dattorro plate, and the single comb and allpass units they are made of.
- **Closest in Max:** `abl.device.reverb~`, `abl.dsp.darkhall~`, `abl.dsp.prism~`, `abl.dsp.quartz~`, `abl.dsp.shimmer~`, `abl.dsp.tides~`, `cverb~`, `bp.Gigaverb (BEAP)`, `bp.Freeverb (BEAP)`
- **Checked:** Bundled: Ableton DSP has abl.device.reverb~ ('Plate reverb') and five abl.dsp reverbs; cverb~ is 'A simple monaural reverberator'; the repo's preferred-objects table lists BEAP's bp.Gigaverb and bp.Freeverb. No configurable feedback-delay-network object was found.
- **Advice for Max:** For a finished reverb Max has more choice than ELSE. What ELSE adds is the kit for building one. The tutorial's rules for the builder: early reflections from feedforward delays whose times are not multiples of each other (primes); four to sixteen feedback lines mixed through sums and differences; a lowpass in the loop for damping.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/echo.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/fdn.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/free.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/giga.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/plate.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/mono.rev~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.10-Filters&Reverb/37-Reverberation/2.[echo.rev~].pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.10-Filters&Reverb/37-Reverberation/3.FDN/1.FDN.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--a-reverb-toolkit-from-building-blocks-to-finished-units`

### ADSR with velocity-scaled gate, curve choice, retrigger inlet and a status outlet (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `adsr~`, `asr~`, `decay~`
- **What it does:** The gate's value sets the peak, and a float gate is read as MIDI velocity (0-127 becomes 0-1). Curves can be exponential with a factor, linear, or a one-pole 'lag' shape. A second inlet retriggers without closing the gate. A right outlet is 1 while the envelope is sounding, meant for switching a voice's DSP on and off. Multichannel gates give one envelope per channel.
- **Closest in Max:** `adsr~`, `function`, `line~`, `curve~`, `thispoly~`
- **Checked:** adsr~ refpage: signal-controllable attack, decay, sustain, release; a non-zero number triggers with that amplitude; attributes legato, retrigger, maxsustain, triggermode; outlets include a 'mute outlet' and a 'new envelope trigger'. No curve attribute is listed.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's adsr~ already has the status idea: its mute outlet is made to drive thispoly~ so an idle poly~ voice stops computing. Use it. What it lacks is a curve setting; for a shaped envelope use function with curve~, or twist~ after a ramp. The velocity convention (gate value is the peak) is the same in both.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/adsr~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/asr~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/decay~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/17-Envelopes/2.Classic.Types/04.ADSR/1.ADSR.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--adsr-with-velocity-scaled-gate-curve-choice-retrigger-inlet-and-a-status-outlet`

### Biquad coefficient tools, and the order trap between Pd and Max (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `bicoeff`, `bicoeff2`, `biquads~`, `biplot`, `zbiplot`, `coeff2pz`, `pz2coeff`
- **What it does:** bicoeff is a draggable response graph that outputs biquad coefficients; bicoeff2 does the same from numbers. biquads~ runs up to 50 sections from one list. biplot draws the response of a coefficient list, zbiplot draws its poles and zeros, and two converters go between coefficients and pole/zero coordinates.
- **Closest in Max:** `filtergraph~`, `filtercoeff~`, `cascade~`, `zplane~`, `filterdesign`
- **Checked:** Refpages. filtergraph~: 'Filter editor' with cascade support and display mode. cascade~: 'Cascaded series of biquad filters' from lists of five. zplane~: 'Graph filter poles and zeros on the Z-plane'. biquad~: y[n] = a0*x[n] + a1*x[n-1] + a2*x[n-2] - b1*y[n-1] - b2*y[n-2], list order a0 a1 a2 b1 b2.
- **Advice for Max:** Max has an equal for each of these. The thing to take away is a porting trap: the tutorial states that Pd's biquad~ takes its five numbers as feedback first (fb1 fb2 ff0 ff1 ff2) and adds the feedback terms, while Max's takes a0 a1 a2 b1 b2 and subtracts them. A coefficient list copied from a Pd patch must be reordered and its two feedback values negated.
- **Notes:** The Pd order is as the tutorial states it; it was not checked against Pd's own reference.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/bicoeff-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/bicoeff2-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/biquads~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/biplot-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/zbiplot-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/coeff2pz-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pz2coeff-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.10-Filters&Reverb/36-Filters(Advanced)/1.Basic.Theory/5.[biquad~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--biquad-coefficient-tools-and-the-order-trap-between-pd-and-max`

### Butterworth filters of any even order from one argument (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `lop.bw~`, `hip.bw~`, `brickwall~`, `crossover~`
- **What it does:** Lowpass and highpass Butterworth filters whose order (2 to 100) is the second argument, built on the biquad cascade. brickwall~ is a fixed 10th-order lowpass and crossover~ a third-order split with both outputs.
- **Closest in Max:** `filterdesign`, `cascade~`, `cross~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. filterdesign: 'Create a filter specification' with order, response, topology, frequency, passband_ripple and stopband_attenuation attributes, output as a dictionary that cascade~ and biquad~ accept. cross~: 'Third-order crossover filter'.
- **Advice for Max:** Max is stronger here: filterdesign covers more than Butterworth and feeds cascade~ directly. Use it instead of chaining biquad~ objects by hand.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/lop.bw~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/hip.bw~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/brickwall~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/crossover~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--butterworth-filters-of-any-even-order-from-one-argument`

### Classic effects as single objects (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `chorus~`, `flanger~`, `phaser~`, `tremolo~`, `rm~`, `vibrato~`, `crusher~`, `drive~`, `vocoder~`, `freq.shift~`, `pitch.shift~`
- **What it does:** One box each for chorus, flanger, phaser with a settable number of stages, tremolo, ring modulation, vibrato, bit crusher with decimation, three kinds of soft clipping, an N-band channel vocoder, frequency shifter and granular pitch shifter. The tutorial shows the patch behind each one.
- **Closest in Max:** `abl.dsp.chorus~`, `abl.dsp.flanger~`, `abl.dsp.phaser~`, `abl.dsp.ringmod~`, `abl.dsp.vibrato~`, `abl.device.redux~`, `overdrive~`, `degrade~`, `freqshift~`, `pitchshift~`, `BEAP modules`
- **Checked:** Bundled packages in Max 9.1.5. Ableton DSP has abl.dsp.chorus~, abl.dsp.flanger~, abl.dsp.phaser~, abl.dsp.ringmod~, abl.dsp.vibrato~, abl.dsp.overdrive~, abl.dsp.saturator~, abl.device.redux~. Core MSP has overdrive~, degrade~, freqshift~ and pitchshift~. No channel vocoder object was found in the registry.
- **Advice for Max:** Since Max 9 bundles the Ableton DSP objects, reach for abl.dsp.* before building a chorus, flanger or phaser from tapin~ and LFOs. A channel vocoder still has to be built: the tutorial's version is N bands, each a bandpass on the carrier multiplied by the running RMS of the modulator, followed by a normalizer.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/chorus~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/flanger~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/phaser~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/tremolo~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rm~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/vibrato~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/crusher~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/drive~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/vocoder~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.06-Filters(Basic)/28-Filter.FX/2.Multiband-Processing/5.Vocoder/2.Vocoder.pd>

### Ideal and band-limited oscillators as named twins (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `bl.saw~`, `bl.saw2~`, `bl.tri~`, `bl.square~`, `bl.vsaw~`, `bl.imp~`, `bl.imp2~`, `bl.osc~`, `bl.wavetable~`
- **What it does:** Each classic wave exists twice: a mathematically exact one that aliases (saw~, tri~, square~) and a band-limited one with the same inlets and a bl. prefix. The help files say to use the exact one for LFO work and the bl. one for audible synthesis. The tutorial says bl.saw~, bl.tri~, bl.square~ and bl.vsaw~ use PolyBLEP and bl.imp~ uses BLIT.
- **Closest in Max:** `saw~`, `tri~`, `rect~`, `phasor~`, `triangle~`, `train~`
- **Checked:** Refpage digests: saw~ 'Antialiased sawtooth oscillator', tri~ 'Antialiased triangular oscillator' (with a duty-cycle argument), rect~ 'Antialiased rectangular (pulse) oscillator'. phasor~'s refpage says to use it for sample-accurate control and to use saw~ for smoother sound. triangle~ and trapezoid~ are lookup shapes driven by a phasor~.
- **Advice for Max:** Max draws the same line with different names: saw~ / tri~ / rect~ are the anti-aliased ones, and the exact shapes come from phasor~ (plus triangle~, or a comparison for a square). Use the phasor~-based shapes for LFOs, clocks and anything that must hit exact values, and the anti-aliased objects for sound. A band-limited variable saw-to-triangle is covered by tri~'s duty cycle.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/bl.saw~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/bl.square~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/bl.tri~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/bl.vsaw~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/bl.osc~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.03-Sound.Generators/12-Oscillators/3.Band.Limited(Anti-Aliased)/5.BLIT.BLEP&more.objects.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--ideal-and-band-limited-oscillators-as-named-twins`

### Independent pitch and time on files and on live input (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tuning and pitch
- **There:** `gran.player~`, `pvoc.player~`, `pvoc.live~`, `stretch.shift~`, `pitch.shift~`
- **What it does:** Players with separate speed and transposition, in a granular version and a phase-vocoder version, plus the same pair for live input through a ring buffer.
- **Closest in Max:** `groove~`, `sfplay~`, `playlist~`, `stretch~`, `pitchshift~`, `retune~`, `abl.dsp.pitchshifter~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. groove~, sfplay~ and playlist~ all carry timestretch, pitchshift, pitchshiftcent, formant and quality attributes. pitchshift~: 'Ztx-based real-time pitchshifting'. stretch~ changes a buffer offline. retune~ detects and corrects pitch.
- **Advice for Max:** Max is ahead: time-stretch and pitch-shift are attributes on the players themselves (groove~ @timestretch 1 @pitchshiftcent 300), and pitchshift~ handles live input. No separate object or patch is needed.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/gran.player~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pvoc.player~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pvoc.live~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/stretch.shift~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pitch.shift~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.09-Spectral.Processing/35-Advanced/3.Phase.Vocoder/1.TimeStretch-PitchShift/2.Cartesian.[pvoc.player~].pd>

### Monophonic note handling with priority, legato and glide (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `mono`, `mono~`
- **What it does:** Remembers held notes and outputs one at a time with last, highest or lowest priority; releasing a note returns to the one still held. A legato mode keeps the first velocity, a glide time is built in, and mono~ outputs pitch and velocity as signals plus an impulse for retriggering envelopes.
- **Closest in Max:** `ddg.mono`, `poly`
- **Checked:** ddg.mono refpage: 'Monophonic Synth Controller for Virtual Synths... implements last/high/low note priority and legato/retrigger phrasing', with mode and legatomode attributes.
- **Advice for Max:** Max ships this as ddg.mono, which few people know because of its name. Use it instead of patching note memory by hand; add glide with a line~ or slide~ on the pitch.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/mono-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/mono~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/18-MIDI-CV-OSC-Net/1.MIDI/3.Monophony.Polyphony/1.Monophonic.pd>

### Multichannel oscillator from a list of frequencies in the box (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Many channels from one generator
- **There:** `sine~ -mc`, `saw~ -mc`, `imp~ -mc`, `white~ -ch`, `dust~ -ch`, `sigs~`, `nchs~`
- **What it does:** A generator typed with -mc and a list of frequencies outputs one multichannel cord with an oscillator per value; 'set <channel> <freq>' changes one of them, and noise sources take -ch <n> for a channel count. A multichannel frequency input does the same thing, so one sine~ can be a whole additive bank with per-partial sync and phase modulation.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.cycle~`, `mc.sig~`, `mc.list~`, `mc.evolve~`, `mc.range~`
- **Checked:** Registry: mc.cycle~, mc.saw~, mc.sig~, mc.list~, mc.range~, mc.gradient~, mc.evolve~ all exist. Refpages: mc.list~ 'Create a multichannel signal from a list of values'; mc.sig~ takes a list; every mc. object has a chans attribute.
- **Advice for Max:** The Max idiom is 'mc.list~ 220 330 440' (or mc.sig~) into the frequency inlet of mc.cycle~, with @chans setting the count. That is two boxes where ELSE uses a flag, and it reads just as clearly. Prefer it over N copies of cycle~.
- **Notes:** The MC 'function' messages that spread values across channels (deviate, spread and so on) are described in the Max user guide and were not re-read here.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/sine~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/impulse~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/white~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/dust~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/sigs~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.05-Synthesis(Basic)/23-Additive.Synthesis/7.[sine~].and.others.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/14-Trigger-LFO-LFNoise/9.Multichannel.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--multichannel-oscillator-from-a-list-of-frequencies-in-the-box`

### One breakpoint envelope object with sustain point, loop, per-segment curves and legato (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `envgen~`, `function`, `function~`, `envelope~`
- **What it does:** envgen~ takes a list of duration-target pairs (up to 1024 segments) and runs it on a bang, gate or impulse. It can hold at a chosen breakpoint until the gate closes, loop, restart from the current level (legato) or ramp to the start over a set retrigger time, and each segment can have its own curve: a factor, a power, a one-pole lag, sine or hann. function is the drawing GUI for it and function~ reads the same list by a 0-1 phase.
- **Closest in Max:** `function`, `line~`, `curve~`, `zigzag~`, `shape~`, `ramp~`, `twist~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. function: breakpoint editor with sustain points (sustain, clearsustain, next) and a setcurve message. curve~: 'Exponential ramp generator' taking target, time and curve factor. zigzag~: 'Linked list function editor' with loopmode, speed and jump. shape~: 'Time-scaled Breakpoint Envelope Generator' read by a phasor ramp, same list format as line~. ramp~: one ramp triggered by a signal.
- **Advice for Max:** Max spreads this over function (drawing and sustain points), line~ or curve~ (playing), zigzag~ (looping and stepping) and shape~ (phase-driven, the equal of function~). The combination function into curve~ covers most of envgen~. Two things to watch when porting: ELSE lists are duration then target, Max's are target then time; and an ELSE list only arms the envelope, where a Max list sent to line~ starts it.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/envgen~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/function-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/function~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/envelope~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/16-Lines/5.[envgen~].pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/17-Envelopes/2.Classic.Types/5.AHDSR.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--one-breakpoint-envelope-object-with-sustain-point-loop-per-segment-curves-and-legato`

### One inlet layout for every oscillator: frequency, sync, phase modulation (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `sine~`, `cosine~`, `saw~`, `saw2~`, `tri~`, `square~`, `vsaw~`, `pulse~`, `impulse~`, `imp~`, `parabolic~`, `gaussian~`, `wavetable~`
- **What it does:** Every ELSE oscillator has the same three inlets: frequency (negative values run backwards), a sync inlet that resets phase on an impulse, and a phase-offset inlet for phase modulation. Flags add a soft-sync mode (-soft, the wave reverses direction instead of jumping) and MIDI-pitch input (-midi).
- **Closest in Max:** `cycle~`, `phasor~`, `saw~`, `tri~`, `rect~`
- **Checked:** Read the refpages. cycle~: inlets are frequency and phase, plus a 'reset' message; no signal sync inlet. saw~: inlets 'frequency' and 'sync trigger', message synctrig. tri~: frequency, duty cycle, sync input. rect~: has synctrig. phasor~: a 'reset' message and a phaseoffset attribute. None lists a soft-sync mode or a MIDI-pitch input.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, signal-rate hard sync is built in only on saw~, tri~ and rect~; phase modulation is built in only on cycle~ (right inlet). For a synced or phase-modulated version of any other shape, drive a lookup from one phasor~ so sync and PM are just arithmetic on the phase. Soft sync (reverse direction at each master period) has to be built, for instance in gen~. Worth copying in any Max oscillator abstraction: keep the same inlet order on every member of the family.
- **Notes:** Whether phasor~ accepts a signal for reset was not checked.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/sine~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/saw~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/square~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/vsaw~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/impulse~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.03-Sound.Generators/12-Oscillators/2.Sync/1.HardSync.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.03-Sound.Generators/12-Oscillators/2.Sync/3.Soft-sync_2.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--one-inlet-layout-for-every-oscillator-frequency-sync-phase-modulation`

### Oscillator and resonator banks set by lists, one channel per partial (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `oscbank~`, `resonbank~`, `resonbank2~`, `bpbank~`
- **What it does:** A bank whose size is the length of the lists it is given: partial ratios, amplitudes, phases or decay times, and a ramp time per element. One inlet multiplies all frequencies, so it acts as the fundamental. With -mc each partial goes out on its own channel. The resonator banks take an excitation signal and are meant for modal synthesis.
- **Closest in Max:** `oscbank~`, `ioscbank~`, `fffb~`, `mc.cycle~`, `resonators~ (CNMAT)`, `abl.dsp.modalresonator~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. oscbank~ and ioscbank~: fixed number of oscillators as the first argument, frequency and magnitude set by index, with smoothing factors. fffb~: fixed filter bank with freq, gain and Q messages. Package search: CNMAT resonators~ (installed) and the bundled abl.dsp.modalresonator~.
- **Advice for Max:** For a bank whose partials are ratios of one fundamental, the MC route is clearer in Max than oscbank~: mc.list~ of ratios times a fundamental into mc.cycle~, then mc.mixdown~. For modal synthesis use CNMAT resonators~ (frequency, gain, decay triples) or abl.dsp.modalresonator~. The idea worth copying from ELSE is the single 'fundamental' multiplier inlet over a list of ratios.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/oscbank~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/resonbank~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/resonbank2~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/bpbank~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.11-Synthesis(Advanced)/39-Physical-Modeling/1.modal/1.introduction.[resonbak~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--oscillator-and-resonator-banks-set-by-lists-one-channel-per-partial`

### Phase-locked clock multiplication and division at signal rate (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tempo and clocks
- **There:** `pimp~`, `pimpmul~`, `polymetro~`, `phaseseq~`, `pulsediv~`, `pulsecount~`, `tempo~`
- **What it does:** pimp~ is a phasor that also outputs an impulse at every wrap, in either direction. pimpmul~ multiplies the phase of such a ramp by any ratio, so a derived clock can never drift from its master, and fractions give polyrhythms. phaseseq~ fires an impulse as the phase passes each value in a list.
- **Closest in Max:** `phasor~`, `rate~`, `subdiv~`, `what~`, `swing~`, `where~`, `ramp~`
- **Checked:** Refpages read. rate~: 'Time-scale the output of a phasor~', with a sync mode. subdiv~: integer subdivision of a phasor, with pattern and probability attributes. what~: impulses when the input crosses each value in a list, plus an index outlet. swing~: splits one ramp into two unequal ones. where~: elapsed and remaining time of a ramp. phasor~ has a lock attribute and tempo-relative rates.
- **Advice for Max:** Max has this family and it is larger than ELSE's: one master phasor~, then rate~ for any ratio, subdiv~ for integer divisions with probability, what~ for impulses at chosen phases (the direct equal of phaseseq~), swing~ and where~. The lesson both libraries teach is the same and worth stating as a rule: derive every clock from one phase ramp by multiplying phase, never run two free oscillators and expect them to stay together.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/pimp~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pimpmul~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/polymetro~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/phaseseq~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pulsediv~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/14-Trigger-LFO-LFNoise/6.clock.metronome/7.[pimpmul~].Phase.Sync.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--phase-locked-clock-multiplication-and-division-at-signal-rate`

### Plain compressor, ducker, expander, gate and normalizer objects (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `compress~`, `duck~`, `expand~`, `noisegate~`, `norm~`
- **What it does:** Dynamics as small objects with threshold, ratio, attack and release. duck~ takes a second signal as the side chain. norm~ holds a running RMS at a target level. The tutorial builds each from a moving RMS, a gain computed in dB and an up/down smoother.
- **Closest in Max:** `abl.device.compressor~`, `abl.dsp.compander~`, `abl.device.limiter~`, `omx.comp~`, `omx.peaklim~`, `limi~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. omx.comp~: many-message OctiMax compressor with gating and a side-chain filter. limi~: lookahead peak limiter. Bundled Ableton DSP: abl.device.compressor~, abl.dsp.compander~, abl.device.limiter~, abl.dsp.transientdesign~.
- **Advice for Max:** Max is well covered. For teaching, the tutorial's compressor arithmetic is clean and ports directly: overshoot = level in dB - threshold; gain change = -overshoot * (1 - 1/ratio), never above 0; smooth with a different time down (attack) and up (release); convert to linear.
- **Notes:** Whether the Ableton compressor object exposes a side-chain inlet was not read.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/compress~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/duck~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/expand~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/noisegate~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/norm~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.02-Basic-Processing/11-Dynamics/5.Compressor.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--plain-compressor-ducker-expander-gate-and-normalizer-objects`

### Polyphony as multichannel pitch and gate signals (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Polyphony and voices
- **There:** `voices~`, `voices`, `mono~`, `synth~`
- **What it does:** voices~ turns MIDI notes into two multichannel signals, pitch and gate, one channel per voice, so a single oscillator and a single envelope object process every voice and no voice subpatch is needed. The tutorial notes it costs somewhat more CPU than cloning a voice patch.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.noteallocator~`, `mc.voiceallocator~`, `mc.target`, `poly~`, `mc.adsr~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. mc.noteallocator~: 'Manage voice numbers for MIDI note events' with voices, steal, hires and mpemode attributes. mc.voiceallocator~ does the same for general events with release and busy-state handling. mc.target formats messages for a chosen MC voice.
- **Advice for Max:** Same design in Max: mc.noteallocator~ assigns voices, and mc. objects carry all voices on one cord. Prefer it to poly~ when the voice is simple, and poly~ when voices need their own patch logic or should stop computing when idle.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/voices~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/voices-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/synth~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/18-MIDI-CV-OSC-Net/1.MIDI/3.Monophony.Polyphony/4.[voices~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--polyphony-as-multichannel-pitch-and-gate-signals`

### Resonance stated as Q, bandwidth or decay time, on every filter (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `lowpass~`, `highpass~`, `bandpass~`, `resonant~`, `resonator~`, `bandstop~`, `eq~`, `lowshelf~`, `highshelf~`, `allpass.2nd~`, `svfilter~`
- **What it does:** One object per filter type, all with signal-rate frequency and resonance. A message switches the resonance unit between Q, bandwidth in octaves and t60 (ms for the ringing to fall 60 dB). All take 'bypass' and 'clear'. bandpass~ keeps its peak at 0 dB while resonant~ gets louder with resonance, and the help files say which is which.
- **Closest in Max:** `biquad~`, `filtercoeff~`, `filtergraph~`, `svf~`, `lores~`, `reson~`, `onepole~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. filtercoeff~: 'Signal-rate filter coefficient generator' with the same type list (lowpass, highpass, bandpass, bandstop, peaknotch, lowshelf, highshelf, resonant, allpass) and gain variants. filtergraph~ offers the same types plus display. reson~, lores~ and svf~ take signal inputs. None of them accepts a decay time as the resonance value.
- **Advice for Max:** Max splits the job: filtercoeff~ (or filtergraph~) makes the coefficients and biquad~ filters. For audio-rate sweeps use filtercoeff~. The unit Max lacks is decay time. When a patch thinks in ring time (modal sounds, pinged filters), convert first: the tutorial gives bandwidth in Hz = ln(1000) / (pi * t60 in seconds), and Q = frequency / bandwidth.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/lowpass~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/bandpass~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/resonant~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/resonator~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/eq~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/lowshelf~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.06-Filters(Basic)/27-Filters.types/2.Filter.Types/2.Resonant/2.Bandpass/4.[resonant~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--resonance-stated-as-q-bandwidth-or-decay-time-on-every-filter`

### Sampler voices that read sustain-loop points from the file (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Polyphony and voices
- **There:** `susloop~`, `sfinfo`, `tabplayer~`, `player~`
- **What it does:** sfinfo reads the instrument chunk of an AIFF file (reference pitch and loop points). susloop~ makes a read index that plays from the start, then loops between two sample points while the gate is held and plays on after release.
- **Closest in Max:** `info~`, `groove~`, `buffer~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. info~: outlets for sampling rate, 'Sample Instrument Info (list)', sustain loop start and end, release loop start and end, total time; 'Looping and tuning information is found in AIFF files'. groove~: loop, loopstart, loopend, loopinterp attributes and setloop, startloop, endloop messages.
- **Advice for Max:** Max has both halves: info~ reports the loop points stored in the file, and groove~ plays with a loop that startloop and endloop control. Wire info~'s sustain-loop outlets into groove~ rather than typing loop points by hand.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/susloop~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/sfinfo-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.07-Sampling.Delay.Granulation/29-Sampling(buffer)/5.Sample-Synth/1.Sampler-Synth.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--sampler-voices-that-read-sustain-loop-points-from-the-file`

### Selectors and gates that crossfade over a set time (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `xselect~`, `xgate~`, `xselect.mc~`, `xgate.mc~`, `select~`
- **What it does:** xselect~ picks one of N inputs and xgate~ sends one input to one of N outlets, both with an equal-power crossfade whose time in ms is an argument. A status outlet reports when a channel has finished fading out. The cyclone help files call these 'much better' than selector~ and gate~.
- **Closest in Max:** `selector~`, `gate~`, `mc.selector~`, `matrix~`
- **Checked:** Refpages and the defaults registry. selector~ and gate~ both have a ramptime attribute ('Sets the ramp time to use for fading connections in milliseconds... will crossfade between inputs when the active input is switched'); its default in transform-defaults.json is 0.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's own selector~ and gate~ already crossfade: the attribute is ramptime and it is off by default. Write 'selector~ 4 @ramptime 20' instead of building a crossfade around it. The cyclone note is about the cloned Max 7 object; the current Max object has the feature.
- **Notes:** The refpage does not say whether the fade is linear or equal power.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/xselect~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/xgate~-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/selector~-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/gate~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--selectors-and-gates-that-crossfade-over-a-set-time`

### Signal-rate step sequencers stepped by impulses (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `impseq~`, `sequencer~`
- **What it does:** On each incoming trigger the object outputs the next value of a list, as a held signal (sequencer~) or as a one-sample impulse of that height (impseq~, where 0 is a rest and the values work as accents). Both send an impulse when the list ends.
- **Closest in Max:** `stash~`, `table~`, `seq~`, `techno~`, `mc.pattern~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. stash~: 'Store and Recall Audio Signal Values', described as usable as an audio-rate step sequencer, with advance-trigger and sample-trigger inlets. table~: signal lookup of a table, with a trigger mode. techno~: signal-driven step sequencer with pitch and amplitude per step. seq~: event sequencer driven by a phasor~.
- **Advice for Max:** stash~ is the nearest Max object to sequencer~: a list as contents and an advance trigger. For accents as impulses, multiply the trigger by the stash~ output. Sample-accurate sequencing in Max should start from these, not from metro and counter.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/impseq~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/sequencer~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/19-Sequencing/5.Rhythmic/4.[impseq~].[phaseseq~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--signal-rate-step-sequencers-stepped-by-impulses`

### Tempo generator whose 'swing' is random timing deviation (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tempo and clocks
- **There:** `tempo`, `tempo~`
- **What it does:** A metronome set in BPM, ms or Hz that outputs bangs (tempo) or impulses (tempo~). Its swing value is a percentage of random deviation per beat, not a long-short pattern; a seed makes the pattern repeatable. A multiplier/divider gives subdivisions.
- **Closest in Max:** `metro`, `phasor~`, `swing~`, `transport`
- **Checked:** Refpages. phasor~ has a 'jitter' attribute: random frequency variation on each phase reset, with a 'limit'. swing~ outputs two unequal ramps per input ramp, with a swing proportion from 0.15 to 0.85. metro takes tempo-relative intervals and a quantize attribute.
- **Advice for Max:** The word means two things. In Max, swing~ is the musical long-short swing, and random timing looseness is phasor~ @jitter. ELSE's swing is the second kind. When porting an ELSE patch, map its swing to jitter, not to swing~.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/tempo-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/tempo~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/14-Trigger-LFO-LFNoise/6.clock.metronome/3.[tempo].[tempo~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--tempo-generator-whose-swing-is-random-timing-deviation`

### Tools for multichannel cords: pick, remap, slice, merge, interleave (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `get~`, `pick~`, `slice~`, `merge~`, `unmerge~`, `lace~`, `delace~`, `repeat~`, `sum~`, `nchs~`, `sigs~`
- **What it does:** get~ builds a new multichannel signal from a list of channel numbers, so channels can be reordered or repeated the way dollar arguments reorder a list. The others take one channel, split at a point, join cords of different widths, interleave, repeat, sum to mono and report the channel count.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.resize~`, `mc.unpack~`, `mc.combine~`, `mc.separate~`, `mc.interleave~`, `mc.deinterleave~`, `mc.dup~`, `mc.channelcount~`, `mc.mixdown~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. mc.resize~: 'Resize a multichannel signal using selected channels', with select and replicate attributes. mc.unpack~ also has select. mc.combine~, mc.separate~, mc.interleave~, mc.deinterleave~, mc.dup~ and mc.channelcount~ match the other ELSE tools one for one.
- **Advice for Max:** Each ELSE tool has a Max equal. The one to remember is channel remapping: mc.resize~ with its select attribute picks or reorders channels, which is what get~ does.
- **Notes:** The exact form of the select attribute's value was not read.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/get~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pick~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/slice~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/merge~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/unmerge~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/lace~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/delace~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/repeat~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/sum~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/nchs~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--tools-for-multichannel-cords-pick-remap-slice-merge-interleave`

### Tuning tools: Scala import, retuning by scale, equal divisions, fraction and cents converters (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `scala`, `retune`, `autotune`, `autotune2`, `eqdiv`, `scale2freq`, `midi2freq`, `freq2midi`, `cents2ratio`, `frac2cents`, `cents2frac`, `scales`
- **What it does:** scala reads a .scl file into a list of cents. retune maps successive keys to successive scale steps; autotune snaps a pitch to the nearest step. eqdiv divides any interval into equal steps. Converters go between cents, ratios, fraction symbols and scale steps, and midi2freq takes the A4 frequency.
- **Closest in Max:** `mtof`, `ftom`, `sfizz~`, `retune~`
- **Checked:** mtof refpage: attributes base (reference frequency, default 440), ref and mid (reference and middle note numbers), scale ('a set of values... for a scale described using the Scala format', cents or ratios such as 5/4), scalename ('a valid scale name from the Scala archive'), map and mapname (Scala keyboard maps). ftom has the same set plus round. sfizz~ has them too.
- **Advice for Max:** Max builds microtuning into mtof itself: 'mtof @scalename <name>' or '@scale <count> <steps>' retunes by a Scala scale with no other object, and @base moves A4. Use that instead of arithmetic around mtof. retune's key-to-step mapping is what mtof with a scale does; autotune's snap-to-nearest is ftom with the same scale and @round.
- **Notes:** That ftom @round snaps to the nearest scale step was inferred from the attribute list, not tested.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/scala-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/retune-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/autotune-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/eqdiv-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/scale2freq-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/midi2freq-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/cents2ratio-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/frac2cents-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.01-The.Basics/03-Intervals-Tuning/8.[retune].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--tuning-tools-scala-import-retuning-by-scale-equal-divisions-fraction-and-cents-converters`

### Voice allocator with release hold, retrigger modes and per-voice message routing (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Polyphony and voices
- **There:** `voices`, `synth~`
- **What it does:** Like a poly allocator, but a voice can stay reserved for a release time after its note-off, repeated notes have three policies (new voice, same voice, or sent to an 'extra' outlet), stealing is optional, and any other message whose first value is a sounding pitch is routed to that note's voice, which is how polyphonic aftertouch reaches the right voice.
- **Closest in Max:** `poly`, `poly~`, `thispoly~`, `mc.noteallocator~`, `mc.voiceallocator~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. poly: voices and a steal-mode argument. poly~: steal, midimode, mpemode, legacynotemode attributes. thispoly~: 'Control poly~ voice allocation and muting'. mc.voiceallocator~: release, releaseevent, holdevent messages and a busy map.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the voice itself reports when it is free (thispoly~ busy and mute, driven by the envelope), so no release time has to be guessed in the allocator. That is the more robust design. Per-note expression routing is what poly~ @mpemode and mc.noteallocator~ @mpemode are for.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/voices-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/synth~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--voice-allocator-with-release-hold-retrigger-modes-and-per-voice-message-routing`

### Wavetable oscillator that scans frames, in one or two dimensions (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `wavetable~`, `wt~`, `wt2d~`, `bl.wavetable~`, `tabreader~`, `tabgen`
- **What it does:** wavetable~ reads a table of any size with a choice of interpolation, and with -n <frames> treats the table as a row of frames that a 0-1 inlet crossfades through. wt2d~ adds a second axis for a grid of frames. No guard points are needed. tabgen fills tables with partial lists, windows or noise.
- **Closest in Max:** `wave~`, `2d.wave~`, `cycle~`, `buffer~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. wave~: reads a start-to-end portion of a buffer~ by a 0-1 signal, with an interp attribute and Hermite bias and tension. 2d.wave~: 'similar to wave~, but with an additional axis'; a range of the file is divided into rows and a Y phase picks the row or rows. buffer~ has fill and apply messages for sine, cosine, sinc and window shapes.
- **Advice for Max:** For frame scanning in Max use 2d.wave~: put the frames end to end in one buffer~, set rows to the frame count, and drive Y with the scan position. That is the same idea as wavetable~ -n. A true two-axis grid like wt2d~ needs two 2d.wave~ readers and a crossfade.
- **Notes:** Whether 2d.wave~ crossfades between neighbouring rows or switches was not confirmed beyond the refpage's 'row(s)'.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/wavetable~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/wt2d~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/tabreader~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/tabgen-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.05-Synthesis(Basic)/22-Wavetable/2.Wavetable.scan.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.05-Synthesis(Basic)/22-Wavetable/4.2D.scanning.[wt2d~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--wavetable-oscillator-that-scans-frames-in-one-or-two-dimensions`

### Audio signals to image rows and back, with fill modes (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `pix_sig2pix~`, `pix_pix2sig~`
- **What it does:** pix_sig2pix~ writes up to four signals into the four channels of an image; pix_pix2sig~ reads an image back as four signals. Both have modes: restart each block, continue where the last block stopped, one line per block, and a 'waterfall' that shifts earlier lines along.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.catch~`, `jit.release~`, `jit.poke~`, `jit.peek~`, `jit.buffer~`
- **Checked:** Read jit.catch~ (signals to matrices; mode, framesize), jit.release~ (matrices to signals), jit.poke~ and jit.peek~ (signal-rate write and read at signal-rate positions) and jit.buffer~ (a buffer~ as a matrix).
- **Advice for Max:** Jitter splits this into more objects but allows more: jit.poke~ and jit.peek~ address any cell at signal rate. Use jit.catch~ @mode 2 @framesize N for a fixed row size (this repo already records that mode 0 gives uneven sizes). A scrolling waterfall is not built in; shift the matrix yourself.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_sig2pix~-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_pix2sig~-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/12.floatingpoint_framebuffer.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--audio-signals-to-image-rows-and-back-with-fill-modes`

### Three signals drawn as a line in 3D (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `scopeXYZ~`
- **What it does:** scopeXYZ~ takes three audio signals as x, y and z and draws the last N samples as a line, strip or points in the scene, with a settable length and line width. It is a one-box Lissajous or phase-space scope that takes transforms and colour from the chain above it.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.catch~`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.gl.graph`
- **Checked:** Read jit.catch~ (signals to matrices; framesize, mode), jit.gl.mesh (draw_mode; vertex matrix on the left inlet) and jit.gl.graph ('renders one-dimensional floating point data as a three-dimensional shape').
- **Advice for Max:** Two objects in Jitter: three signals into jit.catch~ @mode 2 @framesize N, its 3-plane matrix into jit.gl.mesh with a line draw mode. Worth keeping as a snippet, since it is asked for often.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/scopeXYZ~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--three-signals-drawn-as-a-line-in-3d`

### A second Pd process for another CPU core ([pd~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `pd~`, `stdout`
- **What it does:** [pd~] starts another Pd as a subprocess with its own patch and exchanges audio and messages with it. There is also a Max external of the same name that runs Pd inside Max.
- **Closest in Max:** `poly~`, `mc.poly~`
- **Checked:** poly~ refpage: parallel attribute and threadcount message spread voices over threads. No pd~ in this Max install's registry.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, parallelism is per poly~ (@parallel 1). The Max [pd~] external, if installed, would let a Pd patch run inside Max; it is not installed here.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/pd~-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/stdout-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--a-second-pd-process-for-another-cpu-core-pd`

### Arrays: one drawable, editable table shared by control and audio (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `array`, `graph`, `tabread4~`, `tabwrite~`, `tabosc4~`, `sinesum`, `cosinesum`, `normalize`, `array define -k`
- **What it does:** A Pd array is drawn in the patch, edited by dragging, read and written by both control and signal objects, filled by messages such as sinesum, and optionally saved inside the patch.
- **Closest in Max:** `buffer~`, `waveform~`, `table`, `itable`, `multislider`
- **Checked:** buffer~ refpage: fill (constant or function), normalize, read/write. waveform~ refpage: a draw mode with a pencil tool that changes the buffer~ samples. table/itable refpages: integer tables with embed. Pd's manual says buffer~ cannot be drawn into; waveform~'s refpage says otherwise.
- **Advice for Max:** Max splits this in two: buffer~ (+ waveform~ in draw mode) for audio, table/itable or multislider for control data. For a small hand-drawn transfer curve used at audio rate, buffer~ + waveform~ is the match. buffer~ has no 'save in patch' option in its attribute list; table and itable have embed.
- **Source:** <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm> · <Documentation/2.control.examples/15.array.pd> · <Documentation/2.control.examples/16.more.arrays.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/array-object-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--arrays-one-drawable-editable-table-shared-by-control-and-audio`

### Copies of an ordinary abstraction as voices ([clone]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `clone`, `poly`, `next`, `this`, `all`, `resize`, `-di`, `-do`, `-s`, `-x`
- **What it does:** [clone] loads N copies of any abstraction, with no special inlet or outlet objects inside. Each copy gets its own number as $1; a list starting with a number goes to that copy, and outlets tag what they send with the copy number. Voice allocation is a separate object, [poly], wired in front.
- **Closest in Max:** `poly~`, `thispoly~`, `mc.poly~`, `poly`, `in~`, `out~`
- **Checked:** Read the poly~, thispoly~, mc.poly~, in~ and out~ refpages and the userguide polyphony page. poly~ needs in/in~/out/out~ inside the voice patcher; routes by target, note and midinote; has steal, mute/busy through thispoly~, up/down sampling, a voices message and parallel threads. thispoly~ bang reports the instance number. Max also has a separate poly allocator object.
- **Advice for Max:** The Pd split is worth copying in Max when the built-in routing fights you: keep allocation outside (Max's own poly object) and address voices by number with poly~'s target. Inside a voice, read the voice number once with thispoly~ bang, the way a clone copy reads $1. Keep poly~ for what clone lacks: muting idle voices and voice stealing.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/clone-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/poly-help.pd> · <Documentation/3.audio.examples/D11.sampler.poly.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--copies-of-an-ordinary-abstraction-as-voices-clone`

### Decibel scale where 100 dB is full scale (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `dbtorms`, `rmstodb`, `dbtopow`, `env~`
- **What it does:** Pd's dB objects and [env~] put amplitude 1 at 100 dB and clip 0 dB to silence, so a level control runs 0 to 100.
- **Closest in Max:** `dbtoa`, `atodb`, `live.gain~`
- **Checked:** dbtoa and atodb refpage digests read (decibels to a linear value and back). Recorded as a porting trap, not a capability.
- **Advice for Max:** When porting a Pd patch to Max, subtract 100 from every dB value before using Max's dB objects.
- **Notes:** Only the digests of the Max dB objects were read, not where they put 0 dB.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/acoustics-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/env~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--decibel-scale-where-100-db-is-full-scale`

### Delay lines linked by name, with any number of remote taps (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `delwrite~`, `delread~`, `delread4~`, `vd~`
- **What it does:** [delwrite~ name ms] writes a delay line; any [delread~] or [delread4~] with that name, in any window, reads it. A signal sets the delay time of [delread4~].
- **Closest in Max:** `tapin~`, `tapout~`, `delay~`
- **Checked:** tapout~ refpage: the outlet of tapin~ must be cabled to tapout~ for the delay line to work. delay~ refpage read.
- **Advice for Max:** No change for Max; note when porting that the name link becomes a cord from tapin~.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/delay-tilde-objects-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--delay-lines-linked-by-name-with-any-number-of-remote-taps`

### FFT processing in an ordinary subpatch (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `rfft~`, `rifft~`, `fft~`, `ifft~`, `block~`, `tabreceive~`, `lrshift~`
- **What it does:** There is no FFT wrapper. FFT objects work on whatever block the window has, so a subpatch with [block~ 512 4] is a 512-point, overlap-4 analysis; the Hann window, normalisation and overlap-add are patched by hand.
- **Closest in Max:** `pfft~`, `fftin~`, `fftout~`, `fft~`, `ifft~`
- **Checked:** pfft~ refpage: manages windowing, overlap and add with fftin~/fftout~. fft~ refpage: takes FFT size, interval and offset as arguments. Userguide frequency_domain page says pfft~ is the easier route.
- **Advice for Max:** Use pfft~ in Max. The Pd example patches (I03 graphic EQ, I07 phase vocoder) are worth reading as recipes: every step pfft~ hides is visible in them.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/fft~-help.pd> · <Documentation/3.audio.examples/I03.resynthesis.pd> · <Documentation/3.audio.examples/I07.phase.vocoder.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--fft-processing-in-an-ordinary-subpatch`

### Multichannel signals through ordinary objects ([snake~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Many channels from one generator
- **There:** `snake~ in`, `snake~ out`, `clone -d`, `adc~ -m`, `send~`, `catch~`
- **What it does:** Any signal cord can carry several channels. [snake~ in] bundles and [snake~ out] splits; stateless objects such as [*~] and [cos~] process every channel under their normal name, and [clone -di / -do] spreads channels over copies.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.pack~`, `mc.unpack~`, `mc.combine~`, `mc.separate~`, `mc.poly~`
- **Checked:** Read refpages mc.pack~, mc.unpack~, mc.combine~, mc.separate~, mc.poly~. Max keeps multichannel in a parallel mc. family; mc.poly~ gives per-instance inputs and a multichannel output.
- **Advice for Max:** No change for Max. When porting a Pd patch, a [snake~] cord becomes an mc. cord and every object on it needs its mc. name.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/snake-tilde-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/binops-tilde-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/clone-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--multichannel-signals-through-ordinary-objects-snake`

### Offline rendering by message (pd fast-forward) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `fast-forward`, `pd`, `-batch`, `-nogui`
- **What it does:** Sending 'fast-forward <ms>' to pd runs the scheduler as fast as the CPU allows for that much patch time. A patch can render to an array or a file faster than real time and then carry on live.
- **Closest in Max:** `adstatus`, `dsptime~`, `sfrecord~`
- **Checked:** Userguide non_realtime page: select the NonRealTime driver in Audio Status; hardware I/O is off; poll dsptime~; record with sfrecord~.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's way is a driver switch, not a message with a duration. For an automated render, switch the driver, start DSP, watch dsptime~, stop.
- **Notes:** Whether adstatus can switch to the NonRealTime driver by message was not checked.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/pd-messages.pd> · <Documentation/3.audio.examples/B16.long-varispeed.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter5.htm>
- id: `plugdata--offline-rendering-by-message-pd-fast-forward`

### One-line signal expressions, per block and per sample ([expr~], [fexpr~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `expr~`, `fexpr~`, `$v1`, `$x1[-1]`, `$y1[-1]`
- **What it does:** [expr~] evaluates a C-style expression on whole signal blocks. [fexpr~] evaluates per sample and can read earlier input and output samples, so a difference equation or a small chaotic system is typed in one box.
- **Closest in Max:** `gen~`, `gen.codebox~`
- **Checked:** No expr~ or fexpr~ in the registry. gen~ and gen.codebox~ refpages: an embedded Gen patcher or GenExpr text compiled to native code.
- **Advice for Max:** Use gen~ (or gen.codebox~ for the typed form). It is faster than fexpr~, which the Pd help itself warns is expensive. The fexpr~ notation $y1[-1] maps to a history in gen.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/expr-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--one-line-signal-expressions-per-block-and-per-sample-expr-fexpr`

### One-sample feedback loops by setting block size to 1 (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Feedback and trails
- **There:** `block~ 1`, `delread~`, `delwrite~`, `send~`, `receive~`, `fexpr~`
- **What it does:** A subpatch with [block~ 1] computes one sample at a time, so a delay or send/receive loop inside it has a one-sample delay. Recursive filters and Karplus-Strong strings are built this way with ordinary objects.
- **Closest in Max:** `gen~`, `gen.codebox~`, `poly~`
- **Checked:** gen~ refpage and the repo's forum insights (history operator for IIR filters). poly~ has a vs attribute; whether vs 1 is allowed was not checked.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, do single-sample feedback in gen~ with history. It compiles to native code; Pd's block~ 1 pays the per-block overhead on every sample.
- **Notes:** poly~ @vs 1 not verified.
- **Source:** <Documentation/3.audio.examples/G04.control.blocksize.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/send-receive-tilde-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/block~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--one-sample-feedback-loops-by-setting-block-size-to-1`

### Sample-and-hold triggered by a falling control signal (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `samphold~`
- **What it does:** [samphold~] samples its left input whenever its right input decreases, so a [phasor~] triggers it at each wrap with no extra object.
- **Closest in Max:** `sah~`
- **Checked:** sah~ refpage: samples when the trigger crosses a threshold, with a triggermode attribute (default Ascending).
- **Advice for Max:** When porting, the trigger sense flips: Pd fires on a drop, Max's default on a rise past the threshold. Set sah~'s triggermode rather than inverting the signal.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/samphold~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--sample-and-hold-triggered-by-a-falling-control-signal`

### Summing signal bus as its own object pair ([throw~] / [catch~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `throw~`, `catch~`, `send~`, `receive~`
- **What it does:** [throw~] adds into a named bus and one [catch~] reads the sum. [send~] / [receive~] is the opposite shape: exactly one sender, any number of receivers; a second [send~] of the same name is an error.
- **Closest in Max:** `send~`, `receive~`
- **Checked:** send~ refpage: 'When two or more send~ objects are aimed at the same receive~ object, the signals add together.' No throw~ or catch~ in the registry.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's send~ already sums, so one pair does both jobs (see patching/MAX_PATCHING.md on mix buses). When porting, [throw~ x] becomes s~ x and [catch~ x] becomes r~ x.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/throw~-catch~-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/send-receive-tilde-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--summing-signal-bus-as-its-own-object-pair-throw-catch`

### Switching DSP off for one subpatch ([switch~]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `switch~`, `block~`
- **What it does:** A [switch~] in a window turns audio computation for that window and everything inside it on or off with a number. One object per window; it nests.
- **Closest in Max:** `mute~`, `pass~`, `pcontrol`, `poly~`, `thispoly~`, `begin~`
- **Checked:** Read refpages mute~ (disables signal objects in a connected subpatcher), pass~ (zero signal out of a muted subpatcher), pcontrol, thispoly~ (mute message), begin~ (marked OBSOLETE, does nothing since Max 6, 'please use poly~'). No switch~ or block~ in the registry.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the switch lives outside the subpatcher (mute~ wired to it) or in thispoly~. For a patch with heavy optional sections, wrap each in a subpatcher with mute~ and pass~, or make it a poly~ voice; do not use begin~/gate~.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): confirmed: begin~'s refpage says it is obsolete since Max 6 and does nothing.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/block~-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--switching-dsp-off-for-one-subpatch-switch`

### Output safety limiter in the editor (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `safety limiter`
- **What it does:** plugdata's site lists a safety limiter that protects the listener's ears as a feature. No page read says how it works.
- **Closest in Max:** `limi~`, `omx.peaklim~`, `live.gain~`
- **Checked:** Registry has limi~ and omx.peaklim~ as objects a patch must include. No always-on output limiter was looked for in Max's audio settings.
- **Notes:** One phrase on the home page is the only source.
- **Source:** <https://plugdata.org/>

## control surface/UI building

### Bin Picker: clickable thumbnail grid of a media bin (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `Bin Picker`, `Media View`, `Get Media Count`, `Get Media Index`, `Get Media File Name`
- **What it does:** A control that shows the files in a media bin as a grid of names or thumbnails; clicking one sends its number to any player. Media is addressed by index everywhere, so any number can pick a clip.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.playlist`, `playlist~`, `chooser`, `umenu`
- **Checked:** Read refpages: jit.playlist organizes video clips with a visual strip per clip; chooser is a scrolling list of selectable items. Neither is a thumbnail grid that feeds an arbitrary player. Only the first 650 characters of the Bin Picker entry were read.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=135> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=139> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=503> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=504> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=833>

### Control Panels scoped to groups of Scenes, linked by Control ID (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Control Panel`, `Control ID`, `Split Control Panels`, `Join Control Panels`, `Show-Hide Control`, `Set Control Focus`, `Scene Select`, `Next Cue`, `Prev Cue`, `Stage Preview`, `Monitor`, `Timecode`
- **What it does:** The operator interface is a separate panel whose controls reach actor inputs and outputs by ID number, not by cords. A panel belongs to a run of Scenes, so the interface on screen changes as the show moves on. Ready-made controls include a Scene list, GO and BACK buttons, a Stage thumbnail and a timecode display.
- **Closest in Max:** `presentation mode`, `bpatcher`, `patcher tabs`, `pattr`, `thispatcher`
- **Checked:** Max's presentation view is one per patcher; thispatcher setactivetab (refpage read) switches tabs. Nothing ties an interface page to a position in a show. Scene Select, Next Cue, Prev Cue have no counterpart because there are no Scenes.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=184> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=185> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=186> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=188> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=189> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=718> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=725> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=861> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=872> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=880> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=882>

### Map any parameter to any controller by 'learn', with range, filter and curve (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Every parameter addressable: OSC, learn, binding, expressions
- **There:** `Control List`, `Learn`, `Add Control`, `Source Range`, `Target Range`, `Filter`, `Curve`, `Toggle`, `Feedback Device`, `Controls Combiner`, `Macro`
- **What it does:** Right-click a control or switch on Learn, move a key, MIDI control, OSC address, DMX channel, audio band or gamepad stick, and the two are linked. Each link has input and output ranges, a smoothing filter, a response curve drawn in a graph, and can send feedback to motorised or lit controllers.
- **Closest in Max:** `Mappings (userguide topic 'mapping')`, `param.osc`, `gamepad`, `hi`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). The userguide 'mapping' topic: Max's Mappings connect MIDI controllers and the computer keyboard to parameter-enabled interface objects, per patcher, with min, max, exponent, steps, relative, trigger and pickup modes; not available in Max for Live. So MIDI and key learn exist. OSC, DMX, audio and gamepad as learnable sources, filters and drawn curves were not found in that topic.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/9.-master-settings/audio-input-and-beat-detection> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/8.-modules>

### Colour picker that also converts between colour formats (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `colors`
- **What it does:** Opens a colour picker and converts between RGB, hexadecimal, HSL, HSV, CMYK and grey values, remembering recent colours.
- **Closest in Max:** `colorpicker`, `swatch`, `jit.rgb2hsl`
- **Checked:** Refpages. colorpicker: 'Select and output a color'. swatch: 'Choose a color'. Conversion objects in the registry are Jitter matrix operators (jit.rgb2hsl). No list-level converter between RGB, hex and HSL was found.
