### Control-rate oscillators and noise that run without audio (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `lfo`, `phasor`, `impulse`, `pulse`, `pimp`, `lfnoise`, `stepnoise`, `rampnoise`, `randpulse`
- **What it does:** Control versions of the signal generators: an LFO with five waveforms, a phase ramp, a pulse and bang generators, and three kinds of slow noise. They take a frequency in Hz and a refresh rate in ms, run with DSP off, and output in the MIDI range 0-127 by default. A frequency change takes effect at once, which the help file contrasts with a metronome.
- **Closest in Max:** `metro`, `line`, `snapshot~`, `drunk`, `random`
- **Checked:** No control-rate oscillator object in obj-qlookup.json (lfo: not present). The usual Max ways are a signal LFO read by snapshot~, which needs audio on, or metro driving line, expr or a counter.
- **Notes:** A control LFO that needs no audio would be a small v8 or abstraction job: metro at a refresh rate, accumulate phase from the elapsed time, look up the shape.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/lfo-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/phasor-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/impulse-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pulse-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/lfnoise-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/stepnoise-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rampnoise-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--control-rate-oscillators-and-noise-that-run-without-audio`

### Window extras: file drop, mouse lock, opacity, right-click menus (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `gemsdl2window`, `gemglutwindow`, `gemmacoswindow`, `gemglfw3window`
- **What it does:** The window backend objects document messages beyond size and position: per-window opacity, keeping the mouse inside the window, relative mouse movement for camera control, a file dropped on the window reported as a 'drop' event, a right-click menu, hiding the menu bar, and asking for a specific OpenGL profile.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.window`, `jit.world`, `jit.anim.drive`
- **Checked:** Read jit.window (mouse events out the dump outlet; idlemouse, mousewheel, pickray, floating, border, fsmenubar, fullscreen) and jit.world (the same window attributes; transparency there is render-order transparency, not window opacity). No opacity, mouse-confine, relative-mouse or file-drop item is in either attribute list. jit.anim.drive has ui_listen for camera control from the window.
- **Notes:** Which backend plugdata uses is not documented in the files read, so these messages may not all be available there.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemsdl2window-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemglutwindow-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemmacoswindow-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemglfw3window-help.pd>

### Mouse position as a percentage of a region, gated by modifier keys (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Mouse Watcher`, `Stage Mouse Watcher`
- **What it does:** Reports the mouse as 0 to 100 percent across a chosen part of the display, only while a chosen set of modifier keys is held, with a trigger on each move. The Stage version does the same inside one output window and adds click triggers, an inside/outside flag and scroll-wheel values.
- **Closest in Max:** `mousestate`, `modifiers`, `jit.window`, `jit.world`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `mousestate` reports position in pixels plus deltas, buttons and wheel, on a bang or after `poll`, with a `mode` message for the coordinate reference; its description limits it to when the cursor is in a Max patcher window. `modifiers` reports modifier keys on a bang or at a polling rate. `jit.window` and `jit.world` report mouse events on their window from the dump outlet; `jit.window` has `idlemouse` to include movement without a button.
- **Advice for Max:** For interaction on the video output in Max, take the mouse messages from the `jit.world` dump outlet, not from `mousestate`, and divide by the window size so the patch survives a change of resolution. Gate with `modifiers` and a `gate`.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=601 (Actors Reference: Mouse Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=755 (Actors Reference: Stage Mouse Watcher)>
- id: `isadora--mouse-position-as-a-percentage-of-a-region-gated-by-modifier-keys`

### Text entry control with a key filter, length limit, single-line and password options (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `Edit Text`, `Set Control Focus`
- **What it does:** The Edit Text control can be limited to integers, floats, hexadecimal or letters and digits, to a maximum number of characters and to one line. It can be read-only, can show bullets for passwords, and has start-up text. Set Control Focus moves typing to a named control, for forms with several fields.
- **Closest in Max:** `textedit`, `dialog`, `number`, `umenu`
- **Checked:** Read `textedit`'s attributes: `outputmode` (message or single symbol), `keymode`, `lines`, `readonly`, `tabmode`, `separator`, `wordwrap`, `clickmode`, `autoscroll`, `nosymquotes`. A search of the same list for 'password', 'maxchar' and 'filter' found nothing. `dialog` opens a box that returns one symbol.
- **Advice for Max:** Do not ask `textedit` to validate; it cannot. For a number use a number box, for a fixed set a `umenu`, for a one-off string a `dialog`, and check free text in `v8` before it is used. This is the repo's `textedit` rule seen from the other side.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=850 (Controls Reference: Edit Text)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=718 (Actors Reference: Set Control Focus)>
- id: `isadora--text-entry-control-with-a-key-filter-length-limit-single-line-and-password-options`

### Latest-source-wins merge with pickup (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Bind CHOP`, `Override CHOP`
- **What it does:** Several sources (a MIDI fader, an OSC app, the on-screen control) feed one value. Whichever changed most recently wins and the others are brought into line. A pickup option ignores a source until it crosses the current value, so a hardware fader in the wrong place does not make the value jump.
- **Closest in Max:** `Mapping`, `param_connect`, `pattr @bindto`
- **Checked:** Userguide 'Mapping': MIDI and keys set a parameter directly, and an output-mapped port sends the value back to the controller. Userguide 'Connecting Parameters': param_connect is a two-way link between a UI object and a Gen, RNBO, poly~ or jit.gl.slab parameter. pattr refpage: bindto. No object named pickup or takeover in obj-qlookup.json; package searches for 'pickup' and 'soft takeover' found nothing relevant.
- **Advice for Max:** Make the UI object the one merge point: every source sets it and it alone feeds the patch. Where a hardware fader is involved, add pickup in a few lines of v8. A butter.pickup object is worth building.
- **Notes:** Session 1's 'Expression, export and two-way binding on every parameter' covers binding in general; this entry is the merge-and-pickup behaviour only.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Bind_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Override_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--latest-source-wins-merge-with-pickup`

### Panel states as readable values (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Panel Value`, `Panel CHOP`, `Panel Execute DAT`, `Button COMP`, `Slider COMP`
- **What it does:** Every interface element carries a set of states: pressed (per mouse button), rollover, position, drag-out, focus, key. They are read as channels or through callbacks, and fast events can be queued so none are missed.
- **Closest in Max:** `hover`, `mousestate`, `ubutton`, `v8ui`
- **Checked:** hover refpage: sends the scripting name of the object under the cursor. mousestate refpage: button state and cursor position, polled. ubutton refpage: a transparent click region with hilite and toggle. Standard UI objects output their value only.
- **Advice for Max:** For rollover, give objects scripting names and use hover. For press, drag and hover states inside one control, write a v8ui, as the Butter tools do.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Panel_Value> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Panel_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Panel_Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Button_COMP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Slider_COMP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Field_COMP>
- id: `touchdesigner--panel-states-as-readable-values`

### Keyboard ops with learn, held state and release (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Ops.Devices.Keyboard.KeyPress_v2`, `Ops.Devices.Keyboard.KeyPressLearn`, `Ops.Devices.Keyboard.CursorKeys`
- **What it does:** KeyPressLearn has a Learn button: press it, press a key, and the op now triggers on that key's press and release. A switch limits it to when the canvas has focus. CursorKeys gives a held true/false and a pressed trigger for each arrow key, with WASD as an option.
- **Closest in Max:** `key`, `keyup`, `modifiers`, `select`
- **Checked:** key refpage: four outlets (ASCII, platform key code, modifiers, platform-independent code); it only reports 'if its patcher window has focus', and its use in Max for Live is 'strongly discouraged'. keyup refpage: the same on release. Neither has a learn function or a held state; a held key is key and keyup combined by the patch.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max build held-key state from key and keyup with a pair of select objects into a toggle, and show the toggle. Remember key hears nothing unless its patcher window has focus, so test with the render window in front before relying on it in a show.
- **Notes:** How jit.world reports key presses was not read; only the mouse report was.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Keyboard.KeyPress_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Keyboard.KeyPressLearn> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Keyboard.CursorKeys>
- id: `cables-gl--keyboard-ops-with-learn-held-state-and-release`

### Mouse op with a choice of coordinate space (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Ops.Devices.Mouse.Mouse_v4`, `Ops.Devices.Mouse.MouseButtons`, `Ops.Devices.Mouse.MouseDrag`, `Ops.Devices.Mouse.MouseWheel_v2`, `Ops.Devices.Mouse.PointerLock`
- **What it does:** The Mouse op reads the pointer on the output canvas and has a dropdown for the coordinate space: -1 to 1, pixels, display pixels or 0 to 1, plus flip y. It gives click triggers and a button-down value. Separate ops give button events, drag deltas and wheel deltas, and lock the pointer for game-style control.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world (dump outlet)`, `jit.window @idlemouse`, `mousestate`, `jit.gl.picker`, `jit.gl.handle`
- **Checked:** mousestate refpage: reports the mouse 'when the cursor is positioned within a Max patcher window'; modes give screen or patcher coordinates in pixels. jit.world and jit.window refpages: mouse events on the render window come out the dump outlet as 'mouse x y button flags' in pixels, only while the button is down unless idlemouse is on. Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md: GL positions are not pixels, so the numbers need scaling by hand.
- **Advice for Max:** For a mouse over the render window, take it from jit.world's right outlet (with 'sendwindow idlemouse 1' for hover), not from mousestate. Scale to the space you need straight away, in one place: divide by the window size for 0 to 1, or use jit.gl.camera's screentoworld for scene coordinates.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Mouse.Mouse_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Mouse.MouseButtons> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Mouse.MouseDrag> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Mouse.MouseWheel_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Mouse.PointerLock>
- id: `cables-gl--mouse-op-with-a-choice-of-coordinate-space`

### Button control with its own on and off values, two modes and a display-only flag (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Button`, `Slider`, `Display-Only`, `Don't Send Off`
- **What it does:** A Button sends a chosen 'On Value' and 'Off Value', not just 1 and 0. It is momentary or toggling, can be told never to send the off value, can swap the two, and has separate text and colours per state. A 'Display-Only' option, also on Slider, makes it ignore clicks so it serves as an indicator.
- **Closest in Max:** `live.text`, `textbutton`, `ignoreclick`, `select`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `live.text`: `mode` 0 button or 1 toggle, `text` and `texton` labels, `active`. `textbutton`: the same two modes, plus `outputmode` for sending on mouse down or mouse up. `ignoreclick` is listed in the common box refpage (`jbox`). Neither button has settable output values.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max use `live.text` and translate its 1 and 0 with a message box or `select 1 0` directly underneath. For indicators set `ignoreclick 1`, and describe the box as display-only in its label so nobody clicks it.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=838 (Controls Reference: Button)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=875 (Controls Reference: Slider)>
- id: `isadora--button-control-with-its-own-on-and-off-values-two-modes-and-a-display-only-flag`

### Controls that follow the value they control (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Show Value of Linked Properties`, `Slider`, `Dial`, `Number`, `Button`, `Control ID`
- **What it does:** Every control has a 'Show Value of Linked Properties' option. With it on, when anything else changes the linked input, the control moves to match. A control linked to an output always shows that output. With several links the newest value wins.
- **Closest in Max:** `attrui`, `param_connect`, `pattr @bindto`, `set message`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `attrui` shows the current value of the attribute it controls. `toggle` (and other UI objects) list `param_connect`: 'a two-way connection between the object and a parameter of a compatible object ... such as gen~ or jit.gl.slab ... will update if the parameter value changes'. `pattr` can bind to a named object and share its value.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max a control shows the truth only if it sits in the path of the value or is bound to it. Prefer `attrui` and `param_connect`; otherwise put the UI object in series so every source passes through it. A slider beside the data, fed nothing, shows only what the operator last did.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=875 (Controls Reference: Slider)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=847 (Controls Reference: Dial)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=863 (Controls Reference: Number)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=838 (Controls Reference: Button)>
- id: `isadora--controls-that-follow-the-value-they-control`

### Keyboard watcher with a key range, key names and press/release choice (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Keyboard Watcher`, `Key Table Watcher`, `Anything`
- **What it does:** Keyboard Watcher is set to a key or a range of keys, written in quotes, with names for special keys such as 'right-arrow', and to press, release or both. Key Table Watcher holds up to 32 keys and outputs the position of the one pressed, which turns a row of keys into a selector.
- **Closest in Max:** `key`, `keyup`, `numkey`, `select`, `Mappings (Max 9)`
- **Checked:** Read `key` and `keyup`: no inlets and no filter; four outlets give the ASCII code, a platform-specific key code, the modifier keys and a platform-independent key code. The userguide's mapping page describes Max 9 Mappings, which bind key presses or MIDI to parameter-enabled objects with no patching.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max filter keys with `select` under `key`, and take the fourth outlet when the patch must behave the same on Mac and Windows. For operator shortcuts, prefer Max 9 Mappings: the binding is then visible and editable outside the patch, which is what Isadora's Go Triggers give.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=553 (Actors Reference: Keyboard Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=552 (Actors Reference: Key Table Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=393 (Actors Reference: Anything)>
- id: `isadora--keyboard-watcher-with-a-key-range-key-names-and-press-release-choice`

### MIDI input actors with a range filter on every field (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Note On Watcher`, `Note Off Watcher`, `Control Watcher`, `Program Change Watcher`, `Pitch Bend Watcher`, `Mono Pressure Watcher`, `Poly Pressure Watcher`
- **What it does:** Each MIDI watcher takes a single value or a range for port, channel, and the message's own fields (pitch and velocity, or controller and value). A message must fall inside all of them to be seen. The outputs give the matched values and a trigger.
- **Closest in Max:** `notein`, `ctlin`, `split`, `stripnote`, `midiselect`
- **Checked:** Read `notein`: arguments choose a port or device and a channel; outlets are pitch, velocity and channel, with no range filter. `stripnote` passes only note-ons. `split` passes a number range. `midiselect` separates a raw MIDI stream by attribute.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the filter is patched after the input: `notein` to `stripnote` to `split`. That is one more box per condition, but each condition is visible on the patch, and a `kslider` or `live.dial` can sit in the path to show what arrived.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=618 (Actors Reference: Note On Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=451 (Actors Reference: Control Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=651 (Actors Reference: Program Change Watcher)>
- id: `isadora--midi-input-actors-with-a-range-filter-on-every-field`

### Menus and lists that fill themselves from the options of what they control (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `Popup Menu`, `List Selector`, `Radio Button`, `Auto Populate Item List`
- **What it does:** A Popup Menu or List Selector control linked to an input that has a fixed set of options, such as a blend mode or a list of available NDI or Syphon sources, can fill its item list from that input. The list can also be sent to the control as text, one item per line, from any actor output.
- **Closest in Max:** `attrui`, `umenu`, `live.tab`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `attrui` inspects the attributes of the object it is connected to: a menu chooses the attribute and the right half shows a control for its value. `umenu` items are typed in the Inspector, set by messages or a dictionary, or filled from a folder with `autopopulate` and `prefix`.
- **Advice for Max:** Max already has this for real attributes: use `attrui`, which shows an attribute's own option list, in place of a hand-typed `umenu` that can drift from the object. For lists that only exist at run time (devices, files), have the source send its list and fill the `umenu` from it.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=865 (Controls Reference: Popup Menu)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=855 (Controls Reference: List Selector)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=870 (Controls Reference: Radio Button)>
- id: `isadora--menus-and-lists-that-fill-themselves-from-the-options-of-what-they-control`

### Note sender that owns its note-offs (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tuning and pitch
- **There:** `Send Note`, `All Notes Off`, `Mini Sequencer`
- **What it does:** Send Note sends a note-on per trigger and, if a duration is set, the matching note-off later; a 'quiet' switch silences the previous note first. All Notes Off sends note-offs only for notes Isadora knows are sounding. Mini Sequencer plays one of a list of pitches per selection and silences the last one on leaving the Scene.
- **Closest in Max:** `makenote`, `flush`, `midiflush`, `noteout`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `makenote` pairs a pitch with a velocity and sends the note-off after a duration; `stop` sends note-offs for everything it holds, `clear` forgets them, and `repeatmode` sets what a repeated pitch does. `flush` and `midiflush` track note-ons passing through and send the missing note-offs on a bang.
- **Advice for Max:** Same idea in both tools. In Max route every generated note through `makenote`, or put `flush` before `noteout`, and send `stop` or a bang whenever the transport or the section stops. Isadora does this on leaving a Scene; Max will leave notes hanging unless it is wired.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=705 (Actors Reference: Send Note)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=390 (Actors Reference: All Notes Off)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=598 (Actors Reference: Mini Sequencer)>
- id: `isadora--note-sender-that-owns-its-note-offs`

### Showing and hiding groups of controls by a name pattern (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Show-Hide Control`, `Control Address`
- **What it does:** Every control has a text 'Control Address'. Show-Hide Control shows or hides every control whose address matches a pattern with * and ? wildcards, on Scene entry or when its 'visible' input changes. Naming controls '/page1/...' turns one panel into several pages.
- **Closest in Max:** `thispatcher`, `patcher tabs (showontab)`, `setactivetab`
- **Checked:** Read `thispatcher`: it evaluates patcher and scripting messages (the page points to a separate discussion of them, not read) and has `setactivetab`, which changes the active tab of a tabbed patcher by the name shown on the tab.
- **Advice for Max:** For pages of controls in Max, use patcher tabs and `thispatcher setactivetab`, where each page is an ordinary subpatcher and nothing is hidden. Scripted show and hide needs names, a controller and state to keep in step, which is the cost Isadora's pattern-matching hides.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=725 (Actors Reference: Show-Hide Control)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=829 (Controls Reference: Control Address (every control))>
- id: `isadora--showing-and-hiding-groups-of-controls-by-a-name-pattern`

### Controllers get the current values sent back (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Feedback Device`, `Feedback Port`, `Feedback IP`, `handle feedback`
- **What it does:** Each MIDI input can be paired with a feedback device, and OSC has a feedback port and address. When a value changes inside MadMapper, motor faders, button lights and tablet layouts are updated to match.
- **Closest in Max:** `Mappings (userguide topic 'mapping')`, `param.osc (auto, set)`, `udpsend`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topic mapping: if Map is enabled for a MIDI output port, every MIDI-mapped interface object sends its control change or note to that port with no midiout object. Read param.osc's refpage: 'auto' reports parameter changes as OSC, and 'set' dispatches incoming OSC to parameters without triggering output. udpsend's refpage: sends messages and OSC packets to a host and port.
- **Advice for Max:** Turn on Map for the controller's output port in MIDI Setup; that is the whole of MIDI feedback. For a tablet, `param.osc @auto 1` into `udpsend`, and bring incoming values in through its `set` message so they are not echoed straight back.
- **Notes:** Max is level with MadMapper here; the Max feature is easy to miss because it lives in the MIDI Setup window.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences> · <https://madmapper.com/files/09-Modules.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--controllers-get-the-current-values-sent-back`

### Hover help: a status line explains whatever is under the mouse (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Errors and status as data
- **There:** `Tooltip`
- **What it does:** A line at the bottom of the window describes the control the mouse is over, for every control in the program.
- **Closest in Max:** `annotation`, `hint`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topic objects, section Annotations and Hints: @annotation sets the text the Clue bar shows when hovering over an object, and @hint adds a pop-up that appears only when the patcher is locked.
- **Advice for Max:** Give every presentation control a `hint`. It costs one attribute per control and is the only documentation an operator sees in the middle of a show.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/interface-overview> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--hover-help-a-status-line-explains-whatever-is-under-the-mouse`

### Levels multiply down a chain: surface, group, video master, master (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Master`, `Video Master`, `DMX Master`, `Audio Master`, `Group`, `Opacity`
- **What it does:** Each surface has its own opacity. A group has one too, and the surface's value is multiplied by it. Above that sit separate masters for video, DMX, audio and laser, and one master over all of them, each multiplying what is below.
- **Closest in Max:** `color (attribute of every jit.gl object)`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** Read jit.group-gl.maxref.xml: 'color' is red green blue alpha per object, default 1 1 1 1. Read jit.gl.node's refpage: it groups child objects so they can be rendered and captured to a texture together (@capture 1); it lists no opacity of its own. Nothing in either page multiplies one object's level by a parent's.
- **Advice for Max:** Patch the chain on purpose: each layer's alpha is its own fader times its group fader times a master, one `*` per level, with the master fader shown in the presentation. To fade a whole group at once, capture it with jit.gl.node @capture 1 and fade the one captured texture.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/9.-master-settings> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-inspector> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control/osc-commands-and-channels-list>
- id: `madmapper--levels-multiply-down-a-chain-surface-group-video-master-master`

### Mixing two control sources on one parameter is done by a module that says how (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `Controls Combiner`, `Macro Module`
- **What it does:** A parameter takes one Control per kind of source. To let several inputs act on it, a Controls Combiner module mixes them using a chosen combination mode. A Macro module does the reverse: one widget that many Controls point at.
- **Closest in Max:** `maximum`, `* (times)`, `+ (plus)`
- **Checked:** Read maximum's refpage: outputs the greater of the incoming value and its stored one. In Max several message cords into one inlet do not combine: each message simply replaces the last (the repo's CLAUDE.md notes that only signal cords sum). So Max allows any number of sources, which MadMapper does not, but states no rule for how they meet.
- **Advice for Max:** When two things drive one parameter, an LFO and a fader, audio and a preset, put the rule in the patch as a visible object: `maximum`, `*` or `+`. Two cords into one inlet is 'last one wins', and it looks deliberate when it is not.
- **Notes:** The combination modes the Controls Combiner offers are not listed in the documents read.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/8.-modules> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--mixing-two-control-sources-on-one-parameter-is-done-by-a-module-that-says-how`

### A device map between controller numbers and the patch (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `MIDI Mapper Dialog`, `MIDI In Map CHOP`, `MIDI In CHOP`
- **What it does:** A per-device table names sliders s1, s2… and buttons b1, b2… and records which MIDI message each one is. Maps ship for known controllers and users can add their own. A patch reads s1 or b3, so changing controller means editing the map and not the patch. The page says there is no auto-learn.
- **Closest in Max:** `Mapping (MIDI and key learn)`, `ctlin`, `midiinfo`
- **Checked:** Userguide topic 'Mapping': MIDI and key learn straight onto parameter-mode UI objects, stored per patcher, not available in Max for Live; a Map column in MIDI Setup chooses which ports take part, and mapped objects can send their value back out. ctlin refpage: controller number and channel are typed as arguments. Userguide 'MIDI': ports get a letter abbreviation and a channel offset in MIDI Setup.
- **Advice for Max:** Max is ahead on learn, TouchDesigner on the indirection. Use Mapping for a one-off rig. For a patch that must survive a controller swap, keep every controller number in one place (one subpatcher of ctlin objects feeding named s buses, or a dict) and never scatter ctlin arguments through the patch.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/MIDI_Mapper_Dialog> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/MIDI_In_Map_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/MIDI_In_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/MIDI>
- id: `touchdesigner--a-device-map-between-controller-numbers-and-the-patch`

### Keyboard events as a filtered table (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `Keyboard In DAT`, `Keyboard In CHOP`
- **What it does:** One operator logs every key down and key up with its modifiers as table rows, can be limited to listed keys or to named shortcuts, and can be tied to the panels that have focus. The older channel version makes one on/off channel per key.
- **Closest in Max:** `key`, `keyup`, `modifiers`, `Key Mapping`
- **Checked:** key, keyup and modifiers refpages. Userguide 'Mapping': Key Mapping binds a key to any parameter-mode UI object, per patcher, shown with an orange outline.
- **Advice for Max:** Use Key Mapping to bind a key straight to a control. Keep key into select chains for keys that are not controls.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Keyboard_In_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Keyboard_In_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Mouse_In_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--keyboard-events-as-a-filtered-table`

### MIDI events as objects, filtered in a chain, with learn (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiInputDevice_v2`, `Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiNote`, `Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiCC_v3`, `Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiNoteFilter`, `Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiMonitor`
- **What it does:** The device op outputs MIDI events split by kind: note, CC, NRPN, program change, clock. MidiNote and MidiCC pick one note or controller, have a Learn button, and a dropdown that rescales 0 to 127 into 0 to 1 or -1 to 1. Events pass through each op, so filters chain. MidiMonitor shows what is arriving.
- **Closest in Max:** `notein`, `ctlin`, `midiselect`, `Mappings (MIDI map)`, `scale`
- **Checked:** notein refpage: outlets pitch, velocity, channel; ctlin: value for a set controller and channel. midiselect refpage (m4l-ref): splits raw MIDI by attributes note, ctl, bend, pgm, touch. Userguide mapping.json: the Mappings system binds a MIDI controller straight to any UI object with a parameter, by clicking the object and moving the control; it is per patcher and not available in Max for Live. No learn message on ctlin or notein.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's learn lives in Mappings, not in the MIDI objects: turn on Parameter Mode for the control and use Assign MIDI Map. When wiring by hand, rescale right at the input (ctlin into scale 0 127 0. 1.) so the rest of the patch never sees 0 to 127.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiInputDevice_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiNote> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiCC_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiNoteFilter> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiMonitor> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Devices.Midi.MidiOutputDevice>
- id: `cables-gl--midi-events-as-objects-filtered-in-a-chain-with-learn`

### Preset op that crossfades across its list (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Presets and saved state
- **There:** `Ops.Number.Preset`, `Ops.Sidebar.Presets_v2`
- **What it does:** Dragging from the Preset op to a port puts that port under its control. Presets are named and picked from a dropdown. In xfade mode a single fade number moves through the whole list, so 2.25 is three quarters of preset 2 and one quarter of preset 3. In a-b mode two chosen presets are blended from 0 to 1.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattrstorage`, `preset`, `pattr`, `autopattr`
- **Checked:** pattrstorage refpage: recall with a floating-point number recalls 'the interpolated preset' between two slots; recallmulti blends several presets by weight; interp sets the interpolation mode per client. preset refpage: also has recallmulti. Repo scans/c74-forum/forum_insights.md line 347: pattrstorage shows no visual cue of interpolation state.
- **Advice for Max:** Max already does both blends: send 'recall 2.25' to pattrstorage for the walk-through-the-list fade, and recallmulti for weighted mixes. What cables adds is binding by one drag; in Max use autopattr so controls join the storage without a pattr each.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.Preset> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Sidebar.Presets_v2>
- id: `cables-gl--preset-op-that-crossfades-across-its-list`

### Eurorack-style modules with attenuverters, automatic state saving and one-click MIDI learn (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `vco.m~`, `vcf.m~`, `vca.m~`, `adsr.m~`, `lfo.m~`, `seq8.m~`, `plaits.m~`, `pm6.m~`, `presets.m`
- **What it does:** MERDA is a set of ELSE abstractions that look and behave like modules: knobs for each value, signal inlets whose amount is set by an attenuverter knob (noon is zero, right adds, left subtracts and inverts), state saved with the patch with nothing to wire, a presets.m module that captures every module in the patch, and control-click MIDI learn on every knob.
- **Closest in Max:** `BEAP modules (bundled)`, `abl.device.* (bundled)`, `bpatcher`, `pattrstorage`
- **Checked:** Max bundles BEAP (C74/packages/BEAP), a module library loaded in bpatchers, and the Ableton DSP devices. The repo's preferred-objects table already uses BEAP's bp.Gigaverb and bp.Freeverb.
- **Advice for Max:** BEAP is the Max counterpart. The convention worth copying into any modulation input is the attenuverter: one bipolar knob per modulation inlet, centre is off, and the modulation is added to the panel value.
- **Notes:** BEAP's own conventions were not read in this session.
- **Source:** <Extra/else/about.MERDA.pd> · <Extra/else/presets.m-help.pd> · <Extra/else/seq8.m~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/18-MIDI-CV-OSC-Net/2.CV/6.MERDA.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--eurorack-style-modules-with-attenuverters-automatic-state-saving-and-one-click-midi-learn`

### Knob with its own number readout, typed entry, parameter prefix and variable binding (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `knob`
- **What it does:** A knob whose options include a log scale, a settable arc start that double-click returns to, discrete steps with ticks, a number readout that can show always, when active or when typing, entry of a value by typing and Enter, a 'param' name that is put in front of the output so it can go straight into an object, a 'var' name that writes the value into a named variable, and bangs on named sends for tab, enter and MIDI learn.
- **Closest in Max:** `live.dial`, `dial`, `pattr`, `attrui`
- **Checked:** Refpages. live.dial: showname, shownumber, appearance, parameter_mappable. dial: min, mult, size, degrees, mode. Max user guide 'Parameter Mode' lists Range/Enum, Initial Value, Unit Style (including custom units) and Exponent as parameter attributes. pattr binds a control to a name.
- **Advice for Max:** live.dial with Parameter Mode gives the readout, units, exponent, initial value and mapping. The idea Max lacks is the 'param' prefix: an ELSE knob outputs 'cutoff 440' ready for the receiving object, where Max needs a prepend. In Max the cleaner route is attrui for real attributes, which is the repo's rule already.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/knob-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--knob-with-its-own-number-readout-typed-entry-parameter-prefix-and-variable-binding`

### MIDI learn as an object that saves what it learned in the patch (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `midi.learn`, `knob`, `gain~`
- **What it does:** Bang it (or control-click a knob), move a controller, and it remembers which message and channel to listen to; the binding is stored with the patch. ELSE's knob and gain~ have the gesture built in.
- **Closest in Max:** `live.dial`, `ctlin`, `Mappings (Max feature)`
- **Checked:** Max user guide topic 'Mapping': 'The Mappings system allows you to connect MIDI controllers and the computer keyboard directly to user interface objects', for parameter-enabled objects, with min, max, exponent, steps, relative, trigger and pickup modes, and maps can be saved and loaded.
- **Advice for Max:** Max does this at application level, with no objects: enable Parameter Mode on the control and use Mappings. It also covers computer keys. Prefer it to ctlin plumbing when the goal is 'this knob follows that hardware knob'.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/midi.learn-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/knob-help.pd> · <Extra/else/about.MERDA.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--midi-learn-as-an-object-that-saves-what-it-learned-in-the-patch`

### Presets addressed by receive names, with timed morphing built in (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Presets and saved state
- **There:** `presets`, `presets.m`, `retrieve`, `interpolate`, `morph`
- **What it does:** presets is given a list of receive names. Saving reads the current value of whatever is attached to each name; loading sends the values back. It keeps presets in a text file or in the patch, can glide from the current state to a preset over a set time with an exponent, or interpolate under a slider, and each copy of an abstraction can have its own set.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattrstorage`, `autopattr`, `pattr`, `preset`
- **Checked:** pattrstorage refpage: recall with a float recalls an interpolated preset; 'recall' with 3 or 4 arguments interpolates between two presets at a position; messages include fade, interp, priority, storagewindow, read, write, writejson. preset has a pattrstorage attribute.
- **Advice for Max:** pattrstorage is the richer system: named clients, per-client interpolation modes, files. What it does not do by itself is move over time; drive 'recall <a> <b> $1' from a line object for a timed morph. ELSE's built-in morph time is the convenience to copy into a wrapper.
- **Notes:** The pattrstorage 'fade' message was seen in the method list but its description was not read.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/presets-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/retrieve-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/interpolate-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/morph-help.pd> · <Extra/else/presets.m-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--presets-addressed-by-receive-names-with-timed-morphing-built-in`

- **There:** `paint`, `paint_layer_2`, `repaint`, `mouse_down`, `mouse_drag`, `set_size`, `draw_svg`, `Path`, `set_args`
- **What it does:** Defining paint(g) turns a Lua object into a GUI: shapes, paths, text and inline SVG, with mouse callbacks. Extra paint_layer_n functions are cached layers repainted one at a time. set_args stores state in the object's saved arguments.

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `bng`, `tgl`, `nbx`, `hsl`, `vsl`, `hradio`, `vradio`, `vu`, `cnv`, `send`, `receive`
- **What it does:** Every GUI object has a send name and a receive name in its properties. With them set it needs no cords, and the names can contain $0 or $1.
- **Closest in Max:** `send`, `receive`, `pattr`, `toggle`, `number`, `slider`, `dial`
- **Checked:** Refpages of toggle, number, slider and dial list no send or receive attribute. They do list param_connect; the userguide param_connect page says it binds a UI object, without cords, to a named parameter of gen~, rnbo~ and other supplier objects, both ways.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max keep the control on the cord (CLAUDE.md 'Put the Display in the Path'); use s/r only for distance, and param_connect where the target is a gen~ or rnbo~ parameter. When porting, each named Pd GUI becomes a control plus an s and an r.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/bng-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/gui-boxes-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/cnv-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>

### 14-bit controller pairs joined into one value (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `MIDI In DAT`, `MIDI Event DAT`
- **What it does:** The MIDI input log can join a coarse controller (numbers 0–31) and its fine partner (the same number plus 32) into one 14-bit value. The page states what happens when one half is missing. The event log keeps the two messages separate, with operating-system timestamps in milliseconds.
- **Closest in Max:** `ctlin`, `midiparse`, `xbendin`
- **Checked:** midiparse refpage: the hires attribute is 'High-resolution Pitch Bend' only. xbendin refpage: 14-bit pitch bend. ctlin refpage: no 14-bit option. No object in obj-qlookup.json is named for controller pairs. Package search for '14-bit' and '14 bit' returned only an unrelated MPE object. Not checked: Max help patches.
- **Advice for Max:** If a controller sends high-resolution CCs, pair CC n with CC n+32 in a few lines of v8 (coarse × 128 + fine, output when the fine half arrives). A small Butter utility would save doing it per patch.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/MIDI_In_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/MIDI_Event_DAT>
- id: `touchdesigner--14-bit-controller-pairs-joined-into-one-value`

### A receive with a priority, giving ordered delivery (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `gemreceive`
- **What it does:** gemreceive is a named receive with a number: among receivers of the same name, lower numbers get the message first. Gem's whole render order is built on it (gemhead is an abstraction around 'gemreceive __gem_render 50'), and it can be used for any ordered broadcast.
- **Closest in Max:** `receive`, `trigger`
- **Checked:** Read receive.maxref.xml: no arguments or attributes beyond the name, one message (set). Nothing gives an order among receivers of one name. In Max, order is stated with trigger and cords.
- **Notes:** Not a graphics feature, but it is what makes Gem's render order a plain number.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemreceive-help.pd> · <Abstractions/Gem/gemhead.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--a-receive-with-a-priority-giving-ordered-delivery`

### User-defined graphical data structures (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed

- **There:** `struct`, `scalar`, `pointer`, `get`, `set`, `append`, `element`, `getsize`, `setsize`, `drawpolygon`, `filledpolygon`, `drawcurve`, `plot`, `drawnumber`, `drawsymbol`, `drawtext`
- **What it does:** A template declares named fields (float, symbol, text, nested arrays) and drawing instructions that use the fields as coordinates and colours. Each datum is drawn on a canvas, and dragging a drawn point edits the field behind it. Patches walk the data with pointers.

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Serial In Watcher – Text`, `Serial In Watcher – Binary`, `TCP In Watcher - Text`, `TCP In Watcher - Binary`, `Text Parser`, `input parser`
- **What it does:** A watcher holds a pattern describing an incoming message: literal text, numbers of given width, hex, character sets, raw bytes, and bitfields with byte order. Each named element in the pattern becomes an output of the actor, typed as integer, float or text.
- **Closest in Max:** `serial`, `regexp`, `unpack`, `match`, `itoa`, `sprintf`, `v8`
- **Checked:** Read refpages: serial sends and receives bytes; regexp applies PCRE to symbols and lists. Parsing a device protocol in Max is a chain (serial, zl or match, itoa, regexp, unpack) or a script. Nothing turns one pattern string into typed, named outlets, and there is no bitfield extractor found.
- **Notes:** Read in full. Would port well as a v8 object.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=311> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=312> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=313> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=314> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=317> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=318> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=319> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=320> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=321> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=716> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=717> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=764> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=765>
- id: `isadora--declarative-parser-for-serial-and-tcp-streams-that-creates-named-outputs`

### Operations limited to channels picked by a name pattern (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Many channels from one generator
- **There:** `Scope`, `Channel Names`, `Pattern Expansion`, `Pattern Matching`
- **What it does:** Channels have names, and many CHOPs have a Scope setting on their Common page that limits the operator to the channels whose names match a pattern, with * and ? wildcards. So one filter or math step can smooth only the tx channels of fifty, while the rest pass through.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.noise~`, `mc.unpack~`, `mc.list~`
- **Checked:** MC channels are numbered, not named. mc.noise~'s refpage lists a wrapper attribute called target, but its description was not read here, so how far it picks out channels is not settled. No Max refpage read mentions selecting channels by name or wildcard.
- **Advice for Max:** Where a Max patch needs this, keep a fixed channel order and write it down beside the MC cord (a comment saying which channel is which), since there are no names to match. Check what the MC wrapper's target attribute can do before splitting an MC signal apart with mc.unpack~.
- **Notes:** Added by hand 2026-10-02 alongside the entry above.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max side checked only as far as max_check says.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/CHOP_Common_Page> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pattern_Expansion>

### Euclidean rhythm from a step number (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `euclid`
- **What it does:** Given steps, hits and rotation, answers for any step number whether it is a hit or a rest, on separate outlets and as 1 or 0.
- **Closest in Max:** `abl.dsp.euclid~`, `rtt.euclidean~ (Rhythm and Time Toolkit)`, `ts.euclideanSeq (TS.Modular)`
- **Checked:** Bundled Ableton DSP: abl.dsp.euclid~, 'Euclidean rhythm ramp generator', a signal object with frequency, steps and pulses inlets. No control-rate Euclidean object in the registry. Installed packages have rtt.euclidean~ and ts.euclideanSeq.
- **Notes:** At control rate this is a short script in v8. A common formula (general knowledge, not from the help file): step i is a hit when ((i * hits) % steps) < hits.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/euclid-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/19-Sequencing/5.Rhythmic/3.[euclid].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--euclidean-rhythm-from-a-step-number`

### Markov chains of any order, learned from input (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `markov`, `histogram`, `rand.hist`
- **What it does:** Feed markov a stream of numbers, symbols or lists; 'create' builds a chain of the chosen order from what it heard, and each bang outputs the next item. Lists make polyphonic chains, the order can be changed and the chain rebuilt, and the chain can be saved with the patch. The cyclone help files point to it as the alternative to prob and anal.
- **Closest in Max:** `prob`, `anal`, `markov (RTC-lib)`, `gtm.markov (gtm.markov)`, `cage.markov.analysis (cage)`
- **Checked:** Refpages. prob: 'Create a weighted transition table'; anal: 'Make a histogram of number pairs'. Together they are a first-order chain over integers. query_packages.py 'markov' found RTC-lib, gtm.markov and cage objects among installed packages.
- **Notes:** Core Max stops at first order and integers.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/markov-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/prob-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/anal-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/20-Stochastic/7.Markov.Chain/2.MIDI-File.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--markov-chains-of-any-order-learned-from-input`

### Sequences and rhythms written as text with their own notation (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `sequencer`, `pattern`, `score`, `score2`, `list.seq`, `notedur2ratio`
- **What it does:** sequencer steps through a typed list in which '-' joins items into a chord, a lone '-' is a rest, '_' is a tie and '\|' a barline; items can be note names. pattern takes durations as fractions of a whole note (1/8, 1/12 for a triplet, negative for rests) and runs them at a tempo. score and score2 are text scores with bars, time signatures, tempo changes and events placed by beat position or by duration.
- **Closest in Max:** `seq`, `detonate`, `coll`, `text`, `qlist`, `live.step`, `bach.score (bach)`, `bach.roll (bach)`, `mtr`
- **Checked:** Max's sequencing objects store MIDI or timed messages (seq, detonate, mtr, qlist) or steps (live.step). None reads a typed rhythm-and-pitch notation. The bach package (installed) has full notation objects.
- **Notes:** The design worth borrowing for a small Max abstraction or v8 script: keep rhythm (a list of fractions) and pitch (a list of names) as two separate lists that can be recombined, as pattern and sequencer do.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/sequencer-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/pattern-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/score-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/list.seq-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/19-Sequencing/6.[qlist].[text].[score].[score2]/7.[score].pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/19-Sequencing/5.Rhythmic/6.[pattern].pd>
