### Comparisons that report the moment they change, not only their state (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Comparator`, `Text Comparator`, `Inside Range`
- **What it does:** Each comparison actor has three outputs: the 1 or 0 result, a trigger when the result turns true, and a trigger when it turns false. A 'notify' input chooses between sending the result on every input or only when it changes. Inside Range does the same for a low/high range, with 'enter' and 'exit' triggers.
- **Closest in Max:** `>`, `==`, `change`, `togedge`, `select`, `split`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `>` and `==` output 1 or 0 for every number received in the left inlet; neither has a change-only mode or an edge outlet. `change` passes a number only if it differs from the stored one. `togedge` bangs its left outlet on a 0-to-1 transition and its right outlet on 1-to-0. `split` sends in-range numbers left and the rest right, with no enter or exit event.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max a comparison that drives an event needs two more boxes: put `togedge` (or `change` then `select 1 0`) straight under the `>`, or the event re-fires on every incoming number. For a range, feed `split`'s two outlets into `1` and `0` messages, then `togedge`.
- **Notes:** One Isadora actor does the work of three Max objects. The missing `change` is a common cause of events that fire continuously in Max.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=448 (Actors Reference: Comparator)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=770 (Actors Reference: Text Comparator)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=521 (Actors Reference: Inside Range)>
- id: `isadora--comparisons-that-report-the-moment-they-change-not-only-their-state`

### Selector that re-sends the chosen input's current value when the choice changes (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `Selector`, `Value Select`, `Router`, `Gate`
- **What it does:** Selector routes one of several inputs to one output. When the selection changes it immediately sends the current value of the newly chosen input, so the output is never stale. The inputs are typed by the first link made, and can carry video.
- **Closest in Max:** `switch`, `gate`, `value`, `pv`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `switch`: a number in the left inlet 'specifies an open inlet for receiving subsequent messages'. It holds no values, so changing the selection outputs nothing until the newly opened inlet receives a message. `gate` is the same in the other direction.
- **Advice for Max:** After changing a `switch` in Max, the output still shows the old source until the new one speaks. Where that matters, keep each source's latest value in an `int`, `float` or `pv` and bang the chosen one when the selection changes, or do the selection in `v8`.
- **Notes:** A frequent cause of 'the menu changed but nothing happened' in Max patches.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=697 (Actors Reference: Selector)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=804 (Actors Reference: Value Select)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=685 (Actors Reference: Router)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=500 (Actors Reference: Gate)>
- id: `isadora--selector-that-re-sends-the-chosen-input-s-current-value-when-the-choice-changes`

### A table that reports what changed (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `DAT Execute DAT`
- **What it does:** A callback runs when a table changes and is handed the old table, the new one and lists of the rows, columns and cells that were added, removed or changed.
- **Closest in Max:** `dict`, `coll`, `dict.compare`, `pattr`
- **Checked:** dict and coll refpages list no change notification. dict.compare is in obj-qlookup.json (not read in detail). pattr refpage: a bound pattr outputs when its value changes, for single values.
- **Advice for Max:** Send every write to a shared dict or coll through one subpatcher that also sends a `changed <key>` message, so readers know when to refresh. butter_table could report changes the same way.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/DAT_Execute_DAT>
- id: `touchdesigner--a-table-that-reports-what-changed`

### Name patterns that select and generate (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Pattern Matching`, `Pattern Expansion`, `Pattern Replacement`, `Select CHOP`, `Rename CHOP`
- **What it does:** One small pattern language is used everywhere a name is wanted: wildcards, character sets, number ranges with a step, and exclusion. The same brackets generate lists of names, and a replacement form reuses what a wildcard matched when renaming.
- **Closest in Max:** `route`, `regexp`, `sprintf`
- **Checked:** route refpage: exact matches against typed arguments. regexp refpage: PCRE matching and substitution on symbols and lists. sprintf refpage: builds a symbol from a format. No name-range syntax appears in any of them.
- **Advice for Max:** Name families with a numeric suffix (LEVEL_1 … LEVEL_8), generate the names with sprintf from a counter, and select with regexp. When you design a reusable object that takes names, let it accept a pattern.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pattern_Matching> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pattern_Expansion> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pattern_Replacement> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pattern_Matching_Support> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Select_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Rename_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Constant_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--name-patterns-that-select-and-generate`

### Gates and routers that send a set value when closed (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `Ops.Number.GateNumber`, `Ops.String.GateString`, `Ops.Gl.GateTexture`, `Ops.Number.RouteNumber`, `Ops.String.RouteString`, `Ops.Json.RouteObject`
- **What it does:** A closed gate has a stated output: keep the last value, or send a custom one (for textures, an empty one). A router sends the value to one output and can reset the other outputs to a default value. So nothing downstream is left holding a stale value by accident.
- **Closest in Max:** `gate`, `switch`, `route`, `select`
- **Checked:** gate refpage: 0 closes it, a number opens that outlet; messages pass while open. switch refpage: passes messages from one chosen inlet. Neither sends anything on closing or switching, and neither has a default-value setting. The number of ports is fixed by the argument.
- **Advice for Max:** When a Max gate closes, whatever is downstream keeps its last value. If that is wrong for the patch (a level that should drop to 0, a layer that should go blank), send the reset yourself: take the gate's control value through 'sel 0' into a message box holding the idle value.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.GateNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.String.GateString> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.GateTexture> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.RouteNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.String.RouteString> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.RouteObject> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.SwitchNumberMultiPort_v2>
- id: `cables-gl--gates-and-routers-that-send-a-set-value-when-closed`

### Range mapping that clips by default and has log and exponential modes (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `rescale`, `rescale~`
- **What it does:** Maps an input range (0-127 by default, the MIDI range) to an output range. It clips to the output range unless told not to. An exponent bends the curve, and a log mode makes the midpoint the geometric mean, which suits frequencies. The cyclone help files call it 'much simpler and more powerful' than scale.
- **Closest in Max:** `scale`, `zmap`, `clip`, `scale~`
- **Checked:** scale refpage: maps ranges with an optional exponent; a 'classic' attribute. Its description of out-of-range input is in cyclone's clone ('will not clip'). zmap: maps ranges, no exponent argument. clip limits a range.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, scale does not clip, so follow it with clip (or use zmap when no curve is needed). For a frequency control, an exponential mapping with the right base gives the same result as ELSE's log mode. See also the separate entry on scale's classic mode.
- **Notes:** That Max's scale does not clip is stated in cyclone's help file for its clone, not in the Max refpage text that was read.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/rescale-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rescale~-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/scale-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/scale~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--range-mapping-that-clips-by-default-and-has-log-and-exponential-modes`

### One number type (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `float`, `int`, `/`
- **What it does:** Pd has only floats. [/ 4] of 5 is 1.25, and '1' and '1.' are the same atom. [int] exists to truncate on purpose.
- **Closest in Max:** `/`, `scale`, `float`, `number`
- **Checked:** Userguide integers_vs_floats page: objects run in integer or float mode depending on whether the argument has a decimal point; it calls this 'a common source of bugs'.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, write the decimal point on every arithmetic argument that should not truncate ('/ 4.', 'scale 0 127 0. 1.'). A check for integer-mode arithmetic feeding a float display is a candidate rule for the Claude2Max verifier.
- **Source:** <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm> · <Documentation/2.control.examples/04.messages.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/int-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--one-number-type`

### Counter with a step size, limit-or-wrap, floats, and a settable current value (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Counter`, `Float Counter`
- **What it does:** Adds or subtracts a set amount on its 'add' and 'sub' triggers, between a minimum and maximum. A mode chooses between stopping at the ends and wrapping. The current value is itself an input, so it can be preset or given a start value. Float Counter is the same with decimals.
- **Closest in Max:** `counter`, `accum`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `counter` counts whole numbers by one, up, down or up-and-down, between a minimum and maximum, with `set`, `jam`, `inc`, `dec` and carry outlets at the ends. `accum` stores a number and adds or multiplies into it, int or float, with no limits.
- **Advice for Max:** Use `counter` for stepping through indexes; its up-and-down mode and carry outlets go beyond Isadora's. For a bounded float or a step other than one, use `accum` followed by `clip` or `%`, or `v8`. Do not force `counter` to do it.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=452 (Actors Reference: Counter)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=497 (Actors Reference: Float Counter)>
- id: `isadora--counter-with-a-step-size-limit-or-wrap-floats-and-a-settable-current-value`

### Named sets of global values that announce their own changes (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Set Global Values`, `Get Global Values`
- **What it does:** A named collection of values that outlives Scene changes. Set writes the collection; any active Get with the same name outputs all the values whenever a Set is triggered, and also when its own Scene starts. Streams such as video are refused, and a trigger cannot be stored.
- **Closest in Max:** `value`, `pv`, `dict`, `pattr`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `value`: objects with the same name share one stored message, and 'you can get the contents out of a particular value object by sending it a bang', so readers are not told when it changes. `pv` is the same, limited to one patcher and its subpatchers. `dict` is a named store with keys.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max a shared `value` is passive: pair it with an `s`/`r` of the same name when other parts of the patch must react to a change. For a named group of settings use one `dict`, not many `value` objects.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=719 (Actors Reference: Set Global Values)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=501 (Actors Reference: Get Global Values)> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000014936-using-the-global-value-actors>
- id: `isadora--named-sets-of-global-values-that-announce-their-own-changes`

### Table actor with read-on-start and write-on-exit file modes (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `Data Array`
- **What it does:** A list of lines, each with a set number of numeric or text items, with one input and one output per item. Lines are added, inserted, replaced, deleted and recalled by number. A tab-separated text file can back it, with a mode that reads on activation, writes on deactivation, both or neither, and a switch for keeping a copy inside the document.
- **Closest in Max:** `coll`, `dict`, `text`, `closebang`, `jit.cellblock`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `coll`: when a patch with a named coll loads, Max looks for a file of that name and loads it; `embed` saves the data in the patcher; `write` and `writeagain` save on request. `dict` takes a file name as an argument to import on load and has `embed`. `closebang` sends a bang when its patcher window closes. Nothing writes back by itself.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max say where the data lives: `@embed 1` for data that belongs to the patch, a named file for data that outlives it. For write-on-exit, wire `closebang` to `writeagain`. `coll` returns a whole line as a list, so follow it with `unjoin` to get Isadora's one-outlet-per-column.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=457 (Actors Reference: Data Array)>
- id: `isadora--table-actor-with-read-on-start-and-write-on-exit-file-modes`

### Text as its own value type, with small actors for cutting and joining (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `Text Chopper`, `Text Accumulator`, `Text Wrapper`, `Text Comparator`, `Text Formatter`, `Value To Text`, `Character to Number`, `Trigger Text`
- **What it does:** Text is a link type. Small actors take the first, last or middle characters or lines, append to a growing string, wrap at a line length, compare two strings with or without case, and build formatted text from up to nine parameters.
- **Closest in Max:** `string`, `string.sprintf`, `string.substring`, `string.split`, `string.compare`, `sprintf`, `regexp`, `fromsymbol`
- **Checked:** obj-qlookup.json lists about forty `string.*` objects in Max 9 (append, compare, contains, indexof, length, replace, slice, split, sprintf, substring, tolower, trim and others). `string`'s refpage calls a string 'a block of text in memory that is not stored in Max's symbol table', and the userguide's strings page says the purpose-built String objects are easier and more efficient for text work than symbols. Also read the digests of `string.sprintf`, `string.split` and `string.substring`.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max 9, treat text that changes while the patch runs as a `string`, not a symbol, and reach for `string.*` before `sprintf` and `regexp` chains. Max is now at least level with Isadora here, but only if the new family is used.
- **Notes:** Only four of the string objects' pages were opened; the rest are known by registry name.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=769 (Actors Reference: Text Accumulator / Text Chopper)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=770 (Actors Reference: Text Comparator)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=777 (Actors Reference: Text Formatter)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=779 (Actors Reference: Text Wrapper)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=805 (Actors Reference: Value To Text)>
- id: `isadora--text-as-its-own-value-type-with-small-actors-for-cutting-and-joining`

### Any object's settings as a live table (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `Parameter DAT`
- **What it does:** One operator lists another operator's parameters as rows: name, value, mode, expression, type, range, default and menu items. The table updates as the parameters change.
- **Closest in Max:** `getattr`, `attrui`, `pattrhub`, `pattrstorage`
- **Checked:** getattr refpage: attributes attr, listen and prefix, message getattrlist. pattrhub refpage: getstate and getattributes report every pattr object in a patcher. pattrstorage refpage: dump and getclientlist. Userguide 'Parameter Mode': the Parameters Window lists all parameters of a patcher.
- **Advice for Max:** For a readable dump of a patch's state, send getstate to pattrhub or dump to pattrstorage and collect the result in a dict or butter_table. For one attribute of one object, use getattr with listen on instead of polling.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Parameter_DAT>
- id: `touchdesigner--any-object-s-settings-as-a-live-table`

### Cells addressed by row and column names (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Table DAT`, `Select DAT`, `Lookup DAT`, `Merge DAT`, `DAT to CHOP`
- **What it does:** A table cell is read by the names in its first row and first column, in scripts and in operators alike. Rows can be selected by name, by index, by a list of values or by a condition, and tables can be merged by matching names.
- **Closest in Max:** `dict`, `array`, `coll`, `jit.cellblock`
- **Checked:** coll refpage: an index or symbol maps to a list, fields inside the list are positional. dict refpage: keys with get, set, getkeys, plus pull_from_coll and push_to_coll. Userguide 'Arrays': arrays can hold dictionaries. jit.cellblock refpage: cells addressed by column and row number.
- **Advice for Max:** Store records as a dict (or an array of dicts) so fields are read by name. coll rows with positional fields break the first time a column is inserted.
- **Notes:** Session 1's 'Tables as a dataflow type' covers the operator family; this entry is the naming habit.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Table_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Select_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Lookup_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Merge_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/DAT_to_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Working_with_DATs_in_Python> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Reorder_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Insert_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Sort_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Transpose_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Convert_DAT>
- id: `touchdesigner--cells-addressed-by-row-and-column-names`

### Change callbacks with a choice of edge (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `CHOP Execute DAT`, `Parameter Execute DAT`, `OP Execute DAT`, `ParGroup Execute DAT`
- **What it does:** A script is attached to a value and runs on the events you tick: off to on, while on, on to off, while off, any change. Other versions watch parameters (value, expression, mode, pulse) and whole operators (cooked, renamed, rewired, deleted).
- **Closest in Max:** `togedge`, `change`, `select`, `edge~`, `getattr`, `v8`
- **Checked:** togedge refpage: bangs for 0-to-1 and 1-to-0. change refpage: passes only a new value. edge~ refpage: the same for signals. getattr refpage: listen reports attribute changes. v8 refpage: function names answer messages.
- **Advice for Max:** Detect the edge in boxes (togedge, change) and send only the event into v8. A script that receives every value and tests it itself is the TouchDesigner habit carried over, and in Max it runs code at control rate for nothing.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/CHOP_Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Parameter_Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/OP_Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/ParGroup_Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Callback>
- id: `touchdesigner--change-callbacks-with-a-choice-of-edge`

### Curves and lookup tables as data (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `Lookup CHOP`, `Pattern CHOP`, `S Curve CHOP`, `Lookup TOP`, `Ramp TOP`
- **What it does:** A 0-to-1 index reads an interpolated value out of any stored curve, with settings for what happens outside the range and for cyclic curves. Generators make sine, ramp, pulse and S-shaped curves as arrays. The image version remaps colours through a gradient whose keys live in a table.
- **Closest in Max:** `function`, `table`, `scale`, `jit.charmap`, `jit.gradient.ui`
- **Checked:** function refpage: 'You can also get an interpolated y value for any x value', with domain and range attributes. scale refpage: optional exponent argument. jit.charmap refpage: 256-point input-to-output map for matrices. jit.gradient.ui refpage: an editor that outputs one-dimensional four-plane gradient matrices.
- **Advice for Max:** For a transfer curve, send x into function and take y, instead of chaining scale and expr. For a colour remap on the GPU, sample a gradient texture (jit.gradient.ui into jit.gl.texture) inside jit.gl.pix.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Lookup_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pattern_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/S_Curve_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Lookup_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Ramp_TOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--curves-and-lookup-tables-as-data`

### Many channels from one generator, each one different (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Many channels from one generator
- **There:** `Noise CHOP`, `LFO CHOP`, `Pattern CHOP`, `Wave CHOP`, `Channel Names`, `Phase Step per Channel`, `$C`
- **What it does:** One generator makes as many channels as its Channel Names pattern asks for: chan[1-20] gives twenty. Noise CHOP gives every channel its own seed, so the channels differ with no extra setup. Pattern CHOP has a Phase Step per Channel, and in LFO CHOP any parameter can use the channel index $C, so each channel can run at its own rate. The channels are control data and can drive anything: instances, lights, geometry, parameters.
- **Closest in Max:** `mc.noise~`, `mc.rand~`, `mc.cycle~`, `mc.phasor~`, `mc.evolve~`, `mc.gradient~`, `multirange`, `mc.list~`, `mc.snapshot~`, `jit.noise`, `jit.bfg`
- **Checked:** Max's MC objects do the same for signals. An mc.* object with @chans N runs N copies, and the MC wrapper takes messages that set a different value in each copy: deviate (a random spread, width then centre) is described in the userguide topic 'Generating Values for All MC Wrapper Instances', which also covers a range of values, an exponential series and a harmonic series; mc.noise~'s refpage lists the wrapper messages deviate, spread, exponential, harmonic, randomrange and applyvalues. mc.evolve~ and mc.gradient~ (refpages and the userguide topic 'MC Function Generators') spread a breakpoint function across the channels, drawn with multirange. The difference is the rate: MC channels are audio signals. Reading them back as numbers takes mc.snapshot~, whose mc mode sends one value per channel with a 'voice <n>' message from a right outlet (snapshot~ help file, 'mc' tab), not one list.
- **Advice for Max:** For audio, write it the Max way: one mc.* generator with @chans N, then deviate or spread for the per-channel values, or mc.gradient~ for a shaped spread. For visuals or messages, don't route through MC and back: make the N values as a matrix (jit.noise, jit.bfg) or a list. A message-rate generator that outputs a list of N independent noise or LFO values per tick, each channel with its own seed or phase, does not exist in core Max and is worth building.
- **Notes:** Added by hand 2026-10-02 after John asked about TouchDesigner's multichannel function and noise generators; the scans had read these pages but recorded only single-channel noise.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): TouchDesigner pages and Max refpages, userguide topics and the snapshot~ help file read for this entry. Not run in Max.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Noise_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/LFO_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pattern_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Wave_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--many-channels-from-one-generator-each-one-different`

### Named converters at every boundary between kinds of data (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `CHOP to TOP`, `DAT to CHOP`, `CHOP to DAT`, `SOP to CHOP`, `POP to CHOP`, `POP to TOP`
- **What it does:** Wires only join operators of one family, so every crossing is a visible operator named for it. Each has settings for layout: how channels become rows of pixels, which table rows become channels, which point attributes become channels.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fill`, `jit.spill`, `jit.iter`, `jit.catch~`, `jit.release~`, `jit.poke~`, `jit.buffer~`, `snapshot~`, `jit.gl.asyncread`
- **Checked:** Userguide 'Conversion Cheat Sheet': patches for message to audio, audio to message, signal to event and unit conversions. Refpages: jit.fill and jit.spill (list to matrix and back), jit.iter, jit.catch~ and jit.release~ (signal to matrix and back), jit.poke~, jit.buffer~, snapshot~, jit.gl.asyncread (texture read back to the CPU without blocking).
- **Advice for Max:** The same boundaries exist in Max, they are just not enforced. Cross each one once, with one visible converter, and stay on the GPU once you are there: reading a texture back costs a frame of latency even with jit.gl.asyncread.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/CHOP_to_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/DAT_to_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/CHOP_to_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/SOP_to_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/POP_to_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/POP_to_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/CHOP_Techniques> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Shuffle_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--named-converters-at-every-boundary-between-kinds-of-data`

### Text handling without a symbol table (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `Python f-strings`, `Substitute DAT`, `Convert DAT`, `Evaluate DAT`
- **What it does:** Text is edited with Python string expressions in any parameter or table cell, and with operators that substitute patterns or split text into cells.
- **Closest in Max:** `string`, `string.replace`, `string.regexp`, `string.split`, `string.sprintf`, `sprintf`, `regexp`
- **Checked:** obj-qlookup.json lists 40 string.* objects (replace, replaceall, regexp, split, slice, sprintf, trim, tolower, toarray and others). Userguide 'Strings': a String is UTF-8 text held outside the symbol table, and the page says the purpose-built string objects are easier and more efficient than symbols for text work.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max 9 do text editing with string.* objects, not by juggling symbols through sprintf, regexp and zl. Move to v8 when it turns into logic.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Python_f-strings> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Substitute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Convert_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Evaluate_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Text_DAT>
- id: `touchdesigner--text-handling-without-a-symbol-table`

### All variables as one object (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `Ops.Vars.VarSetNumber_v2`, `Ops.Vars.VarGetNumber_v2`, `Ops.Vars.VariablesAsObject`, `Ops.Vars.VariablesFromObject`, `Ops.Vars.VarSetTexture_v2`
- **What it does:** Variables are typed: number, string, array, object, texture. VariablesAsObject returns every variable in the patch as one object, with an optional name prefix as filter. VariablesFromObject writes values back into existing variables from such an object.
- **Closest in Max:** `value`, `pv`, `pattr`, `pattrstorage`, `dict`
- **Checked:** value refpage: same-named value objects share data everywhere. pv refpage: shared only inside one patcher and its subpatches. pattrstorage refpage: methods getclientlist, store, recall, writejson and writexml, so the state it holds can be listed and saved as one file. No way to list all value objects was found in the value refpage.
- **Advice for Max:** Keep the state of a Max patch that must be saved, dumped or restored in pattr objects under one pattrstorage, or in one dict, not in scattered value objects. That gives the 'whole state as one object' that cables gets from VariablesAsObject.
- **Notes:** The cables pages read do not say whether a variable is visible inside sub-patches or across patches. Max is clearer here: value is global, pv is per patcher.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.VarSetNumber_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.VarGetNumber_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.VariablesAsObject> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.VariablesFromObject> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.VarSetTexture_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.VarGetTexture_v2>
- id: `cables-gl--all-variables-as-one-object`

### Arrays and objects copied, not shared (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `Ops.Array.ArraySetNumber_v3`, `Ops.Array.CopyArray`, `Ops.Json.CopyObject`, `setRef`
- **What it does:** The op guidelines say an array op must copy its input and output the copy, and output null when nothing is connected. ArraySetNumber's page says it makes a new array and leaves the original untouched. So an op further up the chain never sees its data changed by an op below.
- **Closest in Max:** `dict`, `array`, `jit.matrix`, `zl`
- **Checked:** Userguide dictionaries.json: 'Two dict objects with the same name will reference the same dictionary', and what travels on a cord is the word dictionary followed by the name. Userguide arrays.json: arrays 'are stored in memory by name', like dictionaries and strings. dict refpage: a clone method. Lists, by contrast, are copied with each message.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, a list is a copy but a dict, array, string, matrix or buffer on a cord is a name pointing at shared data. Before a branch changes one, clone it, or two branches will change each other's data. The cables performance page gives the other side: copying big arrays every frame is slow.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.ArraySetNumber_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.CopyArray> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.ArrayGetNumber> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/guidelines/guidelines> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_creating_ports/dev_ports_array/dev_ports_array> · <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/03_arrays/arrays>
- id: `cables-gl--arrays-and-objects-copied-not-shared`

### Building arrays, objects and strings along a trigger chain (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `Ops.Data.Compose.Array.CompArray`, `Ops.Data.Compose.Array.CompArrayPushNumber`, `Ops.Data.Compose.Object.CompObject`, `Ops.Data.Compose.Object.CompObjectSetNumber`, `Ops.Data.Compose.String.CompString`
- **What it does:** A Comp op opens an empty array, object or string. Ops triggered under it push numbers, set keys or append text. When the chain is done the Comp op outputs the result. Loops under it add one item per pass.
- **Closest in Max:** `dict.pack`, `join`, `zl.group`, `array (append)`, `sprintf`, `combine`
- **Checked:** dict.pack refpage: builds a dictionary from inlets, with keys and triggers attributes. zl.group refpage: collects items and outputs a list at a set length or on bang. array refpage: append, insert and prepend messages. sprintf and combine refpages: build one symbol from several inputs; combine also has a triggers attribute.
- **Advice for Max:** The Max equivalents are collectors, not scopes: zl.group for a list built inside an uzi loop (bang it when the loop's done-outlet fires), dict.pack for a record from several values. Past a few fields, do it in v8, as the repo's preferred-objects table already says.
- **Notes:** None of the Comp op pages has written documentation; the description rests on names, ports and the namespace page.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Data.Compose.Array.CompArray> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Data.Compose.Array.CompArrayPushNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Data.Compose.Object.CompObject> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Data.Compose.Object.CompObjectSetNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Data.Compose.String.CompString>
- id: `cables-gl--building-arrays-objects-and-strings-along-a-trigger-chain`

### Expression ops with named inputs and a validity output (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Errors and status as data
- **There:** `Ops.Math.MathExpression`, `Ops.Math.TriggerMathExpression`, `Ops.Array.Math.ArrayMathExpression`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Math.RgbMathExpression`
- **What it does:** The same typed-expression idea exists for a number, an array and an image. Inputs are named ports: a, b, c, d for numbers; arrays a, b, c with i for the index and len for the length; x, y, z, w and texA, texB for the image one. The functions are JavaScript's Math functions, or GLSL for the image one. The number ops have an Expression Valid output.
- **Closest in Max:** `expr`, `vexpr`, `jit.expr`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.gen`
- **Checked:** expr refpage: C-like expression; inlets come from $i1, $f1, $s1 style arguments, so the inlet's type is part of its name. vexpr refpage: the same for lists, with a scalarmode attribute. jit.expr refpage: expressions that fill a matrix. jit.gl.pix refpage: per-pixel shaders from a gen patcher. None has an outlet that says the expression is valid; a bad expression posts to the console.
- **Advice for Max:** Max has the same three levels, as four objects: expr for numbers, vexpr for lists, jit.expr for matrices, jit.gl.pix for textures. Pick by what flows on the cord, and write $f1 not $i1 unless whole numbers are wanted, since the letter decides the arithmetic.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.MathExpression> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.TriggerMathExpression> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.Math.ArrayMathExpression> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.Math.RgbMathExpression>
- id: `cables-gl--expression-ops-with-named-inputs-and-a-validity-output`

### Named triggers with a prefix filter (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `Ops.Trigger.TriggerSend`, `Ops.Trigger.TriggerReceive`, `Ops.Trigger.TriggerSendNamed`, `Ops.Trigger.TriggerReceiveFilter`
- **What it does:** TriggerSend and TriggerReceive pass a trigger by name, across a patch and into subpatches. TriggerSendNamed takes the name as a string port, so the target can change. TriggerReceiveFilter hears every named trigger whose name starts with a prefix, and outputs the name too.
- **Closest in Max:** `send`, `receive`, `forward`, `route`
- **Checked:** send and receive refpages: one fixed name per object; receive takes a 'set <name>' message to change it. forward refpage: 'the destination receive object of forward can be changed with each message'. route refpage: sorts messages by their first item. No receive that listens to a family of names was found.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, get the 'listen to a group' behaviour with one channel and a tag: send 'cue fade 2000' through [s SHOW] and split it with [route cue scene] at each receiver. Use forward when the destination has to change.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TriggerSend> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TriggerReceive> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TriggerSendNamed> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TriggerReceiveFilter>
- id: `cables-gl--named-triggers-with-a-prefix-filter`

### Nested data read by path with a found flag (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `Ops.Data.JsonPath.ObjectGetNumberByPath`, `Ops.Json.ObjectGetNumber_v2`, `Ops.Json.ObjectDeepSearch`, `Ops.Json.ObjectKeys`
- **What it does:** A path such as data.numbers.1 reads a value from inside nested objects and arrays. There is one op per result type (number, string, array, object), and each has a Found output. ObjectDeepSearch looks for a value anywhere in an object.
- **Closest in Max:** `dict (get with ::)`, `dict.unpack`, `dict.iter`, `dict.codebox`
- **Checked:** Userguide dictionaries.json: get and set take a double colon for a nested dictionary and square brackets for an array element. dict refpage: get answers on the second outlet, contains on the third, getkeys lists keys. dict.unpack refpage: one outlet per named key, with an unmatched option. A missing key gives no output from get.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's path syntax is :: for keys and [n] for array items, sent as 'get farm::animals[0]' to a dict. Since a missing key is silent, send contains first, or use dict.unpack, when the patch must know the difference between 'no such key' and 'no message yet'.
- **Notes:** The exact output of dict get for a missing key was not tested in Max; the refpage text for get is a placeholder.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Data.JsonPath.ObjectGetNumberByPath> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.ObjectGetNumber_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.ObjectDeepSearch> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.ObjectKeys> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.ParseObject_v2>
- id: `cables-gl--nested-data-read-by-path-with-a-found-flag`

### Op lifecycle callbacks: after patch load, on delete (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `op.onLoaded`, `op.init`, `op.onDelete`, `onLinkChanged`
- **What it does:** Top-level code in an op runs when the op is created. onLoaded runs once the whole patch is loaded and all ops are linked, and not when an op is merely added while editing. The docs warn that init is called twice on patch load. onDelete is for cleanup, and onLinkChanged fires when a cable is added or removed.
- **Closest in Max:** `v8 loadbang()`, `freebang`, `patcher lifecycle`
- **Checked:** v8 refpage: the loadbang message 'Invokes the function named loadbang if defined. This message is sent when the file is loaded.' Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md: cords are connected after objects are created, so output sent while a script is still being created may go nowhere; loadbang is phase 5. Repo patching/JITTER_JS_PATCHING.md line 323: an autowatch reload reruns the script but does not fire loadbang again. freebang refpage: bangs when the patcher is freed.
- **Advice for Max:** In a v8 script keep own-state setup at the top level, and put anything that sends to outlets or looks up other objects in loadbang(). Add a way to run that same setup by message, because a script reload during editing will not call loadbang, the same gap cables documents for onLoaded.
- **Notes:** Max has no callback for a cord being connected, which cables has in onLinkChanged.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_callbacks/dev_callbacks> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_ops/dev_ops>
- id: `cables-gl--op-lifecycle-callbacks-after-patch-load-on-delete`

### Read a value only when triggered (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Bypass and stopping unused work
- **There:** `Ops.Trigger.TriggerNumber`, `Ops.Vars.TriggerVarGetNumber`, `Ops.Array.ArrayTrigger`, `Ops.Json.TriggerObject`, `Ops.Array.CopyArray`
- **What it does:** Value cables update whenever the value changes. Where a patch must take a value at one moment instead, a 'trigger' form of the op exists: it holds the input and passes it on only when its trigger port fires. There is one for numbers, strings, arrays, objects and variables.
- **Closest in Max:** `float / int (right inlet)`, `zl.reg`, `jit.matrix @thru 0`, `value`
- **Checked:** float refpage: right inlet 'Set Value Without Output', a bang in the left outputs it. zl.reg refpage: right inlet stores a list, bang sends it. Repo scans/packages/tutorials_insights.md line 112, quoting the jit.matrix refpage: with @thru 0 a matrix is held and output only on bang.
- **Advice for Max:** The cold right inlet plus a bang is Max's latch, and it is built into the language, so no extra object type is needed: [f], [zl.reg], [jit.matrix @thru 0]. Use it whenever a value must be sampled at an event instead of followed.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TriggerNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.TriggerVarGetNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Vars.VarTriggerNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.ArrayTrigger> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.TriggerObject> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.CopyArray>
- id: `cables-gl--read-a-value-only-when-triggered`

### Smooth noise and seeded random as number ops (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Ops.Math.PerlinNoise_v2`, `Ops.Math.RandomNumbers_v4`, `Ops.Math.RandomNumbersFromString`, `Ops.Math.TriggerRandomNumber_v3`
- **What it does:** PerlinNoise gives one smooth noise value for an x, y, z position and a seed. RandomNumbers gives four fixed random numbers for a seed, so a look stays the same on reload. RandomNumbersFromString seeds from text. TriggerRandomNumber has a switch for 'no consecutive duplicates'.
- **Closest in Max:** `random @seed`, `urn`, `drunk`, `jit.mo.time @function perlin`
- **Checked:** random refpage: seed (0 means time-based, non-zero gives the same sequence), cycle (repeat after N outputs), range. urn refpage: no repeats until all values are used. drunk refpage: random walk. C74/interfaces/obj-qlookup.json: no name contains 'perlin'; jit.mo.time has a perlin function over time only.
- **Advice for Max:** For a repeatable look in Max give every random a non-zero @seed. For smooth wandering values use jit.mo.time @function perlin or drunk into slide; there is no object that samples noise at a position, so do that in v8 or gen when it is needed.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.PerlinNoise_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.RandomNumbers_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.RandomNumbersFromString> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.TriggerRandomNumber_v3>
- id: `cables-gl--smooth-noise-and-seeded-random-as-number-ops`

### Trigger utilities: once, every nth, limit rate, by chance, on threshold (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Ops.Trigger.TriggerOnce`, `Ops.Trigger.NthTrigger_v2`, `Ops.Trigger.TriggerLimiter`, `Ops.Trigger.ProbabilityTrigger`, `Ops.Trigger.Threshold`, `Ops.Boolean.MonoFlop`, `Ops.Trigger.IsTriggered`
- **What it does:** Small ops shape a stream of triggers: pass only the first until reset, pass every nth, pass at most one per x milliseconds, pass by a probability with a second output for the misses, fire once when a number crosses a threshold, hold a value for x seconds after a trigger, and report whether a branch ran last frame.
- **Closest in Max:** `onebang`, `counter`, `speedlim`, `qlim`, `decide`, `past`, `delay`
- **Checked:** onebang refpage: lets one bang through until re-armed at the right inlet. counter refpage: counts bangs, with carry outlets. speedlim and qlim refpages: at most one message per time. decide refpage: random 0 or 1. past refpage: bangs when a number rises past a value and re-arms when the input falls back below it. delay refpage: holds a bang for a time. All found; one Max object per cables op, except probability with a set chance, which is random plus a comparison.
- **Advice for Max:** Use this as a translation table when porting: TriggerOnce is onebang, NthTrigger is counter into sel, TriggerLimiter is speedlim, Threshold is past, MonoFlop is a 1 followed by delay into 0. Max is level here; the names are just harder to guess.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TriggerOnce> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.NthTrigger_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TriggerLimiter> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.ProbabilityTrigger> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.Threshold> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Boolean.MonoFlop> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.IsTriggered> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.GateTrigger>
- id: `cables-gl--trigger-utilities-once-every-nth-limit-rate-by-chance-on-threshold`

### Note-length symbols with arbitrary tuplets and ties (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tuning and pitch
- **There:** `notedur2ratio`, `makenote2`, `pattern`, `metronome`
- **What it does:** Converts symbols such as 4n, 8nt, 16nd to a fraction of a whole note. Beyond dotted and triplet forms it accepts quintuplet, septuplet and nonuplet letters, an explicit [x:y] tuplet, double dots and tied values joined with '_'.
- **Closest in Max:** `translate`, `metro`, `makenote`, `transport`
- **Checked:** Max user guide 'Time Value Syntax': tempo-relative note values run from 1n to 128n with dotted (d) and triplet (t) forms; the names found in the page are 1n 1nd 1nt 2n 2nd 2nt 4n 4nd 4nt 8n 8nd 8nt 16n 16nd 16nt 32n 32nd 32nt 64n 64nd 128n. translate converts between time formats. Ticks and bars.beats.units are the other tempo-relative forms.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's note values are built into the time attribute of every timing object, which is better than a converter object, but they stop at dotted and triplet. For a quintuplet or any other division, give the interval in ticks or compute ms from the tempo.
- **Notes:** The same user guide page gives one tick as 1/480; the sentence around it was not read in full, so check what the 480 divides before relying on it.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/notedur2ratio-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/makenote2-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--note-length-symbols-with-arbitrary-tuplets-and-ties`

### Weighted and non-repeating random choice objects (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Random and probability
- **There:** `chance`, `rand.hist`, `rand.dist`, `rand.u`, `rand.list`, `rand.i`, `rand.f`, `spread`
- **What it does:** chance sends a bang to one of several outlets by weight. rand.hist picks an index from a list of weights and has a mode that uses each weighted item up before repeating. rand.dist treats an array as a probability curve. rand.u is an urn. All take a -seed flag and a seed message, and a bare 'seed' asks for a fresh one; with no seed each object gets a new one every time the patch loads.
- **Closest in Max:** `random`, `urn`, `drunk`, `decide`, `table`, `histo`, `prob`
- **Checked:** Refpages. random and drunk have a seed attribute; urn and decide take a seed argument or message. table: a bang gives 'Output a random quantile', that is, a random index weighted by the stored values, and quantile and fquantile do the same from a given number.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's weighted random is a table used as a histogram: store weights, send bang. It is easy to miss because nothing in the name says so. For weighted outlets, follow it with select. urn is the non-repeating one.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/chance-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rand.hist-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rand.dist-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rand.u-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/rand.i-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/20-Stochastic/5.[histogram].[rand.hist].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--weighted-and-non-repeating-random-choice-objects`

### Named, indexed image store (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `pix_buffer`, `pix_buffer_write`, `pix_buffer_read`, `pix_buffer_filmopen`
- **What it does:** pix_buffer is a named store of numbered image slots, described in its help as like a table for images. Separate write and read objects put an image into a slot or fetch one; images can be loaded from and saved to files by slot, and an abstraction reads a whole movie into the slots at start-up.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.matrixset`, `jit.gl.textureset`, `jit.matrix`
- **Checked:** Read jit.matrixset ('a set of internal matrices which may be written to or read from'; index, matrixcount, importmovie, exportmovie) and jit.gl.textureset (Jitter Tools package: the same for textures). Named jit.matrix objects share one store by name.
- **Advice for Max:** Use jit.matrixset for a frame bank and jit.gl.textureset when the frames only go to the screen. Unlike pix_buffer, a matrixset's slots all share one size and type, so resize on the way in.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_buffer-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_buffer_write-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_buffer_read-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_buffer_filmopen-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/25.buffer.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--named-indexed-image-store`

- **There:** `pdlua`, `pd.Class:new():register`, `initialize`, `in_1_float`, `outlet`, `pd.Clock`, `pd.Receive`, `pd.Table`

### Array objects that treat a table as a histogram (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `array quantile`, `array random`, `array sum`, `array min`, `array max`, `array get`, `array set`
- **What it does:** [array random] draws an index with probability proportional to the stored values, and [array quantile] is the same with your own random source. The family also sums, finds min and max, and gets or sets ranges as lists.
- **Closest in Max:** `table`, `histo`, `prob`
- **Checked:** table refpage: bang outputs a random quantile; quantile and fquantile messages; sum, min, max; integer values. histo and prob are in the registry (not read).
- **Advice for Max:** Same idea in both: Max's table bang is Pd's [array random]. Note table holds ints, so scale weights.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/array-object-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata--array-objects-that-treat-a-table-as-a-histogram`

### Destination set on the sender, and 'send' built into storage objects (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `send`, `float`, `int`, `value`, `list store`, `pointer`
- **What it does:** [send] with no argument has an inlet for the destination name. [float], [int], [value], [list store] and [pointer] take 'send name' and deliver their content to that receiver. A name can be a receive, a value, an array, a GUI object or a window.
- **Closest in Max:** `forward`, `receive`, `send`
- **Checked:** Refpages: forward ('destination can be changed with each message', send message), receive (set message), send (no set).
- **Advice for Max:** In Max use forward when the destination changes, and receive's set when the listener should retune.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/send-receive-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/float-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/value-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>

### Stored message lists with search and sequencing ([text]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `text define`, `text get`, `text set`, `text insert`, `text delete`, `text size`, `text tolist`, `text fromlist`, `text search`, `text sequence`
- **What it does:** A text is a list of messages kept in the patch. Lines and items are read and written by number, the best-matching line is found by key with exact, greater, less or nearest tests, and [text sequence] plays the lines as timed messages with $ arguments.
- **Closest in Max:** `coll`, `text`, `qlist`, `dict`, `array`, `string`
- **Checked:** Refpages: coll (store by index or symbol, sort, min/max, embed; no key search with comparisons), text (lines of text: line, query, read, write), qlist (timed message lines with tempo), dict, and Max 9's array and string.
- **Advice for Max:** Max splits the job: coll or dict for stored records, qlist for timed message lists. Pd's 'nearest match' search has no one-object match; do it in v8.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/text-object-help.pd> · <Documentation/2.control.examples/23.sequencing.pd>
- id: `plugdata--stored-message-lists-with-search-and-sequencing-text`

### scale: 'classic' exponential mode is the default in Max, and cyclone changed it (plugdata (cyclone))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `scale`, `scale~`, `linedrive`
- **What it does:** cyclone's clone of scale uses the modern exponential formula by default. Its help file says Max's scale defaults to the older 'classic' formula while scale~ defaults to the modern one, calls that an inconsistency, and calls classic mode buggy. It also notes that before Max 6.0.4 the modern exponent was inverted.
- **Closest in Max:** `scale`, `scale~`
- **Checked:** Max 9.1.5. transform-defaults.json gives scale {'classic': [1]} and scale~ {'classic': [0]}, so the two objects do load with different defaults, as cyclone says. The scale refpage's classic attribute text: classic 1 'is compatible with legacy IRCAM linedrive scaling. This mode is not recommended for new work'; classic 2 keeps the curve shape when the output range is inverted.
- **Advice for Max:** When a Max scale box uses its fifth argument, write '@classic 0' on it. Otherwise it uses the legacy formula, in which an exponent of 2 does not mean a square law and typical values are near 1.06, and it will not match scale~ given the same numbers. With no exponent the mode makes no difference.
- **Notes:** The 'buggy' claim is cyclone's wording; the Max refpage says only 'not recommended for new work'.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): confirmed in transform-defaults.json: scale loads with classic 1, scale~ with classic 0.
- **Source:** <Documentation/10.cyclone/scale-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/scale~-help.pd> · <Documentation/10.cyclone/linedrive-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--scale-classic-exponential-mode-is-the-default-in-max-and-cyclone-changed-it`

Whether jit.gl.model itself honours matrixoutput was not confirmed.

## deployment

### Export a mapping to a small standalone player that syncs with others (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Keeping several machines in step
- **There:** `MiniMad`, `miniMAD`, `Export Video Mapping to MiniMad`, `MiniMad Video`, `MiniMad Light`, `MiniMad Controller`, `Sync Group`
- **What it does:** A project's surfaces and media, or its recorded light sequences, are exported to a Raspberry Pi based box that plays them with no computer. Several boxes on one network cable keep their playback together with no configuration, and can be controlled from MadMapper or by OSC.
- **Closest in Max:** `RNBO`, `standalone`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'raspberry': the userguide 'rnbo' topic says RNBO patchers export to hardware targets like Raspberry Pi; that is audio code, not video mapping or DMX playback. The registry has a 'standalone' object and the userguide a 'standalones_and_collectives' topic, for desktop applications.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs/minimad> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/1.-introduction/extensions-and-companions> · <https://download.madmapper.com/minimad/MiniMad_3_User_Guide.pdf> · <https://madmapper.com/files/02-MiniMad%20Controller.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--export-a-mapping-to-a-small-standalone-player-that-syncs-with-others`

### Run-only, password-locked show files with graded permissions (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `Save As Read Only`, `Lock Document`, `Unlock Document`
- **What it does:** A document can be locked with a password so recipients can run it but not edit the patch. The author decides whether they may still edit the control panels, import media, or save.
- **Closest in Max:** `standalone application`, `collective`
- **Checked:** Read userguide standalones_and_collectives.json headings: Max builds standalone applications and collectives. A standalone hides the patch but is a build, not a lock on an editable file with selectable permissions.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=214> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=215>
- id: `isadora--run-only-password-locked-show-files-with-graded-permissions`

### Render the whole show to files: video per output, laser, DMX and audio (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Offline rendering of a whole show
- **There:** `Export to File`, `Timeline Export`, `Export Video Outputs`, `ILDA Export`, `DMX Export`, `Export Audio Output`, `Export Loop section`
- **What it does:** One export renders a timeline to a movie for each video output, an ILDA file for each laser, a DMX sequence and an audio mix, without having to run in real time.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.record`, `jit.vcr`, `jit.gl.asyncread`, `jit.world`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). jit.record's refpage: saves a sequence of matrices as a movie, with a 'realtime' attribute. jit.vcr: the same with MSP audio. jit.gl.asyncread: reads back the GL framebuffer. The userguide has a 'Non-real-time Processing' topic with a video section, and jit.world's refpage lists 'fixed_delta'. So one video stream can be rendered offline in Max; several outputs plus laser and DMX in one pass cannot.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs/timeline-export> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/1.-introduction/what's-new/madmapper-v6>
- id: `madmapper--render-the-whole-show-to-files-video-per-output-laser-dmx-and-audio`

### Settings for installations that run with nobody there (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed

No interface was found for outside code to drive a running Max patch other than network messages.

### Each output knows its display; fullscreen is one switch for all of them (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Destination`, `Output Size`, `Full Screen Mode`, `Desktop Mode`, `Desktop Window`, `Fullscreen video mode`
- **What it does:** An output has a Destination menu naming a real display, a size that can be copied from that display, and its own Desktop Window button. One command sends every output fullscreen on its display and another brings them back. The project can be set to open fullscreen. The FAQ lists the system settings that break this: mirrored displays, and a maximised interface window on macOS.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world (fullscreen, fsmenubar, esc_fullscreen, rect, windowposition)`, `jit.window`, `jit.displays`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world's and jit.window's refpages: 'fullscreen', 'fsmenubar', 'rect', 'windowposition' / 'pos', 'size', 'border' and 'floating' are separate attributes; neither has an attribute that names a display. Read jit.displays' refpage: 'count' and 'coords <index>' report each display's rectangle, and on Mac it can mirror and unmirror. Which display a fullscreen window lands on is not stated in the refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Do in the patch what MadMapper does in its menu: at load, ask jit.displays for the projector display's coords, move the window there with `rect`, then set `fullscreen 1`, all from one 'show' toggle that drives every jit.world. Check for mirroring first and tell the operator if it is on.
- **Notes:** That a Max window goes fullscreen on the display it sits on is the common practice, but it was not confirmed in a refpage here.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/keyboard-shortcuts> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq> · <https://madmapper.com/files/02-My%20First%20Video%20Mapping.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--each-output-knows-its-display-fullscreen-is-one-switch-for-all-of-them`

## lighting/DMX/lasers

### Art-Net DMX send and receive (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** DMX, Art-Net and sACN in and out
- **There:** `ArtNet Send`, `ArtNet Receive`, `LanBox Channels`
- **What it does:** Sends a DMX frame to an Art-Net node or broadcasts it, at a limited rate, and receives DMX so a lighting desk can drive the patch. Nodes on the network are listed in a menu.
- **Closest in Max:** `fxwdmxusbpro (Max for the Visual Arts)`, `udpsend`, `serial`
- **Checked:** Registry grep 'dmx', 'artnet', 'sacn': none. Refpage grep 'dmx': only a mention in serial. Package library: fxwdmxusbpro only (library entry read: sends DMX to an Enttec DMX USB Pro over serial). No Art-Net or sACN object is installed. udpsend refpage read: it has a rawbytes message, so Art-Net packets could be built by hand.
- **Notes:** Well-known third-party Art-Net externals exist for Max but none is installed, so none was verified.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): no object whose name contains 'artnet' in Max 9's obj-qlookup.json. Third-party packages that are not installed on this machine were not checked.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=403> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=404> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=405> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=554> · <https://troikatronix.com/isadora/lighting-control/> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000042899-controlling-led-strips-via-artnet>
- id: `isadora--art-net-dmx-send-and-receive`

### Lighting-style soft patch that crossfades with the Scenes (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Lighting fixtures driven from the picture or from geometry
- **There:** `Matrix Value Send`, `Matrix Value Receive`, `Matrix Color Send`, `Channel Map`, `Master`
- **What it does:** Cues send levels to logical channels. One receiver maps each logical channel to any set of output channels through an editable table, applies a grand master, and blends levels from two Scenes according to their fade progress, so a Scene crossfade is also a lighting crossfade. Output is formatted text for Art-Net, serial DMX or MIDI.
- **Closest in Max:** `router`, `matrixctrl`, `pattrstorage`, `coll`
- **Checked:** Registry has router and matrixctrl (names seen; refpages not read this session). pattrstorage refpage read: interpolates between stored presets, which is the nearest thing to a level crossfade. No object combines a patch table, a master and cue-driven mixing.
- **Notes:** Matrix Value Receive was read in full. Matrix Color Send, which samples pixel rows or columns of a video into the same channels for LED strips, was read in part; that sampling step alone is not a gap, since jit.spill ('Unroll a matrix into a list') and jit.iter exist in the registry. The soft patch with cue-driven mixing is the idea worth porting.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=580> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=581> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=582> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=583> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=584> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000085235-routing-values-using-the-matrix-value-send-and-receive-actors>
- id: `isadora--lighting-style-soft-patch-that-crossfades-with-the-scenes`

### PJLink projector control (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controlling projectors, cameras and show gear
- **There:** `Send PJLink`
- **What it does:** Sends PJLink commands to a networked projector, with optional password, and outputs the projector's reply. Used to close the shutter between cues instead of projecting black.
- **Closest in Max:** `sadam.tcpClient (Sadam Library)`, `mxj net.tcp.send`, `node.script`
- **Checked:** Registry and package search 'pjlink': none. PJLink is a text protocol over TCP port 4352 (per the manual). Max's core registry has no TCP client: grep 'tcp' matched nothing, and 'net.' matched only jit.net.send and jit.net.recv, which carry matrices. sadam.tcpClient is installed (library entry read: 'Bidirectional TCP client'). max-mxj ships net.tcp.send and net.tcp.recv help files (seen by name, not read).
- **Notes:** Category is the nearest fit: projector hardware control.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=707> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=708> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000067857-using-the-send-pjlink-actor>
- id: `isadora--pjlink-projector-control`

### Art-Net and sACN in and out, with node discovery and many universes (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** DMX, Art-Net and sACN in and out
- **There:** `Art-Net`, `sACN`, `ArtNet In`, `Unicast`, `Universe Synchronization`, `DMX Monitor`, `DMX Router`, `DMX to OSC`, `DMX Input`
- **What it does:** MadMapper sends and receives DMX over Ethernet as Art-Net or sACN, in broadcast or unicast, finds Art-Net nodes by polling, and handles thousands of universes. Incoming DMX can control any parameter. Small modules route universes to USB interfaces, forward a universe as OSC, and show live channel values.
- **Closest in Max:** `udpsend`, `udpreceive`, `fxwdmxusbpro (Max for the Visual Arts)`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'art-net', 'artnet', 'sacn', 'e131': no hits in Max or in the package library. udpsend and udpreceive (refpages: send and receive messages over UDP) are the only transport; the packet format would have to be built by hand.

### DMX fixtures as surfaces that sample the picture under them (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Lighting fixtures driven from the picture or from geometry
- **There:** `MadLight`, `DMX Fixture`, `DMX Line`, `DMX Circle`, `DMX Bézier`, `DMX samplers`, `DMX Filtering`, `Response Curve`
- **What it does:** A light fixture is placed on the output like a video surface and takes its colour from the pixels beneath it, so any video composition drives LEDs and lights directly. Sampling can be one pixel, a box average, or a stretched average for strips, and each fixture has a response curve.
- **Closest in Max:** `fxwdmxusbpro (Max for the Visual Arts)`, `jit.matrix`, `serial`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'dmx': no Max object; the word appears once, in the serial refpage. One package hit: fxwdmxusbpro (an abstraction that sends a list of channel values to an Enttec DMX USB Pro over serial). 'fixture', 'pixel map': nothing. A Max user would read pixels from a jit.matrix and build the channel list by hand.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/5.-dmx-and-led-mapping/dmx-fundamentals> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/5.-dmx-and-led-mapping/dmx-fixtures> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/1.-introduction/what's-new/madmapper-v6>
- id: `madmapper--dmx-fixtures-as-surfaces-that-sample-the-picture-under-them`

### Fixture definitions: channel types, 16-bit pairs, computed channels, wiring order (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Lighting fixtures driven from the picture or from geometry
- **There:** `Fixture Editor`, `DMX Fixture Definitions`, `Pixel Type`, `Pixel Size`, `Assignation`, `Slider`, `Expression`, `16 Bits`, `LED Strip Mode`, `Matrix Mode`, `Skip channels 511–512`
- **What it does:** A fixture type is described once: its pixel grid, the meaning of each channel (colour components, generic sliders, or a value computed by a GLSL expression from colour, position, time and other sliders), 8 or 16 bits, and which of 16 wiring orders its pixels follow. Definitions are files that can be shared. Moving heads use colour channels fed by video and slider channels for pan, tilt and gobo.

### Laser output to ILDA DACs, including over audio channels (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Laser output
- **There:** `MadLaser`, `Laser Output`, `Etherdream`, `ShowNET`, `Helios USB`, `LaserCube`, `IDN`, `FB4`, `Moncha`, `Enable Audio Laser (AVB / Dante)`, `PPS`, `Desired FPS`
- **What it does:** Drives laser projectors directly: coloured paths are turned into frames of points and sent to network or USB DACs, or as five audio channels (X, Y, R, G, B) to an AVB or Dante audio interface. Any number of lasers can be used.
- **Closest in Max:** `vector_3ch_out (Vsynth)`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'ilda', 'etherdream', 'galvo': no hits anywhere. 'laser': one package hit, Vsynth's vector_3ch_out (package entry: sends vector X, Y and brightness signals to audio outputs). That is the same idea as MadMapper's audio laser output, without colour channels or any DAC protocol.
- **Notes:** The audio route is the one a Max patch could plausibly follow, since X/Y/R/G/B as signals is ordinary MSP. Whether vector_3ch_out is meant for lasers or for oscilloscopes was not checked.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/7.-laser-madlaser> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/13.-setup-and-installation/additional-hardware> · <https://madmapper.com/files/MadLaser%20AVB%20Support-1.pdf> · <https://madmapper.com/extensions/madlaser>

### Laser path planning: blanking, corner dwell, speed, draw order, frame rate (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Laser output
- **There:** `Laser Render`, `Max Speed`, `Optimize Angles`, `Angle Min`, `Angle Delay`, `End Repeat`, `In Fade`, `Out Fade`, `Point Intensity`, `Min Points`, `Blank Delay`, `Color Delay`, `Min Voltage`, `Scan Area`, `ILDA Mode`, `Preserve FPS`, `Preserve Image Quality`
- **What it does:** Before points go to the laser, MadMapper orders the paths to shorten beam travel, shares scan time between paths by length, adds points at corners so they stay sharp, blanks the beam between paths, shifts colours in time to match the mirrors, and either holds the frame rate or holds the detail when there is too much to draw.
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). No laser objects in Max or the package library, so none of this exists.
- **Notes:** This is the part that would be hardest to rebuild; it encodes what real scanners can physically do.
- **Source:** <https://madmapper.com/files/MadLaser%20Guide.pdf> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences/madlaser-preferences> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/LaserMaterialsDoc.md>

### Split one laser composition across several lasers (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Laser output
- **There:** `Laser Dispatch`, `Dispatch Count`, `Publish Internal Loopback`, `Laser Buffer`
- **What it does:** A laser composition built on an output with no hardware is looped back as media and shared out between several real lasers, so a picture too complex for one scanner stays bright and steady. Shapes are tracked from frame to frame so each stays on the same laser. The looped composition can also be mesh warped as a whole.

### Trace video into laser paths in real time (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Laser output
- **There:** `Real-Time Video Vectorization`, `Find Paths`, `Find Contours`, `Threshold`, `Thickness`, `Denoizing`, `Canny Size`, `Path Filtering`, `Path Limits`, `Output Polylines`, `Processed Image`
- **What it does:** A movie, image or camera feed is traced every frame on the GPU, either by thinning bright lines to their skeleton or by edge detection. Paths can be filtered by length and capped in number, and the tracing stages can be previewed.
- **Closest in Max:** `cv.jit.findcontours`, `cv.jit.canny`, `jit.sobel`

'vectoriz': nothing relevant. 'contour': cv.jit.findcontours (package entry: traces contours from a binary image as ordered point sequences) and cv.jit.canny (Canny edge detection to 1-pixel binary edges). These run on matrices on the CPU. The line-skeleton mode and the length filtering were not found.
- **Notes:** jit.sobel's refpage: Sobel and Prewitt gradient edge detection on matrices.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/7.-laser-madlaser/real-time-video-vectorization> · <https://madmapper.com/files/MadLaser%20Guide.pdf>

## networking/sync

### IzzyCast: internet video, audio and data sessions between patches (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Internet streaming and remote sessions
- **There:** `IzzyCast Create Session`, `IzzyCast Join Session`, `IzzyCast Broadcaster`, `IzzyCast Receiver`, `IzzyCast Devices`
- **What it does:** A host creates a session and others join by a session number. Each participant can send video, audio and control data to the others, and each incoming stream arrives as an ordinary video stream in the patch. Built on the Zoom Video SDK, so it needs no network set-up; paid by prepaid participant-minutes.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.net.send`, `jit.net.recv`, `node.script (Node for Max package)`, `AOO for Max (package, audio)`
- **Checked:** jit.net.send refpage read: matrices to a jit.net.recv at a known host and port, no relay or NAT traversal. Installed 'AOO for Max' package is audio over network (seen in the package list; its objects were not read). Nothing found carries video between remote patches through a hosted service.
- **Notes:** A paid hosted service, not a pure software feature. The IzzyCast feature pages were fetched and not read.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=524> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=526> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=533> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=535> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=540> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000097342-izzycast-faq> · <https://troikatronix.com/izzycast/>
- id: `isadora--izzycast-internet-video-audio-and-data-sessions-between-patches`

### Switch projectors on and off and close their shutters over the network (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controlling projectors, cameras and show gear
- **There:** `MadProjectorControl`, `PJLink`, `Power`, `Mute`
- **What it does:** A module per projector speaks PJLink: power and shutter buttons that follow the projector's real state, plus model and lamp information. Because the buttons are ordinary parameters, cues and schedulers can run a venue's projectors unattended.
- **Closest in Max:** `sadam.tcpClient (Sadam Library)`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'pjlink': no hits anywhere. 'tcp': Max's own hits are jit.net.send and jit.net.recv, which are for matrices; the package library has sadam.tcpClient, sadam.tcpSender, sadam.tcpReceiver and sadam.tcpServer (Sadam Library, installed). So a TCP connection is available from a package and the PJLink commands would be written by hand.
- **Notes:** The manual describes PJLink only as a network protocol; that it runs over TCP is not stated there, so sadam.tcpClient as the carrier is an assumption to confirm.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/12.-advanced/madprojectorcontrol> · <https://madmapper.com/files/MadMapper%20-%20MadProjectorControl%20Guide.pdf> · <https://madmapper.com/extensions/madprojectorcontrol>
- id: `madmapper--switch-projectors-on-and-off-and-close-their-shutters-over-the-network`

### Frame-locked sync across machines (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Keeping several machines in step
- **There:** `Sync In CHOP`, `Sync Out CHOP`, `Touch In/Out Synced Ports`, `TDSynchro`
- **What it does:** A server and its clients wait for each other every frame so all machines render the same frame, with timeouts and banning of slow clients. A toolkit splits a large canvas across machines and keeps the pieces frame-matched.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.net.send`, `udpsend`
- **Checked:** Searched Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, plus the mira package registry: 1,326 names with digests) and refpage digests and descriptions (1,389 pages: max-ref, msp-ref, jit-ref, m4l-ref and the bundled packages' docs) for sync: sync~, mc.sync~, plugsync~ (audio tempo sync) and jit.gl.asyncread. jit.net.send (refpage read) sends matrices over TCP. Nothing makes several Max instances step frames together.
- **Notes:** Sync In and Out need a Pro licence, per the docs.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Sync_CHOPs_Common> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Syncing_Multiple_Computers> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Sync> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Touch_In/Out_Synced_Ports> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/TDSynchro>
- id: `touchdesigner--frame-locked-sync-across-machines`

- **Notes:** Name-based discovery in Max needs the zero package; network audio needs AOO or Link.
