### Global tempo has a source menu: audio beat detection, Ableton Link, MIDI clock, manual and tap (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tempo and clocks
- **There:** `Global BPM`, `BPM Source`, `Audio Beat Detection`, `Ableton Link`, `MIDI Clock`, `TAP`, `Resync`
- **What it does:** One tempo drives everything tempo-aware. Its source is picked from a menu: detected from the audio input, Ableton Link, MIDI clock, or typed and tapped by hand. There is a Resync button. The FAQ notes that animations stop when the tempo is zero or its source is disconnected.
- **Closest in Max:** `transport (tempo, clocksource, getclocksources)`, `link.session / link.beat / link.phasor~ (Link package)`, `pnp.bpm~ (PnP.Maxtools)`, `metro (quantize)`
- **Checked:** Read transport's refpage: 'tempo', and 'clocksource' with a 'getclocksources' message that lists the active sources at run time; the page does not name them. The installed Link package has link.session (start, stop and tempo of the shared session), link.beat and link.phasor~ refpages, and an extensions folder holding link.clocksource. The package library lists pnp.bpm~ as a tempo estimator. No tap-tempo object was found.
- **Advice for Max:** Keep one `transport` as the only tempo in the patch and feed it from a single selector (a `umenu` of sources). Read `getclocksources` to see what this machine offers. Show the tempo and warn when it is 0, because everything written in note values stops with it.
- **Notes:** Settles part of session 1's open question: transport's sources are reported by getclocksources, and Link ships a clock source extension. The source names were not read, since that needs Max running.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/9.-master-settings/audio-input-and-beat-detection> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control/osc-commands-and-channels-list> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--global-tempo-has-a-source-menu-audio-beat-detection-ableton-link-midi-clock-manual-and-tap`

### OSC listener with wildcards, a backlog counter and a choice about dropping (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `OSC Address Listener`, `OSC Listener`, `OSC Multi Listener`, `OSC Transmit`, `OSC Multi Transmit`
- **What it does:** OSC Address Listener matches an address pattern with * wildcards and outputs the values, the matched address and a JSON copy of the message. A 'one per cycle' switch chooses between always showing the newest message, dropping any that arrive faster than the patch runs, and queueing every message, with a 'pending msgs' output showing the backlog.
- **Closest in Max:** `udpreceive`, `route`, `o.route (odot)`, `osc.packet`, `param.osc`, `speedlim`, `qlim`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `udpreceive` has one data outlet, a `maxqueuesize` message, `defer` and `outputformat`. `osc.packet` stores an OSC packet and converts formats. `param.osc` maps OSC onto a patch's parameters. The package library lists `o.route` as the current address router and marks CNMAT's `osc-route` deprecated. `qlim` and `speedlim` limit message rate.
- **Advice for Max:** Max makes no drop-or-queue decision visible. On any high-rate OSC stream, decide it yourself: `speedlim` or `qlim` after `udpreceive` for 'newest wins', and know that `maxqueuesize` exists. Use `o.route` for address patterns.
- **Notes:** What `udpreceive` does when its queue is full was not read.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=633 (Actors Reference: OSC Address Listener)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=635 (Actors Reference: OSC Listener)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=637 (Actors Reference: OSC Multi Transmit)>
- id: `isadora--osc-listener-with-wildcards-a-backlog-counter-and-a-choice-about-dropping`

### Two ways in for every parameter: a direct address for the raw value, a mapped Control for range, filter and curve (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Copy OSC Address`, `Controls`, `Source Range`, `Target Range`, `Filter`, `OSC Query`
- **What it does:** Every parameter has a fixed OSC address built from the names of things in the project, such as /surfaces/Quad-1/opacity. A value sent there is applied as it is. A Control mapped to the same parameter adds a source range, a target range, a filter and a curve. Renaming a surface changes its address.
- **Closest in Max:** `param.osc`, `Parameter OSC addresses (userguide topic 'OSC')`, `Mappings (userguide topic 'mapping')`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topic OSC: with OSC enabled, parameter-enabled objects get addresses of the form /<patcher name>/param/<parameter name>, with /raw and /normalized under each; OSCQuery can be switched on in the preferences. Read param.osc's refpage: 'info' and 'allparams' output every parameter's details and values as OSC. Wildcards in addresses, which MadMapper accepts, are not mentioned in either.
- **Advice for Max:** Treat patcher names and parameter names as a public interface: choose them before giving addresses to anyone, because a rename breaks every remote. Send 0 to 1 to /normalized from generic controllers and real units to /raw from show control. In a hand-built patch, give a parameter the same two entries: a plain set, and a scaled and smoothed one.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control/osc-commands-and-channels-list> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control>
- id: `madmapper--two-ways-in-for-every-parameter-a-direct-address-for-the-raw-value-a-mapped-control-for-range-filter-and-curve`

### OSC input that names itself (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `OSC In CHOP`, `OSC In DAT`
- **What it does:** Each incoming OSC address becomes a named channel with no routing step. A scope pattern keeps or drops addresses and a setting strips leading address segments. A second operator logs every message as a table row, with type tags and bundle timestamps, and can run a callback per message.
- **Closest in Max:** `udpreceive`, `route`, `o.route (odot package)`, `osc.codebox`, `param.osc`
- **Checked:** udpreceive refpage: attributes defer, outputformat (message, osc_packet, fullpacket, rawbytes) and quiet; it has no address filter, so routing is a second object. route refpage: matches the first element of a message, one outlet per argument. Userguide topic 'OpenSoundControl (OSC)': in Max 9 every parameter-mode object gets an address of the form /<patcher>/param/<name>/<attribute> through a built-in UDP server or param.osc, osc.codebox shows a packet as a dictionary, and OSCQuery can describe the patch. Package library: o.route (odot) is the current address router and OSC-route is marked deprecated.
- **Advice for Max:** For control coming in from an app, use parameter-mode UI objects with Max's built-in OSC (or param.osc) so addresses come from parameter names and no route chain is needed. When routing by hand, put an osc.codebox beside udpreceive as the message monitor.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/OSC_In_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/OSC_In_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/OSC> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/IOS_and_OSC> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/TouchOSC>
- id: `touchdesigner--osc-input-that-names-itself`

### OSC output that sends only what changed (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `OSC Out CHOP`, `OSC Out DAT`
- **What it does:** Channels are sent under their own names. One toggle chooses between sending every channel on every frame and sending only the channels whose value changed. The script version sends a list of messages as one bundle, and the number format (int, float, double) is a menu.
- **Closest in Max:** `udpsend`, `param.osc`, `osc.packet`, `change`
- **Checked:** udpsend refpage: messages host, port, maxpacketsize, maxqueuesize, FullPacket, osc_packet, rawbytes; it sends whatever message arrives. param.osc refpage: attribute auto reports parameter changes, outputmode chooses what is sent. change refpage: passes a number only when it differs from the stored one. Userguide OSC topic: a FullPacket message is transient and must not be stored in a message box or zl.reg.
- **Advice for Max:** Put change before udpsend (or use param.osc @auto 1) so a stream of repeated values does not flood the network. Never park a FullPacket in a message box or zl.reg.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/OSC_Out_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/OSC_Out_DAT>
- id: `touchdesigner--osc-output-that-sends-only-what-changed`

### OSC routing by address level (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `osc.route`, `osc.send`, `osc.receive`, `osc.format`, `osc.parse`
- **What it does:** osc.send and osc.receive take ordinary messages that start with a slash address and need only a port (and optionally a host). osc.route matches one level of the address and passes the rest, so '/hz' also matches '/hz/1'.
- **Closest in Max:** `udpsend`, `udpreceive`, `route`, `o.route (odot)`, `osc.codebox`, `osc.packet`, `param.osc`
- **Checked:** Refpages. udpsend / udpreceive carry OSC. route 'tries to match a message's first argument' to its own arguments, so it matches a whole address symbol, not a level of it. Max 9 has osc.codebox, osc.packet and param.osc. The repo's preferred-objects table names CNMAT's o.route.
- **Advice for Max:** Sending and receiving are the same in Max. For routing by level, plain route only works when the whole address is listed; use o.route (odot is installed), as the repo already advises.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/osc.route-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/osc.send-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/osc.receive-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/osc.format-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/18-MIDI-CV-OSC-Net/3.OSC/2.ELSE.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--osc-routing-by-address-level`

### OSC built and parsed as explicit bytes (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** OSC and MIDI handling
- **There:** `oscformat`, `oscparse`, `netsend -u -b`, `netreceive -u -b`
- **What it does:** [oscformat] turns a list into OSC bytes and [oscparse] turns bytes back into a list; the network objects only move bytes. Type tags are set with a format string.
- **Closest in Max:** `udpsend`, `udpreceive`, `osc.packet`, `osc.codebox`, `param.osc`, `o.route`

- **Advice for Max:** Max is simpler here: udpsend and udpreceive speak OSC themselves.

### Chase external timecode: MTC, LTC, Art-Net timecode (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Timecode as a type, and chasing it
- **There:** `External Sync`, `MIDI MTC`, `Audio LTC`, `ArtNet TimeCode`, `ltc_time`, `Per-Timeline External Sync`
- **What it does:** A timeline can follow timecode from outside: MIDI timecode, LTC arriving as audio, or Art-Net timecode. In expert mode different timelines can follow different sources.
- **Closest in Max:** `transport`, `rtin`, `midiin`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'ltc', 'mtc', 'midi time code': no hits. 'smpte': only in the seq and jit.colorspace refpages. 'timecode': only dsp and adstatus text. rtin's refpage: receives MIDI real time messages; midiin: raw MIDI bytes, from which MTC could be decoded by hand. transport has a 'clocksource' attribute whose possible sources were not looked up. No LTC decoder found.
- **Notes:** Marked unsure because transport's clock sources and third-party LTC externals were not checked.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/1.-introduction/what's-new/release-notes-v6> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/9.-master-settings/audio-input-and-beat-detection> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences/expert-mode> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control>
- id: `madmapper--chase-external-timecode-mtc-ltc-art-net-timecode`

## projection mapping

### Automatic edge blending from overlapping displays, with a wizard (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Automatic edge blending between projectors
- **There:** `Blend Maker`, `Blend Adjustment`, `Edge Blend Mask`, `Global Edge Blend Mask`, `Gamma Curve`
- **What it does:** Drag two displays of one Stage so they overlap and the overlap becomes a soft blend. Curve, gamma and knee are adjusted per edge. A wizard builds grids from 1x1 to 8x1 projectors from a resolution and an overlap. Mask actors do the same from inside a patch.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.meshwarp`, `jit.gl.slab`
- **Checked:** Grepped all refpages and bundled package docs for 'edgeblend' and 'keystone': no files. Userguide grep 'edge blend': none. Package library search 'blend' and 'edge': audio and geometry hits only. jit.gl.meshwarp (refpage read) has apply_mask and mask_mode attributes but no blend-between-projectors function.
- **Notes:** A shader in jit.gl.slab could do it; nothing shipped or installed does.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=235> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=236> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=237> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=238> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=477> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=509>
- id: `isadora--automatic-edge-blending-from-overlapping-displays-with-a-wizard`

### 3D calibration: line up a 3D model with a real object from matched points (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Calibrating a projector to a real object
- **There:** `3D Calibration`, `3D Surface`, `Calibrate`, `Fix Position`, `Reset Calibration`
- **What it does:** Import an OBJ of the real object, pick known points on the model, and place each on the matching point of the object as the projector sees it. MadMapper solves the projector's view, shows the RMS error as points are added, and locks the result.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.model`, `jit.gl.camera`, `cv.jit.calibration`, `cv.jit.unproject`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'calibrat': nothing in Max itself. Package hits: cv.jit.calibration (camera intrinsics and lens distortion from chessboard views), cv.jit.findchessboardcorners, cv.jit.unproject (2D image points to 3D camera coordinates). jit.gl.model's refpage: reads and draws OBJ and other formats. None solves a projector pose from point pairs on a model.
- **Notes:** The cv.jit objects calibrate a camera, not a projector. Whether they could be combined to do this was not tested.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/advanced-3d-and-scanning> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting>
- id: `madmapper--3d-calibration-line-up-a-3d-model-with-a-real-object-from-matched-points`

### Automatic soft-edge blend between overlapping projectors (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Automatic edge blending between projectors
- **There:** `Soft-Edge Blending`, `Soft Edge`, `Auto Setup`, `Width`, `Power`
- **What it does:** Select two or more overlapping quads and press one button; MadMapper works out the shared area and fades each projector's edge so the seam disappears. Width and a gamma value are then tuned per edge. Angled overlaps are handled with a feathered mask inside each surface.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.pix`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'soft edge', 'edge blend' and 'feather': no hits anywhere. 'keystone': no hits. jit.gl.cornerpin's refpage describes repositioning four image corners only. A blend ramp could be written by hand as a shader in jit.gl.slab or jit.gl.pix, but nothing computes the overlap.
- **Notes:** The automatic overlap calculation is the part with no Max equivalent; the ramp itself is a simple shader.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs/soft-edge-blending> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-inspector>
- id: `madmapper--automatic-soft-edge-blend-between-overlapping-projectors`

### Structured-light scan that gives the projector's own view of the scene (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Calibrating a projector to a real object
- **There:** `Space Scanner`, `Spatial Scanner`, `Capture`, `Capture Timing`
- **What it does:** The projector shows a series of stripe patterns while a camera photographs them. The result is an image of the scene as seen from the projector, pixel for pixel, set as the output's background so surfaces can be drawn on it away from the site.

- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'structured light': no hits anywhere. jit.grab's refpage: digitizes video from any digitizer. Capture exists; the pattern projection and decoding do not.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/12.-advanced/special-tools-and-auto-calibration> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/tools-space-scanner> · <https://madmapper.com/files/13-Space%20Scanner%20Guide-1.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--structured-light-scan-that-gives-the-projector-s-own-view-of-the-scene`

### Video wall layout built from panel count, resolution and bezel size (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `Video Wall Template`, `Video Wall Setup`, `Setup bezel`, `Number of screens`, `Destination`
- **What it does:** A template asks how many screens across and down, their resolution, the bezel width and their rotation, and creates the grid of surfaces with the bezel gaps allowed for. An output's destination can also split one canvas across several displays.

No bezel or video-wall helper found.
- **Notes:** Simple arithmetic to build by hand; listed because it is a one-step setup in MadMapper.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/video-wall-setup> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs>

### IzzyMap: slice mapping inside every output, with every point drivable live (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `IzzyMap`, `Projector`, `Triangle`, `Rectangle`, `Bézier Grid`, `Composite`, `sub-slice`, `Solo Master`
- **What it does:** Each output actor contains a mapping editor. Regions of the source are cut out as triangles, quads, curved grids or compound masks built by adding and subtracting shapes, and placed on the surface. Slice position, opacity and individual point coordinates can be published as inputs and moved by sensors while the show runs.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.meshwarp`, `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `vs_output (Vsynth package)`, `av.cornerpin (av-toolbox package)`
- **Checked:** Read refpages: jit.gl.meshwarp ('Video mapping tool', Jitter Tools package) deforms a texture on a 2D mesh with a mask and has meshdim, curvature, nurbs_order, mask_mode, save, read; jit.gl.cornerpin maps a texture by its four corners. Package search 'projection mapping': vs_output only. So one warped surface per object exists; a multi-slice editor with input and output views and compound masks does not.
- **Notes:** The composite-mapping KB article was fetched and not read.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=258> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=277> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=278> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=279> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=280> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=282> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=283> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=284> · <https://troikatronix.com/isadora/projection-mapping/> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000014932-using-izzymap-s-composite-mapping-mode>
- id: `isadora--izzymap-slice-mapping-inside-every-output-with-every-point-drivable-live`

### Alignment aids drawn on the real output (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `Show Test Pattern`, `Video Cursor`, `Laser Cursor`, `Show Info`, `Highlight Selection`, `Highlight Background`, `Object Isolation`, `Grid Generator`
- **What it does:** Each output can show a test pattern, the mouse position as a cross or circle on the projected image (or as a laser beam), the names and outlines of selected surfaces, or only the surfaces being edited. These make it possible to work while looking at the real object instead of the screen.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.text`, `jit.gl.sketch`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). jit.gl.cornerpin's refpage has 'drawcorners', 'corner_color', 'hover' attributes, so its handles are visible in the output window. No ready test pattern or output cursor was found; both can be drawn with jit.gl.sketch or jit.gl.text.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/keyboard-shortcuts> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/9.-master-settings> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/warping-and-geometry>
- id: `madmapper--alignment-aids-drawn-on-the-real-output`

### Bézier masks with feathering that follow the surface or the source (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `Mask`, `Add Mask`, `Feathering`, `Invert Mask`, `Bézier Masking`, `Centered`, `Inside`, `Outside`
- **What it does:** Masks are drawn on the output freehand or point by point and smoothed into Bézier curves. A surface can carry several, each inverted or not, each with a feathered edge. A mask can stay fixed, follow the surface's warp, or follow the input picture. A separate Mask surface cuts every layer beneath it.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.mgraphics`, `jit.gl.sketch`, `av.pix.mask (av-toolbox)`, `jit.keyscreen`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'mask': jit.keyscreen (refpage: a three-source chromakey that builds a mask from a key image) and av.pix.mask (package entry: makes a black/white mask from an image). 'feather': no hits. jit.mgraphics has curve_to, fill and path methods in its refpage, so a mask texture can be drawn from code. Nothing provides drawing a mask on the output with the mouse.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-groups-and-masks> · <https://madmapper.com/files/04-Masking.pdf> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/warping-and-geometry>
- id: `madmapper--b-zier-masks-with-feathering-that-follow-the-surface-or-the-source`

### Layer list of surfaces, each with an input crop and an output shape edited by dragging (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `Video Surfaces`, `Surfaces List`, `Surface Inspector`, `Input View`, `Stage Preview`, `Input Rectangle`, `Video Quad`, `Triangle`, `Circle`, `Mask`
- **What it does:** The whole mapping is a stack of surfaces. For each one you drag a rectangle over the source to choose what it shows, and separately drag its corners on the output to place it. List order is drawing order; groups scale their children's opacity.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.videoplane`, `jit.gl.handle`, `jit.world`, `av.cornerpin (av-toolbox)`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'cornerpin' finds jit.gl.cornerpin and jit.world (which the refpage says contains a jit.gl.cornerpin, enabled with 'enable_cornerpin'). jit.gl.videoplane's refpage: shows video in OpenGL. jit.gl.handle's refpage: turns mouse drags into rotate and position messages. One jit.gl.cornerpin per surface is the Max way; there is no surface list, no input-crop view and no grouping.
- **Notes:** This is MadMapper's whole interface, so it is a design difference more than a missing object. Listed because it is what the other mapping candidates sit on.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/interface-overview> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-types> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/assigning-content-to-surfaces> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-groups-and-masks>
- id: `madmapper--layer-list-of-surfaces-each-with-an-input-crop-and-an-output-shape-edited-by-dragging`

### Line surfaces: paths drawn on the output with media running along them (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `Line`, `Video Line`, `FreeDraw`, `madLine`, `Enable Bezier`, `Match Output Geometry`, `Match Line per segment`, `Match Line chained`, `Match Line Vertical Split`
- **What it does:** Paths are drawn on the output freehand or point by point, with thickness, joins and Bézier curves, to trace edges of architecture or LED strips. A media item is mapped along the path, either per segment, chained over all paths, or by the paths' position on the output, so a pattern can run along an outline.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.path`, `jit.path`, `jit.gl.sketch`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). jit.gl.path's refpage: generates and renders a 3D path as a line, ribbon, tube or extruded contour, storing position, colour, scale and orientation per point; attributes include 'joinstyle' and 'interpmode', and messages 'append', 'edit', 'edithandle'. That covers rendering a path. Drawing it on the output with the mouse and mapping a texture along it were not found.
- **Notes:** The four texturing mode names come from the MadLaser guide's Laser Lines section; the release notes name 'Match Output Geometry' for video lines too.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/freedraw-and-madline> · <https://madmapper.com/files/MadLaser%20Guide.pdf> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting/madmapper-release-notes-history>
- id: `madmapper--line-surfaces-paths-drawn-on-the-output-with-media-running-along-them`

### Mesh warping with free points, Bézier handles and a linked input mesh (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `Mesh Warping`, `Bezier Mesh Warping`, `Add Warping Point`, `Link Input and Output Mesh`, `Show Input Mesh`, `Precision`, `Unlink Tangents`
- **What it does:** A quad gets an internal grid of warp points that are added anywhere, dragged on the output, and given Bézier tangent handles for curved surfaces. By default each point moved on the output is mirrored on the input side so the picture is not stretched; the link can be turned off to edit the two meshes separately.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.nurbs`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.gl.meshwarp`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'warp': no object; three jitter userguide topics mention it. 'bezier': only jit.mgraphics text. jit.gl.cornerpin (refpage: maps a texture by repositioning the image corners, with mouse handles in the window) covers four corners. jit.gl.nurbs (refpage: renders a NURBS surface from a control matrix) and jit.gl.mesh (geometry from matrices) can hold a warped surface, but neither gives draggable warp points on the output.
- **Notes:** A Max user would have to build the point editor. jit.gl.nurbs's refpage lists a 'ctlshow' attribute; whether its control points can be dragged with the mouse was not checked.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max has a warp grid, jit.gl.meshwarp, in the bundled Jitter Tools package (refpage and help file confirmed on disk). The first pass did not search bundled packages.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/warping-and-geometry> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-inspector> · <https://madmapper.com/files/05-Mesh%20Warping.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--mesh-warping-with-free-points-b-zier-handles-and-a-linked-input-mesh`

### Perspective-correct rendering for a viewing position (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `Quad Reprojection`, `Palette:quadReproject`, `Palette:sweetSpot`

### Projector calibration from a 3D model (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Calibrating a projector to a real object
- **There:** `Palette:camSchnappr`
- **What it does:** Matches a virtual camera to a real projector: you click known points of a 3D model where they fall on the physical object, and OpenCV camera calibration solves the projector's position and lens. Supports several projectors with blending.
- **Closest in Max:** `cv.jit.calibration`, `cv.jit.findchessboardcorners`, `jit.gl.camera`
- **Checked:** Registry and refpage search for 'calibrat' returned nothing. packages/query_packages.py search (installed third-party packages) 'calibrat' found cv.jit.calibration and cv.jit.findchessboardcorners (chessboard camera calibration). jit.gl.camera's refpage lists proj_matrix and view_matrix attributes, so a solved matrix could be applied. No object was found that solves a projector pose from model-to-screen point pairs.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:camSchnappr> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Projection_Mapping>
- id: `touchdesigner--projector-calibration-from-a-3d-model`

### Shape-based mapping and masking editor (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Mapping editor: slices, masks and warps on the output
- **There:** `Palette:kantanMapper`, `Kantan UV Helper`
- **What it does:** An editor drawn over the live projector output. You draw quads and freeform bezier outlines on the real object, then give each shape a texture or use it as a mask.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.meshwarp`, `jit.gl.cornerpin`
- **Checked:** Searched Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, plus the mira package registry: 1,326 names with digests) and refpage digests and descriptions (1,389 pages: max-ref, msp-ref, jit-ref, m4l-ref and the bundled packages' docs) for cornerpin, meshwarp, keystone, warp: found jit.gl.cornerpin (four-corner mapping with mouse handles) and jit.gl.meshwarp (refpage: 'Video mapping tool', a 2D mesh deformer with a mask). Neither refpage describes drawing arbitrary bezier shapes and assigning a texture per shape. packages/query_packages.py search (installed third-party packages) 'projection mapping' returned only Vsynth vs_output.
- **Notes:** A later pass should open jit.gl.meshwarp's help file to see how far its mask mode goes.

### Multi-projector examples: screen grid with soft-edge overlap, and curved-wall warp (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `flat_projection`, `nfp_flat_wall`, `nfp_curved_wall`, `gemframebuffer`, `glsl_program`
- **What it does:** Two example abstractions render the scene to one large framebuffer and redraw it per projector. One takes the grid of screens, how they are cabled, and the overlap in pixels, and its shader fades the overlapping edges. The other warps each screen's part with four or nine control points and a shaded edge on each side, and saves the settings to a file.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.meshwarp`, `jit.world`, `jit.gl.slab`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.cornerpin (four corners), jit.gl.meshwarp (Jitter Tools: mesh warp with mask_mode, mesh_matrix, save and read) and jit.world (enable_cornerpin). Searched all refpages for 'soft edge' and 'edge blend': no hits. Jitter's warp tools are better than Gem's; the overlap fade is the missing piece.
- **Notes:** soft_edge.frag was read for its first 60 lines; the nfp shaders were not read. These are example patches, not Gem objects.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/examples/12.multi_screen_projection/01.flat_projection-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/12.multi_screen_projection/flat_projection.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/12.multi_screen_projection/soft_edge.frag> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/12.multi_screen_projection/nfp_curved_wall.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/12.multi_screen_projection/02.nfp-help.pd>

### Corner and warp points can be moved from the keyboard (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Tab`, `Arrow Keys`, `Shift + Arrow Keys`, `Handle`
- **What it does:** Tab steps to the next corner handle. The arrow keys move the selected point by one pixel, and by ten with Shift. The manual says the mouse is too coarse for the last few pixels of an alignment.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.meshwarp (Jitter Tools)`, `key`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.cornerpin's refpage: corners move by mouse in the window or by setting 'upper_left', 'upper_right', 'lower_left', 'lower_right'; it lists no keyboard control. jit.gl.meshwarp's refpage lists keyboard shortcuts for undo and redo only. key's refpage: reports key presses, no inlets.
- **Advice for Max:** Add a small nudge patch to any cornerpin: `key` picks the corner (or Tab cycles), the arrows add or subtract one pixel's worth (1 / window width) to that corner's attribute, Shift makes it ten. It is a handful of objects and saves real time in front of a projector.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/warping-and-geometry> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/interface-overview> · <https://madmapper.com/files/02-My%20First%20Video%20Mapping.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--corner-and-warp-points-can-be-moved-from-the-keyboard`

### Draw order is the list order, so it is never undefined (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Surfaces List`, `Group`, `Mask`
- **What it does:** Surfaces are drawn in the order of the list: the first row is in front, the last is at the back. Dragging a row changes the stacking, and there is no way for two surfaces to have an unknown order.
- **Closest in Max:** `layer (attribute of every jit.gl object)`, `depth_enable`, `jit.gl.layer`, `jit.gl.videoplane`
- **Checked:** Read jit.group-gl.maxref.xml (the shared attributes of the jit.gl objects): 'layer' defaults to 0, rendering goes from low to high, and objects in the same layer have no guaranteed order. Read the userguide topic jitter/depth_layer_blend: every object starts in layer 0 so the order is indeterminate, higher layers draw on top, and layering only works when depth testing is off (@depth_enable 0); jit.gl.layer turns depth testing off and blending on by default.
- **Advice for Max:** Give every overlapping plane its own @layer number and never leave two at 0. Use jit.gl.layer for flat video stacks, since it already has depth testing off. Note the direction: in Max the higher number is in front, the opposite of MadMapper's top-of-list.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max's userguide (Depth Testing and Layering) and the GL attribute refpage settle it: depth testing is per object, on by default, and overrides layer, so each layered object needs depth_enable 0. The repo's docs were corrected 2026-10-03.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-groups-and-masks> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--draw-order-is-the-list-order-so-it-is-never-undefined`

### A warp grid with masks, undo and its own save file (Max has one: jit.gl.meshwarp) (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `Mesh Warping`, `Bezier`, `Add Warping Point`, `Link Input and Output Mesh`, `Feathering`
- **What it does:** A surface can be given a grid of warp points. Points are added one at a time or by subdivision, each can have Bézier handles, masks can be drawn on the same surface, and by default the input mesh follows the output mesh so the picture is not stretched.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.meshwarp (Jitter Tools)`, `jit.gl.cornerpin`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.meshwarp's refpage in the bundled Jitter Tools package (it is not in the main obj-qlookup.json, which is why session 1's search for 'warp' found no object): a draggable mesh set by 'meshdim', curvature through 'nurbs_order' / 'curvature', a 'mask_mode' for drawing masks with 'invert_mask' and 'apply_mask', 'save' and 'read' of the whole state as a JSON file, undo and redo, 'layer', and 'output_texture'. It lists no free-placed points, per-point Bézier handles, linked input mesh or feathering.
- **Advice for Max:** Reach for jit.gl.meshwarp before building a warp editor. Save its state with `save` to a JSON file beside the patch and `read` it at load: a warp is calibration for one room, so it belongs in its own file, not in the piece's presets.
- **Notes:** Corrects session 1's candidate 'Mesh warping with free points, Bézier handles and a linked input mesh', whose Max check did not find this object.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/warping-and-geometry> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-inspector> · <https://madmapper.com/files/05-Mesh%20Warping.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--a-warp-grid-with-masks-undo-and-its-own-save-file-max-has-one-jit-gl-meshwarp`

### Blend mode is a named menu on each layer, and a group keeps blending inside itself (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Blend Mode`, `Appearance`, `Shadow Groups`, `Additive`
- **What it does:** Every surface has a blend mode chosen by name next to its opacity. Putting surfaces in a group keeps their blend modes and masks from affecting surfaces outside the group.
- **Closest in Max:** `blend`, `blend_enable`, `blend_mode`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** Read jit.group-gl.maxref.xml: 'blend' takes a name (add, multiply, screen, exclusion, colorblend, alphablend, coloradd, alphaadd), each equal to a 'blend_mode' pair of numbers; 'blend_enable' defaults to 0. The userguide topic jitter/depth_layer_blend says blending is off by default except on jit.gl.layer. jit.gl.node's refpage: children can be captured together to a texture.
- **Advice for Max:** Write `@blend screen`, not `@blend_mode 4 1`, so the box says what it does, and remember `@blend_enable 1` on anything that is not a jit.gl.layer. To stop a group's blending reaching the layers under it, render the group into jit.gl.node @capture 1 and place the result as one layer.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-inspector> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/surface-groups-and-masks> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--blend-mode-is-a-named-menu-on-each-layer-and-a-group-keeps-blending-inside-itself`

### Crop the source by a rectangle first, then shape the output (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Input View`, `Input Rectangle`, `Stage Preview`, `Texture coordinates`
- **What it does:** Which part of the media a surface shows is set by a rectangle drawn over the source. Where that part lands on the projector is set separately. Changing one never disturbs the other.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fx.subtexture (Jitter Tools)`, `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.butter_crop (Butter_tools)`
- **Checked:** Read jit.fx.subtexture's refpage (bundled Jitter Tools package): it references a sub-region of a texture without copying, with 'offset' and 'dim' in pixels. Read jit.gl.cornerpin's refpage: four corner attributes place a texture in the window. Read the Butter_tools README section on jit.butter_crop: a crop rectangle drawn on the picture passing through it; its README says the cropping inside the box has not yet been tried in Max.
- **Advice for Max:** Keep the two steps as two objects in series: a crop (jit.fx.subtexture, or jit.butter_crop to draw it) feeding a jit.gl.cornerpin or jit.gl.meshwarp. Do not try to crop by pulling the output corners.
- **Notes:** Session 1's candidate on the surface list said Max has no input-crop view; jit.fx.subtexture supplies the crop itself, without a view.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces/assigning-content-to-surfaces> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/interface-overview> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--crop-the-source-by-a-rectangle-first-then-shape-the-output`

### The mapping stage is kept apart from the content and locked for the show (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Publish Internal Loopback`, `Video Buffer`, `Lock`, `Lock Surfaces Movement`, `Loopbacks`
- **What it does:** The manual's advice for anything beyond one projector: compose the content on an off-screen buffer, publish it as a loopback, and map that one source onto the projectors. Cues then change the content and never touch the projector mapping. Surfaces and outputs can be locked against accidental edits.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world (output_texture, enable_cornerpin)`, `jit.gl.cornerpin (enable_mouse, drawcorners)`, `jit.gl.meshwarp (show_ui)`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world's refpage: 'output_texture' captures the scene to a texture and 'enable_cornerpin' shows an internal cornerpin. Read jit.gl.cornerpin's refpage: 'enable_mouse' and 'drawcorners' turn the handles off. jit.gl.meshwarp's refpage has 'show_ui'. The repo's own table (CLAUDE.md, Preferred Objects) already recommends a first jit.world captured to a texture and a second stage that maps it.
- **Advice for Max:** Build two stages: everything creative renders into one texture, and a separate mapping stage places that texture. Give the mapping stage one 'calibrate' toggle that sets `enable_mouse` and `drawcorners` (or `show_ui`), off by default, so a stray click during the show cannot move a corner.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs/loopback-and-advanced-output-techniques> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/4.-surfaces> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/interface-overview>
- id: `madmapper--the-mapping-stage-is-kept-apart-from-the-content-and-locked-for-the-show`

### Project a texture onto meshes from a point in space (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Geometry, meshes and instancing
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.TextureProjection_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Meshes.QuadWarpTexture`
- **What it does:** TextureProjection is a shader effect that maps a texture onto any mesh, instancer or point cloud along a chosen plane or in screen space, in object or world coordinates, blended with the material. It can also drive point size or alpha.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.pbr`, `jit.gl.material`, `jit.gl.cornerpin`, `jit.gl.meshwarp`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): no digest matches 'tex.*projec'. jit.gl.pbr has a tex_gen attribute with listed values, which were not read. For warping output to a projector Max is ahead: jit.gl.cornerpin and jit.gl.meshwarp ('video mapping tool', with mesh, masks and save) against one four-corner op in cables.
- **Notes:** Read jit.gl.pbr's tex_gen values before treating this as a gap. On projection mapping as a whole, cables is the weaker tool.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.TextureProjection_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Meshes.QuadWarpTexture>
- id: `cables-gl--project-a-texture-onto-meshes-from-a-point-in-space`

### Distance-field (MSDF) text with per-character transforms (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.FontMSDF_v2`, `Ops.Gl.TextMeshMSDF_v2`, `SDF Font`
- **What it does:** Text is drawn from a multi-channel signed-distance-field font atlas, so it stays sharp at any size, with border and shadow options. Arrays set the position, scale, rotation and colour of every character. The file browser converts a .ttf into the atlas.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.text`, `jit.gl.textmult`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): jit.gl.text has a mode attribute with values 2d, 3d and outline. jit.gl.textmult treats a list of words as several texts controlled by matrices. userguide (C74/docs/userguide/content): 'MSDF' and 'signed distance' in 0 files. Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, 1,323 names): 'msdf' matches nothing.
- **Notes:** jit.gl.textmult works per word; cables works per character. The 3d and outline modes may already give sharp text in Max, so the practical gain could be small.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.TextMeshMSDF_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.FontMSDF_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/0_howtouse/files/files>
- id: `cables-gl--distance-field-msdf-text-with-per-character-transforms`

### Instancing fed by textures, with billboarding modes (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Geometry, meshes and instancing
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.MeshInstancer_v4`, `Ops.Gl.Meshes.MeshInstancerFromTexture_v3`, `Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.InstancedDisplacementMap_v2`, `Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.InstancedTextureColorize`, `Ops.Extension.WebGpu.MeshInstancer`
- **What it does:** One geometry is drawn many times on the GPU. Per-instance position, rotation, scale, colour and texture coordinates come from arrays, or from textures whose RGB is read as XYZ, so a shader or feedback texture can move the instances with no CPU step. Spherical and cylindrical billboarding are options.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.multiple`, `jit.gl.buffer`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.gl.tf`, `Compute / jit.gp.world`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): jit.gl.multiple draws instances of a named GL object from position, rotate, scale, color and texture matrices. jit.gl.buffer has instanced and instance_div attributes and a texbuf attribute. jit.gl.tf does transform feedback on jit.gl.mesh. packages/query_packages.py search 'particle': the Compute package's jit.gp.* GPU particle system.
- **Notes:** Max can instance. Whether jit.gl.multiple runs on the GPU, and whether instance data can come straight from a texture, was not established from the refpages. Billboarding was not searched for.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.MeshInstancer_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Meshes.MeshInstancerFromTexture_v3> · <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/02_usual_suspects/usual_suspects>
- id: `cables-gl--instancing-fed-by-textures-with-billboarding-modes`

### Shadow filtering choices and per-branch cast/receive (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.Shadow_v3`, `Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.ShadowPass_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Phong.DirectionalLight_v5`, `Ops.Gl.Phong.PointLight_v5`, `Ops.Gl.Phong.SpotLight_v5`
- **What it does:** A Shadow op after any material makes the meshes under it cast or receive shadows, with a choice of four filters (Default, PCF, Poisson, VSM), a sample count, shadow colour and an opacity mask that cuts holes in shadows. The docs explain each filter's cost and the fix for each common artifact.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.light`, `jit.gl.pbr`, `jit.gl.material`, `av-toolbox / av.light.shadows`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): jit.gl.light has shadows, shadowblur, shadowquality, shadowrange and shadowtexoutname attributes; jit.gl.pbr and jit.gl.material have shadow_soft, shadow_hard, shadow_radius, shadow_eps. No attribute naming a filter algorithm was seen. packages/query_packages.py search 'shadow': av-toolbox av.light.shadows and av.pbr.shadows (preset helpers).
- **Notes:** Max has shadows. What cables adds is a named choice of algorithm and the option to turn casting on and off per branch of the scene. The documentation of artifacts is also better than anything found in the Max userguide ('shadow' appears in 2 userguide files, neither about tuning).
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/7_lighting/shadows/shadows> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ShaderEffects.Shadow_v3>
- id: `cables-gl--shadow-filtering-choices-and-per-branch-cast-receive`

### Deforming grid shapes with built-in physics (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `newWave`, `ripple`, `rubber`, `sphere3d`
- **What it does:** newWave is a grid on a mass and spring system: it takes forces at grid points, three spring and three damping constants, and random noise, and waves travel across it. ripple and rubber are grids that are grabbed at a point and let go. sphere3d lets single vertices of a sphere be moved.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.gridshape`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.gen`, `jit.phys.world`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.gridshape (matrixoutput gives the grid as a matrix), jit.gl.mesh and jit.phys.world ('collision detection and rigid body dynamics'). Searched the package library for 'mass spring': no hits. No single Jitter object is a springy textured grid; it is built from a grid matrix and a jit.gen or shader update.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/newWave-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/ripple-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/rubber-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/sphere3d-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/05.newWave.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/06.ripple.pd>

### One object per OpenGL call in the render path (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `GEMglBegin`, `GEMglEnd`, `GEMglVertex3f`, `GEMglColor4f`, `GEMglPushMatrix`, `GEMglPopMatrix`, `GEMglLoadIdentity`, `GEMglClear`, `GEMglGenLists`, `GEMglNewList`, `GEMglEndList`, `GEMglCallList`, `GLdefine`
- **What it does:** Gem wraps nearly every OpenGL 1.2 function as an object named GEM plus the function name (the manual counts more than 250), and GLdefine turns a constant's name into its number. They sit in a chain like any other object, so a patch can draw vertex by vertex, compile a display list, or clear just the depth buffer in the middle of a frame.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.sketch`, `jit.gl.lua`, `depth_clear`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.sketch ('simple turtle graphics to the majority of the OpenGL API'; messages glbegin, glend, glvertex, glcolor, glpushmatrix, glpopmatrix, glenable, gldisable and about 50 more, kept as a command list) and jit.gl.lua (OpenGL through Lua). the common OB3D refpage jit.group-gl.maxref.xml has depth_clear to clear the depth buffer before one object. The userguide topic 'Graphics Engine' says immediate-mode drawing is deprecated in the default glcore engine; whether jit.gl.sketch's gl* messages still work there was not checked.
- **Notes:** Gem's wrappers are legacy OpenGL too; upstream has a newer example folder (15.openGL3.2) that was not read.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/manual/ListObjects.html> · <Documentation/14.gem/GLdefine-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/GEMglBegin-help.pd> · <https://github.com/umlaeute/Gem/blob/HEAD/help/GEMgl-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/09.openGL/01.primQuad.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/09.openGL/02.displayList.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/09.openGL/04.clearZ.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--one-object-per-opengl-call-in-the-render-path`

### Recursive scene graphs (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `gemhead`, `separator`, `nrepeat`, `nnrepeat`, `stack`
- **What it does:** Because a frame is an ordinary message, a subpatch can send it back into itself with a depth counter. The example set draws branching trees this way, and supplies small abstractions (a repeat with a built-in counter, a stack) that make the recursion safe.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.multiple`, `jit.gl.sketch`, `jit.gl.lua`, `v8`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.multiple, jit.gl.sketch (command lists with glpushmatrix / glpopmatrix and drawobject) and jit.gl.lua (scripts OpenGL in Lua). No Jitter object draws by recursion through patch cords; a branching structure has to be computed first (in a script or gen) and handed over as matrices or a command list.
- **Notes:** The Gem examples need gemrepeat and plain Pd objects; the README of the example folder also lists the Zexy library as a requirement.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/examples/13.recursion/03.recursive_spiral.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/13.recursion/04.binary_tree.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/13.recursion/05.n-ary_tree.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/13.recursion/06.breaking_symmetry.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/13.recursion/nrepeat-help.pd>

### Stencil-buffer masking (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `GEMglStencilFunc`, `GEMglStencilOp`, `GEMglClearStencil`, `GEMglEnable`, `GEMglDisable`, `GLdefine`
- **What it does:** Using the OpenGL wrapper objects, one example draws a shape into the stencil buffer and then draws two other objects only where the stencil is, or is not, set. It is a way to cut one 3D object to the outline of another without textures.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.sketch`, `jit.gl.lua`, `jit.gl.node`, `jit.gl.pix`

The usual Jitter route to the same picture is to capture each object to a texture (jit.gl.node @capture 1) and combine them with a mask in jit.gl.pix or jit.gl.slab.
