### A premultiplied-alpha flag that travels with the video stream (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Alpha Tool`, `Movie Player`, `alpha mode`
- **What it does:** Every video stream with an alpha channel carries a flag saying whether its colour is already multiplied by its alpha. Movie Player sets it per movie, and Alpha Tool can flip the flag or actually convert the image. The manual's symptom to look for is a light or dark halo on soft edges.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.layer`, `jit.gl.videoplane`, `blend_mode`
- **Checked:** Searched every Max refpage for 'premult': no hits. The GL group page documents `blend` as named modes over `blend_mode` source and destination factors, where `alphablend` is `6 7` (source alpha, one minus source alpha) and factor 1 is 'one'.
- **Advice for Max:** Max leaves this to the patcher. Dark fringes on soft edges usually mean a premultiplied source drawn with `alphablend`; try `@blend_mode 1 7` on that layer. Note in a comment which kind each source is, because nothing in Max carries the information.
- **Notes:** The `1 7` setting is worked out from the factor table in the refpage; it was not tested in Max.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=392 (Actors Reference: Alpha Tool)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=603 (Actors Reference: Movie Player: alpha mode)>
- id: `isadora--a-premultiplied-alpha-flag-that-travels-with-the-video-stream`

### Text renderer that reports the size of what it drew (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `Text Draw`, `Text/ure`
- **What it does:** Text Draw renders text into a video stream inside a bounding rectangle with padding, alignment, line spacing, a scroll position and a background colour. A 'measure mode' turns on outputs giving the rendered text's position, width and height, in units chosen to match the Shapes actor, so a box can be drawn to fit the words.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.text`
- **Checked:** Read `jit.gl.text`'s attribute list: `align`, `fontname`, `fontsize`, `leadscale`, `line_length` (wraps lines past this length), `tracking`, `screenmode`, `depth`. No attribute or message in the list reports the size of the rendered text.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, fix the box and make the text fit it: set `line_length` to wrap and size the backing shape from that, not from the text. If a caption box must hug its text, check `jit.mgraphics` or a `v8ui` script for text measuring first; neither was read here.
- **Notes:** Only the names in `jit.gl.text`'s refpage were checked; a measuring feature under another name would have been missed.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=772 (Actors Reference: Text Draw)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=779 (Actors Reference: Text/ure)>
- id: `isadora--text-renderer-that-reports-the-size-of-what-it-drew`

### Instancing from any data, one attribute at a time (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Geometry, meshes and instancing
- **There:** `Geometry COMP`, `Instance`
- **What it does:** One geometry object draws many copies. The count comes from the number of samples, table rows, pixels or points in a source. Each attribute (translate, rotate, scale, rotate towards a vector, colour, texture) picks its own source and its own channel or column by name. Each copy's id is available to shaders and to picking.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.multiple`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.gl.buffer`, `jit.gl.picker`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.multiple refpage: draws a named target repeatedly from position_matrix, rotate_matrix, rotatexyz_matrix, scale_matrix, color_matrix and texture_matrix (attributes targetname, glparams, dimparam). jit.gl.buffer refpage: instanced passes a different value to each drawn instance of a jit.gl.mesh, instance_div groups them. jit.gl.picker refpage: reports the name of the object under the mouse, not an instance number. patching/MAX_PATCHING.md records three pitfalls with jit.gl.multiple and jit.gl.buffer.
- **Advice for Max:** For tens to hundreds of copies use jit.gl.multiple with one matrix per attribute. For thousands use jit.gl.mesh with jit.gl.buffer @instanced 1, one buffer per inlet. Aiming copies along a vector has to be computed (jit.gen). A helper that builds these matrices from a dict or table by column name is worth building.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Geometry_COMP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/COMP_Instance_Page> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/COMP_Instance_2_Page> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Instance>
- id: `touchdesigner--instancing-from-any-data-one-attribute-at-a-time`

### Keyers and masks that can output transparency instead of a finished mix (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Chroma Key`, `Luminance Key`, `Add Alpha Channel`, `Alpha Mask`
- **What it does:** The two keyers normally output foreground mixed over background. With their 'alpha' switch on they output the foreground with a transparent hole, for the Projector to composite over whatever is on lower layers. Add Alpha Channel attaches any stream as another stream's alpha. Alpha Mask mixes two streams by a third and has a 'strength' input for fading the mask in.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.chromakey`, `jit.lumakey`, `jit.alphablend`, `jit.butter.alphamask (Butter_tools)`, `jit.gl.layer`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `jit.chromakey` and `jit.lumakey` take `tol` and `fade` and have a `mode`: 0 performs the key and outputs the mix, 1 outputs the key map 'for use elsewhere'. `jit.alphablend` crossfades two matrices by the alpha plane of the left one. These are CPU matrix objects.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, prefer keying to alpha and letting `jit.gl.layer` do the mix: take the key map (`@mode 1`), use it as the alpha of the foreground, and layer it. That keeps the background free to be anything on a lower layer, which is the design Isadora's 'alpha' switch points at.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=435 (Actors Reference: Chroma Key)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=573 (Actors Reference: Luminance Key)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=389 (Actors Reference: Add Alpha Channel)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=391 (Actors Reference: Alpha Mask)>
- id: `isadora--keyers-and-masks-that-can-output-transparency-instead-of-a-finished-mix`

### Output layer with three blend choices and one 'intensity' input (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Projector`, `3D Projector`
- **What it does:** Projector draws a stream on a Stage with a 'layer' number and a 'blend' of additive, transparent or opaque. One 'intensity' input means brightness under additive and opaque, and opacity under transparent, so a fade is always the same input. The manual warns that a transparent image must be on a higher layer than what it sits over.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.layer`, `jit.gl.videoplane`, `layer`, `blend`, `blend_enable`, `color`
- **Checked:** Read the GL group refpage and Max's transform-defaults registry. `layer`: 'the layer number determines the rendering order (low to high). Objects in the same layer have no guarantee which will be rendered first.' `blend` has eight named modes (add, multiply, screen, exclusion, colorblend, alphablend, coloradd, alphaadd). `jit.gl.layer` loads with blending on and depth testing off; `jit.gl.videoplane` with blending off and depth on.
- **Advice for Max:** Max offers more blend modes but no single fade input. Use `jit.gl.layer`, give every one an explicit `@layer`, since equal layers draw in no fixed order, and fade with the alpha of `color` under `alphablend` or by scaling `color`'s red, green and blue under `add`.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=652 (Actors Reference: Projector)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=242 (Compositing with the Projector Actor)>
- id: `isadora--output-layer-with-three-blend-choices-and-one-intensity-input`

### The engine's frame rate is a project setting (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Frame rate and frame-locked timing
- **There:** `Engine FPS`, `Auto`, `Manual`
- **What it does:** The frame rate of the whole engine is either left on Auto, following the display, or set to a fixed number in the project's preferences. The FAQ uses it to limit the rate of an NDI output.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world (fps, interval, displaylink, sync)`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world's refpage: 'fps' and 'interval' only take effect when 'enable' is 1 and 'displaylink' is 0; 'displaylink' (default 1, Mac only) ties drawing to the display's refresh; 'sync' is vertical sync; 'running_fps' reports the real rate.
- **Advice for Max:** On a Mac, `@fps` does nothing until `@displaylink 0` is also set. Decide per patch whether the display or a number sets the rate, write both attributes on the jit.world, and show `running_fps` somewhere the operator can see.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): confirmed in jit.world's refpage: fps and interval 'only have an effect if enable is 1 and displaylink is 0', and transform-defaults.json gives displaylink 1.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--the-engine-s-frame-rate-is-a-project-setting`

### Draw order and correct transparency (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Transparency`, `Geometry COMP`, `Render TOP`
- **What it does:** Transparent objects are either sorted by a draw-priority number per object with blending turned on in the material, or rendered with an order-independent mode that peels the scene in several passes. The page explains how many passes typical scenes need and what the mode costs.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world @transparency`, `jit.world @depth_sort`, `@layer`, `@depth_enable`, `@blend_enable`
- **Checked:** jit.world refpage: transparency 'adds objects with a colour alpha below 1 or blend_enable 1 to a transparency pass composited with the opaque objects with proper depth buffering'; depth_sort sorts objects by distance from the camera before each frame. Userguide 'Depth Testing and Layering': to use layer, turn depth testing off on the object, otherwise depth testing overrides it.
- **Advice for Max:** Before ordering transparent 3D objects by hand, try jit.world @transparency 1 and @depth_sort 1. For flat layers turn depth testing off and use @layer.
- **Notes:** The repo holds two notes that disagree: scans/c74-forum/forum_insights.md says @depth_enable 1 on jit.world, patching/MAX_PATCHING.md says @depth_enable 0. The userguide page read here supports turning it off for layering.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Transparency>
- id: `touchdesigner--draw-order-and-correct-transparency`

### Ramps and smoothing that run on the render frame (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Lag CHOP`, `Filter CHOP`, `Time Slice CHOP`
- **What it does:** All control data is computed once per rendered frame, and time-sliced operators fill in the frames that were skipped. A ramp that feeds a picture therefore never beats against the frame rate.
- **Closest in Max:** `line`, `jit.line`, `jit.mo.time`, `jit.world @fixed_delta`
- **Checked:** line refpage: grain is the output interval, 20 ms by default, on Max's own timer. jit.line refpage: a drop-in replacement for line whose output is timed by the render context, described as removing the stutter of unsynchronised output. jit.mo.time refpage: mode delta gives the time between frames, accum gives running time. jit.world refpage: fixed_delta locks the delta for non-realtime capture.
- **Advice for Max:** For any value that ends up on screen, use jit.line and not line, and scale per-frame motion by jit.mo.time @mode delta. Keep line and line~ for control and audio.
- **Notes:** patching/MAX_PATCHING.md ('Prefer smooth transitions…') currently names `line 0.` for Jitter/GL float parameters; jit.line looks like the better default there. Not tested in Max.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Lag_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Filter_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Time_Slice_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cook> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Frame>
- id: `touchdesigner--ramps-and-smoothing-that-run-on-the-render-frame`

### Transform hierarchy by wiring objects (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `3D Parenting`, `Null COMP`, `Object CHOP`
- **What it does:** 3D objects become parent and child by wiring one under another or by nesting. An empty object serves as a shared parent or as a look-at target. A separate operator reports the position, rotation, bearing and distance of one object relative to another.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.anim.node`, `jit.gl.node`, `jit.gl.camera`
- **Checked:** jit.anim.node refpage: a transform that GL objects bind to, with parent-child relationships; attributes lookat, locklook, inherit_position / rotate / scale, worldpos, worldquat; messages localtoworld, worldtolocal. jit.gl.node refpage: a render group that can be captured. jit.gl.camera refpage: lookat, locklook.
- **Advice for Max:** Max splits what TouchDesigner joins: use jit.anim.node for parent-child transforms and jit.gl.node for render grouping. For the distance between two objects, read each jit.anim.node's worldpos and compute it in expr.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/3D_Parenting> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Null_COMP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Object_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Camera_COMP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Light_COMP>
- id: `touchdesigner--transform-hierarchy-by-wiring-objects`

### Camera as an op above the scene; navigation cameras (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Cameras, lights and materials
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.Matrix.Camera_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Matrix.LookatCamera`, `Ops.Graphics.OrbitControls_v3`, `Ops.Gl.Matrix.WASDCamera_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Perspective`, `Ops.Gl.Orthogonal_v2`
- **What it does:** A camera is an op in the chain and views whatever is triggered below it, so two branches can have two cameras. OrbitControls adds mouse orbit, pan and zoom with distance limits, a smoothness setting and switches for each motion. WASDCamera is a first-person camera with pointer lock.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.camera`, `jit.anim.drive @ui_listen`, `jit.gl.handle`, `jit.world @auto_handle`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.camera refpage: lookat, locklook, tripod, lens_angle, ortho, viewport, layer and capture; it views its whole context or node, and several cameras draw in layer order into viewports. jit.anim.drive refpage: ui_listen makes it take mouse and keyboard messages from the context window, mapped through a ui_map dictionary, with easing. jit.gl.handle refpage: mouse rotation and position for an object.
- **Advice for Max:** For mouse or key navigation in Max, attach jit.anim.drive @ui_listen 1 to the camera instead of patching mouse math by hand. For two views of one scene use two jit.gl.camera objects with different @viewport values; for two different scenes put each in its own jit.gl.node.
- **Notes:** The default key and mouse mapping of jit.anim.drive was not read.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Matrix.Camera_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Matrix.LookatCamera> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.OrbitControls_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Matrix.WASDCamera_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Perspective> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Orthogonal_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Matrix.CameraInfo>
- id: `cables-gl--camera-as-an-op-above-the-scene-navigation-cameras`

### Draw order set by trigger order (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.MainLoop_v2`, `Ops.Trigger.Sequence`, `Ops.Trigger.SequenceMultiPort_v2`, `Ops.Gl.LayerSequence`
- **What it does:** Things are drawn in the order their trigger arrives. A Sequence op has numbered trigger outputs that fire in turn, so the patch itself shows what is drawn first and what is drawn on top. LayerSequence is the same idea named for layers.
- **Closest in Max:** `layer (OB3D attribute)`, `depth_enable (OB3D attribute)`, `jit.world`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** Userguide jitter/depth_layer_blend.json: every 3D object is in layer 0 by default 'and therefore their drawing order is indeterminate'; lower layers draw first; depth testing overrides the layer unless depth_enable is 0. jit.group-gl refpage, layer: 'Objects in the same layer have no guarantee which will be rendered first.' Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md lines 225 and 288 say the same and call it a common stacking bug.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max nothing about the patch layout sets draw order, so give every overlapping GL object its own @layer number, and set @depth_enable 0 on flat overlays. Number the layers in the same order the boxes sit on screen so the patch reads like a cables Sequence.
- **Notes:** The cables pages do not say what happens with two cables from one trigger output. Sequence exists so the order is never left to that.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max's userguide (Depth Testing and Layering) and the GL attribute refpage settle it: depth testing is per object, on by default, and overrides layer, so each layered object needs depth_enable 0. The repo's docs were corrected 2026-10-03.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.Sequence> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.SequenceMultiPort_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.LayerSequence> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.MainLoop_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/guidelines/guidelines>
- id: `cables-gl--draw-order-set-by-trigger-order`

### Drawing one mesh many times with a loop op (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Geometry, meshes and instancing
- **There:** `Ops.Trigger.Repeat_v2`, `Ops.Trigger.Repeat2d`, `Ops.Gl.GridTransform`, `Ops.Gl.RandomCluster`, `Ops.Array.ArrayIteratorNumbers`
- **What it does:** A loop op triggers the ops below it N times in one frame and gives the index. Feed the index into a transform and the same mesh is drawn N times. Repeat2d and GridTransform do rows and columns. The cables performance page says to switch to instancing when the count gets high.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.multiple`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `uzi`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.multiple refpage: draws a named GL object repeatedly from matrices (position_matrix, rotate_matrix, scale_matrix, color_matrix; @glparams lists which). jit.group-gl refpage: with @automatic 1 an object draws once when the renderer is banged; a bang or draw message draws it by hand. uzi refpage: sends many bangs with an index. Drawing one object several times per frame by hand with uzi was not tested.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max go straight to jit.gl.multiple: fill a 3-plane float32 matrix with positions and it draws every copy. There is no cheap 'sketch it with a loop first' step as in cables, so plan the per-copy data as matrices from the start.
- **Notes:** Session 1 already lists GPU instancing from textures as a candidate. This one is about the loop idiom.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.Repeat_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.Repeat2d> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.GridTransform> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.RandomCluster> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.ArrayIteratorNumbers> · <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/02_usual_suspects/usual_suspects>
- id: `cables-gl--drawing-one-mesh-many-times-with-a-loop-op`

### Frame rate limit and unfocused throttle on the main loop (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Frame rate and frame-locked timing
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.MainLoop_v2`, `Ops.Cables.FPS_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Performance`
- **What it does:** MainLoop has ports for an FPS limit, a cap on pixel density for high-resolution screens, and a switch that lowers the frame rate when the window loses focus. It outputs the canvas width, height and pixel density. FPS and Performance report the rate reached.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world @fps / @interval / @displaylink / @sync`, `jit.world @high_res`, `jit.fpsgui`
- **Checked:** jit.world refpage: fps and interval 'only have an effect if enable is 1 and displaylink is 0'; displaylink (default 1, Mac only) syncs drawing to the display; sync (default 1) is vertical sync; enable defaults to 0; a running_fps attribute and a high_res attribute exist. jit.fpsgui refpage: reports fps of what it is fed.
- **Advice for Max:** On a Mac, setting @fps on jit.world does nothing until @displaylink is 0. To cap or lower the frame rate, set @displaylink 0 and then @fps. To save GPU on a high-density screen, check @high_res rather than rendering at full pixel density by default.
- **Notes:** What running_fps and high_res do in detail was not read past their names.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): confirmed in jit.world's refpage: fps and interval 'only have an effect if enable is 1 and displaylink is 0', and transform-defaults.json gives displaylink 1.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.MainLoop_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Cables.FPS_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Performance>
- id: `cables-gl--frame-rate-limit-and-unfocused-throttle-on-the-main-loop`

### Geometry as a value separate from drawing (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Geometry, meshes and instancing
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.RenderGeometry_v2`, `Ops.Graphics.Geometry.TransformGeometry_v2`, `Ops.Graphics.Geometry.GeometryMerge`, `Ops.Graphics.Geometry.CalculateNormals`, `Ops.Graphics.Geometry.GeometryAttributes`, `Ops.Graphics.Geometry.GeometryFromArrays`
- **What it does:** Every mesh op outputs its geometry as an object and has a Render switch. Geometry ops then transform, merge or rebuild that object on the CPU, and RenderGeometry draws the result, in as many places as wanted. Other ops turn geometry into arrays and arrays into geometry.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.geom.* (26 objects)`, `jit.geom.tomesh`, `jit.gl.gridshape (matrix output)`, `jit.gl.mesh`
- **Checked:** Jitter Geometry package (ships in Max): 26 jit.geom refpages including shape, xform, join, subdivide, decimate, normgen, twist, tomatrix, todict and tomesh. jit.geom.tomesh refpage: converts geometry to a jit.gl.mesh and is the faster path when the goal is to draw it. jit.gl.gridshape refpage: left outlet is 'matrix output if enabled'. jit.gl.mesh refpage: vertex, normal, texcoord, color and index matrices in.
- **Advice for Max:** Max now has the same split: build shapes with jit.geom.shape, edit with the jit.geom.* objects, and draw with jit.geom.tomesh. Use that chain for anything that changes the shape itself, and keep jit.gl.gridshape for plain primitives that only need drawing.
- **Notes:** This is near parity. It is listed because older Max habits (gridshape matrix output into jit.gl.mesh) miss the newer objects.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.RenderGeometry_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Geometry.TransformGeometry_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Geometry.GeometryMerge> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Geometry.CalculateNormals> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Geometry.GeometryAttributes> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Geometry.GeometryFromArrays> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Geometry.GeometryInfo> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Meshes.Sphere_v3> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/guidelines/guidelines>
- id: `cables-gl--geometry-as-a-value-separate-from-drawing`

### Lights apply only to what is triggered after them (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.Phong.PointLight_v5`, `Ops.Gl.Phong.DirectionalLight_v5`, `Ops.Gl.Phong.SpotLight_v5`, `Ops.Gl.Phong.AmbientLight_v4`, `Ops.Gl.Phong.ResetLights`
- **What it does:** A light is an op in the chain. Materials placed under it are lit by it. ResetLights removes all lights for the rest of a branch, so part of a scene can be left out of the lighting.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.light`, `lighting_enable (OB3D attribute)`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.light refpage: 18 attributes (type, diffuse, specular, attenuation, spot, shadows); none names the objects it lights, and its only outlet is dumpout. jit.group-gl refpage: lighting_enable is a per-object flag, default 0. jit.gl.material refpage: adapts to 'the number of active lights'.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max a light belongs to the whole context, and each object opts in or out. To leave one object unlit, set @lighting_enable 0 on it or give it a material that ignores lights, instead of looking for a way to aim the light at chosen objects.
- **Notes:** Whether a jit.gl.light inside a jit.gl.node lights only that node's children was not checked.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Phong.PointLight_v5> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Phong.DirectionalLight_v5> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Phong.AmbientLight_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Phong.ResetLights> · <https://cables.gl/docs/7_lighting/lights/lights>
- id: `cables-gl--lights-apply-only-to-what-is-triggered-after-them`

### One frame trigger drives every per-frame op (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.MainLoop_v2`, `Ops.Anim.Smooth`, `Ops.Anim.Spring`, `Ops.Anim.SimpleAnim`, `Ops.Trigger.Interval`
- **What it does:** MainLoop fires once per frame, and anything that must update each frame has a trigger input wired back to it: smoothing, springs, animations, timers. A separate timer op, Interval, exists, and its page warns not to use it for animation that will be recorded.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world (middle outlet)`, `qmetro`, `metro`
- **Checked:** jit.world refpage: outlet 1 is the 'render draw bang'; @drawbang sets whether it fires before or after rendering (default prerender). metro and qmetro refpages: free-running clocks with their own interval. Repo scans/packages/tutorials_insights.md line 138: the Jitter Recipes send that draw bang to 's draw' and hang every per-frame update on 'r draw'.
- **Advice for Max:** Use jit.world's middle outlet as the one frame clock for a visual patch and drive counters, slide, line-style ramps and matrix feedback from it. A separate metro runs at its own rate and drifts against the picture.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.MainLoop_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.Smooth> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.Spring> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.SimpleAnim> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.Interval> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_creating_ports/dev_ports_trigger/dev_ports_trigger>
- id: `cables-gl--one-frame-trigger-drives-every-per-frame-op`

### Render state scoped to the ops under it (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `Ops.Graphics.Transform`, `Ops.Gl.BlendMode`, `Ops.Graphics.DepthTest`, `Ops.Gl.FaceCulling_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.BasicMaterial_v3`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.SetShader`, `Ops.Gl.Identity`
- **What it does:** A transform, blend mode, depth test, culling mode or material is an op in the trigger chain. It applies to everything triggered below it and to nothing else. Two Transform ops in series make a parent and a child. Identity and ResetTransform clear the transform for a branch.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.node`, `jit.anim.node`, `blend_enable / depth_enable / cull_face (OB3D attributes)`, `jit.gl.material`
- **Checked:** jit.group-gl refpage: blend_enable, blend, depth_enable, depth_write, cull_face, poly_mode, shader, texture and anim are attributes on every GL object, each set per object. jit.gl.node refpage discussion: a node 'can overwrite standard OB3D attributes of its child objects', for example poly_mode set on the node replaces it on every child. jit.anim.node refpage: GL objects bind to it for position, rotate and scale, and nodes can be parents of other nodes.
- **Advice for Max:** When several Max GL objects must share a state, do not copy the attribute onto each box. Put them in one jit.gl.node and set the attribute on the node, and for a shared or nested transform bind them to a jit.anim.node. That is Max's version of a cables branch.
- **Notes:** Which attributes a jit.gl.node overwrites was read from the refpage only, not tested in Max.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max's userguide (Depth Testing and Layering) and the GL attribute refpage settle it: depth testing is per object, on by default, and overrides layer, so each layered object needs depth_enable 0. The repo's docs were corrected 2026-10-03.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Transform> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.BlendMode> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.DepthTest> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.FaceCulling_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.BasicMaterial_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.SetShader> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Identity> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ResetTransform> · <https://cables.gl/docs/1_beginner/beginner2_transformations/beginner2_transformations>
- id: `cables-gl--render-state-scoped-to-the-ops-under-it`

### Render-to-texture as a branch with depth and format choices (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.RenderToTexture_v3`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderToTexture_v2`, `Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.DepthTexture_v2`
- **What it does:** RenderToTexture draws its children into a texture. Its ports choose the size, MSAA level, pixel format, wrap and whether to clear. It gives the colour texture and the depth texture as two outputs, ready for depth-based effects.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.node @capture`, `jit.gl.camera @capture`, `jit.gl.pass`, `jit.world @output_texture`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.node refpage: @capture 1 sends the texture out the left outlet; values above 1 give several render targets; adapt, dim, type and fsaa set the texture; children join by drawto name, by the middle outlet, or by being in the same patcher. jit.gl.camera has the same capture attribute. Userguide jitter/render_passes.json: jit.gl.pass works on 'intermediate render targets like the depth buffer' and sets the node's capture itself.
- **Advice for Max:** Use jit.gl.node @capture 1 for a scene you want as a texture, and name it so objects join it by @drawto. For anything that needs the depth buffer use jit.gl.pass on that node instead of trying to pull a depth texture out by hand.
- **Notes:** How to get a plain depth texture from jit.gl.node without jit.gl.pass was not found in the pages read.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.RenderToTexture_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.ShaderToTexture_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.DepthTexture_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ImageCompose.DepthTextureFocus_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/2_intermediate/post-processing_3d_scenes/post-processing_3d_scenes>
- id: `cables-gl--render-to-texture-as-a-branch-with-depth-and-format-choices`

### Screen-space helpers: draw in pixels, project to screen, unproject (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.PixelProjection_v3`, `Ops.Gl.Matrix.ScreenCoordinates_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Matrix.ScreenPosTo3d_v3`, `Ops.Gl.Matrix.Coordinates`, `Ops.Gl.Meshes.FullscreenRectangle_v2`
- **What it does:** PixelProjection makes one unit equal one pixel for the branch under it, with a choice of where 0,0 sits. ScreenCoordinates gives the pixel position of the current transform and a flag for whether it is on screen. ScreenPosTo3d turns a mouse position into a 3D position.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.camera worldtoscreen / screentoworld`, `transform_reset (OB3D attribute)`, `jit.gl.text @screenmode`, `jit.gl.layer`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.camera refpage: worldtoscreen and screentoworld messages convert between world and pixel coordinates and answer on the dump outlet; getviewportray gives a ray for a screen point. jit.group-gl refpage: transform_reset 1 or 2 draws an object in a flat orthographic space for overlays. jit.gl.text refpage: screenmode keeps text a fixed pixel size at a projected position. Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md: Max GL positions are normalised, not pixels.
- **Advice for Max:** For an overlay or HUD in Max, set @transform_reset 2 on the object so it ignores the camera, and give it a high @layer. To pin something to a 3D point or to pick with the mouse, ask jit.gl.camera with worldtoscreen or screentoworld; there is no pixel-unit drawing mode, so convert once and keep the scale in one place.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.PixelProjection_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Matrix.ScreenCoordinates_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Matrix.ScreenPosTo3d_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Matrix.Coordinates> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Meshes.FullscreenRectangle_v2>
- id: `cables-gl--screen-space-helpers-draw-in-pixels-project-to-screen-unproject`

### Any geometry as the particle, and per-particle data as messages (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Particles
- **There:** `part_render`, `part_info`, `part_information`, `part_move`
- **What it does:** part_render draws the chain below it once per particle, so each particle can be a cube, a model or text. part_information sends every particle's position, colour, velocity, size and age out as messages once per frame, which lets a patch drive sound or anything else from individual particles.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.multiple`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.iter`, `jit.spill`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.multiple (draws a named object once per cell of its position / scale / color matrices) and jit.iter / jit.spill (matrix cells out as lists). In Jitter the particle data is already a matrix, so there is nothing to ask for.
- **Advice for Max:** Feed the particle matrix's position planes to jit.gl.multiple to draw a real object per particle. To sonify particles, read a few cells with jit.spill; do not iterate thousands of cells into the message thread every frame.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/part_render-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/part_information-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/part_info-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/part_move-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/06.particle/07.render.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/06.particle/08.info.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--any-geometry-as-the-particle-and-per-particle-data-as-messages`

### Bangs before and after any part of the frame (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `trigger`, `render_trigger`, `gemhead`
- **What it does:** Since the frame is a message, 't a b' gives a bang just before or just after the part of the chain below it is drawn. The examples use it to grab the screen after one object is drawn but before the next. A chain can also be switched off with a 0 to its gemhead, or drawn on demand with a bang.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `automatic`, `enable`, `draw`, `layer`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world (drawbang: 'determines if the middle-outlet draw bang happens before or after rendering', one bang per frame) and the common OB3D refpage jit.group-gl.maxref.xml (automatic, enable, and the draw / drawraw / drawimmediate messages).
- **Advice for Max:** Jitter gives one hook per frame, not one per object. For work that must happen between two objects, set them @automatic 0 and send 'draw' to each in the order wanted from one trigger, or put the first in a jit.gl.node @capture 1 and use its texture.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/render_trigger-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/08.Snapshot2.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_snap-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemhead-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--bangs-before-and-after-any-part-of-the-frame`

### Draw order as one number on the head of each chain, changeable while running (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `gemhead`, `alpha`, `depth`
- **What it does:** Each gemhead takes a priority (default 50): lower is drawn first. The number is an argument or a 'set' message, so a patch can re-order transparent objects as they pass each other. Depth testing and blending are separate objects placed in the chain where they are wanted.
- **Closest in Max:** `layer`, `depth_enable`, `depth_write`, `blend_enable`, `jit.gl.layer`, `jit.world`
- **Checked:** Read the common OB3D refpage jit.group-gl.maxref.xml (layer: 'determines the rendering order (low to high)', same layer has no guaranteed order; depth_enable default 1; depth_write; blend_enable default 0), the userguide topic 'Depth Testing and Layering' (says to turn depth_enable off when layering, 'otherwise depth testing will override the layer value'; jit.gl.layer turns depth off and blending on by default) and jit.world (depth_sort attribute). This repo's own forum notes give two opposite instructions about depth_enable with layers, which shows how easily it is got wrong.
- **Advice for Max:** Treat order in Jitter as two settings, not one: give every renderable an explicit @layer, and decide @depth_enable per object. For a flat stack use @depth_enable 0 @blend_enable 1 (or jit.gl.layer); for transparent objects inside a 3D scene use @depth_write 0 with a higher @layer, as the userguide says.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max's userguide (Depth Testing and Layering) and the GL attribute refpage settle it: depth testing is per object, on by default, and overrides layer, so each layered object needs depth_enable 0. The repo's docs were corrected 2026-10-03.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemhead-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/alpha-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/depth-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/14.RenderOrder.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--draw-order-as-one-number-on-the-head-of-each-chain-changeable-while-running`

### Drawing one shape many times by re-sending the frame (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `gemlist`, `gemrepeat`, `until`, `separator`
- **What it does:** gemlist stores the frame message and sends it again on a bang, so a loop draws the same shape many times, each pass adding a little more translate, rotate or scale. gemrepeat does it in one box. A hundred circles in a spiral is five boxes.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.multiple`, `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.gl.sketch`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.multiple (draws a named jit.gl object repeatedly from matrices; glparams position, rotate, rotatexyz, scale, color, texture), jit.gl.mesh (instances attribute, undocumented text) and jit.gl.sketch (drawobject message draws a named object inside a command list). OB3D objects also take draw / drawraw with automatic 0.
- **Advice for Max:** In Jitter the copies come from data, not from a loop: fill position / rotate / scale matrices (jit.gen or jit.expr make a spiral in one object) and give them to jit.gl.multiple. It scales to thousands, which a Gem loop does not; Gem's loop is easier to read for a dozen.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemlist-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/19.pointer.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/20.double-gemhead_vs_repeat.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/22.double-iterative.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--drawing-one-shape-many-times-by-re-sending-the-frame`

### Geometry from tables, filled at audio rate (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tables, lists and data
- **There:** `gemvertexbuffer`, `tabwrite~`, `glsl_program`
- **What it does:** gemvertexbuffer draws a vertex buffer whose positions, colours, normals, texture coordinates and named shader attributes are read from Pd tables, whole or one component at a time, with an offset. Since tables can be written by signals, a hundred thousand points can follow an audio-rate equation.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.mesh`, `jit.gl.buffer`, `jit.catch~`, `jit.buffer~`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.mesh ('creates a geometric surface from a jit.matrix'; vertex_matrix, color_matrix, normal_matrix, texcoord_matrix, index_matrix, vertex_attr_matrix, draw_mode), jit.gl.buffer (named GPU buffer for jit.gl.mesh and jit.gl.tf), jit.catch~ and jit.buffer~.
- **Advice for Max:** In Jitter the table is a matrix: three signals into jit.catch~ give a 3-plane position matrix for jit.gl.mesh in one cord. Custom per-vertex shader inputs go in through vertex_attr_matrix.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemvertexbuffer-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/16.vertexbuffer_attributes.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/15.GLSL4/01.basic.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--geometry-from-tables-filled-at-audio-rate`

### Overlay chains that ignore the camera (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `gemhead`, `text2d`
- **What it does:** A gemhead with a negative priority is drawn after every positive one and is not affected by changes of viewpoint. It is the stock way to keep a logo, a caption or a help text fixed on screen over a moving scene.
- **Closest in Max:** `transform_reset`, `layer`, `depth_enable`, `jit.gl.layer`, `jit.gl.text`
- **Checked:** Read the common OB3D refpage jit.group-gl.maxref.xml: transform_reset 1 or 2 sets the modelview and projection to identity before drawing the object, 'useful for sprite or billboard overlays'. The userguide topic 'Depth Testing and Layering' gives the overlay recipe: depth_enable 0, blend_enable 1 and a higher layer. jit.gl.text has screenmode for fixed-size 2D text.
- **Advice for Max:** For a heads-up overlay in Jitter, set three things on the overlay object: @transform_reset 2, @depth_enable 0 and a @layer above the scene. Gem needs one number; Jitter needs all three, so write them in the box.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max's userguide (Depth Testing and Layering) and the GL attribute refpage settle it: depth testing is per object, on by default, and overrides layer, so each layered object needs depth_enable 0. The repo's docs were corrected 2026-10-03.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemhead-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/03.View_OSD.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--overlay-chains-that-ignore-the-camera`

### Particles assembled from small objects with shaped emitters, velocities and sinks (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Particles
- **There:** `part_head`, `part_source`, `part_velocity`, `part_sink`, `part_gravity`, `part_orbitpoint`, `part_follow`, `part_damp`, `part_killold`, `part_killslow`, `part_targetcolor`, `part_targetsize`, `part_color`, `part_size`, `part_vertex`, `part_draw`
- **What it does:** A particle system is a chain: head, properties of new particles, a source, behaviours, removal, a renderer. Source, velocity and sink all take a shape from one list (point, line, triangle, plane, box, sphere, cylinder, cone, blob, disc, rectangle), so 'emit from a disc, speeds inside a cone, die at a plane' is three boxes.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.p.shiva`, `jit.p.vishnu`, `jit.p.bounds`, `jit.gp.world`, `jit.gp.emitter`, `jit.gp.force`, `jit.gp.obstacle`, `jit.gp.render`, `jit.gl.mesh`
- **Checked:** Read jit.p.shiva (emit, life, init and their variances), jit.p.vishnu (one force; pos, speed, pitch, yaw with variances; mode 1 uses values already in the matrix as start positions) and jit.p.bounds (box limits). The Compute package installed in ~/Documents/Max 9/Packages adds jit.gp.world, jit.gp.emitter (mode, rate, life, size), jit.gp.force (fieldtype, radius) and jit.gp.obstacle (shape, action) on the GPU; it is third-party, not part of Max.
- **Advice for Max:** Jitter's core particle objects have no emitter shapes: to emit from a disc, write the start positions into the matrix yourself and set jit.p.vishnu @mode 1. With the Compute package, use jit.gp.emitter and jit.gp.obstacle instead. Either way, plan the system in Gem's three shapes (where born, which way, where they die) before choosing objects.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/part_head-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/part_source-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/part_velocity-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/part_sink-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/part_orbitpoint-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/06.particle/04.combo.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/06.particle/09.sink.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/gem.release_notes.txt>
- id: `plugdata-gem--particles-assembled-from-small-objects-with-shaped-emitters-velocities-and-sinks`

### Reading back the transform that has built up on a chain (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `gemlist_info`, `gemlist_matrix`
- **What it does:** Placed anywhere on a chain, gemlist_info outputs the accumulated transform at that point as translation, rotation, scale and shear; gemlist_matrix outputs the 16-number matrix. The examples use them to pass a transform to a shader and to export vertex positions.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.anim.node`, `jit.gl.camera`
- **Checked:** Read jit.anim.node: attributes transform ('get and set the full local 4x4 matrix'), worldpos, worldquat, worldrot, worldscale, worldtransform, and the messages localtoworld / worldtolocal. jit.gl.camera has view_matrix, proj_matrix and viewproj_matrix attributes.
- **Advice for Max:** To learn where a nested Jitter object really is, bind it to a jit.anim.node and query getworldpos / getworldtransform there; do not recompute the parent chain by hand.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemlist_info-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemlist_matrix-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/15.GLSL4/02.transformation.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/11.obj-exporter/obj_exporter-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--reading-back-the-transform-that-has-built-up-on-a-chain`

### Render any chain into a texture with an explicit size, format and frustum (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `gemframebuffer`, `pix_texture`
- **What it does:** Everything below a gemframebuffer is drawn into a texture instead of the window. Messages set the texture's size, pixel type (byte, int, float), format (including 32-bit float RGBA), background colour, its own frustum and the texture unit. Several chains can draw into the same framebuffer.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.node`, `jit.gl.camera`, `jit.world`, `jit.gl.texture`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.node (capture: 'rendering is captured to a texture and the texture name is output'; values above 1 give multiple render targets; adapt, dim, type, erase_color, fsaa), jit.gl.camera (capture, out_name, viewport, ortho) and jit.world (output_texture / capture).
- **Advice for Max:** The Jitter equivalent is jit.gl.node @capture 1 with @adapt 0 @dim set by hand when the size matters, plus a jit.gl.camera inside the node when the captured view should differ from the window's. The porting trap runs the other way: a Gem framebuffer's camera is at the origin, a Jitter node inherits the context's camera.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemframebuffer-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/10.framebuffer.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/11.multiples_gemhead_in_a_framebuffer.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/12.floatingpoint_framebuffer.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/23.SplitScreen.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--render-any-chain-into-a-texture-with-an-explicit-size-format-and-frustum`

### Render chain: drawing state is a visible path of objects (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `gemhead`, `color`, `colorRGB`, `translateXYZ`, `rotateXYZ`, `scaleXYZ`, `square`, `cube`
- **What it does:** A shape is drawn by a chain: gemhead at the top, then objects that each change one piece of state (transform, colour, texture), then the shape. Every object changes what follows it on the cord, so the whole recipe can be read top to bottom in the patch.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `jit.gl.gridshape`, `jit.anim.node`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world, jit.gl.gridshape, jit.anim.node and the common OB3D refpage jit.group-gl.maxref.xml. In Jitter position, rotatexyz, scale, color, texture, layer and blend are attributes of each drawing object; objects join a context by name (drawto) or by sitting in the same patcher as jit.world. Nothing is wired between a transform and a shape unless a jit.anim.node is used.
- **Advice for Max:** A Jitter scene's recipe is hidden in attributes. Put the attributes that matter in the box text or on attrui boxes, and when several objects share a transform, use a named jit.anim.node wired to them so the sharing can be seen.
- **Notes:** The Gem way costs a box per state change; the Jitter way costs readability. Neither is wrong.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/manual/BasicObj.html> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemhead-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/Gem-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/01.basic/01.redSquare.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--render-chain-drawing-state-is-a-visible-path-of-objects`

### Scope for transforms: separator (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `separator`, `pix_separator`, `pix_buf`
- **What it does:** When a chain branches, a transform in one branch affects the other. separator saves the matrices when the frame reaches it and restores them when everything below has been drawn, so branches are independent. It does not restore colour. pix_separator does the same job for image chains.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.anim.node`, `jit.gl.node`, `jit.gl.sketch`
- **Checked:** Read jit.anim.node (parent and child nodes; inherit_position, inherit_rotate, inherit_scale; worldpos) and jit.gl.node (hierarchical rendering groups). Jitter objects do not leak transforms into each other, so nothing like separator is needed; shared transforms are opt-in. jit.gl.sketch has glpushmatrix and glpopmatrix messages for hand-drawn command lists.
- **Advice for Max:** Nothing to copy, but know the difference when porting a Gem patch: a Gem branch below one rotate becomes, in Jitter, several objects bound to one jit.anim.node (anim attribute) or placed in one jit.gl.node.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/separator-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_buf-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/01.Separator.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--scope-for-transforms-separator`

### Several windows, and moving a chain between them by message (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `gemwin`, `gemhead`
- **What it does:** A second argument to gemwin names a context, so two gemwin objects are two windows. A chain is assigned with the message 'context <name>' to its gemhead and can be switched to the other window while running. Sharing one texture across windows works only on some systems and backends.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `drawto`, `jit.window`, `jit.gl.texture`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world (name as first argument; shared: 'sets the window as a shared OpenGL context'), jit.window (shared) and the common OB3D refpage jit.group-gl.maxref.xml (drawto: 'the named drawing context in which to draw'). The userguide topic 'Graphics Processing' has sections on context names and offscreen contexts.
- **Advice for Max:** Name every jit.world when a patch has more than one, and set @drawto on every object; do not rely on 'same patcher' to pick the context. A texture made in one context is not automatically usable in another: turn on @shared for the windows, or render once to a texture and show it in both.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemwin-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemhead-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/14.multiple_windows/01.basic_example.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/14.multiple_windows/02.switch_context.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/14.multiple_windows/03.texture_sharing.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--several-windows-and-moving-a-chain-between-them-by-message`

### The texture upload as its own visible step (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `pix_texture`, `pix_coordinate`, `pix_multitexture`
- **What it does:** pix_texture is the point where a CPU image becomes a texture, and its messages are the texture's settings: filtering, repeat, rectangle or normalised coordinates, how it combines with the surface colour, and the texture unit. pix_coordinate sets the four texture-coordinate pairs of the shape below from one list.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.texture`, `texture`, `tex_map`, `jit.gl.mesh`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.texture (filter, wrap, rectangle default 1: 'must be referenced using sampler2DRect'; type; adapt), the common OB3D refpage jit.group-gl.maxref.xml (texture attribute, 'if multiple textures are specified, uses multi-texturing'; tex_map, tex_plane_s, tex_plane_t) and jit.gl.mesh (texcoord_matrix). jit.gl.videoplane's attribute list has no texture-coordinate setting.
- **Advice for Max:** Both systems default to rectangle textures, and both break shaders and repeating in the same way. In Jitter, put an explicit jit.gl.texture @rectangle 0 in front of anything that needs 0-to-1 coordinates or wrapping, instead of letting the object convert the matrix for you. For custom coordinates on a quad, use jit.gl.mesh with a texcoord matrix.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_texture-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_coordinate-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_multitexture-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/manual/Texture.html> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/07.texture/02.TexCoord.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/10.glsl/05.multitexture.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--the-texture-upload-as-its-own-visible-step`

### Material and effect compiled into one shader, drawn straight on the output (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `Materials`, `Surface FX`, `fxColorForPixel`, `FX_NORM_PIXEL`, `Render To Texture`, `REQUIRES_TEXTURE`, `REQUIRES_LAST_FRAME`, `mm_LastFrame`
- **What it does:** A generative material and the effect on its surface are merged into one program and drawn directly at the output's resolution, with no intermediate texture, so it stays sharp on any size of surface. A switch renders it to a fixed-size texture instead when that is cheaper or when an effect needs to sample it many times.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.shader`, `jit.gl.material`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). jit.gl.slab processes textures stage by stage; jit.gl.shader binds one shader to geometry (refpage: compiles and submits a vertex and fragment program). Whether Max can merge two separately written shaders automatically was not found and not tested.
- **Source:** <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MaterialsDoc.md> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MadMapperSurfaceFxDoc.md> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin>
- id: `madmapper--material-and-effect-compiled-into-one-shader-drawn-straight-on-the-output`

### Font outlines and SVG paths as geometry, and variable-font animation (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Vector media: Lottie, SVG, font outlines
- **There:** `Ops.Extension.OpenType.OpentypeFont`, `Ops.Extension.OpenType.OpentypeToSvgPath`, `Ops.Graphics.Geometry.SvgPathToGeometry_v3`, `Ops.Graphics.Geometry.Triangulate2dPath`, `Ops.Extension.FontKit.GlyphAxisMorph`, `Ops.Extension.FontKit.GlyphTransformAnim`, `Ops.Gl.Textures.TextureSVG_v2`, `SVG path points`
- **What it does:** A font's glyphs are read out as SVG path strings, and an SVG path becomes a filled 3D geometry or an array of points. The FontKit extension animates a variable font's axes (weight, width) separately for each glyph.

- **Notes:** No Max route from an SVG path or a font outline to mesh vertices was found. Variable-font axes were not found at all.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Graphics.Geometry.SvgPathToGeometry_v3> · <https://cables.gl/ops/Ops.Extension.FontKit> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Extension.FontKit.GlyphAxisMorph> · <https://cables.gl/ops/Ops.Extension.OpenType> · <https://cables.gl/docs/0_howtouse/files/files>

### GPU-compressed textures (KTX2) (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.GLTF.KtxCompression_v2`
- **What it does:** Loads a KTX2 texture file and reports which compressed formats the GPU supports. Compressed textures stay small in GPU memory.

### Pre-warming shaders and GPU uploads during loading (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader uniforms become controls
- **There:** `Ops.TimeLine.PreRender`, `Ops.TimeLine.DemoPrerender`, `Ops.Cables.LoadingStatus_v2`, `Ops.Cables.LoadingJob`
- **What it does:** PreRender draws the patch at a series of timeline times while a loading bar shows, so shaders are compiled and assets uploaded before playback starts. DemoPrerender records the moments in a run where heavy events caused frame drops and pre-renders exactly those. LoadingStatus reports asset-loading progress to the patch.
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): 'loading' and 'progress' match no name or digest. No Max object was found that reports GPU or asset loading progress or pre-renders at future times.
- **Notes:** Useful for timed pieces where the first appearance of a scene stutters. A Max patch could fake it by drawing everything once behind a cover; no built-in was found.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.TimeLine.PreRender> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.TimeLine.DemoPrerender> · <https://cables.gl/ops/Ops.Cables>

### Paint mode: a window that is not cleared, drawn only on demand (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Bypass and stopping unused work
- **There:** `gemwin`, `gemhead`, `depth`
- **What it does:** With 'buffer 1' the window is single-buffered and is not cleared between frames, and a chain draws only when its gemhead receives a bang. Moving shapes then leave trails, which the examples use for video painting and tablet drawing.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `jit.gl.render`, `jit.gl.node`, `jit.gl.texture`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world (erase_color is 'background and clear color as RGBA'; bang / draw 'should only be called if enable is 0') and jit.gl.render (erase message) and jit.gl.texture (autoclear attribute, erase_color). No refpage read states how to keep the previous frame; the repo's forum notes do it with texture feedback.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/gemwin-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/depth-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.video/01.VideoPaint.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/08.io/02.Tablet.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/07.Snapshot1.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--paint-mode-a-window-that-is-not-cleared-drawn-only-on-demand`

### Multiple blobs with size, bounding box and orientation, and stable IDs (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `pix_multiblob`, `pix_blobtracker`
- **What it does:** pix_multiblob finds up to N bright regions and reports for each a weighted centre, size, bounding box and the angle of its main axis. pix_blobtracker, an abstraction on top, matches blobs between frames so each keeps its number.
- **Closest in Max:** `cv.jit.label`, `cv.jit.blobs.centroids`, `cv.jit.blobs.bounds`, `cv.jit.blobs.direction`, `cv.jit.touches`
- **Checked:** Core Jitter has no blob object in any refpage read. The installed cv.jit package covers it in pieces: cv.jit.label, then cv.jit.blobs.centroids / bounds / direction, and cv.jit.touches for persistent IDs (package library entries read).
- **Notes:** pix_blobtracker needs the iemmatrix library, which was not checked for in plugdata.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_multiblob-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_blobtracker-help.pd> · <Abstractions/Gem/pix_blobtracker.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.video/06.frame_diff_tracking.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--multiple-blobs-with-size-bounding-box-and-orientation-and-stable-ids`

### Per-pixel colour classes and adaptive local threshold (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `pix_colorclassify`, `pix_equal`, `pix_threshold_bernsen`
- **What it does:** pix_colorclassify labels every pixel as red, green, blue, yellow, black, white or unsure, and outputs an image of those labels. pix_equal marks the pixels inside a colour range as white. pix_threshold_bernsen thresholds a grey image tile by tile, using each neighbourhood's own minimum and maximum.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.chromakey`, `jit.findbounds`, `cv.jit.threshold`, `upshot_colortracking`
- **Checked:** Read jit.chromakey (keys on distance from one reference colour; 'an alternate mode outputs a matrix with the keying values') and jit.findbounds (range test). Nothing read classifies into several named colours. cv.jit.threshold (adaptive, local) and Upshot's upshot_colortracking are installed packages.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_colorclassify-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_equal-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_threshold_bernsen-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.video/08.color_classification.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--per-pixel-colour-classes-and-adaptive-local-threshold`

### Weighted centre of brightness or of one colour channel (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Random and probability
- **There:** `pix_blob`
- **What it does:** pix_blob outputs the centre of gravity of an image and a size, weighting each pixel by a chosen channel (grey, red, green, blue, alpha) or by a custom mix of channels. With pix_movement writing motion into the alpha channel it becomes a simple 'where is the movement' tracker.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.findbounds`, `jit.3m`, `cv.jit.centroids`
- **Checked:** Read jit.findbounds (minimum and maximum points holding values in a range) and jit.3m (min, mean, max per plane). Neither gives a centroid. The installed cv.jit package has cv.jit.centroids (centre of mass and mass of a one-plane image), per the package library entry.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_blob-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/23.gravity.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.video/03.movement_detection.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--weighted-centre-of-brightness-or-of-one-colour-channel`

### Change filter with a minimum-change amount, that reports its value on start (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Start-up, loading and initial values
- **There:** `Value Changed`
- **What it does:** Passes a value on only when it differs from the last output by at least a set amount, with a trigger alongside. When its Scene starts it sends the current input value once, so whatever follows begins in the right state.
- **Closest in Max:** `change`, `round`
- **Checked:** Read `change`: passes a number only if different from the stored one; its modes report increase or decrease. There is no tolerance, so a float that jitters in its last digits passes every time. `round` rounds to a multiple of a given number.
- **Advice for Max:** For noisy floats in Max, quantise before `change` (`round 0.01` then `change`), or write the dead band in `v8`. Also copy the second half: a filter that holds state should send it once at load, or the first real change is the first thing downstream ever sees.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=802 (Actors Reference: Value Changed)>
- id: `isadora--change-filter-with-a-minimum-change-amount-that-reports-its-value-on-start`

### Threshold with a dead band built in (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Compare Guarded`
- **What it does:** A threshold with separate low and high values. The output turns on above the high value and off below the low value, and nothing happens in between, so a noisy value sitting near one threshold does not chatter. It also sends a trigger for each crossing.
- **Closest in Max:** `past`, `dot.schmitt (Digital Orchestra Toolbox)`, `mo.schmitt (modulo)`, `dk.schmitt (Data Knot)`, `thresh~`
- **Checked:** Searched obj-qlookup.json for 'hyst' and 'schmitt': no core object. Read `past`: one threshold, bangs when a number rises above it, and re-arms only after a lower number or a `clear`. `thresh~` is the signal-rate version (digest only read). The package library lists `dot.schmitt` (high and low thresholds, one trigger outlet), `mo.schmitt` and `dk.schmitt`.
- **Advice for Max:** Never put a bare `>` or `past` on sensor data. Use `dot.schmitt` or `mo.schmitt` when the package is installed; otherwise write the two-threshold latch in a few lines of `v8`. A core-only abstraction for this is worth having.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=449 (Actors Reference: Compare Guarded)>
- id: `isadora--threshold-with-a-dead-band-built-in`
