- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Hold Range`, `Max Value Hold`, `Min Value Hold`, `Calc Brightness`, `Limit-Scale Value`
- **What it does:** Hold Range reports the lowest and highest value ever seen and the span between them. Calc Brightness has a measure trigger that watches a set number of frames and then rescales its output to the range it saw. Limit-Scale Value clips an input to one range and then maps it to another.
- **Closest in Max:** `peak`, `trough`, `scale`, `zmap`, `clip`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `peak` outputs a number when it exceeds the stored peak; `trough` does the opposite, and its stored value starts at 128. `scale` maps one range to another and its refpage says inputs 'are not clipped to this range'. `zmap` clips to its ranges but 'does not allow inverted scaling'.
- **Advice for Max:** The Max calibration pattern is `peak` and `trough` feeding the input-range inlets of `scale`. Two traps: `trough` starts at 128, so send it a large value first, and `scale` overshoots outside its input range, so follow it with `clip` or use `zmap` when no inversion is needed.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=518 (Actors Reference: Hold Range)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=590 (Actors Reference: Max Value Hold)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=597 (Actors Reference: Min Value Hold)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=420 (Actors Reference: Calc Brightness)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=562 (Actors Reference: Limit-Scale Value)>
- id: `isadora--running-minimum-and-maximum-and-self-calibrating-ranges`

### Smoothing and decay that run on their own clock (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Smoother`, `Decay Generator`
- **What it does:** Smoother filters a noisy number with one 0-to-1 smoothing amount and outputs at its own frequency, whether or not new input arrives. Decay Generator is a level that jumps up by a set amount on each trigger and falls at a set amount per second, giving a measure of how busy a trigger stream is.
- **Closest in Max:** `slide`, `metro`, `mo.smooth (modulo)`, `dot.aggregate.leaky (Digital Orchestra Toolbox)`
- **Checked:** Read `slide`: separate slide-up and slide-down values, and a bang 'performs the same function as float using the last input value'. It produces output only when it receives something. The package library lists `mo.smooth` (leaky integrator with up and down times in ms) and `dot.aggregate.leaky` (integrator with a leak).
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, drive `slide` from a `metro` so it keeps settling after the sensor goes quiet; without the clock it freezes part-way. For an activity meter use `dot.aggregate.leaky`, or `accum` plus a clocked subtraction.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=735 (Actors Reference: Smoother)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=465 (Actors Reference: Decay Generator)>
- id: `isadora--smoothing-and-decay-that-run-on-their-own-clock`

### Game controllers arrive with named buttons, sticks and triggers (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Gamepad`, `Left Stick X/Y`, `Triggers`, `D-Pad`
- **What it does:** PlayStation and Xbox controllers and generic USB HID devices are control sources. Their inputs are offered by name, and each mapping has the same range, filter, curve and toggle settings as any other Control.
- **Closest in Max:** `gamepad`, `hi`
- **Checked:** Read gamepad's refpage: reports button, joystick, trigger and sensor events from all connected controllers through the SDL library's mapped names, can load extra controller mappings, and sends rumble and LED colour back with 'senddevice'. The registry digest for hi calls it legacy.
- **Advice for Max:** Use `gamepad`, not `hi`, for controllers: the inputs come out already named, and it can drive rumble and the light bar, which MadMapper's page does not offer.
- **Notes:** Max is at least level here.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control>
- id: `madmapper--game-controllers-arrive-with-named-buttons-sticks-and-triggers`

### Motion and background-subtraction objects that write a mask (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `pix_movement`, `pix_movement2`, `pix_background`, `pix_diff`
- **What it does:** pix_movement compares each frame with the last and writes the change into the alpha channel. pix_movement2 uses two earlier frames and a stored background with a threshold that adapts. pix_background stores a reference frame on 'reset' and blacks out everything within a tolerance of it.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.op`, `jit.matrix`, `cv.jit.framesub`, `cv.jit.stddev`
- **Checked:** Read jit.op (per-plane operators; the object mappings file lists absdiff) and jit.matrix (thru 0 holds a reference frame). cv.jit.framesub and cv.jit.stddev are in the installed cv.jit package. No single core object stores a background and subtracts it.
- **Advice for Max:** Build it from two objects: a jit.matrix @thru 0 that is banged to store the background, and jit.op @op absdiff against the live frame, then a threshold. Give the store a visible 'capture background' button, as Gem's 'reset' message does.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_movement-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_movement2-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_background-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.video/07.bg_subtract_tracking.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--motion-and-background-subtraction-objects-that-write-a-mask`

### Image to vector outlines (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `Trace POP`, `Trace SOP`
- **What it does:** Traces the bright areas of an image into line strips or triangles, live, for drawing with lasers or extruding.
- **Closest in Max:** `xray.jit.contourmap`, `jit.gl.isosurf`
- **Checked:** Searched Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, plus the mira package registry: 1,326 names with digests) and refpage digests and descriptions (1,389 pages: max-ref, msp-ref, jit-ref, m4l-ref and the bundled packages' docs) for contour, outline, vectorize, trace: no match by meaning. packages/query_packages.py search (installed third-party packages) 'contour' found xray.jit.contourmap, which was not read.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Trace_POP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Trace_SOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--image-to-vector-outlines`

### Fiducial, AR-marker, hand-contour and hit-zone objects (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `pix_fiducialtrack`, `pix_artoolkit`, `pix_hit`, `pix_mano`, `pix_drum`
- **What it does:** Upstream Gem's extra folder has objects that find reacTable-style fiducial markers, ARToolKit markers with their 3D pose, hands entering the frame (with fingertips and valleys), and whether bright pixels fall inside user-drawn circles, rectangles or lines.
- **Closest in Max:** `cv.jit.faces`, `cv.jit.blobs.bounds`
- **Checked:** Searched the installed-package library for 'fiducial', 'marker tracking' and 'hand tracking': no hits. cv.jit has face detection and blob bounds. Max's jweb-based ML examples were not searched.
- **Notes:** These help files are NOT in the plugdata 0.9.3 copy, so the objects are probably not in plugdata either. Listed as an upstream Gem capability only.
- **Source:** <https://github.com/umlaeute/Gem/blob/HEAD/extra/pix_fiducialtrack/pix_fiducialtrack-help.pd> · <https://github.com/umlaeute/Gem/blob/HEAD/extra/pix_artoolkit/pix_artoolkit-help.pd> · <https://github.com/umlaeute/Gem/blob/HEAD/extra/pix_hit/pix_hit-help.pd> · <https://github.com/umlaeute/Gem/blob/HEAD/extra/pix_mano/pix_mano-help.pd> · <https://github.com/umlaeute/Gem/blob/HEAD/extra/pix_drum/pix_drum-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--fiducial-ar-marker-hand-contour-and-hit-zone-objects`

## system model

### Data and timing on one cord or two (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** In Max one message is both the data and the moment, and hot and cold inlets decide which inputs act. cables.gl puts data and 'run now' on two different kinds of cable. In TouchDesigner a wire carries no data at all; it only says where to fetch it.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Because a Max cord often carries a name rather than the data, the cord does not show you the content. Look at it with a probe or a display object in the path. Keep each change of kind as a visible converter object, as TouchDesigner forces you to.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--data-and-timing-on-one-cord-or-two`

### Every setting as a named parameter (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** In TouchDesigner, MadMapper, Isadora and cables.gl every setting is a named parameter with a range and a control. In Max that layer is opt-in: attributes everywhere, Parameter Mode on top for the objects that need it. Pd has none. Only TouchDesigner lets a setting be an expression, an export from a channel, or a two-way binding.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Turn on Parameter Mode for the controls an operator or a host should reach, so they gain a range, an initial value, mapping and an OSC address in one step. Max has no expressions on attributes, so a computed setting is an explicit object feeding an attrui or a message, where it can be seen.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--every-setting-as-a-named-parameter`

### How names reach other parts (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** Max and Pd names are global unless a prefix makes them local. TouchDesigner names are paths in a hierarchy, and every reference is drawn as a line. Isadora keeps each Scene sealed, with numbered channels between them. MadMapper's names form one tree that is also the OSC address of every parameter.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** A send/receive pair in Max is a link nobody can see, unlike a TouchDesigner reference. Keep such links few, name them clearly in capitals, use a cord when the two ends are close, and give every name inside a copied thing its per-copy prefix.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--how-names-reach-other-parts`

### How rendering is organised (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** Jitter joins objects to a named context and orders them with depth testing or a layer number on each object. cables.gl and Gem treat the chain as a render tree: a transform or material applies to everything under it. TouchDesigner makes rendering an operator whose output is just another image. MadMapper and Isadora draw by list order or layer number.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** For 2D layering in Jitter, set @layer on each object and turn depth testing off on those same objects, or use jit.gl.layer, whose defaults are already depth off and blending on. Max's userguide says depth testing overrides the layer value otherwise. This corrects the repo's own forum notes, which put depth_enable on jit.world.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--how-rendering-is-organised`

### Scenes and cues built in or built by hand (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** Isadora and MadMapper build scenes, cues and transitions into the program. Max, TouchDesigner, cables.gl and Pd build them out of ordinary parts. A Max theatre or installation patch has to design its own scene layer.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** A Max piece with sections needs its scene layer designed early: what each section turns on and off, how state is stored per section (pattrstorage or Snapshots), how cues are listed (qlist or coll), and how sections fade. Isadora's rule that an inactive section does nothing is worth copying: switch whole sections off, not just their outputs.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--scenes-and-cues-built-in-or-built-by-hand`

### Types on the cord (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** TouchDesigner wires join only one family and every change of kind is a visible converter node. Isadora links check types and rescale numbers on every link. cables.gl colours ports by type with a catch-all Object type. Max sends small values as atoms and big data by name, with converter objects between domains.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Because a Max cord often carries a name rather than the data, the cord does not show you the content. Look at it with a probe or a display object in the path. Keep each change of kind as a visible converter object, as TouchDesigner forces you to.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--types-on-the-cord`

### Using more cores (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** Most of these tools do the main work on one thread. TouchDesigner (Engine COMP) and Pd ([pd~]) reach for a second process. Max has separate scheduler and audio threads and can spread audio across top-level patchers or poly~ voices.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Max has no per-object cost display, so find a slow part by switching sections off one at a time while watching the CPU and frame-rate meters.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--using-more-cores`

### What a saved file keeps (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** Max and Pd save the structure of a patch, not its current values, so every Max patch needs a storage choice. TouchDesigner, MadMapper, Isadora and cables.gl save every setting's value as part of the file, and a reopened project looks as it did.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Decide how a Max patch keeps its state before building it: Snapshots, pattrstorage with autopattr, or initial values in Parameter Mode. Without one, every control reopens at its default, and the operator sees a patch that has forgotten everything.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--what-a-saved-file-keeps`

### What decides order (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** In Max, several cords from one outlet fire right to left by where their destinations sit on screen. No other tool lets position decide that: Pd uses the order the cords were made, which the patch does not show; TouchDesigner orders by dependency, cables.gl by the trigger graph, MadMapper by its lists. Isadora uses position only where several links meet one input. Only Max and Pd need trigger as the everyday way to say what happens first.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Whenever one outlet feeds more than one place and the order matters, use trigger. A patch that leans on screen position breaks when someone tidies the layout, and the same patch copied into Pd runs in a different order.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--what-decides-order`

### What makes work happen: push, pull or frame loop (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** Max pushes: an event fires and the message runs down the cords. TouchDesigner pulls: a node computes only when an output asks for it and something it depends on changed. MadMapper, cables.gl and Gem run a frame loop. Isadora pulls from the end of each chain. So in the other tools unwatched work often costs nothing, while in Max it runs until something upstream stops it.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, unused work keeps running until something upstream stops it, so stop it at the top: turn off the metro or close a gate above the heavy part, and switch whole audio sections off with mute~ or pcontrol. Put the stop above the expensive object, not below it.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--what-makes-work-happen-push-pull-or-frame-loop`

### Where errors show up (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** TouchDesigner, cables.gl and Isadora show errors on the node, and TouchDesigner repeats the mark on every component that contains it. Max and Pd print to a console that links back to the object. TouchDesigner and cables.gl also show each node's cost; Max shows only whole-patch meters.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** Since a Max error is only a console line, give script objects a status outlet and show it in the patch, the way many cables.gl ops have a 'has error' output. Missing objects and unknown attributes are mostly silent in Max, so check names before shipping.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--where-errors-show-up`

### Where the finished work runs (several)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** System model
- **What it does:** plugdata runs the whole patch, editor included, as a plug-in in any DAW, and Heavy compiles patches to C++, plug-ins and Daisy. cables.gl exports a web page. TouchDesigner has a player-only runtime. Max builds standalones, Max for Live devices and RNBO exports. MadMapper and Isadora run only in their own app, with MadMapper's hardware players as the exception.
- **Checked:** Max's side is from scans/max-gaps/max_system_model.json, written from Max's userguide and refpages.
- **Advice for Max:** When a patch must run inside a DAW other than Live, or on embedded hardware, RNBO is Max's route. Plan for its narrower object set from the start rather than porting a finished patch.
- **Notes:** System-level comparison, 2026-10-03. Each tool's side is in scans/<tool>/<tool>_system_model.json.
- **Source:** <scans/max-gaps/system_differences.md>
- id: `system--where-the-finished-work-runs`

## timeline/cueing/show control

### Cue Sheet: operator prompts that fire keys (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Cue Sheet`, `Cue Sheet Editor`, `Keyboard Watcher`
- **What it does:** Each Scene holds a list of cues with a line of text telling the operator what to wait for. Taking a cue shows the next prompt and sends that cue's key to the patch as if typed.
- **Closest in Max:** `qlist`, `coll`, `umenu`, `comment`
- **Checked:** Registry and package grep for 'cue' as above; nothing presents a prompt list to an operator. Would be built from coll or qlist plus a display.
- **Notes:** Small feature; listed because it is a ready-made operator surface.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=219> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=220> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=221>
- id: `isadora--cue-sheet-operator-prompts-that-fire-keys`

### Cue numbering with point cues and renumbering (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Cue Numbering`, `Scene Index`, `Manual`, `Automatic`, `Renumber Cues`, `Jump to Cue`, `Next Cue`, `Prev Cue`
- **What it does:** Each Scene carries a cue number separate from its position. Cues can be numbered by hand with decimals, or kept in order automatically with inserted cues getting point numbers such as 8.5. A patch or an operator button can go to a cue by number.
- **Closest in Max:** `mo.cue (modulo package)`, `upshot_cues (Upshot package)`, `qlist`, `umenu`
- **Checked:** Registry grep 'cue': none. Package search 'cue': mo.cue ('set numbered cues and trigger events') and upshot_cues ('program cues'); only their library entries were read, not their patches. qlist refpage read: sequential message list, no numbering scheme.
- **Notes:** mo.cue may cover go-to-by-number; nothing found does insert-between numbering.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=144> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=145> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=146> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=147> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=549> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=861> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=868>
- id: `isadora--cue-numbering-with-point-cues-and-renumbering`

### MIDI Show Control send and receive by named fields (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controlling projectors, cameras and show gear
- **There:** `Send MIDI Show Control`, `MIDI Show Control Watcher`
- **What it does:** Builds and decodes MIDI Show Control messages from named fields: device ID, command format, command, cue number, cue list, cue path, and timecode for timed commands. The sender's inputs change to match the command chosen.
- **Closest in Max:** `sxformat`, `sysexin`, `midiout`
- **Checked:** Registry grep 'msc', 'showcontrol': none. Package search 'show control': none. sxformat refpage read: formats system exclusive bytes from arguments. MSC is sysex, so it can be assembled by hand; no object knows the protocol.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=596> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=701> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=702>
- id: `isadora--midi-show-control-send-and-receive-by-named-fields`

### Scene list: a document as a row of independent patches, one active at a time (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Scene`, `Scene List`, `Scene Editor`, `Enter Scene Trigger`, `Enter Scene Value`, `Current Scene Number`, `Get Scene Name`
- **What it does:** A show file is a row of self-contained patches called Scenes. Activating one starts all its actors and media; leaving it stops them. The row is the running order of the show.
- **Closest in Max:** `pcontrol`, `thispatcher`, `pattrstorage`, `qlist`, `mo.cue (modulo package)`
- **Checked:** Grepped obj-qlookup.json (core registry, 1,323 names) for 'scene' and 'cue': no names. Grepped the userguide for 'scene': only GL-scene and Live-API uses. Package library search 'scene' and 'cue': mo.cue (numbered cue trigger) and upshot_cues ('program cues'), neither a container of patches. Read refpages: pcontrol opens, closes and loads patcher windows; thispatcher setactivetab switches tabs; qlist stores timed messages. None starts and stops a whole patch with its media as a unit.
- **Notes:** The single largest gap. Everything a Max user builds by hand around poly~, pcontrol, mute and loadbang is the host's job here.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=140> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=141> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=142> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=483> · <https://troikatronix.com/isadora/cueing-show-control/>
- id: `isadora--scene-list-a-document-as-a-row-of-independent-patches-one-active-at-a-time`

### Scene transitions with per-scene fade in, fade out and layering mode (Isadora)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Jump`, `Jump++`, `Jump By Name`, `Jump to Cue`, `Fade In Time`, `Fade Out Time`, `Crossfade Mode`, `Scene Intensity`
- **What it does:** Changing Scene crossfades the whole outgoing and incoming patch, video and sound together, over times stored on each Scene or given to the Jump actor. The new Scene can be added, drawn above or drawn below the old one.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.xfade`, `pattrstorage`, `line`
- **Checked:** Read refpages: jit.xfade crossfades two matrices; pattrstorage has 'fade' and 'recall' messages that interpolate stored parameter values. Neither fades two running patches against each other. No registry name contains 'fade' beyond these (jit.xfade found by grep 'xfade').
- **Notes:** Depends on the Scene model above; listed separately because the fade is the part operators use every cue.

- **What it does:** Timecode is a value type that converts itself to seconds, frames or text depending on what it is linked to, and compares correctly across frame rates. Actors read incoming MIDI Timecode, fire at a given time, and lock a movie to it by trimming speed and jumping when drift is too large.

Package library search 'timecode', 'mtc', 'ltc': nothing relevant. rtin refpage read: MIDI real-time messages (clock, start, stop), not quarter-frame timecode. transport and timepoint work in Max's own musical time.
- **Notes:** MTC quarter-frame messages could be decoded from midiin by hand. Only MTC input is documented for Isadora; no LTC.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): no object whose name contains 'timecode / mtc' in Max 9's obj-qlookup.json. Third-party packages that are not installed on this machine were not checked.

Used for a continuous music bed, an always-on sensor or lighting patch, or parts of a heavy patch that run only when needed.

### Cue grid: snapshots of chosen parameters, each with its own transition (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Scenes`, `Cues`, `Cue Bank`, `Edit`, `CUE ALL`, `Update Scene`, `Launch Quantize`, `Transition Type`, `Auto Play`, `Live mode`
- **What it does:** A scene stores everything; a cue stores only the parameters you click while in Edit mode. Starting a cue fades each stored parameter from its present value to the stored one, with a transition that can be set per parameter. Cues sit in a grid of banks, rows and columns; one item per row plays at a time, and launches can wait for the beat.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattrstorage`, `preset`, `pattr`, `autopattr`, `Snapshots`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). pattrstorage's refpage: saves and recalls presets of client objects, with 'recall' accepting a fractional preset number for interpolation, 'recallmulti' for a weighted mix, 'interp' to set each client's interpolation mode, and 'active' and 'priority' per client. Userguide topics 'presets_and_interpolation' and 'snapshots' cover the same ground. Max can store and interpolate presets; a timed fade, per-parameter fade settings, and a grid with one-per-row playback would have to be patched.
- **Notes:** pattrstorage's 'fade' message is marked deprecated in its refpage (identical to recall).
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/10.-timelines/scenes-and-cues> · <https://madmapper.com/files/06-Scenes%20and%20Cues.pdf> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control/osc-commands-and-channels-list>
- id: `madmapper--cue-grid-snapshots-of-chosen-parameters-each-with-its-own-transition`

### Keyframe any parameter by touching it while Record is on (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Keyframe timeline over any parameter
- **There:** `Timelines`, `Animate`, `Record`, `Keyframe Interpolation`, `None`, `Linear`, `Bezier`, `Auto Curve`, `Simplify`, `Quantize`, `OSC Tracks`, `MIDI Tracks`
- **What it does:** Any cue can be given a timeline. With Record on, moving a control writes a keyframe at the playhead and adds a track for that parameter if there was none. Keyframes interpolate as steps, lines, hand-shaped Bézier curves or automatic smooth curves. Tracks can also send OSC and MIDI to other equipment.
- **Closest in Max:** `mtr`, `function`, `jit.anim.path`, `jit.path`, `seq`, `detonate`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). 'timeline': no Max object (package hits are Link's link.beat and link.phasor~, and bach.roll). 'keyframe': no object. mtr (refpage: records messages and plays them back in sequence) records gestures as events, not editable curves. function (refpage: breakpoint function editor, with 'setcurve') is one curve per object. jit.path and jit.anim.path interpolate between stored points. No editor that shows many parameters as tracks on one time axis was found.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/10.-timelines/creating-and-editing-timelines> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/10.-timelines/track-types-and-inspectors> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/1.-introduction/what's-new/release-notes-v6>

### Named main and synced clocks, and a metronome that understands compound and additive time signatures (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tempo and clocks
- **There:** `clock`, `metronome`, `metronome~`, `polymetro`, `speed`
- **What it does:** clock is a master clock by name; other clock objects marked -s follow it at a ratio that can be a fraction, and stay locked when the ratio changes. metronome takes a time signature (including additive ones such as 3/4 + 3/8, grouped ones such as 15/8 in 5 groups, and non-power-of-two denominators), a beat length, and outputs bar, sub-bar, beat and sub-beat counts plus a 0-1 beat phase. speed produces an accelerando or ritardando over a number of beats.
- **Closest in Max:** `transport`, `metro`, `timepoint`, `when`, `translate`, `phasor~`, `rate~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. transport: 'Control a clock', named, with tempo, a timesig message and outlets for bars, beats, units, resolution, tempo, time signature and state. metro: tempo-relative interval, quantize and transport attributes. timepoint: bang at a transport time. when: report transport time. No reference to additive or grouped signatures was found; timesig is a single numerator/denominator.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: Max's transport is the master clock, and any number of metro objects with note-value intervals (4n, 8nt) stay locked to it; that covers clock and polymetro. What it does not have is additive or grouped signatures or a sub-bar count, so a 7/8 grouped 2+2+3 needs its own counter logic after the transport. A tempo ramp is a line object into the transport's tempo. How a transport handles a signature change in mid-bar was not read.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/clock-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/metronome-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/polymetro-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/speed-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.04-Control/14-Trigger-LFO-LFNoise/6.clock.metronome/5.[clock].[polymetro].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--named-main-and-synced-clocks-and-a-metronome-that-understands-compound-and-additive-time-signatures`

### A drawn score that is also the sequence (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `struct`, `plot`, `pointer traverse`, `sort`, `clone`
- **What it does:** The data-structure tutorial builds a score window where each event is a shape with pitch and loudness curves; the same data is walked by a pointer and played. Events are dragged, copied and re-sorted by x.
- **Closest in Max:** `function`, `live.step`, `bach.roll`, `mtr`, `seq`
- **Checked:** Preferred-objects table in CLAUDE.md (live.step, function). query_packages.py 'bach.roll': bach is installed and has a proportional score editor. No core object pairs free-form drawn shapes with user-defined fields.
- **Source:** <Documentation/4.data.structures/09.sequencer.pd> · <Documentation/4.data.structures/voice.pd> · <Documentation/4.data.structures/data-array.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm>
- id: `plugdata--a-drawn-score-that-is-also-the-sequence`

### Control-rate LFO as one object (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Wave Generator`
- **What it does:** Outputs a continuously changing number following a sine, square, triangle, sawtooth or stepped-random wave, at 0.0001 to 999 Hz. It has a phase offset, a reset trigger, and a 'once' mode that runs a single cycle per reset.
- **Closest in Max:** `phasor~ + snapshot~`, `line`, `mo.lfo~ (modulo)`, `vs_lfo (Vsynth)`, `Abl.LFO~`
- **Checked:** Searched obj-qlookup.json for 'lfo': no core object. The package library lists `mo.lfo~` (signal and data outlets), `vs_lfo` (texture and data, needs Vsynth's renderer) and `Abl.LFO~`. The core route is a signal oscillator sampled down to messages, which needs audio running; that part is from the repo's patching notes, not a refpage read this session.
- **Advice for Max:** A message-rate LFO with shape, phase, reset and one-shot is worth building as a small `v8` object, so video and lighting patches do not need DSP switched on to animate a number.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=825 (Actors Reference: Wave Generator)>
- id: `isadora--control-rate-lfo-as-one-object`

### Move toward a target at a fixed rate (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Seek Target Value`
- **What it does:** The output moves continuously toward whatever target value it is given, at a set amount per second, and stops when it gets there. A new target simply changes where it is heading.
- **Closest in Max:** `line`, `slide`, `rampsmooth~`
- **Checked:** Searched obj-qlookup.json for 'slew': nothing at message rate. Read `slide`: it filters each incoming value by a formula applied per input, so it moves only when a value or a bang arrives and its speed depends on how often that happens. `line` reaches a target in a given time, not at a given rate.
- **Advice for Max:** For constant-speed motion in Max, read the current value, compute time = distance / rate, and send target and time to `line`. Do not use `slide` for anything that must take a known time. A small 'seek' abstraction around `line` is worth keeping.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=697 (Actors Reference: Seek Target Value)>
- id: `isadora--move-toward-a-target-at-a-fixed-rate`

### Stopwatch with run, pause and reset (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Timer`
- **What it does:** Outputs elapsed time continuously. Three triggers run it, pause it and reset it to zero; after a pause, run carries on from the paused time.
- **Closest in Max:** `timer`, `clocker`, `cpuclock`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `timer` reports the time between a bang in its left inlet and a bang in its right. `clocker` reports elapsed time at an interval, with start, `stop` and `reset`; a bang while stopped starts the count and a bang while running resets it. Neither has a pause that keeps the elapsed time.
- **Advice for Max:** A pausable show clock in Max needs an accumulator: add each `clocker` run to an `accum` when it stops, or keep the start time and the paused total in `v8`. Worth a small reusable object, since every show patch wants one.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=789 (Actors Reference: Timer)>
- id: `isadora--stopwatch-with-run-pause-and-reset`

### Tap tempo with averaging (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tempo and clocks
- **There:** `Tap Tempo`, `Loop Calculator`
- **What it does:** Tap Tempo measures the rate of incoming triggers and outputs it in beats per minute and in Hz, averaged over a chosen number of taps. Loop Calculator turns tapped tempo into a loop length for a given movie or sound.
- **Closest in Max:** `timer`, `tempo`, `upshot_transport (Upshot)`
- **Checked:** Searched obj-qlookup.json for 'tap': only delay-line taps. `tempo` generates a metronome from a BPM; it does not measure one (digest read). `timer` gives the time between two bangs. The package library lists `upshot_transport` with a tap-tempo inlet.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max build it from `timer`: feed one bang to both inlets through `t b b`, average the last few intervals, and take 60000 divided by the mean. Worth keeping as an abstraction with the tap count as an argument.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=764 (Actors Reference: Tap Tempo)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=571 (Actors Reference: Loop Calculator)>
- id: `isadora--tap-tempo-with-averaging`

### A preset shows when the live values no longer match what it stored (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Presets and saved state
- **There:** `Update Scene`, `Update Cue`, `Update from current values`, `Record`, `Edit`
- **What it does:** After a cue is recalled and then changed by hand, the changed controls are outlined, so the operator can see the cue is no longer what is on stage. One command writes the live values back into the cue, and several cues can be updated at once.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattrstorage (getedited, getcurrent, storeagain, lock)`
- **Checked:** Read pattrstorage's refpage: 'getedited' reports 1 when the data of the current preset has been edited, 'getcurrent' reports the active preset, 'storeagain' stores into the last-used slot, and 'lock' protects a slot from being overwritten. Nothing marks which control changed.
- **Advice for Max:** Poll `getedited` after any change and light one indicator beside the preset control; put an 'Update' button on `storeagain`. Lock finished presets so a stray store cannot overwrite them.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/10.-timelines/scenes-and-cues> · <https://madmapper.com/files/06-Scenes%20and%20Cues.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--a-preset-shows-when-the-live-values-no-longer-match-what-it-stored`

### Easing shape as a menu choice (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Interpolate CHOP`, `Pulse CHOP`, `Timer CHOP`, `S Curve CHOP`, `Join CHOP`
- **What it does:** Operators that move between values offer the shape of the move as a menu: linear, ease in and out, cubic and others. The timer can ease its custom channels between segments in the same way.
- **Closest in Max:** `line`, `curve~`, `function`, `jit.anim.drive`, `butter_ease.js (Butter_tools)`
- **Checked:** line refpage: linear ramps only. curve~ refpage: exponential ramps, signal only. function refpage: per-segment curve factor in Curve mode. jit.anim.drive refpage: easefunc linear, quadratic or cubic, for 3D transforms only. Package search for 'easing' found a demo patch in Max for the Visual Arts and nothing reusable. Butter_tools/javascript/butter_ease.js exists (file listed, not read this session).
- **Advice for Max:** Run a 0-to-1 line through an easing function (butter_ease.js, or function used as a lookup) and scale afterwards. A butter.ease object, a line with an @ease menu, is worth building.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Interpolate_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pulse_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Timer_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/S_Curve_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Join_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--easing-shape-as-a-menu-choice`

### Lag with separate rise and fall, overshoot and speed limits (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Lag CHOP`
- **What it does:** One operator slows changes with different times for rising and falling, can overshoot the target, and can cap the speed and the acceleration of a value. It can treat several channels as one vector so a point moves along a straight line.
- **Closest in Max:** `slide`, `slide~`, `rampsmooth~`, `deltaclip~`
- **Checked:** slide refpage: logarithmic smoothing with slide-up and slide-down arguments. rampsmooth~ refpage: linear ramps of a set number of samples up and down. deltaclip~ refpage: limits the change between samples, a speed limit for signals. None has overshoot, an acceleration limit or a vector mode. No lag object in obj-qlookup.json.
- **Advice for Max:** Use slide for rise and fall smoothing of a number and deltaclip~ for a speed limit on a signal. Overshoot and acceleration limits need gen~ or v8; a butter.lag with those four settings is worth building.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Lag_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--lag-with-separate-rise-and-fall-overshoot-and-speed-limits`

### One control interface for every time-based component (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `Palette:initializeStart`
- **What it does:** A template gives any time-based component the same controls (Initialize, Start, Play, Speed, Cue, go to done) and the same output channels (ready, running, done, fraction). The page explains three ways to install it into your own component.
- **Closest in Max:** `transport`, `jit.movie`, `playlist~`, `mtr`, `jit.anim.path`
- **Checked:** Refpages: jit.movie uses start, stop, rate, position; playlist~ uses a clip number, pause, resume; mtr uses play, stop, rewind, speed; jit.anim.path uses start, stop, play, rate; transport uses its own state. Each object has its own verbs.
- **Advice for Max:** Adopt one convention for your own abstractions and Butter tools: messages init, start, play 0/1, speed and cue, and outlets for ready, running, done and fraction. A patch that mixes five players then reads the same way everywhere.
- **Notes:** Session 1's 'Timing engine with segments, states and callbacks' covers the Timer CHOP itself.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:initializeStart> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Timer_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--one-control-interface-for-every-time-based-component`

### Spring follower for any value (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Spring CHOP`
- **What it does:** A value follows its input as if on a spring, with settings for spring constant, mass and damping. The input can be a target position or a force.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.anim.drive`
- **Checked:** jit.anim.drive refpage: springto moves a 3D object with a mass-spring simulation (spring_stiff, spring_mass, spring_damp), for transforms only. A name search of obj-qlookup.json for 'spring' found only jit.phys objects; package search for 'spring' found nothing relevant.
- **Advice for Max:** For a 3D object use jit.anim.drive springto. For a plain number, a mass-spring step is a few lines in gen or v8, and is worth packaging once as a Butter tool.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Spring_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--spring-follower-for-any-value`

### Easing choice built into mapping and animation ops (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Ops.Math.MapRange`, `Ops.Math.Ease`, `Ops.Anim.AnimNumber`, `Ops.Anim.SimpleAnim`, `Ops.Anim.Crossfade`, `Ops.Array.EaseArray`, `Ops.Math.SchlickBias`
- **What it does:** An Easing dropdown sits on the range-mapping op and on nearly every animation op: AnimNumber, SimpleAnim, BoolAnim, Crossfade, RandomAnim, DelayedNumber, AnimArray, MeshMorph. Ease and EaseArray apply a curve to a number or an array. The Ease page points to easings.net for the shapes.
- **Closest in Max:** `line`, `scale`, `function`, `ease (ease package)`, `jit.anim.drive @easefunc`
- **Checked:** line refpage: 'Generate ramps and line segments'; attributes compatmode, floatoutput, grain, maxpoints; no curve. scale refpage: a sixth inlet for an exponential base only. function refpage: a curve mode whose output suits curve~. jit.anim.drive refpage: easefunc, easein, easeout, for 3D transforms. The ease package (installed in ~/Documents/Max 9/Packages, not in the base registry) has ease, list.ease, ease~, jit.ease and ease.xfade, each with function, input_range and output_range attributes.
- **Advice for Max:** For an eased move in Max, run a plain line from 0 to 1 and pass it through [ease] from the ease package, then scale to the range; do not approximate it with scale's exponent. A wrapper abstraction 'line with an easing argument' would save that three-box chain everywhere.
- **Notes:** The ease package comes from Cycling '74 through the Package Manager. A patch that uses it needs it installed.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.MapRange> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.Ease> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.AnimNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.SimpleAnim> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.BoolAnim> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.Crossfade> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.DelayedNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.AnimArray_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.EaseArray> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.SchlickBias>
- id: `cables-gl--easing-choice-built-into-mapping-and-animation-ops`

### Follow-the-input animation ops: ease to value, smooth, spring (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Ops.Anim.AnimNumber`, `Ops.Anim.Smooth`, `Ops.Anim.Spring`, `Ops.Anim.BoolAnim`
- **What it does:** AnimNumber glides to whatever number arrives, over a set duration. Smooth filters jumps, with separate factors for rising and falling. Spring chases the target with damping and stiffness. BoolAnim turns a true/false into an eased move between two values.
- **Closest in Max:** `slide`, `line`, `rampsmooth~`, `jit.anim.drive springto`
- **Checked:** slide refpage: 'Smooth values logarithmically', with slide-up and slide-down inlets, which matches Smooth. line refpage: needs a target and a time in one message, so following an input takes a message box in front. C74/interfaces/obj-qlookup.json: no object name contains 'spring'. jit.anim.drive refpage: springto with spring_stiff, spring_damp and spring_mass, for 3D transforms only. packages/query_packages.py search 'spring' and 'easing' returned nothing usable for a plain number.
- **Advice for Max:** Use slide for smoothing with different rise and fall, and a '$1 200' message box into line for glide-to-value. Max has no spring for a plain number, so a small v8 or gen object with stiffness and damping, driven by the frame bang, is worth building.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.AnimNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.Smooth> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.Spring> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.BoolAnim>

### Clock that pauses in place and deals its ticks round-robin (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Tempo and clocks
- **There:** `Pulse Generator`, `Sequential Trigger`, `Trigger Divider`
- **What it does:** Pulse Generator fires at a frequency in Hz. Turning 'run/stop' off pauses it and turning it on continues from where it was in the cycle; 'reset' starts the cycle again. With several outputs it fires them in turn. Sequential Trigger and Trigger Divider do the round-robin and the divide-by-N on any trigger stream.
- **Closest in Max:** `metro`, `cycle`, `counter`, `tempo`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `metro` takes an interval in milliseconds or a note value, starts with a bang or non-zero number and stops with 0 or `stop`; there is an `active` attribute but no pause that keeps the position inside the interval. `cycle` sends each bang or number to the next outlet in turn, with `set` to choose the next outlet. `counter` counts bangs within a range.
- **Advice for Max:** Round-robin triggers are `metro` into `cycle N`; a divider is `counter` into `select`. Note that Max thinks in milliseconds per tick and Isadora in ticks per second. If a pulse must resume mid-interval after a pause, Max needs a `v8` or signal-rate clock; `metro` always starts a fresh interval.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=655 (Actors Reference: Pulse Generator)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=715 (Actors Reference: Sequential Trigger)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=792 (Actors Reference: Trigger Divider)>
- id: `isadora--clock-that-pauses-in-place-and-deals-its-ticks-round-robin`

### Eased ramp with a speed limit, pause and a 'done' trigger (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Ease In-Out`, `Ease In-Out 2D`, `Curvature`
- **What it does:** Ramps from a start to an end value over a duration along an s-curve whose steepness is one input. A 'max delta' input caps the rate of change, stretching the ramp when needed. It sends a trigger on completion; the 2D version ramps a point, with pause, resume and a 'moving' flag. Curvature bends any value along a two-control-point curve drawn on the actor.
- **Closest in Max:** `line`, `ease (ease package)`, `function`, `butter_ease.js (Butter_tools)`
- **Checked:** Read `line` (linear ramps, pause and resume) and the installed ease package's `ease.maxref.xml`: `ease` applies a chosen easing function to an incoming float, with `input_range` and `output_range` attributes. It shapes a value; it does not generate the ramp. `function` has per-segment curve values (`setcurve`, `xyc`).
- **Advice for Max:** Keep the two jobs apart in Max: `line` makes a linear 0-to-1 ramp, `ease` shapes it. That is more flexible than Isadora's single actor. What Max lacks is the rate cap: work out the time as distance divided by maximum speed before sending the pair to `line`.
- **Notes:** `ease` is a third-party package on this machine, not part of Max.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=474 (Actors Reference: Ease In-Out)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=475 (Actors Reference: Ease In-Out 2D)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=456 (Actors Reference: Curvature)>
- id: `isadora--eased-ramp-with-a-speed-limit-pause-and-a-done-trigger`

### Multi-segment envelope that can advance one segment per trigger (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `Envelope Generator`, `Envelope Generator++`
- **What it does:** A ramp of up to nine segments (99 in the ++ version), each with a time and a target value. A trigger mode chooses between running the whole envelope per trigger and running one segment per trigger, with an end trigger either per segment or at the end.
- **Closest in Max:** `line`, `function`, `bline`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `line` takes a list of target-time pairs and has `pause`, `resume` and `stop`. `function` is a drawn breakpoint editor that outputs a list for `line`; breakpoints can be marked as sustain points, a bang plays up to the first sustain point and `next` continues from it. `bline` advances a ramp one step per bang.
- **Advice for Max:** The Max match for 'one segment per trigger' is `function` with sustain points: bang to start, `next` on each later cue. The envelope is then drawn, not typed into eighteen inputs, which is the better editing surface. Use it for cue-stepped fades.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=484 (Actors Reference: Envelope Generator)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=485 (Actors Reference: Envelope Generator++)>
- id: `isadora--multi-segment-envelope-that-can-advance-one-segment-per-trigger`

### Named actors for 'nothing happened', 'too many at once' and 'all together' (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Trigger Delay`, `Multi Blocker`, `Simultaneity`, `Pulse Values`
- **What it does:** Trigger Delay restarts its wait on every new trigger, so it fires only after triggers stop arriving. Multi Blocker passes the first value and drops any that follow within a set time. Simultaneity fires once when all of its trigger inputs have fired inside a time window.
- **Closest in Max:** `delay`, `speedlim`, `onebang`, `buddy`, `thresh`, `quickthresh`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `delay` holds a bang for a time and has `stop`; its refpage does not say what a second bang does while one is pending, and its help file has no comment on it either. `speedlim` passes a message only after a minimum time since the last one, with a `usurp` option to keep the newest. `buddy` waits until every inlet has received something, with no time limit. `thresh` and `quickthresh` collect numbers that arrive close together into one list.
- **Advice for Max:** The nearest thing to Multi Blocker is `speedlim`; check in the patch whether a held message is dropped or sent late, since Multi Blocker drops. For 'all inputs within N ms' Max has no single object: `buddy` never times out, so add a `delay` that sends it `clear`, or do it in `v8`. For 'no input for N ms', test what `delay` does on a second bang in the patch itself before relying on it, since the refpage is silent.
- **Notes:** The `delay` re-trigger behaviour is the one thing here not confirmed from Max's own docs.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=791 (Actors Reference: Trigger Delay)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=610 (Actors Reference: Multi Blocker)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=727 (Actors Reference: Simultaneity)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=656 (Actors Reference: Pulse Values)>
- id: `isadora--named-actors-for-nothing-happened-too-many-at-once-and-all-together`

### An LFO is a ready-made module: shape, free or tempo-synced, speed, phase, range, and a bank of ten with spread (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Oscillator`, `Oscillator 2D`, `Oscillator Bank`, `Signal`, `Timing`, `Phase`, `Offset`
- **What it does:** An Oscillator module outputs a value between 0 and 1 with a chosen wave shape, running at a set speed or locked to the tempo, with phase and range. The 2D version gives two related values; the Bank gives ten whose phases are spread by one Offset control. Any parameter can take one as its control.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.mo.time (jit.mo)`, `jit.mo.func (jit.mo)`
- **Checked:** Read jit.mo.time's refpage (bundled jit.mo package): in 'mode function' it outputs line, sin, saw or perlin values in step with the render context, with 'freq', 'phase', 'speed', 'scale', 'offset', and an 'interval' in Max time format so it can be tempo-relative. Read jit.mo.func's refpage: the same functions spread across the cells of a matrix, with 'freq' and 'phase', which gives many phase-offset values at once.
- **Advice for Max:** For values that drive visuals, use jit.mo.time @mode function; it ticks with the render, so there is no metro to keep in step. Its `interval` takes Max time values, so a note value locks it to the transport's tempo. For a spread of related values, read them from one jit.mo.func matrix.
- **Source:** <https://madmapper.com/files/09-Modules.pdf> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/8.-modules>
- id: `madmapper--an-lfo-is-a-ready-made-module-shape-free-or-tempo-synced-speed-phase-range-and-a-bank-of-ten-with-spread`

### One master speed and one freeze act on everything that moves (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Master Speed`, `Freeze Engine`, `Freeze Video Output`, `engine_speed`, `CLOCK_TIME`
- **What it does:** Master Settings has a speed that scales every animated thing at once, shaders and audio included, a Freeze Engine that stops it all, and a Freeze Video Output that holds the picture while playback carries on underneath. Shaders that must ignore the speed read wall-clock time instead.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.mo.time (jit.mo)`, `transport`, `jit.world (enable)`
- **Checked:** Read jit.mo.time's refpage (bundled jit.mo package): outputs accumulated time, frame delta or a function, in step with jit.world, with a 'speed' multiplier and an 'interval' in Max time format. transport's refpage: one tempo for everything tempo-relative. jit.world's 'enable' stops all rendering. Nothing found scales every animation in a patch at once.
- **Advice for Max:** Give a patch one time source: a single jit.mo.time (or the transport) whose output every shader and animation reads, with one speed control in front of it. Then 'slow everything down' and 'freeze' are one control each instead of a hunt through the patch.
- **Notes:** How to hold the last rendered frame in Max while the scene keeps running was not looked up.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/9.-master-settings> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control/osc-commands-and-channels-list> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf> · <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/madmappersoftware/MadMapper-Materials/HEAD/MaterialsDoc.md>
- id: `madmapper--one-master-speed-and-one-freeze-act-on-everything-that-moves`

### Presets leave playing media alone unless told to restart it (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Presets and saved state
- **There:** `Scenes`, `Cues`, `Cue Inspector`, `Replay`
- **What it does:** A Scene stores everything and so tends to restart media when recalled; the manual warns about this and steers operators to Cues, which store only chosen parameters. The FAQ's fix for audio that restarts on a cue is to untick the player's Replay value inside that cue.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattrstorage (active, subscribemode, subscribe, priority)`
- **Checked:** Read pattrstorage's refpage: 'active <client> 0' leaves a client out of every recall; 'subscribemode' with 'subscribe' makes the object manage only a listed set of clients; 'priority' sets the order clients are restored in.
- **Advice for Max:** Decide which controls are state and which are actions. Leave play, restart and file-load controls out of pattrstorage (`active <name> 0`, or `@subscribemode 1` with an explicit list), or every preset recall will restart the clips. Use one pattrstorage per concern (looks, media, mix) the way MadMapper uses one row per concern.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/10.-timelines/scenes-and-cues> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--presets-leave-playing-media-alone-unless-told-to-restart-it`

### A units menu beside every time value (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Lag CHOP`, `Filter CHOP`, `Timer CHOP`, `Movie File In TOP`
- **What it does:** Almost every time setting has a menu beside it for samples, frames or seconds, and movie and timer positions add a fraction or index unit. The same operator can therefore be set in frames for a picture and in seconds for a cue.
- **Closest in Max:** `Time Value Syntax`, `metro`, `translate`, `transport`
- **Checked:** Userguide 'Time Value Syntax': fixed units ms, hh:mm:ss, samples and hz, and tempo-relative units ticks, bars.beats.units and note values. metro refpage: interval in that syntax, quantize, transport. translate refpage: converts between the time formats. No frames unit is listed.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's time values are tempo-aware where TouchDesigner's are frame-aware. For anything musical write note values and use a transport. For anything counted in frames, count jit.world draw bangs, because Max has no frames unit.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Lag_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Filter_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Timer_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Movie_File_In_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Frame>
- id: `touchdesigner--a-units-menu-beside-every-time-value`

### Envelope from an event, with shaped segments (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `Trigger CHOP`
- **What it does:** A pulse starts a delay, attack, peak, decay, sustain and release envelope. Each segment has a length and a shape, and several overlapping envelopes can be added together.
- **Closest in Max:** `function`, `line`, `curve~`, `adsr~`
- **Checked:** function refpage: breakpoint editor with sustain points; next continues from a sustain point; mode Curve adds a curve factor per segment and its output then suits curve~. adsr~ refpage: signal envelope with legato, retrigger and triggermode. line refpage: takes the list function produces.
- **Advice for Max:** function into line (jit.line for a picture) gives an editable envelope with sustain through the next message. Use adsr~ when the envelope must be a signal.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Trigger_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--envelope-from-an-event-with-shaped-segments`

### Integrate a rate, differentiate a position (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Speed CHOP`, `Slope CHOP`
- **What it does:** One operator adds up a rate over time to give a position, with reset and limits. Its partner gives the rate of change of a value. Together they let you edit speed and get position back.
- **Closest in Max:** `accum`, `jit.mo.time`, `jit.anim.drive`, `delta~`
- **Checked:** accum refpage: stores a number and adds or multiplies into it. jit.mo.time refpage: mode accum outputs accumulated running time, with a speed attribute. jit.anim.drive refpage: move, turn and grow apply relative motion over time. delta~ refpage: sample-to-sample difference of a signal.
- **Advice for Max:** Drive continuous motion from a rate that is accumulated every frame (jit.anim.drive turn, or jit.mo.time accum with speed), so changing the speed never makes the position jump. A ramp to an absolute value does jump.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Speed_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Slope_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--integrate-a-rate-differentiate-a-position`

### Recording control gestures (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Record CHOP`, `Trail CHOP`
- **What it does:** One operator records incoming channels while an Active input is on and then outputs the recording. Another shows the recent history of any channel as a scrolling graph.
- **Closest in Max:** `mtr`, `seq`, `capture`, `multislider`
- **Checked:** mtr refpage: records and plays back messages on tracks; bindto ties a track to a UI object by scripting name with no cords; sync follows a transport; loop, speed, write and read. seq refpage: MIDI. multislider refpage: 'Displays data as either an array of sliders or a scrolling display'. capture refpage: stores values for viewing.
- **Advice for Max:** Max is strong here: mtr @bindto records a control and plays the gesture back into it without a single cord. For a history graph, set a multislider to its scrolling display.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Record_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Trail_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--recording-control-gestures`

### Smooth, seedable noise as a control source (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Many channels from one generator
- **There:** `Noise CHOP`
- **What it does:** One operator makes a continuous irregular curve that never repeats. Settings include type (smooth, Brownian, white and others), seed, period, harmonics and roughness. It keeps running across timeline loops and can be sampled at any point of a 3D noise space.
- **Closest in Max:** `random`, `drunk`, `rand~`, `jit.mo.time`, `jit.mo.func`, `jit.bfg`
- **Checked:** random refpage: attributes seed, range, cycle. drunk refpage: random walk with seed and stepsize. rand~ refpage: band-limited random signal that interpolates between values at a set frequency. jit.mo.time refpage: mode function with function perlin gives a Perlin-noise value in step with the render. jit.mo.func refpage: functions line, sin, saw, tri, perlin. jit.bfg refpage: basis functions in any number of dimensions.
- **Advice for Max:** For organic drift use jit.mo.time @mode function @function perlin (frame-synced) or rand~ into snapshot~, not random into line. Set @seed on random or drunk when a show has to repeat exactly.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Noise_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--smooth-seedable-noise-as-a-control-source`

### Smoothing a whole list at once (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Easing, smoothing and ramps
- **There:** `Filter CHOP`, `Filter per Sample`
- **What it does:** One filter smooths every channel, and with a per-sample option every element of a long channel gets its own filter. Filter shapes include Gaussian, box, spike removal and one that keeps ramps. The width is given in samples, frames or seconds.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.slide`, `jit.fill`, `jit.spill`, `mc.slide~`, `mc.line~`, `zl.median`
- **Checked:** slide refpage: one number at a time. jit.slide refpage: cell-by-cell smoothing with slide_up and slide_down. jit.fill and jit.spill refpages: list into a matrix plane and back. mc.slide~, mc.rampsmooth~ and mc.line~ are in obj-qlookup.json as multichannel versions. zl.median refpage: median of a list.
- **Advice for Max:** To smooth a list, do not unpack it into N slide objects. Write it into a matrix (jit.fill), run jit.slide, and read it back (jit.spill), or at signal rate send it through one mc.line~ or mc.slide~. For spikes, collect a short history with zl.stream and take zl.median.
- **Notes:** The jit.fill → jit.slide → jit.spill chain is assembled from the refpages and has not been run in Max.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Filter_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Filter_per_Sample>
- id: `touchdesigner--smoothing-a-whole-list-at-once`

### Thresholds with a release level and a re-trigger delay (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Count CHOP`, `Trigger CHOP`, `Logic CHOP`
- **What it does:** Trigger operators take a trigger threshold, a separate release threshold, a re-trigger delay and a minimum trigger length as plain settings. A logic operator turns a value into on/off by bounds and offers rising edge, falling edge, toggle and radio-button behaviour from a menu.
- **Closest in Max:** `thresh~`, `past`, `togedge`, `edge~`, `onebang`, `speedlim`
- **Checked:** thresh~ refpage: low (reset) and high (set) thresholds for a signal. past refpage: bangs when a number rises above a value. togedge and edge~ refpages: bangs on zero / non-zero transitions. onebang and speedlim refpages: gate or rate-limit repeated events. Package search: Digital Orchestra Toolbox dot.schmitt~ (digest: 'Detect triggers from envelope of a signal') and CNMAT 2threshattack~ (a two-threshold attack detector).
- **Advice for Max:** For a signal use thresh~. At message rate build the two thresholds explicitly and add speedlim or onebang as the re-trigger guard. Never put a bare `>` on a noisy sensor.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Count_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Trigger_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Logic_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--thresholds-with-a-release-level-and-a-re-trigger-delay`

### Finished trigger on every animation op (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Ops.Anim.AnimNumber`, `Ops.Anim.SimpleAnim`, `Ops.Anim.BoolAnim`, `Ops.Anim.InOutInAnim`, `Ops.Boolean.MonoFlop`
- **What it does:** The op guidelines ask every animation op for a finished flag, and put a finished trigger to the right of the normal trigger output. InOutInAnim also has Started and Middle triggers. MonoFlop has Activated and Ended. Steps chain by wiring one op's finished trigger to the next op's start.
- **Closest in Max:** `line (right outlet)`, `bline`, `jit.anim.path @endreport`, `jit.anim.drive @evalreport`, `delay`
- **Checked:** line refpage: outlet 1 'Signals End of Ramp'. bline refpage: outlet 1 bangs when it reaches the destination. jit.anim.path refpage: endreport and loopreport attributes. jit.anim.drive refpage: evalreport, off by default, reports progress of moveto, rotateto and scaleto out the dumpout.
- **Advice for Max:** Chain Max animation steps from the 'done' outlet of the step before (line's right outlet), not from a delay set to the same number of milliseconds. On the Jitter animation objects the report is off by default, so switch on @endreport or @evalreport first.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.AnimNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.SimpleAnim> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.BoolAnim> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.InOutInAnim> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.RandomAnim_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Boolean.MonoFlop> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/guidelines/guidelines>
- id: `cables-gl--finished-trigger-on-every-animation-op`

### LFO and timer ops for control values (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `Ops.Anim.LFO_v3`, `Ops.Anim.SineAnim`, `Ops.Anim.Timer_v2`, `Ops.Anim.TimeDelta`
- **What it does:** LFO takes a time value and gives a wave by type, frequency, phase and output range. Timer is a clock with play, reset and a speed multiplier, and can follow the timeline. TimeDelta gives the time since the last frame.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.mo.time`, `clocker`, `timer`, `cycle~ with snapshot~`
- **Checked:** C74/interfaces/obj-qlookup.json: no object name contains 'lfo'. jit.mo.time refpage (jit.mo package, shipped in Max): modes accum, function and delta; in function mode the function attribute is line, sin, saw or perlin, with freq, phase, scale, offset and speed; the page says it makes 'float LFOs and ramps in sync with the animation graph'. clocker and timer refpages: elapsed time in milliseconds.
- **Advice for Max:** For a control-rate LFO in a Jitter patch use jit.mo.time @mode function, which is frame-locked like the cables op. Its delta mode is the Max answer to TimeDelta, and is what keeps motion speed the same when the frame rate changes.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.LFO_v3> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.SineAnim> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.Timer_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.TimeDelta>
- id: `cables-gl--lfo-and-timer-ops-for-control-values`

### Switching scenes by routing the render trigger (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `Ops.Trigger.TimedSequence`, `Ops.Trigger.RouteTrigger_v2`, `Ops.Trigger.RouteTriggerAnimated`, `Ops.Trigger.SwitchTrigger`, `Ops.Anim.Bang`
- **What it does:** A scene is a branch, and a scene switch is an op that sends the frame trigger to one branch by index. An unselected branch is not run at all. TimedSequence also has an always-on output, and its page says to pair it with the Bang animation op for animated transitions between scenes.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.node @enable`, `enable (OB3D attribute)`, `jit.gl.node @capture`, `jit.fx.tr.xfade`
- **Checked:** jit.group-gl refpage: every GL object has an enable flag; drawing stops when it is 0. jit.gl.node refpage discussion: a node can overwrite standard OB3D attributes on its children, and with @capture 1 it outputs the scene as a texture. jit.fx.tr.xfade refpage: a cross fade between textures; the jit.fx.tr.* family has other transitions.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max make each scene a jit.gl.node. Switch by sending enable 0 or 1 to the nodes, and for a dissolve capture both nodes to textures and cross-fade them with a jit.fx.tr.* object, disabling the old node when the fade's done-signal arrives.
- **Notes:** That enable set on a jit.gl.node stops all its children was inferred from the refpage discussion, not tested.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.TimedSequence> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.RouteTrigger_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.RouteTriggerAnimated> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.SwitchTrigger> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.Bang>
- id: `cables-gl--switching-scenes-by-routing-the-render-trigger`

### mtr: what the clone leaves out shows what Max's mtr can do (plugdata (cyclone))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Polyphony and voices
- **There:** `mtr`
- **What it does:** cyclone's help file lists the Max 7 mtr features it has not implemented: event editing messages (addevent, cleareventat, deleteeventat), playat and playatms, touch recording, definelengthandstop, dictionary and JSON output, and every transport-related attribute (transport, sync, bindto, autostart, autostarttime, quantize), plus length, selection, mode and nextmode.
- **Closest in Max:** `mtr`
- **Checked:** mtr refpage attributes: autostart, autostarttime, bindto, embed, length, loop, mode, nextmode, quantize, selection, speed, sync, trackspeed, transport. All the attributes cyclone names are present in Max 9.1.5.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's mtr is a transport-aware, quantizing, loopable message recorder, not just the simple recorder older patches use it as. Before building a looper for control data, read its refpage: sync, quantize, length and loop cover most of it.
- **Source:** <Documentation/10.cyclone/mtr-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--mtr-what-the-clone-leaves-out-shows-what-max-s-mtr-can-do`

### seq with pause, continue and a text editor (plugdata (cyclone))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `seq`
- **What it does:** cyclone's seq adds 'pause' and 'continue' messages, opens an editor window on click that shows the sequence as lines of time and raw MIDI bytes, and reads and writes that same text format as .txt files. It lacks Max's 'dump', and still merges all tracks when writing.
- **Closest in Max:** `seq`, `mtr`, `detonate`
- **Checked:** seq refpage method list: bang, int, float, addeventdelay, append, clear, delay, dump, hook, print, read, record, start, stop, tick, write. No pause or continue. Attributes: overridetempo, sequencetempo, tempo.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's seq cannot pause. To pause and resume, run it from an external clock: 'start -1' and feed 'tick' messages (24 per quarter note), then stop and restart the ticks. That is the mechanism cyclone's help file documents for tempo control, and it gives pause for free.
- **Notes:** That 'start -1' plus tick works this way in Max is taken from cyclone's description of the cloned behaviour and the presence of 'tick' in Max's method list.
- **Source:** <Documentation/10.cyclone/seq-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--seq-with-pause-continue-and-a-text-editor`

## video I/O & playback

- **There:** `RTMP Streamer`

- **Checked:** Registry and package search 'rtmp', 'stream': no streaming objects. The usual Max route is Syphon or NDI into separate streaming software. KB article URL is from the index; fetched, not read.
