### Driving displays without the desktop, with hardware frame lock (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Direct Display Out TOP`, `Hardware Frame-Lock`, `Window COMP`
- **What it does:** Outputs straight to GPU display ports, bypassing the Windows desktop compositor, and locks buffer swaps across GPUs and machines with NVIDIA Quadro Sync.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.window`, `jit.world`, `jit.displays`
- **Checked:** Searched Max object registry (interfaces/obj-qlookup.json, plus the mira package registry: 1,326 names with digests) and refpage digests and descriptions (1,389 pages: max-ref, msp-ref, jit-ref, m4l-ref and the bundled packages' docs) and the Max userguide for genlock, frame lock, vsync: no match in the userguide. jit.window and jit.world (refpages read) have sync and fullscreen attributes; jit.displays reports and sets monitor modes. Nothing addresses desktop-free output or swap groups.
- **Notes:** Windows and workstation NVIDIA cards only.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Direct_Display_Out_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Hardware_Frame_Lock> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Perfect_Playback>

### Media preloading handled by the host at every Scene change (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Preload Scene`, `Picture Preload`, `Sound Preload`, `Loaded Media`
- **What it does:** Before a jump, Isadora reads which clips the next Scene will play, from where and at what speed, loads them in the background while the current Scene keeps running, and jumps only when they are ready. An actor can run the same preload early so a later cut is instant.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.polymovie (Jitter Tools package)`, `jit.movie (asyncread)`, `sfplay~ (preload)`
- **Checked:** Refpage grep 'preload' found jit.polymovie, sfplay~, sflist~. Read jit.polymovie refpage: manages multiple jit.movie instances and preloads files for fast switching (Jitter Tools package). jit.movie has an asyncread message. Preloading exists per object; deciding what to preload from the next cue's contents does not.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=650> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=645> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=745> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000014938-understanding-preloading-safely-using-the-preload-actors> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000029774-use-the-initialize-feature-instead-of-enter-scene-value-to-initialize-media-inputs>
- id: `isadora--media-preloading-handled-by-the-host-at-every-scene-change`

### Offline rendering by slowing the whole clock (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Offline rendering of a whole show
- **There:** `Render Speed`, `Record Settings`, `Abort Recording On Dropped Frames`
- **What it does:** A recording setting slows Isadora's internal clock to a percentage, so media and all time-based actors run slower and a heavy Scene records at full frame rate without dropped frames.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.record (@realtime 0)`, `Non-real-time Processing`, `jit.world (fixed_delta)`
- **Checked:** Read userguide non_realtime.json headings: it has sections for non-real-time audio and video. jit.record refpage: a realtime attribute, default off; when on, frames are written on a clock at the set frame rate rather than as each matrix arrives, which implies that with it off every arriving matrix becomes one frame. jit.world has a fixed_delta attribute (seen in its attribute list; description not read). So Max can render offline; the one-setting, whole-patch slowdown is the difference.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=245> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=246>
- id: `isadora--offline-rendering-by-slowing-the-whole-clock`

### Stages: numbered outputs decoupled from physical displays, saved per document (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Stage Setup`, `Stage`, `Virtual Stage`, `Display`, `Force Stage Preview`, `Show-Hide Stages`, `Get Stage Size`, `Stage Preview`
- **What it does:** Patches render to a Stage number. A set-up window, saved with the document, says which displays make up each Stage, at what resolution, flipped or not. If a display is missing the Stage opens as a preview window, so the same file runs on a laptop and in the theatre. Virtual Stages are off-screen canvases at any size.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `jit.window`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** Read refpages: jit.world wraps a window, renderer and corner pin; its attribute list has fullscreen, rect, size, windowposition, enable_cornerpin, output_texture and no display-assignment table. jit.gl.node captures a sub-scene to a texture, the nearest thing to a Virtual Stage. Display assignment in Max is a window rectangle set per patch.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=225> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=226> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=227> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=228> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=230> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=231> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=726> · <https://troikatronix.com/isadora/multi-projector-setups-blending-and-routing/>

Syphon: package objects jit.gl.syphonserver and jit.gl.syphonclient (Syphon package, installed). Spout: named in Vsynth's package entries as needing a Spout package that is not installed here (and is Windows only). NDI: no object in the registry, refpages or package library; the userguide 'video' topic names Syphon, Spout and OBS for recording but not NDI. Internal loopback is ordinary texture routing in Max (jit.gl.node, jit.gl.texture).

### Offline render of a patch to an image sequence at a fixed frame rate (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Offline rendering of a whole show
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.RenderAnim_v2`, `Ops.Gl.MediaRecorder_v2`, `Ops.Gl.SaveScreenShot_v3`, `Ops.Gl.DownloadTexture_v3`
- **What it does:** RenderAnim steps the patch frame by frame at a chosen frame rate for a set number of seconds or frames and saves PNGs (zipped if wanted) or a WebM file, with optional transparency and a chosen size. Because the timeline drives the animation, the result does not depend on how fast the machine renders.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.record`, `jit.vcr`, `jit.world`, `jit.gl.asyncread`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): jit.record (record a movie file), jit.vcr, jit.gl.asyncread. jit.record refpage: a realtime attribute, default off. jit.world refpage attribute list includes fixed_delta, and so do jit.phys.world and jit.anim.path. userguide (C74/docs/userguide/content): 'non-realtime' in 0 files.
- **Notes:** Max looks able to do this by combining a fixed time step with non-realtime recording, but the recipe was not found written down and was not tested. May not be a gap at all.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.RenderAnim_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.MediaRecorder_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/faq/features/recording/recording>
- id: `cables-gl--offline-render-of-a-patch-to-an-image-sequence-at-a-fixed-frame-rate`

### Time-axis filters with raw coefficients (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `pix_tIIR`, `pix_biquad`, `pix_motionblur`, `pix_delay`
- **What it does:** pix_tIIR filters each pixel along time with as many feedback and feed-forward taps as its arguments ask for, one inlet per coefficient. pix_biquad is the two-pole case and takes the same six coefficients as Pd's audio biquad, so a filter designed for sound can be applied to video brightness over frames.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fx.tp.filter`, `jit.slide`, `jit.fx.slide`, `jit.fx.tp.delay`
- **Checked:** Read jit.fx.tp.filter (Jitter Tools: 'temporal filtering to a texture using a two-pole, two-zero filter', set by filtertype, cutoff and q), jit.slide (slide_up, slide_down), and jit.fx.tp.delay (delay, max_delay). No object read takes raw coefficients or more than two poles.
- **Notes:** Arbitrary coefficients are a few lines in jit.gl.pix with feedback, but that is building it, not having it.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_tIIR-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_biquad-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_motionblur-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/22.biquad.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/24.time-filtering.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--time-axis-filters-with-raw-coefficients`

### A folder of numbered stills plays as a movie, with frame rate, cache and auto scan (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Movie playback
- **There:** `Images Folders`, `Image Folder Inspector`, `Cache Size`, `Auto Scan`, `Sizing Mode`
- **What it does:** A folder of numbered images is one media item that plays like a movie. It has its own frames per second, a setting for how many frames to preload, the same loop modes as movies, and it picks up files added to the folder while running.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.textureset (Jitter Tools)`, `jit.matrixset`, `jit.movie`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.textureset's refpage (bundled Jitter Tools package): it keeps a set of textures for storage and resequencing, written by 'index' and read with 'outputtexture', the texture version of jit.matrixset. jit.movie's refpage has nothing about image sequences. No object that takes a folder and plays it was found; a folder-watching object was not searched for.
- **Advice for Max:** For a short sequence, load the frames into jit.gl.textureset once and step through them with a counter at the wanted rate. For a long one, turn the stills into a HAP movie first; that is less patching and plays better.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-formats-and-video-codecs> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--a-folder-of-numbered-stills-plays-as-a-movie-with-frame-rate-cache-and-auto-scan`

### Many layers composited in one node (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Composite TOP`, `Layer Mix TOP`, `Layer TOP`, `Over TOP`, `Fit TOP`, `Palette:blendModes`, `Palette:multiMix`
- **What it does:** One operator composites any number of inputs with one blend operation. A newer one gives every layer its own crop, fit, justify, transform, opacity, level and blend mode and builds a single shader for the whole stack. Inputs can be wired or named by pattern.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.layer`, `jit.gl.videoplane`, `jit.gl.node`, `jit.fx.co.*`, `jit.gl.slab`
- **Checked:** Userguide 'Depth Testing and Layering': layer, depth_enable, blend_enable and blend on every drawn object; jit.gl.layer turns depth testing off and blending on by default. jit.gl.node refpage: capture 1 renders its children to a texture. Jitter Tools has 27 jit.fx.co refpages, each a two-input composite. jit.gl.slab refpage: up to 32 inputs for a custom shader. jit.gl.videoplane refpage: preserve_aspect.
- **Advice for Max:** For a stack of N layers, draw each source on a jit.gl.layer with its own @layer inside a jit.gl.node @capture 1 and take one texture out. Chaining two-input jit.fx.co objects grows with every layer. Fit and justify are done by hand.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): Max's userguide (Depth Testing and Layering) and the GL attribute refpage settle it: depth testing is per object, on by default, and overrides layer, so each layered object needs depth_enable 0. The repo's docs were corrected 2026-10-03.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Composite_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Layer_Mix_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Layer_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Over_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Fit_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:blendModes> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:multiMix>
- id: `touchdesigner--many-layers-composited-in-one-node`

### Texture validity, fallback and gating ops (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Errors and status as data
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.ValidTexture`, `Ops.Gl.OrTexture`, `Ops.Gl.GateTexture`, `Ops.Gl.Textures.TextureInfo_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Texture_v3`
- **What it does:** ValidTexture passes a texture or swaps in a default and reports Is Valid. OrTexture outputs the first valid one of several inputs. GateTexture closes to either the last texture or an empty one. The loader reports Loaded and Loading, and TextureInfo says whether a texture is the built-in default.
- **Closest in Max:** `texture (OB3D attribute)`, `gate`, `jit.fpsgui`, `jit.noise fallback`
- **Checked:** C74/interfaces/obj-qlookup.json: no object name contains 'valid', 'default' or 'fallback'. Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md, Common Pitfalls: a texture cord sets the receiving object's texture attribute, and the attribute stays after the cord is removed or the source stops, so the last frame keeps showing. Same file line 228: any media input should have a generative fallback. gate refpage: a closed gate passes nothing.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max a missing source is silent: the last texture stays up. Wire a fallback texture on purpose and switch to it when the source reports a failed load or stops sending frames. A small 'first valid of N textures' abstraction would be worth having.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.ValidTexture> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.OrTexture> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.GateTexture> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Textures.TextureInfo_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Texture_v3>
- id: `cables-gl--texture-validity-fallback-and-gating-ops`

### Image chains that are only recomputed when something changed (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Bypass and stopping unused work
- **There:** `pix_buf`, `pix_separator`, `pix_image`, `pix_film`
- **What it does:** Gem reprocesses a pix chain only when the source image changes or a parameter of a pix object changes; otherwise the cached result is used. pix_buf stores a result so the expensive objects above it are skipped, and 'profile 2' on the window turns caching off to measure the real cost.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.change`, `jit.matrix`, `jit.world`
- **Checked:** Read jit.movie (unique: 'only new frames from the movie are output when the object receives a bang') and jit.change ('only pass different frames'). A Jitter matrix chain does its work every time a matrix arrives; nothing caches by default, and jit.world sends a draw bang every frame.
- **Advice for Max:** Do not drive a CPU matrix chain from the render bang when its input changes rarely. Use jit.movie @unique 1, or jit.change after the source, and let the last result sit in a jit.matrix or a texture. A still image through a jit.gl.slab chain needs one bang, not thirty a second.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/manual/Pixes.html> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/manual/Texture.html> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_buf-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/manual/GemFaq.html>
- id: `plugdata-gem--image-chains-that-are-only-recomputed-when-something-changed`

### A stated rule for mixing streams of different sizes (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Effect chains: blend, amount and mask on each stage
- **There:** `Video Mixer`, `frame scale`, `Video Image Processing`, `Chop Pixels`, `Scaler`
- **What it does:** When two streams of different resolution meet, one is scaled to match. A preference sets the default rule, and Video Mixer's 'frame scale' input can override it per actor: largest, smallest, or the minimum or maximum width or height. The manual tells users to keep every stream the same size because the scaling costs time.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.slab`, `jit.gl.pix`, `jit.xfade`, `adapt`, `dim`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `jit.gl.slab` and `jit.gl.pix`: `adapt` (default 1) makes the output size match the input; with it off, `dim` sets the size, and `dimscale` multiplies it. `jit.xfade`'s own page lists only the crossfade amount; the matrix-operator group page gives every such object `adapt`.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the output size of a two-input slab follows an input unless told otherwise. Where sources differ, set `@adapt 0 @dim <w> <h>` on the mixing object so the size is a decision written on the box, and keep every source at that size upstream.
- **Notes:** Which input `adapt` follows on a multi-input slab was not read.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=809 (Actors Reference: Video Mixer)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=431 (Actors Reference: Chop Pixels)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=327 (Optimizing for Speed: Tip 5)>
- id: `isadora--a-stated-rule-for-mixing-streams-of-different-sizes`

### Codec advice tied to how the clip will be played (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Movie Player`, `HAP`, `Mixed Format Media Warning`
- **What it does:** The documentation gives one rule: clips that are scrubbed, reversed, sped up or looped tightly should use a codec that stores every frame whole (HAP, ProRes, Photo JPEG), and H.264 is only for playing straight through at normal speed. It also asks for one codec, one resolution and one frame rate per project, with the target frame rate matched to the display's refresh rate.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie @engine`, `hap (bundled package)`, `VIDDLL (bundled package)`
- **Checked:** Listed Max's bundled packages: `hap` and `VIDDLL` are among them. Read `jit.movie`: the `engine` attribute chooses the video backend (avf, qt or viddll) and can only be set in the box. Max's own codec guidance was not read this session.
- **Advice for Max:** The rule is about codecs, not Isadora, so apply it in Max too: before debugging stutter in a `jit.movie` that scrubs or runs backwards, check the file's codec. Keep one codec and one frame rate per piece.
- **Source:** <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000092349-mixed-format-media-warning> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/5000692142-optimizing-for-speed-making-isadora-run-faster> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=327 (Troubleshooting: Optimizing for Speed)>
- id: `isadora--codec-advice-tied-to-how-the-clip-will-be-played`

### Frame bank and video delay as single actors (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `Buffer`, `Video Delay`, `Freeze`
- **What it does:** Buffer stores a set number of frames in turn and outputs whichever one its 'select' input names, with a mode that stops when full and a trigger when the last frame is stored. Video Delay is a chain of frames of a set length. Freeze holds a frame while a switch is on, or grabs one on a trigger.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.matrixset`, `jit.gl.textureset`
- **Checked:** Read `jit.matrixset`: a set of `matrixcount` matrices; `index` chooses which one an incoming matrix is written to; `thru` passes input on; it can import and export movies. A refpage exists for `jit.gl.textureset` ('mimics the functionality of jit.matrixset but outputs textures'), but that name is not in the core obj-qlookup.json, so whether it loads here was not confirmed.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the frame bank is `jit.matrixset` with a `counter` on `index`; the 'stop when full' and 'last frame' signals come from the counter's carry outlet. Check that `jit.gl.textureset` instantiates before planning a GPU version around it.
- **Notes:** How a stored matrix is read back out of `jit.matrixset` was not read; only the write side is described above.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=416 (Actors Reference: Buffer)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=805 (Actors Reference: Video Delay)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=499 (Actors Reference: Freeze)>
- id: `isadora--frame-bank-and-video-delay-as-single-actors`

### Live inputs as shared numbered channels, configured in one window (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Video In Watcher`, `Capture Control`, `Live Capture Settings`
- **What it does:** Camera and audio devices are assigned to four capture channels in one settings window. A patch only names a channel number: Video In Watcher outputs its video, and Capture Control starts or stops it. Any number of watchers can read the same channel, and moving to another machine means re-assigning devices in one place.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.grab`, `send`, `umenu`
- **Checked:** Read `jit.grab`: each object opens a device itself (`open`, `close`), chosen with `vdevice`, `input` and `format`; `getvdevlist` reports the devices; `uniqueid` is 'the unique ID string for the currently selected video device' and 'remains consistent'; `output_texture` gives a texture; `unique` passes only new frames.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max keep one `jit.grab` per camera, in one place in the patch, and share its output by name, so the device choice exists once. Fill a `umenu` from `getvdevlist`, and store `uniqueid`, not the list index, so the right camera is found after a re-plug.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=807 (Actors Reference: Video In Watcher)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=426 (Actors Reference: Capture Control)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=286 (Live Video & Audio Input)>
- id: `isadora--live-inputs-as-shared-numbered-channels-configured-in-one-window`

### Media addressed by a number in a shared bin (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `Get Media Count`, `Get Media Index`, `Get Media File Name`, `Picture Player`, `Picture Preload`
- **What it does:** Every player actor names its file by an index into the document's Media View. Three actors count the items in a bin, convert bin and item numbers to that index, and return the file name. The show moves to another machine by keeping media folders beside the document. Picture Player sends a frame only when the picture, visibility or Scene changes.
- **Closest in Max:** `folder`, `umenu`, `jit.playlist`, `playlist~`, `polybuffer~`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `folder` lists the files in a folder, 'especially useful for loading a umenu'. `umenu`: `autopopulate` with a folder path as `prefix` fills it at load, filtered by `types`. `jit.playlist`: a number plays the clip with that index, counting from 1; `playlist~` numbers its clips from 1 in its `selection` and `setclip` messages. `polybuffer~` names its buffers by index.
- **Advice for Max:** Max has no media bin, so build the index once: a `umenu @autopopulate 1` on a media folder beside the patch, or one `jit.playlist`. Then address clips by number everywhere else. Keep the folder next to the patch so the piece moves as one directory.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=503 (Actors Reference: Get Media Count / Get Media File Name)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=504 (Actors Reference: Get Media Index)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=644 (Actors Reference: Picture Player)> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/5000692144-how-to-move-an-isadora-show-to-another-computer>
- id: `isadora--media-addressed-by-a-number-in-a-shared-bin`

### Movie play segment as start plus length, with position measured inside it (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Movie playback
- **There:** `Movie Player`, `Media Percent to Time`, `Media Time to Percent`, `Loop Calculator`
- **What it does:** The part of a movie that plays is set by 'play start' and 'play length', both as a percentage of the file. The 'position' input jumps within that segment. Loop is off, on or palindrome. Outputs give a trigger per new frame, a trigger at each loop end and the position. Helper actors convert between seconds and percent for a given file.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.playlist`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `jit.movie`: `looppoints` in the movie's time units, plus `looppoints_ms` and `looppoints_secs`; `position` is normalised 0 to 1 over the whole movie; `loopreport` sends `loopnotify` at the loop point (both ends in palindrome); `unique` outputs only new frames; `rate` sets speed. `jit.playlist`: `selection <clip> <start> <end>` in a normalised range, `selectionms`, `loopreport`, `reportprogress`.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max state loop points in seconds (`looppoints_secs`) or use `jit.playlist`'s `selection`, and turn on `@loopreport 1` and `@unique 1` to get Isadora's loop-end and new-frame triggers. Max's `position` covers the whole file, not the loop, so rescale it when scrubbing inside a loop.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=603 (Actors Reference: Movie Player)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=592 (Actors Reference: Media Percent to Time)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=593 (Actors Reference: Media Time to Percent)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=571 (Actors Reference: Loop Calculator)>
- id: `isadora--movie-play-segment-as-start-plus-length-with-position-measured-inside-it`

### A movie's sound level can follow its layer's opacity (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Audio Linked To Opacity`, `Audio Routing`
- **What it does:** A switch on each movie ties its audio level to the opacity of the surface it is on, so fading the picture fades the sound. The FAQ gives this as the way to fade audio between cues.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie (vol)`, `jit.movie~`
- **Checked:** Read jit.movie's refpage: 'vol' is the movie sound volume. No attribute links it to any drawing object's colour or alpha.
- **Advice for Max:** Send one fader to both places: the layer's alpha and the movie's `vol` (or a `live.gain~` after jit.movie~). A video crossfade that leaves the old clip's sound at full level is the usual result of forgetting this.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--a-movie-s-sound-level-can-follow-its-layer-s-opacity`

### Changing a layer's media crossfades; changing its effect cannot (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `Fade Time`, `FX`, `Visual`
- **What it does:** When a cue with a fade time swaps the media on a surface, MadMapper draws the surface twice during the fade, old media and new, at changing opacity. It does not do this for effects: a different effect just switches, and the FAQ says to use two surfaces to fade between effects.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fx.tr.xfade (Jitter Tools)`, `jit.xfade`, `jit.playlist`
- **Checked:** Read jit.fx.tr.xfade's refpage (bundled Jitter Tools package): a crossfade between two textures set by 'xfade', 0 to 1. The same folder holds other transitions (jit.fx.tr.dissolve, slide, gridwipe, zoomfade and more). jit.xfade's registry digest: crossfade between two matrices. jit.playlist's refpage shows no crossfade between clips.
- **Advice for Max:** Build clip changes as A/B: two players into jit.fx.tr.xfade, a `line` on `xfade`, and load the next clip into whichever side is at zero. Fading between two effect chains works the same way in Max, which MadMapper cannot do on one surface.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--changing-a-layer-s-media-crossfades-changing-its-effect-cannot`

### Each output has a frame delay to line up slow and fast projectors (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `Delay`, `Frame Delay`
- **What it does:** Every video output has a delay, counted in frames, so a fast display can be held back to match a slower projector in the same setup.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fx.delay (Jitter Tools)`
- **Checked:** Read jit.fx.delay's refpage (bundled Jitter Tools package): delays a texture by 'delay' frames, up to 'max_delay' (default 30), with 'interp' (default 1) blending between the current and the delayed frame.
- **Advice for Max:** Put jit.fx.delay on the texture going to the faster display and set `@interp 0` so frames are delayed, not blended. One object per output, placed in the mapping stage.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/6.-outputs>
- id: `madmapper--each-output-has-a-frame-delay-to-line-up-slow-and-fast-projectors`

### Movie loop behaviour is a named choice: loop, play once and hold, ping-pong, random frames (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Movie playback
- **There:** `Playback Mode`, `Movies Inspector`, `Loop in`, `Loop out`, `Speed`
- **What it does:** A movie's inspector has transport buttons, a playback mode chosen by name, a speed in percent, and loop in and out points that limit playback to part of the file.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.playlist`
- **Checked:** Read jit.movie's refpage: 'loop' is an integer whose only described value is the default 1 (start to end); 'loopreport' mentions a palindrome mode; 'looppoints', 'loopstart', 'loopend' and 'rate' cover the in and out points and the speed. Read jit.playlist's refpage: 'loop' is on or off per clip, 'selection' sets a clip's start and end, and any jit.movie attribute can be set per clip with 'setclip'. The refpage does not list what each 'loop' number means, and holding the last frame was not found in it.
- **Advice for Max:** Do not leave a bare number box on `loop`. Put an `attrui` on it so the mode shows its own name, and label the control with the behaviour (loop, once, back and forth). For random frames, drive the `frame` message from `random`.
- **Notes:** The meaning of each jit.movie 'loop' value should be confirmed in the help file before writing labels.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin>
- id: `madmapper--movie-loop-behaviour-is-a-named-choice-loop-play-once-and-hold-ping-pong-random-frames`

### One plain codec rule: HAP for scrubbing and many layers, H.264 only for straight playback (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Draw order and layering stated outright
- **There:** `HAP`, `HAP Alpha`, `HAP Q`, `ProRes`, `Keyframe Distance`, `Use Hardware Acceleration`
- **What it does:** The manual says which codec to use for what. HAP is decoded on the graphics card and suits scrubbing, reverse and many layers, but needs a fast drive. H.264 and similar are for forward playback only; if they must be scrubbed, export them with a keyframe on every frame. It also says to add up the data rates of all clips and check the drive can deliver them.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.playlist`, `hap (bundled package)`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topics jitter/video and jitter/video_engine: both video engines play HAP natively; the video page suggests HAP, or loading the file into memory, for random access and resequencing. The bundled 'hap' package describes itself as a HAP video engine for efficient realtime playback. Max's pages give no keyframe or drive-speed advice.
- **Advice for Max:** Same rule in Max: transcode to HAP anything that is scrubbed, reversed, retriggered or layered. If a file must stay H.264, export it all-intra. Check the drive's read speed against the sum of the clips playing at once.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-formats-and-video-codecs> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/12.-advanced/performance-optimization> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--one-plain-codec-rule-hap-for-scrubbing-and-many-layers-h-264-only-for-straight-playback`

### Sources that are not on screen stop working unless marked 'keep running' (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Bypass and stopping unused work
- **There:** `Keep running (uses more CPU)`, `Preview`, `Low Bandw.`
- **What it does:** A network video source is only processed while a visible surface uses it; a tick box keeps it running in the background for instant availability, and is labelled as costing more. The media preview and the two editing views can be switched off to save rendering.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie (start, stop)`, `jit.grab (open, close)`, `enable (jit.gl objects and jit.fx objects)`, `jit.pwindow`
- **Checked:** Read the refpages: jit.movie has 'start' and 'stop'; jit.grab has 'open' and 'close'; the shared jit.gl attribute 'enable' turns an object's drawing off; the jit.fx objects have 'enable' and 'bypass'. Nothing stops a source by itself when its layer becomes invisible.
- **Advice for Max:** When a layer's opacity reaches 0, also stop what feeds it: `stop` the movie, `enable 0` on its effects. Where an instant return matters more than the cost, leave it running and say so in a comment. Give every preview a toggle; a preview is a second render.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/11.-live-performance-and-control> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin> · <https://madmapper.com/files/01-Introduction%20to%20the%20User%20Interface.pdf>
- id: `madmapper--sources-that-are-not-on-screen-stop-working-unless-marked-keep-running`

### The manual says when hardware decoding breaks audio routing (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Use Hardware Acceleration`, `FFMPEG Player`, `Audio Routing`
- **What it does:** On macOS, hardware decoding of H.264 and HEVC sends the movie's sound to the system's default output and ignores the program's own audio settings. The manual says so and recommends leaving it off when the sound matters.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.movie~ (VIDDLL)`, `mc.jit.movie~`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topic jitter/video: jit.movie plays audio directly through the video engine, not through Max; jit.movie~ brings the audio tracks into MSP, and mc.jit.movie~ gives them as multichannel audio. jit.movie~'s refpage (bundled VIDDLL package): plays a movie and routes its audio into MSP.
- **Advice for Max:** When a movie's sound has to pass through the patch, for a level, a mute, an effect or a chosen output, use jit.movie~ and not jit.movie. With jit.movie the sound goes out of the computer without ever reaching a cord.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-formats-and-video-codecs> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/media-bin>
- id: `madmapper--the-manual-says-when-hardware-decoding-breaks-audio-routing`

### Two clips kept frame-locked by putting them side by side in one file (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Input Rectangle`, `Synchronize two videos`
- **What it does:** The documented way to keep two videos in step is to make one wide file with both side by side, then let two surfaces each crop their half. One decoder cannot drift from itself.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.fx.subtexture (Jitter Tools)`
- **Checked:** Read jit.fx.subtexture's refpage: a sub-region of a texture by 'offset' and 'dim', without copying. jit.movie's refpage offers 'time' and 'frame' for seeking but nothing that slaves one movie to another.
- **Advice for Max:** For clips that must stay in step, including one picture split across projectors, play one wide movie and cut it with jit.fx.subtexture. Do not start two jit.movie objects together and hope.
- **Notes:** Not tested in Max; the reasoning is MadMapper's, applied to Max objects whose refpages were read.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/15.-troubleshooting> · <https://madmapper.com/madmapper/faq>
- id: `madmapper--two-clips-kept-frame-locked-by-putting-them-side-by-side-in-one-file`

### A frame cache on the GPU for delay, freeze and loop (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Cache TOP`, `Cache Select TOP`
- **What it does:** One operator stores the last N images in graphics memory. Reading it with a negative index is a delay, switching capture off is a freeze, animating the index is a loop, and it can fill itself on load. A companion reads any stored image by index.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fx.tp.delay`, `jit.gl.textureset`, `jit.matrixset`
- **Checked:** jit.fx.tp.delay refpage: delays a texture by a number of frames, with max_delay and frame interpolation. jit.gl.textureset refpage: a set of textures written and read by index. jit.matrixset refpage: the same for matrices on the CPU.
- **Advice for Max:** Pick by job in Max: jit.fx.tp.delay for a delay, jit.gl.textureset for a frame store read by index (and for a freeze, by no longer writing to the slot you are showing).
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cache_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cache_Select_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pre-Filling>
- id: `touchdesigner--a-frame-cache-on-the-gpu-for-delay-freeze-and-loop`

### Blending between N inputs with a fractional index (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Cross CHOP`, `Switch TOP`, `Blend CHOP`, `Cross TOP`, `Switch CHOP`
- **What it does:** A fractional index selects among several inputs and blends the two nearest: 1.5 is half of input 2 and half of input 3. It exists for channels and for images. A weighted version mixes all inputs at once.
- **Closest in Max:** `selector~`, `matrix~`, `jit.fx.tr.xfade`, `pattrstorage`, `preset`
- **Checked:** selector~ refpage: ramptime attribute for a click-free switch. matrix~ refpage: ramp attribute. jit.fx.tr.xfade and jit.xfade refpages: two inputs. pattrstorage and preset refpages: a float such as 1.5 interpolates between presets 1 and 2, and recallmulti mixes several by weight.
- **Advice for Max:** For audio use selector~ @ramptime. For whole sets of settings Max already has the fractional index: send pattrstorage or preset a float. For N textures there is no single object, so chain two-input crossfades or write one jit.gl.pix.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cross_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Switch_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Blend_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cross_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Switch_CHOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--blending-between-n-inputs-with-a-fractional-index`

### Gapless clip switching with preload and crossfade (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Start-up, loading and initial values
- **There:** `Palette:movieBlender`, `Palette/moviePlayer`, `Palette:moviePlaylist`, `Palette:autoMediaPlayer`, `Switch TOP`, `Cross TOP`
- **What it does:** Ready-made player components take a file or a folder and handle the rest: preload before playing, crossfade or fade through a colour, still-image duration, what to do at the end (loop, hold, fade, play next), and Initialize / Start controls. A switch operator can blend between neighbouring inputs.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.polymovie`, `jit.playlist`, `jit.movie`, `jit.fx.tr.xfade`, `jit.xfade`
- **Checked:** jit.polymovie refpage: manages several jit.movie instances and 'allows for fast file switching by preloading the files'; messages readfolder, appendmovie, play <index>; attribute target addresses several outputs at once; a jit.gl.polymovie form outputs textures. jit.playlist refpage: clips with selections, next, loop. jit.movie refpage: asyncread. jit.fx.tr.xfade refpage: crossfade between two textures; Jitter Tools has eight more jit.fx.tr transitions.
- **Advice for Max:** Where a cut must be clean, play preloaded clips from jit.gl.polymovie on two targets into jit.fx.tr.xfade, instead of reading a new file into one jit.movie. A clip-player abstraction with preload, crossfade time, end behaviour and a done bang is worth building.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:movieBlender> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette/moviePlayer> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:moviePlaylist> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:autoMediaPlayer> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Switch_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cross_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Pre-Filling>
- id: `touchdesigner--gapless-clip-switching-with-preload-and-crossfade`

### Movie position driven by an index (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Movie playback
- **There:** `Movie File In TOP`, `Audio Movie CHOP`
- **What it does:** A movie has three play modes: locked to the timeline, driven by an index you supply, or free-running. The index can be in frames, seconds or a 0-to-1 fraction, or come from a timecode. A separate audio operator follows whichever movie it is pointed at.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.movie~`
- **Checked:** jit.movie refpage: attributes position (0 to 1), time, rate, loop points, autostart, unique; messages frame, jump, frame_coarse, start, stop, asyncread. jit.movie~ refpage: frame_true ('slower, more accurate'), loadram, cache_size. Userguide 'Video': files are encoded for linear playback, and loadram (viddll engine) or HAP makes jumping cheaper.
- **Advice for Max:** When several clips must stay locked together, do not start them and hope. Drive each one's frame or position from one counter on the render tick, and use HAP or loadram so seeking is cheap.
- **Notes:** Advice assembled from the refpages; not tested in Max. Session 1's 'Tunable high-rate movie playback' covers decoding and read-ahead, not play modes.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Movie_File_In_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Audio_Movie_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Movie_Playback>
- id: `touchdesigner--movie-position-driven-by-an-index`

### Media players report their state on output ports (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `Ops.Gl.Textures.VideoTexture_v4`, `Ops.Audio.AudioPlayer`, `Ops.WebAudio.AudioBufferPlayer_v2`
- **What it does:** The video op has always-on outputs for duration, progress, current time, Loading, Playing, Can Play Through, an Ended trigger, a Texture Updated trigger, Has Error and an error message. The audio player has Playing, Duration, Current Time and a Has Ended trigger.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.playlist`, `playlist~`, `sfplay~`
- **Checked:** jit.movie refpage: two outlets, frames and dumpout. State is asked for and switched on: @loopreport sends loopnotify at the loop point, @framereport sends timing per frame, and attributes such as duration, time and position are read with get messages. jit.playlist refpage: @loopreport and @reportprogress.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max turn the reports on (@loopreport 1, @reportprogress 1) and route the right outlet, so the patch reacts to 'ended' and 'position' instead of guessing with timers. Show those values next to the player, since Max does not show them by default.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Textures.VideoTexture_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Audio.AudioPlayer> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.WebAudio.AudioBufferPlayer_v2>
- id: `cables-gl--media-players-report-their-state-on-output-ports`

### Explicit colour space on every image (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `pix_rgba`, `pix_yuv`, `pix_grey`, `pix_convert`, `pix_film`, `pix_chroma_key`
- **What it does:** A pix is RGBA, YUV or grey, and the default from a movie file differs by operating system. Objects that take a colour as a parameter then behave differently, and some effects silently do nothing on the wrong format. The help files tell the reader to convert explicitly before such objects.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.grab`, `jit.colorspace`, `jit.gl.videoplane`
- **Checked:** Read jit.movie and jit.grab (colormode: 'the output matrix's color encoding', default argb on jit.grab) and jit.colorspace (converts a 4-plane char matrix between colour spaces). Jitter's default is the same on every platform, and the plane order is ARGB, not RGBA.
- **Advice for Max:** When porting numbers from Gem, remember the plane order differs (Gem RGBA, Jitter ARGB) and that a uyvy colormode in Jitter changes what a matrix's planes mean exactly as YUV does in Gem. Write @colormode in the box whenever a later object takes a colour or a plane index.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_film-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_chroma_key-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_convert-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_yuv-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/05.film.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--explicit-colour-space-on-every-image`

### Frame-addressed movie playback (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Movie playback
- **There:** `pix_film`, `pix_movie`
- **What it does:** pix_film outputs the frame whose number is sent to it; it does not play unless told 'auto 1', and in auto mode it does not loop but bangs at the end. pix_movie is the same decoder writing straight to a texture. The patch therefore owns the clock: any counter, ramp or audio phase can drive the frame.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.playlist`
- **Checked:** Read jit.movie: autostart default 1, loop default 1, rate, the frame and jump messages, framedump, and output_texture ('an OpenGL texture is output instead of a Jitter matrix').
- **Advice for Max:** For scrubbing or audio-locked playback in Jitter, turn the transport off (@autostart 0, or rate 0) and send frame numbers, as Gem does by default; leaving the movie playing while also sending frames makes the two fight. Use @output_texture 1 unless a CPU object needs the pixels.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_film-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_movie-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/05.film.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/05.movie.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--frame-addressed-movie-playback`

### Preload an image sequence into texture memory (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Start-up, loading and initial values
- **There:** `pix_imageInPlace`, `pix_multiimage`
- **What it does:** pix_imageInPlace takes a file name with a wildcard and a count, loads the numbered images, and on 'download' sends them all to the graphics card. Switching between them is then instant. pix_multiimage is the CPU version that still allows pix processing.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.gl.textureset`, `jit.matrixset`, `jit.gl.texture`
- **Checked:** Read jit.gl.textureset (index, clear, outputtexture; 'mimics the functionality of jit.matrixset but outputs textures') and jit.gl.texture (file attribute, read message). No refpage read takes a wildcard file name; the loading loop is the patch's job.
- **Advice for Max:** For instant image switching in Jitter, load each file once into a slot of jit.gl.textureset at start-up and change @index at run time. Reading a file on every switch is the stall Gem's object exists to avoid.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_imageInPlace-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_multiimage-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/04.pix/04.ImageInPlace.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/Documentation/manual/Texture.html>
- id: `plugdata-gem--preload-an-image-sequence-into-texture-memory`

### Tunable high-rate movie playback (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Movie playback
- **There:** `Movie File In TOP`, `Info CHOP`, `Hap`, `NotchLC`
- **What it does:** Exposes each playback stage for tuning: pre-read frame count, hardware decode, an unbuffered high-rate disk read mode, and live readouts of read time, decode time and dropped frames. Covers Hap variants, NotchLC, ProRes and EXR sequences.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.movie`, `jit.playlist`, `jit.openexr`
- **Checked:** Read jit.movie's refpage (engine attribute, asyncread, texture output) and saw the bundled hap package folder. The refpage attribute list shows no pre-read count or decode-time readout, but engine-specific options were not read.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Movie_File_In_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Movie_Playback> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Hap> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/NotchLC> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/OpenEXR>
- id: `touchdesigner--tunable-high-rate-movie-playback`

### An element of the page captured as an image for a texture (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Web page as a texture
- **There:** `Ops.Extension.HtmlToTexture.HtmlToTexture`
- **What it does:** Takes an HTML element and outputs an image data URL of it, with a progress value and a finished trigger. The op page has no description.
- **Closest in Max:** `jweb`, `jit.desktop`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): jweb's rendermode attribute has an offscreen value that draws to the patcher window, not to a texture. jit.desktop copies a screen rectangle into a matrix, which would capture a visible jweb.
- **Notes:** The cables FAQ says a website cannot be rendered to a texture, which conflicts with this op's name. What the op really does is not documented.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Extension.HtmlToTexture.HtmlToTexture> · <https://cables.gl/docs/faq/features/htmltexture/htmltexture>
- id: `cables-gl--an-element-of-the-page-captured-as-an-image-for-a-texture`

## workflow/authoring

### Text prompt to a working shader with controls (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `MadAI`, `Mad AI`, `Using MadAI`, `MadAI Credits`
- **What it does:** Typing a description produces a material, laser material or 2D effect, complete with sliders for the parameters asked for; further prompts refine it, and the code stays editable. It runs on the maker's servers, one credit per prompt.
- **Checked:** Not searched in Max: this is a paid online service, not a program feature that could be looked up as an object.
- **Notes:** Listed for completeness. The FAQ page cited was read only in its MadAI and MadLaser general sections.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/12.-advanced/mad-ai-extension> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/3.-media/online-material-library> · <https://madmapper.com/extensions/FAQ>
- id: `madmapper--text-prompt-to-a-working-shader-with-controls`

### Authoring in a browser tab with nothing to install (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `cables.gl editor`, `MIT License`
- **What it does:** The editor runs in a web browser. The docs say no licence is bought, the runtime is MIT licensed and the main features stay free; storage, upload size and exports per day have limits tied to supporter levels.
- **Checked:** Max is a desktop application at /Applications/Max.app. No browser-hosted Max editor appears in the userguide file list. Max's licence terms were not looked up.
- **Notes:** A property of the platform, not a feature to copy. It matters when choosing a tool for a class where students cannot install software.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/faq/general/general> · <https://cables.gl/docs/faq/licence_payment/licence/licence> · <https://cables.gl/docs/faq/licence_payment/payment/payment> · <https://cables.gl/docs/faq/teaching/teaching>
- id: `cables-gl--authoring-in-a-browser-tab-with-nothing-to-install`

### Several people editing one patch at once (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Multiplayer`, `Pilot`, `Participant`
- **What it does:** Several users open the same patch. One is the Pilot and makes the changes; the others see cursors, selections and the Pilot's view, follow parameter changes and the rendered output live, can ask for the pilot seat, and can chat.
- **Closest in Max:** `Projects (Max feature)`
- **Checked:** userguide (C74/docs/userguide/content): 'multiplayer' in 0 files; 'collaborat' in 1 file (standalones_and_collectives.json), which says a Project is the document to use with a version control tool such as Git. refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): 'collab' matches nothing. packages/query_packages.py search 'collab': the Collab-Hub package, which shares data between performers' patches, not the editing of one patch.
- **Notes:** One editor at a time, so closer to screen sharing with hand-over than to simultaneous editing. Useful for teaching.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/5_1_permissions/4_multiplayer/multiplayer> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_1_permissions/0_users/users>
- id: `cables-gl--several-people-editing-one-patch-at-once`

### A box abstraction that reacts to being clicked (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `click`, `properties`
- **What it does:** Placed inside an abstraction, click sends a bang when the abstraction's box is clicked in the parent patch, and properties does the same when its Properties menu item is chosen. ELSE uses this so that clicking player~ opens a file dialog and clicking a table object opens its data.
- **Closest in Max:** `bpatcher`, `ubutton`, `hover`, `mousestate`
- **Checked:** No object in obj-qlookup.json reports clicks on the box of the abstraction that contains it. In Max, double-clicking an abstraction's box opens its patcher. A bpatcher can hold a ubutton, but that is a UI, which the repo's rules say an abstraction should not have.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/click-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/properties-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--a-box-abstraction-that-reacts-to-being-clicked`

### Per-patch declaration of what the patch needs ([declare]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Not in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `declare -path`, `declare -lib`, `declare -stdpath`, `else/knob`
- **What it does:** [declare -path folder -lib name] in a patch adds search folders and loads libraries for that patch alone, so the patch states its own dependencies. A 'library/object' prefix picks which library an object comes from.
- **Closest in Max:** `filepath`, `Projects`, `Package Manager`
- **Checked:** Userguide search_path, package_manager and externals pages: paths are global (File Preferences, Packages folder) or per Project. filepath refpage: manages the search path, 'or manage paths specific to a single patch'. No in-patch dependency declaration or name prefix found.
- **Notes:** filepath's per-patch mode was read only as one sentence.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/declare-help.pd> · <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter4.htm>
- id: `plugdata--per-patch-declaration-of-what-the-patch-needs-declare`

### Blind Mode: edit one Scene while another runs the show (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Blind Mode`
- **What it does:** Borrowed from lighting consoles. The operator opens and edits a different Scene while the active Scene keeps playing and keeps answering Go Triggers. The Scene being edited stays paused.
- **Closest in Max:** `patcher editing while running`
- **Checked:** No registry or userguide hit for 'blind'. In Max any patcher can be edited while others run, so the live half exists; there is no way to edit a patch that is guaranteed not to execute, which is what blind means.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=223> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=224> · <https://troikatronix.com/isadora/cueing-show-control/>
- id: `isadora--blind-mode-edit-one-scene-while-another-runs-the-show`

### Scene snapshots of every input, with per-actor opt-out (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scenes and cues as the structure of a show
- **There:** `Snapshots`, `Take Snapshot`, `Recall Snapshot`, `Update Snapshot`, `Disable Actor Snapshot`
- **What it does:** One click stores every input value of every actor in the Scene, with no set-up. Snapshots are recalled by click or by actor, and chosen actors can be told to ignore recalls.
- **Closest in Max:** `preset`, `pattrstorage`, `autopattr`, `Snapshots (patcher snapshots)`
- **Checked:** Read refpages: preset stores and recalls user-interface objects' values; pattrstorage stores and recalls pattr presets and interpolates between them. Read userguide snapshots.json and presets_and_interpolation.json headings. Max is the stronger system (interpolation, files, priorities) but needs objects bound or named; Isadora captures every input automatically.
- **Notes:** Arguably Max is ahead here. Kept because zero-setup capture of all inputs is the difference.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=181> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=182> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=183> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=680> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=763> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=794>
- id: `isadora--scene-snapshots-of-every-input-with-per-actor-opt-out`

### User Actors: embedded sub-patches that update every copy on request (Isadora)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `User Actor`, `Macro`, `Save & Update All`, `New Instance`, `Global Toolbox`, `Local Toolbox`, `User Actor On/Off`
- **What it does:** A wrapped group of actors is stored inside the document yet keeps a shared identity. Closing its editor offers to rewrite every copy in every open document, keeping links and hand-set input values, or to split it off as a new variant. An on/off actor inside it stops its contents to save processing.
- **Closest in Max:** `abstraction`, `patcher (p)`, `bpatcher`, `poly~`
- **Checked:** Max abstractions are files and update everywhere by nature; p subpatchers are embedded copies that never update each other. The embedded-but-synchronised middle case was not found. Based on the repo's userguide notes on abstractions and subpatchers, not re-read this session.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=171> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=172> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=173> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=177> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=178> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=795> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000085286-keeping-global-user-actors-in-sync-when-collaborating>
- id: `isadora--user-actors-embedded-sub-patches-that-update-every-copy-on-request`

### In-app shader library: install, publish and remix community shaders (MadMapper)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Shader authoring conveniences
- **There:** `Online Material Library`, `Library`, `Install + Add to Project`, `Publish`, `Favorites`, `Offline Library`
- **What it does:** A panel lists shaders and effects published by other users; one click installs one into the project, and your own can be published publicly or kept private on your account. The whole library is cached for offline use.
- **Closest in Max:** `Package Manager`
- **Checked:** Searched Max 9.1.5's object registry (obj-qlookup.json), refpage names, digests and text, the userguide topics, and the repo's package library (query_packages.py). The userguide has a 'package_manager' topic: Max installs whole packages from inside the application. There is no per-shader library with publishing.

### A live viewer in every node (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Profiling and live views of the running graph
- **There:** `Viewer`, `Node Viewer`, `Viewer Active Flag`
- **What it does:** Every operator shows its current output in the node itself (image, graph, table or 3D view), and the viewer can take mouse input.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.pwindow`, `scope~`, `number`
- **Checked:** The Max userguide's Debugging and Probing topic covers hovering a patch cord to inspect message, signal and matrix data (the probe). Seeing data at all times needs a display object such as jit.pwindow, scope~ or number added by hand.
- **Notes:** A difference of design more than a missing feature. This repo's 'Put the Display in the Path' rule covers the same need.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Viewer> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Network> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/First_Things_to_Know_about_TouchDesigner>
- id: `touchdesigner--a-live-viewer-in-every-node`

### Demand-driven evaluation (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Cook`, `Dependency`
- **What it does:** A node computes only when something downstream needs its result and an input has changed, so unused branches cost nothing without being switched off by hand.
- **Closest in Max:** `gate`, `poly~`, `jit.gl.node`
- **Checked:** Max evaluates when a message arrives. Unused branches are stopped by hand, with gate, by muting poly~ voices or by disabling GL objects.
- **Notes:** A model difference. Listed because it shapes how large shows are optimised.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cook> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Dependency> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Procedural> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Event>
- id: `touchdesigner--demand-driven-evaluation`

### Per-node performance profiling (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Profiling and live views of the running graph
- **There:** `Performance Monitor`, `Palette:probe`, `Perform CHOP`, `Perform DAT`, `Info CHOP`
- **What it does:** Shows what computed in a frame, in order, with CPU and GPU time per node. It can wait for a slow frame and capture it, and frame statistics are available as data.
- **Closest in Max:** `adstatus`, `jit.fpsgui`, `cpuclock`

The userguide search for 'profil' matched only unrelated uses. No per-object timing view was found.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Performance_Monitor> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette:probe> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Perform_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Perform_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Optimize>

### Every input is a visible, stored parameter (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Every parameter addressable: OSC, learn, binding, expressions
- **There:** `parameter panel`, `op.inFloat`, `op.inBool`, `op.inDropDown`, `op.setPortGroup`, `port.shouldLink`
- **What it does:** Each input port of an op also shows as a control in a side panel with its current value. The typed value is saved with the patch and comes back when a cable is removed. Ports are typed and colour-coded, and a cable can be dropped on a parameter name.
- **Closest in Max:** `attrui`, `Inspector (Max feature)`
- **Checked:** This repo's CLAUDE.md (If You Mention an Attribute, Show It; Drive a Real Attribute With attrui) describes the Max side: attributes show in the Inspector or an attrui, while values sent to inlets are not displayed or stored by the object. attrui refpage digest: 'Inspect attributes'.
- **Notes:** A design difference more than a missing object. It removes a class of 'what value is this object holding?' questions that Max answers with extra display boxes.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_ops/dev_ops> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_creating_ports/dev_creating_ports> · <https://cables.gl/docs/0_howtouse/ui_walkthrough/ui_walkthrough> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/object_ports/object_ports>
- id: `cables-gl--every-input-is-a-visible-stored-parameter`

### Public patches open in the editor from a link, and can be cloned (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Public patches`, `Unlisted - Allow Editor`, `Clone`, `Patchlists`, `Example patch`
- **What it does:** A public or unlisted patch opens in the full editor for anyone with the link, who can change it without saving and clone it to their own account. Every op's page links an example patch that opens the same way, and lists patches that use the op.
- **Closest in Max:** `help files (Max feature)`, `Max Compressed Text (Max feature)`
- **Checked:** This repo's CLAUDE.md describes the Max ways to pass a patch on: a .maxpat file, or Max Compressed Text pasted with File > New From Clipboard, and a help file per object. Each needs Max installed on the reader's machine. No hosted, open-by-link patch viewer was found in the userguide file list.
- **Notes:** The teaching value is that a student can open a working example with one click and nothing installed.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/5_1_permissions/1_patches/patches> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_1_permissions/5_patchlists/patchlists> · <https://cables.gl/docs/faq/features/sharing/sharing> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_ops/dev_ops>

### Versions in op names, with update prompts, and permission namespaces (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `Ops.Patch.`, `Ops.User.`, `Ops.Team.`, `Ops.Extension.`, `_v2`, `.Deprecated.`, `.Dev.`, `rename`
- **What it does:** An op's version is part of its name. Old versions keep working in old patches, are hidden from the add-op list, and the editor offers to update them. Renaming an op updates every patch that uses it. Four namespaces decide who may use and edit an op: one patch, one user, a team, or everyone.
- **Closest in Max:** `Package Manager (Max feature)`, `abstractions (Max feature)`, `Projects (Max feature)`
- **Checked:** userguide (C74/docs/userguide/content): abstractions.json, packages.json and projects.json exist; 'version control\|git' in 0 files by that pattern, though the Git sentence above was found under 'collaborat'. This repo's CLAUDE.md (A Reusable Object Has One Home on the Search Path) describes Max resolving a file by name on the search path, with no version in the name. No Max mechanism was found that keeps two versions of an abstraction side by side and prompts to update.
- **Notes:** Depends on cables being a hosted service that knows every patch. Still, the naming rule alone is something a Max package could adopt.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_renaming/dev_renaming> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_1_permissions/3_ops/ops> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/patchingops/subpatchops>
- id: `cables-gl--versions-in-op-names-with-update-prompts-and-permission-namespaces`

### An abstraction that can read and rewrite its own box arguments (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `args`, `dollsym`, `sender`, `receiver`
- **What it does:** args outputs the arguments typed on the abstraction's box, optionally split at a marker symbol, and can also replace them, so the box text changes and is saved. dollsym, sender and receiver expand the instance number of a parent patch from inside a child.
- **Closest in Max:** `patcherargs`, `thispatcher`, `pattr`
- **Checked:** patcherargs refpage: 'Retrieves patcher arguments and parses attribute style arguments'; normal arguments from the left outlet and @-style ones from the right; methods are bang only. Nothing in it writes arguments back.
- **Notes:** Reading is covered by patcherargs, which the repo already requires. Writing back the box text from inside is not a Max idiom; state belongs in pattr.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/args-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/dollsym-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/sender-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/receiver-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--an-abstraction-that-can-read-and-rewrite-its-own-box-arguments`

### An in-path monitor that shows any message and passes it on (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `display`, `numbox~`, `note`
- **What it does:** display is a box that prints whatever message passes through it, resizes to fit, and sends the message on unchanged from its outlet. numbox~ does the same for a signal value and can also generate one.
- **Closest in Max:** `message`, `number`, `print`, `number~`
- **Checked:** No 'display' object in obj-qlookup.json. A message box shows a message set through its right inlet but does not pass it on; number boxes pass numbers through; print writes to the console (it has a popup attribute); number~ shows a signal.
- **Notes:** What a Max patcher can do meanwhile: The repo's rule 'put the display in the path' is easy for numbers (number box) and awkward for lists and symbols, because the message box is a dead end. A two-outlet idiom covers it: trigger the message to the consumer and to 'prepend set' into a message box. An abstraction or v8ui that does both in one box would be a useful Butter tool.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/display-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/numbox~-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--an-in-path-monitor-that-shows-any-message-and-passes-it-on`

### Load-time bangs before the parent's cords exist, and after everything else (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `loadbanger`, `lb`, `initmess`
- **What it does:** loadbanger sends N bangs right to left at load, and again on any message or click. With -init it fires earlier, inside abstractions first and before the parent's connections are made, which lets an abstraction build its own inlets. With -fin it fires after all ordinary load bangs.
- **Closest in Max:** `loadbang`, `loadmess`, `bangbang`, `patcherargs`, `closebang`, `deferlow`
- **Checked:** Refpages. loadbang: bang when the patcher is loaded. loadmess: message at load, with a defer attribute ('output is deferred through the lower priority main thread'). No object offers a choice of load phase.
- **Notes:** In Max, 'after everything else' is loadbang into deferlow, or loadmess @defer 1. The order between an abstraction's and its parent's loadbangs is covered in the repo's user-guide notes, not here.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/loadbanger-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/initmess-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--load-time-bangs-before-the-parent-s-cords-exist-and-after-everything-else`

### Objects that report the state of the patcher window (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `canvas.edit`, `canvas.vis`, `canvas.active`, `canvas.zoom`, `canvas.bounds`, `canvas.pos`, `canvas.name`, `canvas.mouse`, `fontsize`
- **What it does:** A family that reports, for the current window or a parent at a given depth: whether it is in edit mode, visible, frontmost or zoomed, its bounds on screen, a subpatch's position in its parent, its name, and mouse position inside it.
- **Closest in Max:** `active`, `thispatcher`, `mousestate`, `hover`, `v8`
- **Checked:** Refpages. active: 1 when the patcher window is active. thispatcher: sends messages to a patcher. mousestate and hover report the mouse. No object reports edit (unlocked) state or zoom.
- **Notes:** Lock state and window geometry are reachable from JavaScript through the patcher object; that was not verified in this session.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/canvas.edit-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/canvas.vis-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/canvas.active-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/canvas.bounds-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/canvas.mouse-help.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--objects-that-report-the-state-of-the-patcher-window`

### An abstraction saves its own state inside the parent patch ([savestate]) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `savestate`
- **What it does:** [savestate] in an abstraction bangs when the parent patch is saved, takes lists in reply, stores them in the parent's file, and hands them back when the parent loads. Each copy keeps its own.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattr`, `autopattr`, `pattrstorage`, `savebang`
- **Checked:** pattr refpage: autorestore outputs the last-saved value on load. savebang refpage: bangs when its patcher window is saved. Userguide snapshots page: patcher snapshots are embedded and save subpatcher parameters. Whether a pattr inside an abstraction file is stored per instance in the parent was not established from these pages.
- **Source:** <Documentation/5.reference/savestate-help.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/savestate-ex1.pd> · <Documentation/5.reference/savestate-ex2.pd>

### Behaviour pinned to an older version (pd compatibility) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** Partly in Max · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `compatibility`
- **What it does:** 'compatibility 0.52' sent to pd restores the pre-fix behaviour of objects changed since, and each affected object's help says which version changed it.
- **Closest in Max:** `fft~`, `pfft~`, `poly~`
- **Checked:** Per-object attributes found in refpages: fft~ and pfft~ 'legacy', poly~ 'legacynotemode'. No global version switch found in the userguide pages read.
