### Automatic range scaling on every link (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Every parameter addressable: OSC, learn, binding, expressions
- **There:** `Limit Min`, `Limit Max`, `Scale Min`, `Scale Max`, `Limit-Scale Value`
- **What it does:** Each output has a range and each input has a range, and a value is mapped from one to the other as it crosses the link, with inversion by swapping the input's limits. Most connections need no scaling actor.
- **Closest in Max:** `scale`, `zmap`, `live.dial (parameter range)`
- **Checked:** Max has the scale object (registry). Scaling is an object on the cord, not a property of the connection. zmap and parameter ranges not re-read this session.
- **Notes:** A design difference, not a missing function. Better in Isadora for beginners; hides the mapping from view, which Max's patch-is-the-documentation rule would dislike.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=163> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=164> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=165> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=166> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000113815-understanding-and-using-port-shapes-and-fills>
- id: `isadora--automatic-range-scaling-on-every-link`

### Sub-patch ports declared with a name, type, range and help text (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `User Input`, `User Output`, `User Actor`, `Macro`
- **What it does:** Each port of a sub-patch is an actor whose dialog sets the port's name, data type (integer, float, trigger, on/off, video, a media file number and others), its minimum and maximum, and the help text shown on hover. Port order is set in a reorder dialog, not by where the actors sit. The sub-patch itself has its own help text.
- **Closest in Max:** `inlet`, `outlet`, `patcherargs`, `setinletassist (v8)`
- **Checked:** Read `inlet`: its attributes are `comment` (the hover text, 'can only be set using the Comment setting in the inlet object's Inspector'), `cool` and a colour. No type or range. The userguide's subpatchers page says inlet order follows the position of the inlet objects, so moving one re-wires the parent.
- **Advice for Max:** Max gives a port only a hover comment, so put the type and range in it ('float 0.-1.: mix') and clamp inside with `clip`. Keep inlets in a strict left-to-right row and never nudge one sideways past another. This is the repo's labelling rule; Isadora shows what it is standing in for.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=796 (Actors Reference: User Input)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=797 (Actors Reference: User Output)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=794 (Actors Reference: User Actor)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=175 (User Actors & Macros: data type list)>
- id: `isadora--sub-patch-ports-declared-with-a-name-type-range-and-help-text`

### Switching a whole sub-patch off from inside it (Isadora)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `User Actor On/Off`, `User Actor`, `Macro`
- **What it does:** An actor placed inside a User Actor or Macro, with one 'enable' input. Off, every actor inside stops executing. Wired to a User Input, it gives the sub-patch an on/off input of its own.
- **Closest in Max:** `pcontrol`, `mute~`, `poly~`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `pcontrol`: 'the word enable followed by a 1 or a 0 will turn MIDI and Audio' on or off in connected subpatchers. `mute~` disables only the signal objects in a subpatcher. `poly~`'s `mute` turns off signal processing for one instance. None of them stops ordinary messages, timers or Jitter objects inside a `p` or abstraction.
- **Advice for Max:** Give every heavy Max abstraction an enable inlet that gates its own clocks and inputs at the top, and sets `@enable` on its GL objects. For audio, load it in `poly~` and mute the instance. There is no one-box equivalent, so make it a convention.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=795 (Actors Reference: User Actor On/Off)> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/5000692142-optimizing-for-speed-making-isadora-run-faster>
- id: `isadora--switching-a-whole-sub-patch-off-from-inside-it`

### A checklist for a black render (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Errors and status as data
- **There:** `Why is My Render Black`
- **What it does:** A short docs page walks through a black render in order: show a checker background to see whether anything draws, add a light or a constant material, render a cube map to look in every direction, check near and far planes, check the render flags at each level, check alpha.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world @erase_color`, `jit.gl.camera @near_clip / @far_clip`, `jit.gl.material`
- **Checked:** No equivalent page among the Jitter userguide topics read (video, video_engine, textures, graphics_processing, render_passes, depth_layer_blend, jxs_file_format, geometry). jit.world refpage: erase_color, enable. jit.gl.camera refpage: near_clip, far_clip, lens_angle. patching/JITTER_JS_PATCHING.md is cited in CLAUDE.md as listing four silent black-render traps for JS matrix code.
- **Advice for Max:** Write the same kind of ordered checklist into patching/MAX_PATCHING.md: a non-black erase_color to see whether anything draws, the context name each object draws to, enable, near and far clip, texture size, lighting.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Why_is_My_Render_Black>
- id: `touchdesigner--a-checklist-for-a-black-render`

### Component inputs that state and check what they expect (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `In CHOP`, `Out CHOP`, `In TOP`, `Out TOP`
- **What it does:** The input operator inside a component has a label shown on hover. It can require a set number of channels and raise an error when the input does not match, and it can keep only the channels with given names.
- **Closest in Max:** `inlet`, `outlet`, `print`
- **Checked:** inlet and outlet refpages: attributes comment, cool (inlet), style and tricolor; nothing that checks what arrives. Userguide 'Abstractions' and 'Subpatchers': the comment attribute becomes the hover text. print refpage: level 2 (Error) messages also reach any error object in the patcher.
- **Advice for Max:** In an abstraction, check the input directly under the inlet (route by message type, zl.len for list length) and report a bad input with print @level 2, so it does not fail silently further down.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/In_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Out_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/In_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Out_TOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--component-inputs-that-state-and-check-what-they-expect`

### Debug displays that show out-of-range and per-pixel values (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Scaling, clipping and ranges
- **There:** `Ops.Ui.VizTexture`, `Ops.Ui.VizTextureTable`, `Ops.Ui.VizArrayTable_v2`, `Ops.Ui.VizObject`, `Ops.Ui.VizLogger`, `Ops.Ui.VizTrigger`
- **What it does:** Viz ops sit on a cable and pass the data through while showing it in the patch. VizTexture can animate colours that fall outside 0 to 1, show one channel, pick a mip level and print the value at a pixel. VizTextureTable lists pixel values as a table. VizObject shows any object with a search field. VizTrigger counts triggers.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.pwindow`, `jit.fpsgui`, `jit.cellblock`, `dict.view`, `jit.gl.texture tomatrix`, `print`
- **Checked:** jit.pwindow refpage: 20 attributes (size, interp, planemap, srcrect and others); none marks out-of-range values or reads a pixel. jit.fpsgui refpage: reports fps, type, dim and planecount. jit.cellblock refpage: takes jit_matrix and shows cell values. jit.gl.texture refpage: a tomatrix message copies a texture into a matrix. dict.view refpage: shows a dictionary, no search.
- **Advice for Max:** When a float texture carries data (positions, velocities), a jit.pwindow tells you almost nothing. Read it back into a small float32 matrix and look at the numbers in jit.cellblock, and keep that probe in the patch marked as debug. A 'texture probe' abstraction doing this is worth building.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.VizTexture> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.VizTextureTable> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.VizArrayTable_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.VizObject> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.VizLogger> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.VizTrigger> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.VizNumberBar>
- id: `cables-gl--debug-displays-that-show-out-of-range-and-per-pixel-values`

### Errors and validity reported on the op and on output ports (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `op.setUiError`, `Has Errors`, `Expression Valid`, `Found`, `Valid Index`, `Ops.Number.FilterValidNumber`, `Ops.Gl.Shader.ErrorMaterial`
- **What it does:** An op can show a hint, a warning or an error on its own box, with a red dot for errors. Many ops also output their own status: Has Errors on the custom shader, Expression Valid on the math expression, Found on object lookups, Valid Index on array reads, Not a number on string parsing, Has Error and a message on the video player. Filter ops keep the last valid value.
- **Closest in Max:** `Max Console`, `error`, `print @level`, `dict contains`
- **Checked:** error refpage: 'Listens for and reports Max errors as message output', switched on with a 1; it reports all errors, not one object's. print refpage: level attribute with Print, Warn, Error and Bug styles. dict refpage: contains answers 0 or 1 on the third outlet, a separate question from get. Repo CLAUDE.md opening section: Max accepts unknown attributes and missing files without an error at the object.
- **Advice for Max:** When writing a v8 or v8ui object, give it a status outlet (ok, or an error word and text) and have it say when input was rejected, the way cables ops do. In a patch that must run unattended, add one [error] object feeding a visible comment so console errors are seen.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.CustomShader_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.MathExpression> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.ObjectGetNumber_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Array.ArrayGetNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.String.StringToNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Textures.VideoTexture_v4> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.FilterValidNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Json.ParseObject_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Shader.ErrorMaterial> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Templates.UiTestOp> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_callbacks/dev_callbacks>
- id: `cables-gl--errors-and-validity-reported-on-the-op-and-on-output-ports`

### Loading status as values and triggers (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In Max, better elsewhere · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Start-up, loading and initial values
- **There:** `Ops.Cables.LoadingStatus_v2`, `Ops.Cables.LoadingJob`, `Ops.Cables.Ops.OpsLoadingStatus`, `Ops.Html.Utils.LoadingIndicator_v2`, `Ops.Html.FontsLoaded`
- **What it does:** One op reports whether anything in the patch is still loading, a progress number, the list of open jobs, and a trigger when the first load is finished. A patch can add its own job while some condition is true. Loader ops each have Loaded or Loading outputs.
- **Closest in Max:** `loadbang`, `loadmess`, `buffer~ (right outlet)`, `jit.movie asyncread`, `dspstate~`
- **Checked:** Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md (from the userguide's patcher_lifecycle topic): loading runs in eight phases and loadbang fires fifth; a buffer~ with a file argument loads during object creation, later replace messages are asynchronous. buffer~ refpage: right outlet sends a bang 'when File Read or File Write operation completed'. jit.movie refpage: asyncread reads in the background. loadbang and loadmess refpages: fire when the patch opens, with no notion of media still loading. No patch-wide progress object was found.
- **Advice for Max:** Do not start a piece from loadbang alone. Collect each loader's own 'done' signal (buffer~'s right outlet, jit.movie's read report) and open a gate when all have arrived. A reusable 'wait for N loads' abstraction with a progress outlet would cover what LoadingStatus does.
- **Notes:** The exact message jit.movie sends when a read finishes was not read from its refpage; check it before wiring.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Cables.LoadingStatus_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Cables.LoadingJob> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Cables.Ops.OpsLoadingStatus> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Html.Utils.LoadingIndicator_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Html.FontsLoaded> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Html.FontFile_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Texture_v3>
- id: `cables-gl--loading-status-as-values-and-triggers`

### A start value on any input, applied on activation and pushed through the patch (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Start-up, loading and initial values
- **There:** `Init`, `Enter Scene Value`, `Enter Scene Trigger`, `2.0 Actor Processing Mode`
- **What it does:** Any input can be given an 'Init' value that it takes each time its Scene starts. Since version 2 that value also makes the actor execute, so results travel downstream. Without Init, an input keeps the value it had when the Scene was left. Enter Scene Value and Enter Scene Trigger are the patched alternative.
- **Closest in Max:** `loadmess`, `loadbang`, `pattr`, `object arguments`
- **Checked:** Read `loadmess` (sends its typed message at load, with a `defer` option), `loadbang`, and `pattr` (`autorestore`, `initial`). The userguide's patcher_lifecycle page says a patcher is initialised in phases, with objects created before any loadbang or loadmess fires, and subpatchers firing before their parents.
- **Advice for Max:** A Max creation argument is Isadora's old behaviour: it sets the object silently and nothing downstream hears about it. When later objects or displays must start in agreement, send the value through the control with `loadmess`. When they need not, the argument alone is enough and an extra `loadmess` is clutter.
- **Notes:** The Isadora author's own account of why silent initial values caused trouble is in the KB article.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=118 (Tutorial 8: Initializing Property Values)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=483 (Actors Reference: Enter Scene Trigger / Enter Scene Value)> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000026371-understanding-the-new-isadora-v2-0-actor-processing-mode>
- id: `isadora--a-start-value-on-any-input-applied-on-activation-and-pushed-through-the-patch`

### Evaluation by pulling from the end of each chain, top-to-bottom for ties (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `2.0 Actor Processing Mode`, `General Service Task`, `Cycles`
- **What it does:** Isadora works in cycles. Each cycle it starts from actors at the end of a chain and executes whatever is upstream of each input. When several links feed one input, the upstream actors run from top to bottom of the screen and the last one to produce a value wins. Every input holds its latest value, and a trigger input fires when any new value reaches it.
- **Closest in Max:** `trigger`, `bangbang`, `defer`, `scheduler`
- **Checked:** The repo's `patching/MAX_PATCHING.md` (promoted from Max's userguide) says Max walks the graph depth-first within one event and that `trigger` fires right to left; the repo's `CLAUDE.md` quotes `trigger`'s refpage for the same order. Neither page was re-opened this session. Max pushes each message as it happens; there is no per-cycle pass and no stored value on a cold inlet until something sets it.
- **Advice for Max:** Do not carry Isadora habits into Max: position on screen is not a safe way to order things, several cords into one inlet arrive as separate events, and an inlet has no value until it receives one. Make order explicit with `trigger`, and convert values to bangs explicitly with `t b`.
- **Notes:** The KB article describes version 2; the v4 manual does not restate the algorithm.
- **Source:** <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000026371-understanding-the-new-isadora-v2-0-actor-processing-mode> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=115 (Tutorial 8: property types and triggers)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=200 (Preferences: General Service Task)>
- id: `isadora--evaluation-by-pulling-from-the-end-of-each-chain-top-to-bottom-for-ties`

### Performance figures as values inside the patch and on the operator panel (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Errors and status as data
- **There:** `Performance Monitor`, `FPS`, `Monitor`, `Stage Preview`, `Grapher`
- **What it does:** Performance Monitor outputs the target frame rate, the actual frame rate, the patch cycles per second and the share of time spent on video. An FPS control puts the frame rate on the operator's panel. Monitor and Stage Preview show any stream or a whole output as a thumbnail with a frame-rate cap. Grapher plots values over time as a video stream.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fpsgui`, `jit.world getrunning_fps`, `jit.pwindow`, `Matrix Probe`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `jit.fpsgui` displays fps, ms per frame, and a matrix's type, dimensions and plane count for whatever passes through it. `jit.world`: `getrunning_fps` 'will return the actual frames per second rendered by this context'. The userguide's debugging page describes event, signal and matrix probes on patch cords, enabled from the Debug menu.
- **Advice for Max:** Put a `jit.fpsgui` in the presentation view of any show patch, and poll `getrunning_fps` to raise a visible warning when it falls below target. Max's probes cover Isadora's hover-over-a-link preview, but only while editing.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=643 (Actors Reference: Performance Monitor)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=854 (Controls Reference: FPS)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=859 (Controls Reference: Monitor)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=880 (Controls Reference: Stage Preview)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=516 (Actors Reference: Grapher)>
- id: `isadora--performance-figures-as-values-inside-the-patch-and-on-the-operator-panel`

### Routing and bypass that stop the upstream work (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Bypass and stopping unused work
- **There:** `Gate`, `Router`, `Selector`, `exec src`, `bypass`, `active`, `visible`
- **What it does:** Gate, Router and Selector have a hidden 'exec src' input. Set to 'gated', every actor upstream of a closed path stops executing, and the links turn red to show it. Separately, nearly every video effect has a 'bypass' input, Projector has 'active', and Movie Player has 'visible', which unloads the movie when off.
- **Closest in Max:** `gate`, `switch`, `jit.gl.slab @bypass`, `jit.gl.pix @bypass`, `enable (jit.gl objects)`, `metro`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. Max objects send messages onward when they receive them, so a closed `gate` below a chain does not stop the chain computing. `jit.gl.slab` has `bypass` ('the input is passed through to the output without processing'); `jit.gl.pix` lists `bypass` with no description. The GL group page gives every GL object `enable` ('drawing is enabled'). The matrix-operator group page lists no bypass attribute.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max, close the path at the top: put the `gate` above the expensive chain, or stop the `metro` that drives it. A gate at the bottom saves nothing. Use `@bypass` on slabs and `@enable 0` on GL objects, and expose both where the operator can see them.
- **Source:** <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/13000014834-making-your-patch-more-efficient-using-the-gate-selector-and-router-actors> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=685 (Actors Reference: Router)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=652 (Actors Reference: Projector)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=603 (Actors Reference: Movie Player)> · <https://support.troikatronix.com/support/solutions/articles/5000692142-optimizing-for-speed-making-isadora-run-faster>
- id: `isadora--routing-and-bypass-that-stop-the-upstream-work`

### Toggle whose starting state is declared: on, off, or as last saved (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Start-up, loading and initial values
- **There:** `Toggle`
- **What it does:** A trigger-driven on/off switch with an 'init' input: 'on' or 'off' sends that state every time the Scene starts, and 'none' keeps whatever state was saved with the document.
- **Closest in Max:** `toggle`, `loadmess`, `pattr`, `parameter_enable`
- **Checked:** Read refpages. `toggle` has no start-state attribute of its own; it has `parameter_enable` ('setting initial parameter values in Max'). `pattr` has `autorestore` (output the last-saved value at load) and `initial` (restore this value instead). `loadmess` sends a typed message at load.
- **Advice for Max:** Decide for every stateful control in Max which of the three it is: always starts off (nothing needed), always starts at a value (`loadmess` through the control), or restores (`pattr` or parameter mode). Isadora makes the choice visible on the actor; in Max it is spread over three mechanisms, so say which one in a comment.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=789 (Actors Reference: Toggle)>
- id: `isadora--toggle-whose-starting-state-is-declared-on-off-or-as-last-saved`

### Wireless sends on numbered, typed channels that also carry video (Isadora)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `Broadcaster`, `Listener`
- **What it does:** Broadcaster sends any value, including a video stream, on a channel number. Every Listener on that number in any active Scene receives it. A Listener declares the type it outputs and converts to it, and has a trigger output beside its value. Their main documented use is passing values between Scenes that run at the same time.
- **Closest in Max:** `send`, `receive`, `send~`, `receive~`
- **Checked:** Read `send` and `receive`: names, not numbers; any message; reaches every `receive` of the same name in every open patcher window. The repo's patching notes quote the refpage that the order two receivers fire in is not deterministic.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's names are easier to read than channel numbers; keep them descriptive. The scoping differs: Isadora limits a broadcast to active Scenes, while Max's `s`/`r` reach every open patch, so anything loaded twice needs `#0` or `---` in the name.
- **Source:** <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=415 (Actors Reference: Broadcaster)> · <https://troikatronix.com/files/isadora-manual.pdf#page=566 (Actors Reference: Listener)>
- id: `isadora--wireless-sends-on-numbered-typed-channels-that-also-carry-video`

### A project is a folder, and one command collects every outside file into it (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `.madproject`, `Collect External Resources...`, `Export Project To`, `Resources Folder`
- **What it does:** A project is a folder holding the project files, auto-saves and a resources folder. Collect External Resources copies every referenced movie, sound and image into it, so the folder can be moved to the show computer or archived whole.
- **Closest in Max:** `Projects (userguide topic 'projects')`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topic projects: a Max Project collects patchers, media, code and externals, is saved automatically, and puts its own files ahead of the global search path. Manage Project offers Consolidate (copy all dependencies into the project folder) and Archive (one .maxzip file). Whether a media file named only inside a message box counts as a dependency was not checked.
- **Advice for Max:** For anything that will run on another machine, save it as a Project and Consolidate before moving it. Keep media in the project folder and load it by file name, not by an absolute path, so the copy still finds it.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-project-file-structure> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/12.-advanced/project-backup-and-recovery>
- id: `madmapper--a-project-is-a-folder-and-one-command-collects-every-outside-file-into-it`

### Saving during a show is treated as a risk: a countdown, a pause button, and rotating backups (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Controls and panels
- **There:** `AutoSave`, `Max Backup Count`, `Project Backup`, `Crash Recovery`
- **What it does:** AutoSave writes recovery files, never over the project itself, and shows a ten-second countdown first so a performer can cancel it; the manual gives a drop in frame rate during the save as one reason. A toolbar button pauses it until quit. Each manual save keeps the previous version, up to a set number.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattrstorage (savemode, backupmode)`, `Projects`, `Max crash recovery (preference)`
- **Checked:** Read pattrstorage's refpage: 'savemode' chooses between prompting and saving the preset file automatically when the patcher is saved or freed; 'backupmode' keeps a set number of rotating backup files. The userguide says projects are saved automatically, and a page on messages to Max mentions crash recovery as a Max preference.
- **Advice for Max:** Set `@backupmode` on pattrstorage so a bad store during rehearsal can be undone from the previous file. Do not write files while performing: trigger preset saves from an explicit button used between pieces, not from a timer.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/12.-advanced/project-backup-and-recovery> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences> · <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/interface-overview>
- id: `madmapper--saving-during-a-show-is-treated-as-a-risk-a-countdown-a-pause-button-and-rotating-backups`

### Settings are split in two: this computer, and this project (MadMapper)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Files, projects and media management
- **There:** `Application`, `Project`, `Preferences`
- **What it does:** Preferences have two tabs. Application holds what belongs to the machine: audio device, capture devices, graphics options. Project holds what belongs to the piece and travels with it: MIDI devices, OSC ports, DMX routing, engine frame rate, whether to open fullscreen.
- **Closest in Max:** `Max preferences`, `patcher inspector`, `pattrstorage`, `dict`
- **Checked:** Read the userguide topic OSC: the UDP server is switched on either for the whole application in the preferences or for one patcher in the patcher inspector. The video_engine topic: the video engine is an application preference that an object can override with @engine. The mapping topic: mappings belong to a patcher. So Max has both levels, but a patch's own machine choices (which display, which port, which camera) have no set home.
- **Advice for Max:** Keep machine-specific choices out of the piece's presets. Put display index, audio device, MIDI port names and camera names in one small config file (a `dict` read at load) so that moving to another computer means editing one file, and the presets stay about the piece.
- **Source:** <https://docs.madmapper.com/madmapper/6/2.-the-interface/madmapper-preferences>
- id: `madmapper--settings-are-split-in-two-this-computer-and-this-project`

### Addresses are paths, with names scoped by default (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `Network Path`, `Operator Shortcuts`, `Reference`, `Parameter Reference`
- **What it does:** Every operator has a path. A reference can be relative, can name a parent component, or can use one global shortcut. The editor draws a dashed line for each reference, and one form of lookup raises an error when the target is missing.
- **Closest in Max:** `send`, `receive`, `pvar`, `pattrforward`
- **Checked:** send and receive refpages: one name reaches every patcher window. pvar refpage: reaches a named object in the same patcher only. pattrforward refpage: sends a message to a named object, including one inside a subpatcher, abstraction or bpatcher. Userguide 'Abstractions': the #0 prefix gives a number unique to each instance.
- **Advice for Max:** Max's default is the reverse of TouchDesigner's: a name is global unless you scope it. Inside anything loaded more than once, prefix names with #0 (or --- in a Live device). To reach one object inside a nested patcher, use pattrforward with its path instead of a global send.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Network_Path> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Operator_Shortcuts> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Reference> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Parameter_Reference>
- id: `touchdesigner--addresses-are-paths-with-names-scoped-by-default`

### Bypass on every node, and stopping a whole component (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `Bypass Flag`, `Cooking Flag`
- **What it does:** Every operator has a bypass switch that passes its first input straight through, for testing a network without rewiring. A component has a switch that stops everything inside it from computing while keeping its last outputs.
- **Closest in Max:** `@bypass on jit.gl.slab / jit.gl.pix / jit.fx.*`, `mute~`, `pcontrol`, `poly~`
- **Checked:** jit.gl.slab, jit.gl.pix and jit.fx refpages list a bypass attribute. mute~ refpage: turns off the signal objects in a subpatcher, and notes that pcontrol disables all objects. poly~ refpage: mute and bypass messages. Ordinary Max objects have no bypass.
- **Advice for Max:** Use @bypass on GPU effects for a quick comparison and mute~ for audio subpatchers. Give your own abstractions a bypass message, since Max will not supply one.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Bypass_Flag> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cooking_Flag> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Troubleshooting_in_TouchDesigner>
- id: `touchdesigner--bypass-on-every-node-and-stopping-a-whole-component`

### Component-local settings read from anywhere inside (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `Internal Parameters`, `Component Variables`
- **What it does:** A component can hold a private block of typed parameters that any operator inside it reads by a short name. The lookup climbs through parent components until it finds the name. The values are saved with the file.
- **Closest in Max:** `pv`, `value`, `pattr`, `patcherargs`
- **Checked:** pv refpage: a named value shared only within one patcher and its subpatches. value refpage: shared by every object of that name. pattr refpage: stores a value with the patcher (autorestore, initial). patcherargs refpage: outputs the arguments and @attribute pairs typed on the box.
- **Advice for Max:** Use pv NAME for state that belongs to one abstraction and its subpatchers, and keep value or s / r for sharing that is meant to cross patchers. Add a pattr when the value must be saved.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Internal_Parameters> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Component_Variables>
- id: `touchdesigner--component-local-settings-read-from-anywhere-inside`

### Errors collected as data (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Errors and status as data
- **There:** `Error DAT`, `Errors Dialog`, `Troubleshooting in TouchDesigner`
- **What it does:** A node with an error shows a marker, and so does every component that contains it. A dialog lists all current errors and jumps to each one. A table operator logs errors with severity, type and source, filtered by pattern, with a callback per error.
- **Closest in Max:** `error`, `console`, `print`
- **Checked:** error refpage: 'Listens for and reports Max errors as message output', for cases where the Max window is not shown. console refpage: mirrors the Max Console inside a patcher with classfilter, patcherfilter, textfilter and showonlyerrors. print refpage: level 2 posts in the Error style and reaches error objects. Userguide 'Debugging and Probing': watchpoints on cords that print, monitor or break, with stepping.
- **Advice for Max:** In a show patch, put a console @showonlyerrors 1 (or error into a display) where the operator can see it, so a failure is not hidden in a window nobody has open. In your own abstractions report problems with print @level 2.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Error_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Errors_Dialog> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Troubleshooting_in_TouchDesigner>

### Fetching data by reference with a visible link (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Select CHOP`, `Select TOP`, `Select DAT`, `Null CHOP`, `Null TOP`, `Null DAT`
- **What it does:** A consumer names the operator it wants, with name patterns and renaming, and the editor draws a dashed line to it. A Null at the end of a chain gives consumers one stable thing to point at while the chain above it changes. Selecting an image shares the texture and does not copy it.
- **Closest in Max:** `send`, `receive`, `jit.gl.texture`, `jit.gl.slab @out_name`
- **Checked:** send and receive refpages (no visible link). Userguide 'Textures': every texture has a name and objects pass jit_gl_texture <name>. jit.gl.slab, jit.gl.node and jit.gl.camera refpages: out_name / out_names attributes name the output texture.
- **Advice for Max:** End each chain in one named terminal (an s NAME, or a named jit.gl.texture or out_name) and have consumers attach to that name only. Max draws no line for a wireless link, so say in a comment at the receiving end where the sender is.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Select_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Select_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Select_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Null_CHOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Null_TOP> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Null_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Instance>
- id: `touchdesigner--fetching-data-by-reference-with-a-visible-link`

### Frame start and frame end as script hooks (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Execute DAT`, `Run Command Examples`
- **What it does:** A script operator has callbacks for the start and end of every frame, for project start, node creation, exit, play and pause, and devices being plugged in. A delayed call can be set in frames or milliseconds, at the end of the current frame, on wall-clock time, and in a named group that can be cancelled.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `delay`, `pipe`, `deferlow`, `qmetro`
- **Checked:** jit.world refpage: attribute drawbang (prerender or postrender) sets when its middle outlet sends the draw bang; fps and interval apply only when displaylink is off. delay and pipe refpages: time-value syntax, quantize, transport. deferlow refpage: puts a message at the tail of the low-priority queue. Userguide 'Scheduler and Priority'.
- **Advice for Max:** In a Jitter patch, treat jit.world's draw bang as the frame tick. Drive per-frame work from it (drawbang chooses before or after rendering) and not from a separate metro whose phase drifts against the frames. Keep delay and pipe for clock time and deferlow for 'after this event has finished'.
- **Review:** 2026-10-02 (Claude): confirmed in jit.world's refpage: fps and interval 'only have an effect if enable is 1 and displaylink is 0', and transform-defaults.json gives displaylink 1.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Run_Command_Examples> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Frame> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Callback>
- id: `touchdesigner--frame-start-and-frame-end-as-script-hooks`

### Start-up order you can state (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Execute DAT`, `Storage`
- **What it does:** Script operators have separate callbacks for project start, node creation, exit and a device being plugged in or removed. Several start scripts run in the alphanumeric order of their names. Stored values can be given a start-up value that overrides whatever was saved.
- **Closest in Max:** `loadbang`, `loadmess`, `patcherargs`, `pattr`, `closebang`, `freebang`, `midiinfo`
- **Checked:** Userguide 'Patcher Lifecycle': objects, then cords, then parameter initial values, then pattr restore, then loadbang and loadmess, then live.thisdevice, window, patcherargs output, DSP; subpatchers before parents within a phase; closebang and freebang on closing; the page advises not to assume order and to use trigger. midiinfo refpage: autopollinput and autopolloutput report device list changes.
- **Advice for Max:** Use one loadbang into a trigger for anything that depends on order, and rely on the documented phases only between kinds of object. For devices that come and go, midiinfo's autopoll attributes are the MIDI equivalent of a device-change callback.
- **Source:** <https://docs.derivative.ca/Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Storage> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/OP_Execute_DAT> · <https://docs.derivative.ca/Cache_TOP>
- id: `touchdesigner--start-up-order-you-can-state`

### Text that stays in step with an external editor (TouchDesigner)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Text and strings
- **There:** `Text DAT`, `File In DAT`, `Folder`
- **What it does:** A text or table operator can be tied to a file: edits in an outside editor reload into the project and edits in the project write back. File settings also accept web addresses.
- **Closest in Max:** `filewatch`, `jit.gl.slab @autowatch`, `jit.gl.shader @autowatch`, `node.script @watch`, `dict`, `coll`
- **Checked:** filewatch refpage: bangs when a named file changes. jit.gl.slab and jit.gl.shader refpages: autowatch recompiles when the file is edited. node.script refpage: watch attribute. dict and coll refpages: read and readagain.
- **Advice for Max:** Scripts and shaders already watch their files in Max. For data files, wire filewatch into readagain on the dict, coll or text that holds them.

### Explicit left-to-right value sequencing (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Ops.Number.NumberSequence`, `Ops.Number.SequenceNumbers`, `Ops.String.SequenceStrings`, `Ops.Json.SequenceObjects_v2`, `Ops.Gl.Textures.SequenceTextures`
- **What it does:** NumberSequence copies one value to several output ports, setting them in order from left to right, then fires a trigger when all are set. The same op exists for strings, objects and textures. Its page says it is for cases where several ops depend on one value and the order matters.
- **Closest in Max:** `trigger`, `bangbang`
- **Checked:** trigger refpage: 'Outputs any input received in order from right to left'. bangbang refpage: bangs out each outlet in right-to-left order. Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md lines 359 and 618: Max follows one path to its end before the next, which is what makes trigger's order reliable.
- **Advice for Max:** Both tools solve fan-out order with a dedicated object, but in opposite directions: cables counts left to right, Max right to left. When porting a patch, reverse the outlet order, and never leave order to several cords from one outlet.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.NumberSequence> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.SequenceNumbers> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Gl.Textures.SequenceTextures> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Trigger.Sequence>
- id: `cables-gl--explicit-left-to-right-value-sequencing`

### Freeze a live value into the patch (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `Ops.Number.FreezeNumber`, `Ops.String.FreezeString`, `Ops.Website.LocalStorageNumber`
- **What it does:** FreezeNumber has a button that copies the current input to its output and keeps it there, including after the patch is reloaded. FreezeString does the same for text. LocalStorageNumber keeps a number in the browser between sessions under a key.
- **Closest in Max:** `pattr @autorestore`, `message box with prepend set`, `preset`, `dict @embed`
- **Checked:** pattr refpage: autorestore (default 1) outputs the last-saved value when the patcher loads; initial sets a fixed value to restore instead. dict refpage: embed saves the contents with the patcher. Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md: pattr restore is phase 4 of loading, before loadbang.
- **Advice for Max:** To keep a tuned value in a Max patch, bind the control to a pattr (autorestore is on by default) and save the patch, or capture it into a message box with 'prepend set'. A plain number box does not keep its value on reload.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.FreezeNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.String.FreezeString> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Website.LocalStorageNumber>
- id: `cables-gl--freeze-a-live-value-into-the-patch`

### Port declaration decides the control drawn (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `op.inFloat`, `op.inBool`, `op.inDropDown`, `op.inTriggerButton`, `op.inStringEditor`, `setPortGroup`
- **What it does:** When an op is written, the call that creates each input also picks its control in the parameter panel: a number field or slider, a checkbox, a dropdown, a button, a text editor. Ports are grouped under headings with one call. The op pages show these groups as headings in the port list.
- **Closest in Max:** `declareattribute (v8 / v8ui)`, `attrui`, `Inspector`
- **Checked:** Repo CLAUDE.md, 'Match the Generated Control to the Attribute's Value Space': in a v8 script declareattribute's style (onoff, enum, enumindex, rgba) picks the control that attrui and the Inspector draw, and with no style everything is a bare number field. Same file: the Inspector sorts by label, and grouping is done by the patch author with headers. v8 refpage: three attributes of its own (embed, parameter_enable, parameter_mappable).
- **Advice for Max:** Treat style on declareattribute as required, the way a cables author must choose inBool or inDropDown. Max has no call that groups attributes, so group them by a shared leading word in the label, and group the attrui boxes in the help patch.
- **Notes:** The Max side here is from the repo's own rules, which were checked against Max when written; declareattribute was not re-read today.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_creating_ports/dev_creating_ports> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_gui_ui_attributes/dev_gui_ui_attributes> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_creating_ports/dev_ports_trigger/dev_ports_trigger> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Templates.UiTestOp> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Anim.BoolAnim>
- id: `cables-gl--port-declaration-decides-the-control-drawn`

### Sub-patch ports made by dragging a cable (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `Ops.Ui.SubPatch`, `Ops.Ui.PatchInput`, `Ops.Ui.PatchOutput`, `SubPatchOp`
- **What it does:** A sub-patch gets a new port when a cable is dragged onto its 'create port' port. The port takes its type and name from what was connected. Inside, PatchInput and PatchOutput hold the other ends.
- **Closest in Max:** `patcher`, `inlet`, `outlet`, `patcherargs`
- **Checked:** inlet refpage: the comment attribute 'Sets the mouse-over Assistance message for the inlet' and can only be set in the Inspector. patcher refpage: no attribute for port names or types. Max inlets are untyped and unnamed unless the author fills in the comment. Repo CLAUDE.md asks for that comment on every inlet and outlet.
- **Advice for Max:** A Max subpatcher's ports say nothing by default, so fill in the comment on every inlet and outlet with the message type and purpose. That hover text is the only place the 'type' of a Max port lives.
- **Notes:** The PatchOutput page carries the line 'Currently not working'; the SubPatch page describes it as working.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.SubPatch> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.PatchInput> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Ui.PatchOutput> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/patchingops/subpatchops>
- id: `cables-gl--sub-patch-ports-made-by-dragging-a-cable`

### Typed ports with named conversion ops (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Encapsulation: ports, arguments, on and off
- **There:** `Ops.String.NumberToString_v2`, `Ops.String.StringToNumber`, `Ops.Boolean.ParseBoolean_v2`, `Ops.Boolean.BoolToNumber_v2`, `Ops.Number.Integer`
- **What it does:** Every port has a type, and a cable joins like to like. Changing type is always a visible op whose name carries both types, such as NumberToString. StringToNumber has a 'Not a number' output. Number ports are floats; an integer is a marked kind of number port.
- **Closest in Max:** `int / float message types`, `tosymbol`, `fromsymbol`, `sprintf`, `number / flonum`
- **Checked:** Userguide integers_vs_floats.json: many objects run in an integer mode or a float mode chosen by whether an argument has a decimal point; an integer-mode object drops the fraction, and the page calls this 'a common source of bugs', with / and scale as examples. tosymbol, fromsymbol and sprintf refpages: the conversion objects. Userguide conversion.json: a cheat sheet of conversions between messages, signals and matrices.
- **Advice for Max:** Max will not stop a float going into an integer object; it truncates without a word. Write a decimal point on every argument that should be float (* 1., scale 0. 127. 0. 1.), and put a flonum on the cord while testing.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.String.NumberToString_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.String.StringToNumber> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Boolean.ParseBoolean_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Boolean.BoolToNumber_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.Integer> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Boolean.IfTrueThen_v2> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/guidelines/guidelines>
- id: `cables-gl--typed-ports-with-named-conversion-ops`

### Value cables fire only on a real change (cables.gl)

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `onChange`, `setRef`, `Ops.Number.TriggerOnChangeNumber_v2`, `Ops.Math.Delta`
- **What it does:** An op's onChange callback runs when a number or string port gets a different value. The docs say arrays and objects must be sent with setRef so that a change is seen even when it is the same array. TriggerOnChange ops turn a change into a trigger.
- **Closest in Max:** `change`, `speedlim`, `onebang`
- **Checked:** change refpage: outputs a number only if it differs from the stored one; extra outlets report 0-to-non-zero and non-zero-to-0. Repo patching/MAX_PATCHING.md line 381: a fast source into v8 or file I/O needs speedlim, qlim or onebang. In Max every message is passed on, repeats included.
- **Advice for Max:** Max objects re-send the same value as often as they are asked to. Put a [change] after any source that repeats (a snapshot~, a sensor, a UI object banged every frame) before it reaches v8, a dict write or a Jitter object.
- **Notes:** The cables statement 'only called if the value actually changed' is on the dev_ops page in the paragraph about unlinking a port; the setRef note supports the general reading.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Number.TriggerOnChangeNumber_v2> · <https://cables.gl/op/Ops.Math.Delta> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_ops/dev_ops> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_callbacks/dev_callbacks> · <https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_creating_ports/dev_ports_array/dev_ports_array>
- id: `cables-gl--value-cables-fire-only-on-a-real-change`

### An output object with fader, mute and DSP switch built in (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Filters and DSP conventions
- **There:** `out~`, `out4~`, `out8~`, `out.mc~`, `gain~`, `gain2~`, `level~`, `setdsp~`
- **What it does:** out~ is the audio output with a level slider, a mute button and a DSP toggle in one box; moving the slider turns audio on. The fader law is quartic by default, with dB and linear as options, and a maximum gain can be set. gain~ and level~ are the same faders without the output, with MIDI learn and state saving.
- **Closest in Max:** `ezdac~`, `live.gain~`, `gain~`
- **Checked:** Refpages. ezdac~: output and on/off button, no level control. live.gain~: 'Decibel volume slider and monitor', range -70 to 6 dB, with metering. gain~: slider with interp and scale attributes.
- **Advice for Max:** The repo's rule already gives the Max answer: a long live.gain~ before ezdac~. That is two boxes for what out~ does in one, and it adds a meter. Nothing to change; the point is that an example patch should always end in a level control, which ELSE enforces by making it the output object.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/out~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/out.mc~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/gain~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/level~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/setdsp~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.01-The.Basics/05-Gain.Adjustment/3.Quartic.[gain~].[out~].pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--an-output-object-with-fader-mute-and-dsp-switch-built-in`

### Faster-than-real-time rendering from inside the patch (plugdata (ELSE))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **There:** `batch.rec~`, `batch.write~`
- **What it does:** Send a duration and the object runs the audio engine as fast as the CPU allows, recording the result to a sound file or into an array, then returns to normal. Built on Pd's fast-forward message.
- **Closest in Max:** `sfrecord~`, `dsptime~`, `adstatus`
- **Checked:** Max user guide topic 'Non-real-time Processing': select the NonRealTime driver in the Audio Status window; hardware input and output are disabled and the audio thread runs as fast as possible; a patch needs a way to start audio, a way to watch progress (dsptime~) and a way to stop.
- **Advice for Max:** Max does this by switching the audio driver, not by a message to one object, so the whole patch renders offline and there is no sound while it runs. Record with sfrecord~ and stop from dsptime~.
- **Source:** <Documentation/9.else/batch.rec~-help.pd> · <Documentation/9.else/batch.write~-help.pd> · <Documentation/12.live-electronics-tutorial/Part.07-Sampling.Delay.Granulation/29-Sampling(buffer)/4.Record/3.Batch.record.pd>
- id: `plugdata-else--faster-than-real-time-rendering-from-inside-the-patch`

### Messages typed into the object box after a semicolon (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `gemargs`, `rectangle`
- **What it does:** Any Gem object can carry start-up messages in its box, after a semicolon: 'rectangle 4 3; draw line; width 3'. They are sent when the object is made. gemargs lets an abstraction read its own arguments and such messages the same way.
- **Closest in Max:** `patcherargs`, `loadmess`, `attrui`
- **Checked:** No refpage was needed for the Max side of this: Max object boxes take @attribute value pairs, which the repo's CLAUDE.md covers (attributes in the box; patcherargs for abstractions; loadmess for messages). A message that is not an attribute cannot be typed into a Max box.
- **Advice for Max:** Gem's form works for any message; Max's works only for attributes, but those can be read back and shown in attrui. When a Max object needs a start-up message that is not an attribute, that is the case for a loadmess, and one loadmess can carry several values.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/Gem-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/gemargs-help.pd> · <Abstractions/Gem/gemargs.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--messages-typed-into-the-object-box-after-a-semicolon`

### Save the window to an image file at a chosen resolution (plugdata (Gem))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Textures and image formats
- **There:** `pix_write`, `pix_writer`, `pix_snap`, `snapshotHD`, `gemframebuffer`
- **What it does:** pix_write saves the window to a numbered TIFF or JPEG on a bang or automatically every frame. The snapshotHD example abstraction replaces the gemhead and renders the same scene through a framebuffer at any size, for instance 4000 x 2000, to a file.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.world`, `jit.gl.node`, `jit.gl.asyncread`, `jit.matrix`
- **Checked:** Read jit.world (output_matrix: scene 'read back to an internal matrix'), jit.gl.node (capture with adapt and dim), jit.gl.asyncread (reads the context back through pixel buffer objects), jit.gl.texture (tomatrix) and jit.matrix (exportimage: png, tiff or jpeg).
- **Advice for Max:** For a print-size still in Jitter: put the scene in a jit.gl.node @capture 1 @adapt 0 @dim <w> <h>, send the texture to a jit.matrix, then exportimage. Do not raise the window size to get a bigger file.
- **Source:** <Documentation/14.gem/pix_write-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/pix_writer-help.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/09.SnapshotSave.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/09.SnapshotSaveHD.pd> · <Documentation/14.gem/examples/02.advanced/snapshotHD.pd>
- id: `plugdata-gem--save-the-window-to-an-image-file-at-a-chosen-resolution`

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **There:** `pd-name`, `obj`, `msg`, `connect`, `disconnect`, `clear`, `vis`, `namecanvas`, `pdcontrol sendcanvas`, `menusave`, `loadbang`
- **What it does:** Any window accepts 'obj x y name args', 'msg', 'connect', 'disconnect', 'clear' and 'vis' at the receiver pd-<name>, so one patch can write another. Objects are addressed by creation order.
- **Closest in Max:** `thispatcher`
- **Checked:** thispatcher refpage: the script message gives 'dynamic control over object creation, deletion, sizing and positioning, and patching', with objects addressed by scripting name.

### Fan-out order that cannot be seen, and an editor command that fixes it (Triggerize) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Logic, gates and comparisons
- **There:** `trigger`, `t`, `Triggerize`
- **What it does:** When one outlet feeds several inlets, Pd fires in the order the cords were made, which the patch does not show. Triggerize (Ctrl+T) on the source object inserts a [t a a ...] with the cords in their current order.
- **Closest in Max:** `trigger`
- **Checked:** patching/MAX_PATCHING.md: Max fires several cords from one outlet right to left by position; trigger refpage. No Max menu command of this kind was looked for.
- **Advice for Max:** In Max the order is visible but still easy to get wrong when boxes move. A converter or verifier step that replaces order-dependent fan-outs with an explicit trigger is the Max version of Triggerize and worth building.
- **Source:** <Documentation/1.manual/resources/chapter2.htm> · <Documentation/2.control.examples/03.connections.pd>
- id: `plugdata--fan-out-order-that-cannot-be-seen-and-an-editor-command-that-fixes-it-triggerize`

- **There:** `loadbang`, `message`, `text`

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence high · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Names and scope: send, receive, variables
- **There:** `$0`, `$1`, `send`, `receive`, `array define`, `value`
- **What it does:** $0 in an object box is a number unique to the patch, in any patch, so local names need no abstraction. It does not expand in message boxes; the value is passed in through [f $0].
- **Closest in Max:** `#0`, `pv`, `patcherargs`, `---`
- **Checked:** Userguide abstractions page: #0 is replaced by a number unique to each abstraction instance, and in a message box only at the start of a word. CLAUDE.md table 'Every Global Name Inside a Copied Thing Is Per-Instance'. Pd manual 2.11.1 states Max's #0 works only inside abstractions.
- **Advice for Max:** No change: the repo's per-instance naming rule already covers Max. Porting note: Pd's $0 in a top-level patch has no Max twin; use pv or plain names there.

### Reload every script object with one message, state kept (pdx.lua) (plugdata (Pd vanilla))

- **Max:** In both, done differently · confidence low · unreviewed
- **There:** `pdluax reload`, `pdx.lua`, `prereload`, `postreload`, `pd-remote`
- **What it does:** The message '; pdluax reload' reloads the code of all Lua objects in all open patches without re-running their constructors, so their state survives. A text editor can send it over UDP on save.
- **Closest in Max:** `v8`, `js`
- **Checked:** CLAUDE.md 'Embed the Script in Every v8 Box': autowatch reloads a v8 script from its file; Max caches scripts for the session. Whether a v8 reload keeps object state was not checked.

- **What it does:** Dropping a file on the editor converts it to a form the ops read: FBX to GLB, HDR to an RGBE PNG, CSV and MIDI to JSON, a point-cloud text file to JSON. No asset-conversion step was found in Max's file handling.

### Per-op CPU profiler and a GPU statistics op (cables.gl)

- **Max:** Unsure · confidence medium · unreviewed
- **Theme:** Profiling and live views of the running graph
- **There:** `Profiler`, `Ops.Gl.Performance`, `Ops.Gl.PerformanceMeasure`, `Ops.Debug.StopWatch`, `Ops.Debug.ProfileGL`
- **What it does:** A profiler in the Tools menu lists CPU use per op and per subpatch. The Performance op shows frame rate, CPU and GPU milliseconds per frame, and counts of shader binds, draw calls, mesh uploads and render-to-texture passes.
- **Closest in Max:** `jit.fpsgui`, `cpuclock`, `adstatus`
- **Checked:** refpage names and digests (1,940 .maxref.xml files under C74): 'fps' gives jit.fpsgui (FPS meter); 'cpu' gives cpuclock; adstatus reports audio driver settings. userguide (C74/docs/userguide/content): 'profil' appears in 4 files, all about the OpenGL core profile or unrelated; none describes profiling a patch. jit.world has a running_fps attribute.
- **Notes:** No per-object CPU profile or GPU draw statistics were found for Max. The userguide search was by keyword only.
- **Source:** <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/01_tools/tools> · <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/01_tools/02_profiler/profiler_howto> · <https://cables.gl/docs/9_performance_optimization/01_tools/01_performance/performance>
- id: `cables-gl--per-op-cpu-profiler-and-a-gpu-statistics-op`
